## 20.165 *** THE FULL RECORD DID NOT FIX THE HANG. THE ROSTER FIX REPRODUCES, BUT A ***
## *** FORGED PEER BREAKS setup:orbit EITHER WAY. STOP FORGING; GO WATCH THE REAL ***
## *** ADMISSION ON THE PEER CHANNEL. (2026-08-29 ~02:1x, boot p2(103)) ***

Boot p2(103) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-103.md, gate PASS + L14; client `ba6013a2d08cbd46`, mac only,
rig control). The member record was built by copying 0xF8 bytes of a LIVE record (index 0,
self's) and substituting the xuid, replacing p2(102)'s 8-byte partial write.

RESULT - the pre-named SECOND CONTENT NEGATIVE fired:
```
inject result=called member=1 members_now=1 slot=1 machine_now=0x846C8338F7D022E6
Adding player xuid=0x110000130aa9ec5   (self)
Adding player xuid=0x110000130aa9ec6   (the rig)
last state: setup:orbit        <- black screen with moving frames, never reaches in_world
```

CLAIM 1 - THE ROSTER FIX REPRODUCES (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION, twice now). Both p2(102) and
p2(103) named the peer's xuid. 20.104's closure is not a one-off. The admission thesis -
slot plus member record makes the adoption path name a peer - is solid.

CLAIM 2 - COMPLETENESS WAS NOT THE CAUSE. 20.164 inferred the freeze came from writing 8
of ~0xF8 meaningful bytes into a record flagged PRESENT. A verbatim copy of a live record
changed nothing: same stall at setup:orbit. That inference is REFUTED and should not be
carried forward.

CLAIM 3 - WHAT THIS ACTUALLY MEANS, and it is a scope problem, not a bug. The injection
makes the client believe it has a peer. The client then tries to USE that peer during
activity setup - and the peer we forged does not exist to the networking layer. The record
carries SELF's address block (the documented approximation in BOOT_BRIEF_p2-103), so any
consumer that routes or compares on it sees two records claiming one address. Forging an
entry in a table is cheap; forging a PARTICIPANT that survives being talked to is not.
Note the symptom shifted from a hard freeze (p2(102)) to a black screen with moving frames
(p2(103)) - the client is alive and rendering, waiting on something, which is consistent
with waiting on a peer that cannot answer.

CLAIM 4 - U7 SAYS STOP. Two forge attempts, two broken clients, one boot each. The rule is
to escalate to the instrument rather than take a third swing, and there is an obvious one
we have never pointed at this question. p2(96) proved the type-0x0A admission join never
reaches the SERVER - but the two clients have had DTLS peer channels established in both
directions since 20.144, so they ARE talking to each other directly. The admission
exchange, if it happens at all, happens THERE, and TOOLS.md already registers the
instrument: `tcpdump -i en0 ... udp port 3097` (filter BOTH ways, read by size and cadence
since the payload is encrypted).
That reframes the question from "how do we forge a peer" to "what does the real admission
exchange look like, and why does it not complete" - which is answerable without touching
client memory and without another boot that breaks a machine.

STATE: mac disarmed (admission_inject=false) and restored to the write-free
`5d7e6bd61e078c24`. Rig never armed. Both known-good. The injection code stays in the tree,
switched off; it is not wrong, it is just insufficient on its own.

WHAT IS BANKED AND SHOULD NOT BE RE-DERIVED: the slot creator's full ABI and semantics
(20.162), that a fabricated a7 is accepted at creation AND by the adoption consumer
(20.163/20.164), the complete slot and member-record geometry, and that slot+record makes
the roster name a peer (20.164, reproduced here).

## 20.164 *** THE ROSTER NAMED THE PEER. 20.104 IS CLOSED AND THE ADMISSION THESIS IS ***
## *** PROVEN END TO END - AND THE SAME BOOT FROZE AT setup:orbit. THE RECORD IS ***
## *** REAL ENOUGH TO BE READ AND TOO INCOMPLETE TO SURVIVE. (2026-08-29 ~01:4x) ***

Boot p2(102) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-102.md, gate PASS + L14; client `dfd0721abce6f1af`, inject
armed on the MAC ONLY, rig control). Both halves: slot creation via the game's own
creator, then the member record.

THE RESULT (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION):
```
inject result=called member=1 members_now=1 slot=1 ret=69011201
       machine_now=0x846C8338F7D022E6 id=steamid:76561198776753862#d499def495
census peer=1 ok=1 state=5 machine=0x846C8338F7D022E6 members=1 first_idx=1
       first_xuid=0x110000130AA9EC6 first_flags=0x003D
Adding player xuid=0x110000130aa9ec5     <- self
Adding player xuid=0x110000130aa9ec6     <- THE RIG
```

CLAIM 1 - 20.104 IS CLOSED. The mac's roster named the PEER's xuid. That question - "who
calls Adding player, and why does it only ever name self" - has been open since 20.104
and is now answered by execution: the caller is add_candidates via the peer-adoption arm,
it has no self/peer filter, and it named the peer the moment the peer had a slot AND a
member record. The roster-caller lane's chain is confirmed end to end, including its
INFERRED reserve->admit dependency.

CLAIM 2 - A FABRICATED a7 SURVIVES THE CONSUMER LAYER. 20.162 left open whether the
per-session machine id had to be the peer's real value. It does not: the value used was
the peer's SESSION-FORM machine id (0x846C8338F7D022E6), and the adoption path accepted
it, matched the peer, and logged its xuid. BOOT_BRIEF_p2-102's second content negative
did NOT fire. The p2(63) fabricated-id hazard did not bite HERE - see CLAIM 3 for where
it did.

CLAIM 3 - AND THE BOOT FROZE AT setup:orbit. The injection is early (session setup); the
client then reached `setup:orbit`, launched activity selection, and hung - the log tail is
`application_state` flapping active/partial, the signature of an unresponsive window. It
never reached `activity:in_world`. So the fix works and is not yet usable.
CAUSE (INFERRED, not proven): the member record is INCOMPLETE. ADMIT writes 48 bytes of
address block across three movups (+0x3c00, +0x3c10, +0x3c20; 20.159 raw PHASE 6). The
injection wrote EIGHT - the xuid - and left +0x3c10/+0x3c20 zero, on the stated principle
of "write only what the adoption path reads, so a wrong guess stays inert" (20.157).
THAT PRINCIPLE WAS WRONG HERE, and the error is instructive: setting flags 0x3D sets bit
0, which marks the record PRESENT. A present record is visible to EVERY consumer, not
only the one whose reads we mapped. Writing a subset is only safe while nothing can see
the record; the present bit is exactly what removes that protection.

STATE: mac disarmed (admission_inject=false) and restored to the write-free
`5d7e6bd61e078c24`; rig never armed. Both known-good.

NEXT, and it is the same discipline that made the slot half work. Fill the FULL 48-byte
address block by copying it from a live record - member record 0 is self's, present and
complete every boot - and substitute only the identity, exactly as a5/a6 were handled for
the slot (20.162/20.163). Replay-with-substitution has now succeeded twice and
partial-synthesis has failed twice; prefer it. Then re-arm on the mac alone and watch for
`setup:orbit` surviving.
OPEN, and worth stating: we do not know WHICH consumer hangs. If filling the block does
not fix it, the next step is a caller-capture or a probe on the record's other readers
rather than another guess at its contents.

## 20.163 *** THE INJECTION WORKS AT THE SLOT LAYER: A FABRICATED a7 WAS ACCEPTED AND ***
## *** SLOT 1 NOW EXISTS, state=5, NAMING THE PEER. BUT members=0, SO THE ROSTER IS ***
## *** UNCHANGED - THE SECOND HALF IS REQUIRED. (2026-08-29 ~01:1x, boot p2(101)) ***

Boot p2(101) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-101.md, gate PASS + L14; client `530c56b85d229b5f` both
machines, inject armed on the MAC ONLY, rig as control). First call INTO a game function
in this chain. No crash; the mac reached the Tower normally.

RESULT (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION):
```
inject result=called slot=1 ret=69011201 machine_now=0x846C8338F7D022E6
       id=steamid:76561198776753862#515decabd494d021
census peer=1 ok=1 state=5 machine=0x846C8338F7D022E6 members=0 first_idx=-1 first_xuid=0x0
```

CLAIM 1 - THE SLOT HALF WORKS, AND THE FABRICATED a7 WAS ACCEPTED. The game's own creator
took our arguments, returned non-zero, and populated slot 1 with the injected machine id
and state=5 - the same state slot 0 (self) carries and the state the tick gate wants. The
identity substitution produced a well-formed string naming the RIG's steam id. So
20.162's candidate (iii) holds AT THIS LAYER: a7 does not have to be the peer's true
per-session value for slot creation to succeed. Whether a later consumer matches on it is
still open.

CLAIM 2 - AND IT IS NOT SUFFICIENT. `members=0`, `first_idx=-1`. The roster is unchanged:
`Adding player` still names only the mac's own xuid (0x110000130AA9EC5, twice); the rig's
0x110000130AA9EC6 never appears. The world swap is unchanged too - PUB56 still never gets
its slice-set-switch task. This is BOOT_BRIEF_p2-101's THIRD CONTENT NEGATIVE, pre-named,
and it CONFIRMS 20.158's two-half analysis by execution: the adoption path walks the new
slot, reads members=0, and logs nothing for it. A slot without a member record is a peer
the client can see but has nothing to say about.

CLAIM 3 - THE MACHINE IDS ROTATE WITHIN A BOOT (new, incidental, and it matters). The
census caught slot 0 changing identity mid-session:
```
peer=0 state=5  machine=0xB161F691D70F6AA4
peer=0 state=10 machine=0x0
peer=0 state=5  machine=0x666F87E51973009A
```
So the a7-family id is not merely per-boot - it is re-keyed during a session. Anything
that pins a peer by that value must expect it to move. This further argues that a7 is a
transient handle rather than an identity, which is consistent with a fabricated one being
accepted.

NEXT, AND IT IS SMALL. Enable the second half - the member record - which we already
wrote in p2(97) and which has been sitting orphaned because it had no slot to attach to
(20.158). It must write, for member index 1 (index 0 belongs to slot 0):
```
  rec+0x3c00 = the peer's xuid (0x110000130AA9EC6 from the mac's side)
  rec+0x3c30 = 0x3D          <- NOTE: the REAL value measured in p2(97), not ADMIT's
                                documented 0x1D. Use what a live record carries.
  rec+0x3b78 = 0             (membership byte; flagsA is `== 0`)
  slot1+0xe8 = 1, slot1+0xf0 = 1   (member count and index list)
```
The three fields are the entire surface the adoption path reads (20.157), the geometry is
verified at runtime (p2(97) ok=1), and the member-record array is far enough from the slot
array in address terms (+0x3c00 offsets) that the shared base does not alias. If the
roster then names the rig, 20.104 closes and the admission thesis is proven end to end.
If it does not, the fabricated a7 fails at the CONSUMER layer even though it passed at the
creation layer, and the real value must be obtained.

## 20.162 *** THE SLOT CREATOR IS FULLY CHARACTERISED. THE "MYSTERY 0x50 BLOB" IS AN ***
## *** ASCII IDENTITY STRING. ONE UNKNOWN LEFT: WHERE A PEER'S PER-SESSION MACHINE ***
## *** ID COMES FROM. (2026-08-29 ~00:5x, boot p2(100)) ***

Boot p2(100) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-100.md, gate PASS + L14; client `c12eca660aeadcb8` BOTH
machines). The p2(99) crash re-run with a7/a8 logged as values. No crash, both clients
reached the Tower, five arg lines per machine as the brief claimed.

CLAIM 1 - THE FULL CALL, MEASURED (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION, both machines):
```
  rcx = arena (netmgr+0x860)      edx = slot index (0)     r8d = kind 5   r9d = 86657
  a5  = POINTER to an ASCII identity string, null-padded in a fixed buffer:
          mac "steamid:76561198776753861#222173074d011059"
          rig "steamid:76561198776753862#de639d825a68a7dd"
  a6  = POINTER to a STATIC GLOBAL - the same RVA 0x20D4A67 on BOTH machines
        (mac 0x1420D4A67, rig 0x7FF758DD4A67) - leading with the machine id in the
        SESSION-STRING form of 20.153 (mac DC0FA61D6307F015, rig E622D0F738836C84),
        then parameters; its tail differs per machine and per run.
  a7  = A VALUE, not a pointer: the machine id being registered. It equals
        machine_after exactly on both (mac 0xD32C745C65330BE1, rig 0x985C20472070242F).
        THIS is what p2(99) dereferenced and died on.
  a8  = A VALUE: a timestamp (mac 0xD74D, rig 0x9FEC).
```

CLAIM 2 - THE "MYSTERY BLOB" IS SOLVED (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION). 20.160 named the
0x50-byte block at slot+0x50 as the one field a hand-written injection could not
reproduce. The slot48 dump shows it plainly:
```
  slot+0x48 = 0x0000000000000056        (the 0x56 of 20.159's store list, confirmed live)
  slot+0x50 = "steamid:76561198776753861#222173074d011059" null-padded
```
It is simply a5's identity string copied in. There is no opaque blob. The slot layout is
now known end to end: +0x48 = 0x56, +0x50 identity string, +0xA4 = 6, +0xB0 = 8,
+0xB8 = the session-form machine id (from a6's head), +0xC8 = a7.

CLAIM 3 - CONSEQUENCE FOR THE INJECTION. Both roads from 20.160 are now open, and the
safe one is preferred: CALL the real creator 0x1417692E0 with rcx = arena, edx = 1,
r8d = 5, r9d = 86657, a5 = a synthesised identity string for the peer, a6 = a synthesised
block carrying the peer's session-form machine id, a7 = the peer's machine id, a8 = a
tick. The game's own code then writes every field correctly, including whatever sets
flags bit 5 that ADMIT's recorded stores never explained (20.158). No hand-forging.

THE ONE REMAINING UNKNOWN, stated precisely rather than guessed. a7 is per-boot and
per-machine - it differed on every boot on record (mac 0x1D34C7FAE1AFD61D p2(98),
0x7DC8B0D3D51BC392 p2(99), 0xD32C745C65330BE1 p2(100)) - so it is a per-session id, not
hardware-derived, and OUR SERVER HAS NEVER SEEN IT. The server's machine ids are the
other family entirely (machine=0xA60FDC012E083054 / machine_rev=0x5430082E01DC0FA6). For
the mac to create a slot naming the rig, it must obtain the RIG's a7. Three candidates,
none tested:
  (i)  the clients already exchange it on the peer channel (established both ways since
       20.144) and we could read it there;
  (ii) the client reports it somewhere our server can capture, in a field we have not
       decoded;
  (iii) it does not need to be the peer's real value at all - it may be a purely local
       key, in which case a consistent fabricated value works. NOTE this is exactly the
       p2(63) "never return fabricated ids to client enumeration loops" hazard and must
       not be assumed; 20.153 showed the client's tracking lookups are machine-id-keyed,
       so a wrong value plausibly fails those lookups even if the slot is created.
Deciding between them is the next question, and (iii) is testable cheaply BECAUSE the
injection is switch-gated: create the slot with a fabricated a7 and read whether the
roster names the peer.

PROCESS NOTE. The p2(99) crash cost one boot and produced two of the five fields anyway;
the remediation cost one more and produced all five. Both were cheaper than writing the
injection against the apply-site ABI, which is what the crash was ultimately protecting
against.

## 20.161 *** SELF-INFLICTED: p2(99) CRASHED BOTH CLIENTS AT CHARACTER SELECTION. I ***
## *** TOOK THE ABI FROM CALL SITES p2(98) HAD JUST PROVEN NEVER RUN. THE CAPTURE ***
## *** STILL ANSWERED ITS QUESTION BEFORE IT DIED. (2026-08-29 ~00:3x) ***

INCIDENT. Boot p2(99) froze/crashed both clients at character selection, before orbit.
Both machines were rolled back to p2(98) `5d7e6bd61e078c24` (verified by hash on each)
and are known-good again. Server never changed and needed no rollback.

CAUSE (VERIFIED, and it is mine). The p2(99) delta over p2(98) was a one-shot hexdump of
the slot creator's four stack arguments. I derived the argument shapes from the two call
sites inside fn 0x141781800, the message-30 apply - THE SITES p2(98) HAD JUST PROVEN
NEVER EXECUTE (20.160) - and applied that reading to the join-gate call site
0x141772A99, which actually fires and which I never opened. Its arguments differ:
```
  0x141772a6d  call 0x1402fe650        ; the timestamp helper
  0x141772a72  mov  [rsp+0x38], rax    ; a8 = A TIMESTAMP, not a pointer
  0x141772a79  mov  [rsp+0x30], rbx    ; a7 = a bare register, never shown to be a pointer
  0x141772a7e  lea  rax, [rbp+0x1b0]
  0x141772a90  mov  [rsp+0x20], rax    ; a5 = pointer
  0x141772a85  mov  [rsp+0x28], rsi    ; a6 = pointer (return of 0x1402ffd20)
```
Only a5 and a6 are pointers. Dereferencing a7 killed the process. The mac log pins it
exactly: `tag=a5 ok=1`, `tag=a6 ok=1`, then `application_state: Suspend state changed
from [active] to [partial]` with no a7 line. The `__try/__except` guard did not save it -
do not rely on SEH inside a `noexcept` detour to make a bad dereference survivable.
LESSON, and it is the same one this session kept finding in OTHER people's work: a call
site that does not run cannot tell you the shape of one that does. 20.160 literally
recorded "finding a call site is not finding a call" one entry earlier, about this same
pair of functions, and I then took an ABI from the dead ones.

WHAT IT ANSWERED ANYWAY (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION, before the crash):
```
a5 -> "steamid:76561198776753861#05b9854d131d8769"   (an identity STRING)
a6 -> DC0FA61D6307F015 00000000 ... 40010000 005A0005 ...
      (leads with the machine id in the SESSION-STRING form of 20.153, then parameters)
```
Both are PEER-SPECIFIC identity data, which is the third content negative
BOOT_BRIEF_p2-99 pre-named. So an injection CANNOT replay these pointers as-is: it must
build an a5 string and an a6 block naming the OTHER machine. Both values are already
known to us - the peer's steamid and its machine id are in every boot's logs - so this
is tractable, and it is a synthesis job rather than a copy job.
STILL UNKNOWN: a6's full layout past the machine id, and the produced slot's 0x50-byte
blob (the slot48/slot88 dumps never ran).

REMEDIATION. The dumper now hexdumps a5 and a6 only and logs a7/a8 as raw VALUES, never
dereferenced. Built, NOT deployed - the machines stay on p2(98) until the user chooses to
boot again. The p2(97) injection remains switched off and still has no valid target.

PROCESS NOTE FOR THE NEXT PROBE: before dereferencing any argument, read the call site
that the previous boot PROVED executes, not the one that is convenient to read. Where a
register's provenance is not established, log it as a value.

## 20.160 *** THE SERVER-SIDE ROAD IS CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT: THE MESSAGE-30 APPLY ***
## *** NEVER REACHES THE SLOT CREATOR. ONLY THE JOIN GATE DOES, WITH KIND 5. ***
## *** kind=10 CREATES NOTHING. (2026-08-29 ~00:1x, boot p2(98)) ***

Boot p2(98) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-98.md, gate PASS + L14; client `5d7e6bd61e078c24` BOTH
machines; server unchanged p2(96)). Log-only observer on the peer-slot creator
0x1417692E0 recording its CALLER return address. Injection still switched off.

RESULT, both machines, identical:
```
MAC caller=0x1772A9E idx=0 kind=5  flag=86657      before=0x0 after=0x1D34C7FAE1AFD61D
    caller=0x176929A idx=0 kind=10 flag=0          before=0x0 after=0x0
    caller=0x176929A idx=1 kind=10 flag=2489159556 before=0x0 after=0x0
RIG caller=0x1772A9E idx=0 kind=5  flag=86657      before=0x0 after=0x9CE86D46E739E86A
    caller=0x176929A idx=0 kind=10 flag=0          before=0x0 after=0x0
    caller=0x176929A idx=1 kind=10 flag=1331643107 before=0x0 after=0x0
apply-path callers (0x1782C9E / 0x1782D36): ZERO occurrences on either machine.
```

CLAIM 1 - THE SERVER-SIDE ROAD IS CLOSED (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION). 20.159 found two
slot-creator call sites inside fn 0x141781800, the message-30 apply, and opened the
possibility that a body we already publish could create a peer slot. Neither call site
executes in a real co-located session, on either machine. They are reachable code that
this flow never enters. The BOOT_BRIEF_p2-98 first content negative fired exactly as
pre-named: only the join gate creates slots, so the fix is NOT server-side and the DLL
injection is the road. 20.159's narrow reopening of the body-shaping class is hereby
closed again, this time by measurement rather than by the 20.113 blanket.
SELF-CORRECTION: 20.159 read the call sites and inferred a road from them. Finding a
call site is not finding a call. Measuring first was right.

CLAIM 2 - KIND SEMANTICS CORRECTED (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION). The creating call carries
**kind=5**, not 10. The two kind=10 calls (from fn 0x141769230) write NOTHING - machine
id stays 0 across both. So kind 0x0A is not "create a slot", and the roster-caller lane's
type-0x0A framing plus my own 20.159 reading of `r8d=0xa` as a create-kind are BOTH
wrong. Whatever kind 10 does, it is not slot creation.

CLAIM 3 - SLOT 1 IS THE PEER'S PLACE, AND IT IS FREE (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION). The kind=10
caller probes idx=0 AND idx=1 on both machines; idx=1 is touched and left empty. Combined
with p2(97) (slot 0 = self, fully populated), the target for an injection is unambiguous:
slot index 1, currently unused, on each machine, naming the OTHER machine.

WHAT IS NOW FULLY SPECIFIED BY MEASUREMENT (no inference left in these):
  - the creator, its geometry, and the field that marks a live slot (20.159, p2(97) ok=1)
  - which caller creates, and with what kind (this entry)
  - that slot 0 is self and slot 1 is free (p2(97) + this entry)
  - the flags a REAL member record carries: 0x3D, not ADMIT's documented 0x1D (20.158)
  - that no server-published body can reach any of it (this entry)

NEXT, AND THE OPEN PROBLEM IN IT. The injection must now create the SLOT (what the join
gate does at kind=5) and then the member record (what ADMIT does), for slot 1. Two ways,
and the choice is not yet decidable:
  (a) CALL the real creator 0x1417692E0. Safest for correctness - the game's own code
      writes every field, including whatever sets flags bit 5 that ADMIT's recorded
      stores do not explain. But it takes EIGHT arguments, four of them stack pointers to
      structures the join gate builds, and their contents are unknown.
  (b) HAND-WRITE the slot fields. We know the store list from 20.159 (+0x48=0x56, a
      0x50-byte blob at +0x50, +0xa0/+0xa4, +0xb0=8, +0xb8, +0xc8=machine id, +0xd8) but
      NOT the blob's contents.
Both stall on the same unknown, so the next probe is ARGUMENT CAPTURE on the creator: our
observer already sits on it, so record the four stack arguments and the memory they point
at during the real kind=5 call. That says whether the structures are peer-specific (in
which case a replay must substitute, not copy) or session-generic (in which case replay
with a changed index and machine id is viable). Cheap, one boot, and it is the last
unknown before the injection can be written correctly rather than guessed.

## 20.159 *** COLLISION RESOLVED: 0x1417692E0 IS ONE FUNCTION AND IT IS THE PEER-SLOT ***
## *** CREATOR. TWO OF ITS FIVE CALLERS ARE INSIDE THE MESSAGE-30 APPLY - THE HANDLER ***
## *** FOR THE BODIES WE PUBLISH. (2026-08-28 ~23:5x, static, no boot) ***

Continues 20.158. Static only; nothing executed.

CLAIM 1 - THE COLLISION IS RESOLVED: ONE FUNCTION, TWO NAMES FOR ONE STRUCTURE
(VERIFIED-BY-READING). fn 0x1417692E0 (0x1417692E0..0x14176970A, 1066B) takes an object
in RCX and an index in EDX and derives THREE parallel arrays from that ONE base:
```
  0x14176932b  imul rbx, rdx, 0xb8   ; rbx = obj + idx*0xb8      (the slot array)
  0x141769332  imul r13, rdx, 0x38   ; r13 = obj + idx*0x38
  0x14176931d  add  rcx, 0x1758      ; rcx = obj + 0x1758 + idx*0x120  (peer states)
  0x14176933a  lea  rax,[rax+rax*8] / shl rax,5   ; idx*288
```
That is EXACTLY roster-caller's geometry (arena = netmgr+0x860; slots stride 0xb8; states
at arena+0x1758 stride 0x120) and EXACTLY 20.151/20.152's ("the peer table at state+0x1758,
stride 288"). Therefore 20.152's `state` and roster-caller's `arena` ARE THE SAME OBJECT,
and the "managed-session member table" and the "netmgr peer slots" are THE SAME 0xb8-stride
array. Neither lane was wrong about the function; they named one structure two ways.
RESIDUAL DISCREPANCY, recorded not resolved: 20.152 put the member array at state+0x40,
while this function bases the 0xb8 array at obj+0. 20.152 derived +0x40 from a different
function (removal 0x1417aa4f0). Do not rely on the +0x40 base without re-reading it.

CLAIM 2 - IT IS THE PEER-SLOT CREATOR (VERIFIED-BY-READING). Its stores through rbx
(= obj + idx*0xb8) include `mov qword [rbx+0xc8], rax` at 0x1417695bc - the peer slot's
MACHINE ID, the exact field the p2(97) census read as the marker of a live slot - plus
+0x48 = 0x56, a 0x50-byte movups block at +0x50..+0x9f, +0xa0/+0xa4, +0xb0 = 8, +0xb8,
+0xd8. This answers 20.158's open question: this is what creates a netmgr peer slot.

CLAIM 3 - AND TWO OF ITS CALLERS ARE IN THE MESSAGE-30 APPLY (VERIFIED-BY-READING).
Five references, all direct calls:
```
  0x141769295  in fn 0x141769230 (164B)
  0x141772a99  in fn 0x141772440 (3513B)   <- also calls ADMIT at +0x857: the join gate
  0x141782c9e  in fn 0x141781800 (7374B)   <- THE MESSAGE-30 MEMBERSHIP APPLY
  0x141782d36  in fn 0x141781800            <- same
  0x1417abc90  in fn 0x1417ABC40 (127B)
```
fn 0x141781800 is the apply that 20.145/20.152 identified as the client's message-30
handler - the consumer of the bodies THIS SERVER PUBLISHES. Both of its call sites pass
`r8d = 0xa`, i.e. KIND 10 - the same "type-0x0A" the roster-caller lane attributed to a
client-to-client join request. On this reading 0x0A is a KIND ARGUMENT to slot creation,
not necessarily a distinct wire message, which is consistent with p2(96) finding no id-10
on the wire at all.

CONSEQUENCE, and it is a strategic one. roster-caller CLAIM 3 states "the BAP type-12
membership the fork publishes does NOT write this array". The apply DOES call the slot
creator, twice. That claim is now in doubt. It does NOT follow that our bodies can create
a slot today - the calls are guarded - but it does mean the SERVER-SIDE route is not
excluded by construction, and the p2(97) DLL-injection plan is no longer obviously the
only path.
DISCIPLINE NOTE: 20.113 retired "make a peer appear by SHAPING a BAP body" as a CLASS,
on the strength of blind member-row sweeps. This is not a resumption of that. It is a
specific, code-grounded call path with a named guard, which is different evidence, not a
re-derivation from the old evidence. Reopen narrowly, on this path only; the blind sweeps
stay retired.

CLAIM 4 - THE GUARD (VERIFIED-BY-READING, cause NOT yet established). The slot-create
block in the apply runs only when `byte [rsp+0x51] == 0` (0x141782ae4, `jne 0x1417831b8`
skips it). That local is zero-initialised and set to 1 at 0x141781ca5 by an earlier block
which also emits a retail log line (formatter 0x140092e50 -> emit 0x14035d860, with
r9d = dword[r15+0xc]) and whose string comes from one of two sources depending on whether
r14 is null. The `je 0x141782c3f` immediately above the block is only the log-verbosity
test, not a guard. WHAT CONDITION REACHES THAT REJECT BLOCK IS OPEN - the trace stops
here, deliberately, rather than guess a fifth time.

NEXT (cheap, and it settles the strategy): hook fn 0x1417692E0 itself and log
(obj, index, kind, machineId) per call. It fires on every slot creation from every
caller, so one boot shows how many slots each client creates, with which kind, and from
which path - and whether the message-30 apply ever reaches it for a peer. If it does, the
fix is server-side in the body we already publish; if it never does, the DLL injection is
the road and it must create the slot, not just the member record (20.158).

## 20.158 *** THE ADMISSION CENSUS REFUTES THE INJECTION TARGET: THE PEER HAS NO ***
## *** PEER SLOT AT ALL. SLOT 0 IS SELF, FULLY POPULATED. THE GAP IS SLOT CREATION, ***
## *** NOT MEMBER-RECORD ADMISSION. (2026-08-28 ~23:5x, boot p2(97)) ***

Boot p2(97) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-97.md, gate PASS + L14; client `4951f587da240dba` BOTH
machines; server unchanged p2(96)). The injection shipped SWITCHED OFF and the census
half ran alone, exactly as the brief specified (`install ... census=1 inject=0` on both,
zero `stage=inject` lines).

RESULT, both machines, identical shape:
```
MAC  peer=0 ok=1 state=5 machine=0x2A2A21C73725326F members=1
     first_idx=0 first_xuid=0x110000130AA9EC5 first_flags=0x003D
RIG  peer=0 ok=1 state=5 machine=0x65556BF27BB8602  members=1
     first_idx=0 first_xuid=0x110000130AA9EC6 first_flags=0x003D
MAC own xuid 0x110000130aa9ec5   RIG own xuid 0x110000130aa9ec6
```

CLAIM 1 - THE GEOMETRY HOLDS (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION). `ok=1` on both: the roster-caller
lane's static reading is correct at runtime - arena netmgr+0x860, peer stride 0xb8,
machine id +0xc8, member count +0xe8, member list +0xf0, state stride 0x120 at +0x1758,
member record stride 0x1a8 with the address block at +0x3c00. The second content
negative did not fire.

CLAIM 2 - AND IT REFUTES THE INJECTION TARGET (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION). Peer slot 0 holds
SELF on each machine - its first member record carries that machine's OWN xuid - and it
is FULLY POPULATED (members=1). Across eight scanned slots there is no second one: the
other machine has NO PEER SLOT AT ALL.
CONSEQUENCE: the injection as built would have been a silent NO-OP. It searches for a
slot that names a machine id but holds zero members, and no such slot exists. It would
have found nothing, written nothing, and we would have read the unchanged roster as
"the injection failed" instead of "the injection had no target". The census boot bought
exactly the thing it was for - though the failure it caught was a no-op, not the freeze
I was guarding against.

CLAIM 3 - THE ADOPTION ARM IS NOT BROKEN (VERIFIED). It walks the slots, finds slot 0,
passes the selection (`cmp state,3 / jne -> selected`; state is 5), reads that slot's
member record and logs its xuid. That IS the 20.104 symptom: the roster names only self
because self is the only peer slot there is. Nothing in the adoption path needs fixing.

CLAIM 4 - THE FLAGS ARE NOT WHAT ADMIT ALONE WRITES. A real record reads flags 0x003D
(bits 0,2,3,4,5); the ADMIT stores the roster-caller lane recorded produce 0x1D (bits
0,2,3,4). Bit 5 is set by something else on the path. Forging 0x1D would have produced a
record measurably unlike a real one - a second thing the census caught before the write.

WHERE THIS MOVES THE ROAD. The blocker is one level earlier than the lane framed it:
peer-SLOT creation, not member-record admission. A slot must exist (machine id at +0xc8,
a state, a member list) before any member record matters. In the lane's chain that is the
RESERVE step (0x1417692E0) rather than ADMIT (0x141777EC0), and the injection must
therefore do BOTH halves - create the slot, then populate its record - not just the
second. The p2(97) injection code stays in the tree, switched off; it needs its target
rebuilt, not its switch flipped.
OPEN: what creates a netmgr peer slot. field_xref on netmgr+0x928 (slot 0's machine id)
returns only 32-bit writes in unrelated ranges - false positives, the field is a qword -
so the writer needs a different approach than a displacement scan. The lane's reserve
candidate 0x1417692E0 is the entry point to read next. NOTE the address collision to
avoid conflating: 20.152 called 0x1417692E0 the managed-session "member-add"; whether
that is the same function serving both structures, or two readings of one address, is
UNRESOLVED and must be settled before building on it.

## 20.157 *** ROUTE A IS CLOSED: THE ADMISSION JOIN NEVER REACHES THIS SERVER. AND ***
## *** THE Z-LEG POLLERS DO RUN - THEY REJECT ON dword[obj+0x524] == -1, NOT ON THE ***
## *** +0x200 GUARD. (2026-08-28 ~23:3x, boot p2(96)) ***

Boot p2(96) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-96.md, structural gate PASS; L14 verified PER BINARY - see
the tool note below). Two logging-only censuses, server `b9b0f3823f74d1bf` + client
`d7eef1bafc54ea63` both machines. No behaviour change either side. No stall.

HALF A - THE MESSAGE-ID CENSUS (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION, and it redirects the plan).
Every id the group-host pump received, whole boot, both clients co-located:
```
  id=12 joinComplete      dispatched=1
  id=26 peerEstablish     dispatched=1
  id=29 timeSynchronize   dispatched=1
  id=31 peerProperties    dispatched=1
  id=34 playerAdd         dispatched=1
  id=37 playerProperties  dispatched=1
  id=39 parameterRequest  dispatched=1
  stage=msg_unhandled: ZERO LINES
```
NO id=10. NO id=11 (peerConnect). Nothing fell through to migration::consume at all.
CONSEQUENCE: the type-0x0A ADMISSION JOIN (roster-caller lane, blockers-research #2)
does not reach this server, so ROUTE A - a server-side relay of client-to-client
session messages - CANNOT carry admission. Route B (in-process reserve/admit injection
through our own DLL) is the only path to it. This is the content negative
BOOT_BRIEF_p2-96 pre-named as "the one that most changes the plan", and it fired.
SCOPE CAVEAT (do not overstate): the census covers the group-host pump only. It proves
nothing dies silently THERE; it does not prove id 10 reaches no other server surface.
What it does prove is that the relay design in blockers-research #5 cannot reach it.

HALF B - THE POLLER CENSUS (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION, both machines identical).
The two type-3-accepting pollers DO run for the public transition:
```
  n=17  which=B  type=3  gate=0  region=56  regionA=48  regionB=-1
  n=18  which=A  type=3  gate=0  region=56  regionA=48  regionB=-1
```
THE +0x200 SUSPECT IS EXONERATED: `gate=0`, the guard passes. 20.156's "new primary
suspect" was wrong and is retired.
THE REAL REJECT: poller B's next guard is `cmp dword [rcx+0x524], 0x1ff / ja -> skip`
(0x140e25a5f). `dword [obj+0x524]` reads -1 on EVERY poll line, EVERY transition type,
BOTH machines - and -1 unsigned is 0xFFFFFFFF, which is above 0x1ff, so the reject is
taken every single time. The field is never populated at all.
Note the private path does not need it: types 2 and 4 get their phase query from the
CONSTRUCTOR (20.156), so the pollers' rejection is invisible for them. The z-leg is the
only transition that depends on this path, which is why only it stalls.

CLAIM - WHO SHOULD WRITE +0x524 (VERIFIED-BY-READING, not yet a cause). field_xref.py
finds 21 writers; exactly one is in the transition-manager's range: fn 0x140E1D400 at
0x140e1d45f, which writes the +0x524 / +0x528 / +0x52c triple together from its own
arguments. Its guards:
```
  call 0x1412aadf0 ; test al,al ; jne -> skip     (predicate must be FALSE)
  cmp  byte [rdi+0x208], al ; je -> skip           (byte +0x208 must be NON-ZERO)
```
+0x208 is adjacent to +0x209 (the transition type). Whether this setter is simply never
called, or is called and turned back by one of those two guards, is OPEN.

WHERE THIS LEAVES THE ROAD. Half A is the actionable result: admission cannot be fixed
from the server, and the roster-caller lane already mapped the client-side target
(ADMIT 0x141777EC0, the reserve->admit gate, netmgr member records stride 0x1a8,
address block at rec+0x3c00). That is a named end-to-end path that rests on a verified
reading plus tonight's runtime proof, not on a hypothesis. Half B stays open behind it:
the leading (UNPROVEN) hypothesis is that admission also feeds whatever populates
+0x524, in which case one fix clears both; if it does not, +0x524 is chased separately
with fn 0x140E1D400 as the entry point.

TOOL NOTE (gap, not a bug): gate_boot.py --literals requires EVERY declared literal in
EVERY named binary, so it correctly refused a boot shipping instruments in two. Taken as
a structural GATE PASS plus an explicit per-binary literal check (server literals in the
exe, client literals in the DLL, all four present). A per-target literal mapping would
close the gap; not changed mid-flight on a safety tool.

## 20.156 *** p2(95) RUNTIME PROBE: THE PHASE QUERY IS NEVER CALLED FOR THE PUBLIC ***
## *** TRANSITION - BY DESIGN. normal_z_leg IS TYPE 3, THE ONE transitioning:* TYPE ***
## *** THE CONSTRUCTOR SKIPS. THE PHASE BRANCH IS CLOSED. (2026-08-28 ~22:5x) ***

Boot p2(95) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-95.md, gate PASS + L14; client DLL `4cb3887f6ecd669c` BOTH
machines, LOGGING ONLY; server unchanged p2(93)). Log-only detour on the phase query
fn 0x140E22C70, reporting its answer plus both gate bytes. Hot-path safe by
construction (logs only on tuple change, cap 64) - no stall, 3 lines per machine.

RESULT - THE BRIEF'S SECOND CONTENT NEGATIVE FIRED, IDENTICALLY ON BOTH MACHINES:
```
t=58875 Starting transition 'transitioning:orbit' PRV24     -> t=58876 phase=4 region=24
t=80074 Starting transition 'initial_slice_set' PRV48       -> t=80075 phase=2 region=48
                                                             -> t=80111 phase=4 region=48
t=101341 Starting transition 'normal_z_leg' PUB56.56        -> (NOTHING, ever)
```
Every private/orbit transition start is followed by a phase query within 1 ms. The
public one is followed by none. NOT a filter artifact: a phase-4 answer would have
assigned the switch task (as it does for 24 and 48), so the tuple would necessarily
have differed and logged. VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION: the phase query is never invoked for
the public transition.

CLAIM 1 - WHY, AND IT IS DELIBERATE (VERIFIED). fn 0x140E22C70 has exactly one
reference in .text - a tail `jmp` from the wrapper fn 0x140E23240 - and its address is
never taken. The wrapper has five callers, one of which is the transition CONSTRUCTOR
(fn 0x140E2B120, which is why our queries land 1 ms after each start line). The
constructor guards that call:
```
0x140e2b588  cmp  r14d, 2          ; r14 = the transition type
0x140e2b58c  je   -> call phase query
0x140e2b58e  lea  eax, [r14 - 4]
0x140e2b592  cmp  eax, 3
0x140e2b595  jbe  -> call phase query      ; types 4,5,6,7
0x140e2b5a1  jmp  -> SKIP
```
r14 indexes the type-name table at 0x141C27960, which decodes (the pointers there are
UNRELOCATED runtime addresses; the base derives from the 20.152 anchor
0x7ff6b14968e0 == static 0x141ca68e0, giving dump base 0x7ff6af7f0000):
```
 [0] idle                      [6] transitioning:debug_initiated
 [1] transitioning:SENTINEL    [7] transitioning:teleportation
 [2] transitioning:initial_slice_set   CALLED   <- PRV48
 [3] transitioning:normal_z_leg        SKIPPED  <- PUB56, ours
 [4] transitioning:orbit               CALLED   <- PRV24
 [5] transitioning:repair              CALLED
 [8..11] error:SENTINEL / precaching_failed / matchmaking_failed /
         starting_public_current_activity_instance
```
normal_z_leg is type 3 - the ONLY `transitioning:*` type the constructor skips. The
type is stored at `byte [obj+0x209]` (constructor, 0x140e2b382).

CLAIM 2 - THE PHASE BRANCH IS CLOSED. A z-leg's phase is not evaluated at construction
by design, so the whole 20.154/20.155 line - the phase enum, the +0x2bc/+0x2c1 gate,
which half fails - describes the PRIVATE mechanism and does not apply to our case. Do
not resume it. What we learned there stands as a map; it is simply not our road.

CLAIM 3 - WHERE IT MOVES (VERIFIED guards, NOT yet a cause). Of the wrapper's other
four callers, two exclude type 3 and two accept it:
```
  fn 0x140E24A20 : cmp cl,2 / jg  -> out      excludes 3
  fn 0x140E25270 : cmp cl,8 / jge -> out      (further guards unread)
  fn 0x140E244C0 : cmp cl,5 / jg  -> out      ACCEPTS 3
  fn 0x140E25A30 : cmp al,7 / jg  -> out      ACCEPTS 3
```
Neither accepting function ran for region 56 this boot. BOTH of the two that read the
type also gate on a byte not seen before: `cmp byte [obj+0x200], 0 / jne -> out`
(0x140e24a42 and 0x140e25a48). A nonzero +0x200 would silently disable the poll paths,
which is exactly the observed shape: transition constructed, never polled, never
completed. NEW PRIMARY SUSPECT, unproven.
ALTERNATIVE, equally unproven: a z-leg may not be poll-driven at all but event-driven
from the activity layer - enum [11] `error:starting_public_current_activity_instance`
shows the public instance start is a distinct operation with its own error state, and
the recurring `Updating public bubble reservation peer request ... to '0' slots`
(20.132) sits in that same layer.

INSTRUMENT LESSON (mine, not the game's): this probe hooked ONE function and learned
only that it is never called. A probe on the state machine's ENTRY POINTS - the two
type-3-accepting pollers, logging `[obj+0x200]`, `[obj+0x209]` and the region - would
have answered "is it polled at all, and which guard rejects it" in the SAME boot.
Probe the paths that should run, not only the one you suspect.

NEXT: hook fn 0x140E25A30 and fn 0x140E244C0 (both take the object in rcx), logging
type/+0x200/region on change. One boot; it either shows the poll running and rejecting
at a named guard, or not running at all - and the second answer moves the whole
question up into the activity layer.

## 20.155 *** PROVENANCE ALERT: destiny2_unpacked_full.exe WAS DUMPED FROM A HOOKED ***
## *** PROCESS. THE PUBLIC SWITCH-NOW GATE IS TWO BYTES, AND THE PREDICATE BEHIND ***
## *** ONE OF THEM CALLS INTO OUR OWN steam_api64.dll. (2026-08-28 ~22:2x, trace lane) ***

Static trace continuing 20.154, no boot. New instrument registered: RE_scripts/field_xref.py
(struct-field xref, oracle-bearing; see TOOLS.md, and note its disp32-only limit).

CLAIM 1 - THE PUBLIC SWITCH-NOW GATE (VERIFIED, two independent sites). A public
transition reaches phase 4 ('switch-now') only when BOTH hold:
```
cmp byte [obj + 0x2bc], 1      ; is this a public/shared region?
cmp byte [obj + 0x2c1], 2      ; the second gate
```
Read at 0x140e22ff5 (the phase fn 0x140E22C70) and again at 0x140e26d8a (the consumer
fn 0x140E267D0). CORRECTION to 20.154: the fall-through does NOT return 0 - it is
`xor eax,eax / comiss xmm9,xmm7 / setae al / add eax,2`, returning 2 or 3. Phases 2/3
are "still progressing"; our public transition is parked on one of them forever.

CLAIM 2 - THE TOKEN IS NOT THE PUBLIC BLOCKER (VERIFIED). `byte [obj+0x350]` is the
client's own transition token: the constructor does `inc byte [rsi+0x350]` with a
wrap-past-zero guard, which is literally the log's `token advanced to 002`. The
per-slot comparison `byte[obj+0x660][ dword[obj+0x53c] ] == byte[obj+0x350]` is real,
but it sits AFTER the +0x2bc short-circuit, so a public transition never reaches it.
That closes the token candidate for the THIRD time and for the first time on control
flow rather than on values.

CLAIM 3 - THE PROVENANCE ALERT (VERIFIED, and it outlives this lane).
`byte [obj+0x2bc]` is set in the constructor from
`(regionIndex <= 0x1ff) && call 0x140C210F0`. Following 0x140C210F0:
  0x140C210F0: `E9 63 F8 7C BF`  jmp rel32 -> 0x7FF601FE0958   (live-dump addresses)
  0x7FF601FE0958: `FF 25 F2 FF FF FF`  jmp qword [rip-0xE] -> 0x7FF9CD7C6D30
  0x7FF9CD7C6D30 is inside steam_api64.dll (base 0x7FF9CD7A0000) at RVA 0x26D30 -
  and that DLL exports exactly 17 functions, all in RVA 0x1080..0x1640. The target is
  NOT an export, named or ordinal.
A game call site can only reach a NON-EXPORTED address in our module through a runtime
patch (IAT overwrite, Detours, or a vtable slot we populate). CONSEQUENCE, and this is
the part that generalises: destiny2_unpacked_full.exe was dumped from a process with
our DLL already injected, so AT EVERY HOOKED CALL SITE THE FIRST BYTES IN OUR PRIMARY
STATIC REFERENCE ARE OUR OWN PATCH, NOT THE GAME'S CODE. The bytes at 0x140C210F0 are
identical in the unpacked exe and in the live dump, which is how this was caught. Any
past or future lane that disassembled a hooked function from that image read our hook.
U14 (assert artifact provenance) now has a concrete instance; treat a `jmp rel32` at a
function's first byte in that image as a hook until proven otherwise.

OPEN / NOT ESTABLISHED (do not infer these): WHICH hook owns 0x140C210F0 - nothing in
the current fork source names it and config_getter declares no RVA; whether it is still
hooked in today's build; and WHICH half of the gate fails at runtime. The last one is
the only question that blocks a fix, and static analysis of this artifact cannot answer
it - the answer is a runtime read.

NEXT (named): a logging-only probe on fn 0x140E22C70 reporting, per call, its return
value plus `byte[obj+0x2bc]`, `byte[obj+0x2c1]` and `dword[obj+0x2b0]` (the region
index). ~15 lines in the existing hook framework, one boot, and it forks cleanly:
+0x2bc != 1 means the client does not classify PUB56 as public AND the deciding
predicate is our own code; +0x2bc == 1 with +0x2c1 != 2 gives that byte's real value
against the disc/ctng/cntd ladder. Either branch names the fix.

## 20.154 *** THE PHASE IS A COMPUTED RETURN VALUE, NOT A PUBLISHED FIELD: fn ***
## *** 0x140E22C70 ANSWERS 4 ('switch-now') OR 0, AND A PUBLIC TRANSITION ALWAYS ***
## *** GETS 0. THE DECIDING READ IS A PER-MEMBER-SLOT BYTE ARRAY. (2026-08-28 ~21:5x) ***

Boot p2(94) (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-94.md, gate PASS + L14 literals; client DLL
`003baf5ff811ff79` BOTH machines, LOGGING ONLY; server unchanged at p2(93)
`50d1f4cccbad0a56`). Caller-capture (LESSONS 18c) armed on six transition-manager
lines after two publish hypotheses were spent guessing at the switch (U7 escalation).

INSTRUMENT RESULT - all 16 targets fired, all six new ones resolved (mac base
0x140000000; pdata_bounds.py owning functions):
  Transition to Orbit slice-set                0xE22FE0 -> fn 0x140E22C70 (+0x370)
  Transition to private slice-set              0xE22E80 -> fn 0x140E22C70 (+0x210)
  Finished precaching slice-set                0xE21C97 -> fn 0x140E21800 (+0x497)
  slice-set-switch task is not possible yet    0xE26D6E -> fn 0x140E267D0 (+0x59E)
  is being assigned the slice-set-switch task  0xE275C4 -> fn 0x140E267D0 (+0xDF4)
  Stopping transition of type                  0xE2B79E -> fn 0x140E2B660 (chained fragment)
One tight cluster at 0x140E2xxxx = the slice_set_transition_manager class. The
SECOND CONTENT NEGATIVE of the brief did NOT fire: the two simulator arms are 352
bytes apart, genuinely separate call sites, so splitting the target string was right.

CLAIM 1 - THE PHASE IS A RETURN VALUE (VERIFIED, disassembly). fn 0x140E22C70 is
asked for a transition's phase and answers with eax: `mov eax,4` on three paths
(0x140e22e8b, 0x140e22feb, 0x140e2313b - all three are the simulator arms) and
`xor eax,eax` on the single fall-through (0x140e23142). There is no stored phase
variable to publish into. 4 = 'switch-now'; 0 = not yet.

CLAIM 2 - A PUBLIC TRANSITION REACHES NEITHER OUTCOME (VERIFIED, logs + disassembly).
fn 0x140E267D0 holds BOTH consumer outcomes - "not possible yet" at +0x59E and "is
being assigned the slice-set-switch task" at +0xDF4. PUB56 produces NEITHER line on
either machine, across p2(90), p2(93) and p2(94). That is now explained: the phase
answer is 0, so the consumer's phase-gated block is never entered at all. Its own
guards read `byte [r14+0x20a]` (bit 0 / bit 2 / >=0x80 arms) with the phase in ebx
(`cmp ebx,3`; `lea eax,[rbx-5]; cmp eax,2; jbe` = 5..7), i.e. the enum is at least 0..7.

CLAIM 3 - THE DECIDING READ (VERIFIED, verbatim at 0x140e22d28..0x140e22d59):
```
movzx  edx, byte  [rdi + 0x2bc]      ; nonzero routes AWAY from the simulator
test   dl, dl
jne    <no simulate>
cmp    dword [rdi + 0x628], -1       ; == -1 -> other arm
je     <other arm>
movsxd rcx,  dword [rdi + 0x53c]     ; a STORED SLOT INDEX, not a loop counter
movzx  eax,  byte  [rdi + 0x350]     ; a single reference byte
cmp    byte  [rcx + rdi + 0x660], al ; array[myslot] == reference ?
jne    <other arm>                   ; match -> log ':simulator:' -> return 4
```
A byte array at +0x660 indexed by the object's own stored slot number and compared
against one reference byte. That is the shape of the region record's per-member
transition-token lanes (kRegionTokenCount = 32, "one transition-token byte for every
member slot"), which this host writes from `snapshot.transitionToken`.

CORRECTION TO 20.153's DISMISSAL OF THE TOKEN. 20.153 retired the transition-token
candidate on the grounds that we publish token=2 and the client's PUB56 leg reads
`token advanced to 002`. That checked the VALUE and never checked WHICH LANE the
client reads. The read is per-slot (`[rdi+0x53c]`), so the slot index matters
independently of the value, and the candidate is back open. The teleport-block
retirement in 20.153 stands unchanged (it is a mirror of a client message-22 report;
see that entry).

RENDER OBSERVATION (logged, unexplained, NOT acted on). On this boot the mac black-
screened BEFORE its spawn animation (subclass swap did nothing) while the rig entered
cleanly - a visible difference on a boot whose only edit was six strings in a log
observer's target table. Both clients' logs are identical through the state ladder and
both stop at `Finished precaching slice-set 'PUB56.56'`, so nothing in the networked
path diverged. Best available explanation, INFERRED not verified: caller-capture adds
six `strstr` calls per line on the game's own logging thread, which is real work on a
hot path and can shift local timing. If it recurs on a boot WITHOUT the instrument it
is something else. Do not treat this as evidence about the transition.

OPEN (the trace, static, no boot needed): name `[rdi+0x53c]` (member slot index?),
`[rdi+0x660]` (the 32 token lanes?) and `[rdi+0x2bc]` (the flag that routes public
transitions away from the simulator in the first place). Deliverable to follow.

## 20.153 *** THE p2(90) ASYMMETRY WAS A MISREAD: BOTH CLIENTS RAN THE IDENTICAL ***
## *** LADDER AND BOTH STOPPED AT THE SAME PLACE - THE PUBLIC SLICE-SET TRANSITION ***
## *** NEVER TAKES ITS SWITCH TASK. CAUSE: THE HOST PUBLISHES slice=48 WHILE THE ***
## *** CLIENT SITS IN region=56. FIX = p2(93). (2026-08-28 ~21:2x, review lane) ***

Independent review of the 20.145-20.152 chain (HANDOFF_2026-08-28_EVENING_GATE-REVIEW).
Three of its conclusions do not survive the logs.

RETRACTED - THE PEER-PROPERTIES ASYMMETRY (20.152's primary lead). "The rig received the
mac's world-start status; the mac never received the rig's" is a misread of whose steamid
is in the line. EVERY `peer-properties ... accepted` line on BOTH machines names peer #0
with THAT MACHINE'S OWN steamid, in that machine's OWN fireteam (rig ...862 in
fireteam:62084141:87601126; mac ...861 in fireteam:F5195CDD:D1C20A30). Neither client ever
received the other's peer-properties. The relay IS absent - group_host.cpp kPeerPropertiesId
reads the header, logs `stage=properties result=read`, and drops the 408-byte body
(verified-by-reading) - but it is absent SYMMETRICALLY and is not the blocker.

RETRACTED - "THE MAC'S WORLD NEVER INSTANTIATES / BLACK SCREEN". Both clients crossed the
managed-session-start gate (the mac timed out of the 5 s prologue-filler at t=90259 and
carried on, exactly as the rig did), both entered `activity:in_world` (rig t=87390, mac
t=106469) rendering city_tower_social_d2, both citizen-joined PUB56.56 SUCCESSFULLY (rig
t=88187, mac t=107757), both established PUBLIC TARGET EST-Y, both finished precaching
slice-set 56 (rig t=88484, mac t=109243). The state_manager ladders are identical line for
line; the ui_stage ladders are identical and both end at ENUM(47). The mac is ~19 s behind
the rig and that is the whole difference.

THE ACTUAL WALL (verified-by-reading, both logs). After "Finished precaching slice-set
PUB56.56" NEITHER client is ever assigned the slice-set-switch task, and the transition
never completes. Compare the three transitions this boot:
  PRV24 orbit   -> `:simulator: advancing simulated phase directly to switch-now`
                -> `is being assigned the slice-set-switch task` -> `Stopping ... completed`
  PRV48 private -> same three lines
  PUB56 public  -> `Starting a new transition of type transitioning:normal_z_leg ... token
                   advanced to 002` and then NOTHING. No simulator line, no switch task,
                   no completion.
A private or orbit slice-set transition SELF-SIMULATES its phase, so it completes with no
server agreement at all. A public `normal_z_leg` has no simulator arm - it waits for the
authority. Both clients are still rendering PRV48, their own private Tower; nobody has ever
been in the shared instance visually. This is 20.132's "one missing message", now located.

THE CAUSE. Every `ev=activity stage=roster` line in the p2(90) boot reads `region=56
slice=48` - 494 of them, both accounts, for the entire run. `effective_region`
(activity_roster_snapshot.cpp) lets the client's reported region win for `region.index` but
leaves `region.arrival` on the destination's arrival bubble, and BOTH publish paths that
name a slice set read the arrival: the roster's spawn override
(`snapshot.spawnSliceSet = region.arrival`) and the global-activity-state body's
`sliceSetIndex` (activity_global_state_push.cpp resolve_state). Those are the only two
arrival_slice_set callers. Meanwhile activity_membership_query.h documents a reported region
as beating "the destination's own arrival slice set wherever the host must say where the
player is" - the intent was already written down; only the index obeyed it. Region index and
slice-set index share one numbering (bubble x 8; region_reader.h static_asserts; PUB56.56 /
PRV48.48 name form), so a reported region index is directly usable as a slice-set index.

FLAGS (review question 2) - the conclusion holds, both of 20.152's arguments do not.
(a) THE MAPPING IS NOT PROVEN. The client's member descriptor at 0x141ca68e0 carries
[+0]=field, [+4]=tag, [+8]=alignment, [+9]=-alignment, [+0xc]=size - there is NO per-field
record offset; the copier walks fields sequentially with alignment. Fields 11/12 are
1-byte, size 1, align 1 (dumped, verified-by-reading). The gate readers really are
`byte [base + idx*0xb8 + 0xed/0xef]` (0x14177a080 / 0x14177a060, disassembled). But
"fields 11/12 ARE record +181/+183" is an inference from "only 1-byte fields, last two",
not a byte proof, and the one-byte hole at +182 is exactly the smell of a guess.
(b) BOTH EXPERIMENTS ARE VOID. snapshot_builder.h:63 states the invariant: "Member 0 is the
host. The host entry echoes the RECIPIENT's join id and takes the recipient's state ... a
table that names the recipient's id on exactly two entries, both at the same rung, tells it
the join is over once that rung is established." Every recipient appears TWICE - as
members[0] and as members[recipient+1]. p2(92) skipped only the second, so it wrote flags
into one half of a pair the client requires to match; in the solo phase (host + self) every
row it touched was a self-mirror. "The session disappeared while we were joining" is that
invariant breaking, not the flags being rejected. A real peers-only test must skip BOTH
rows. It has never been run, and it is not on the critical path.
(c) The conclusion "the flags are not the lever" stands on the stronger ground that both
clients cross that gate anyway and the wall is elsewhere entirely.

MACHINE-ID WINDOW - CLOSED, NOT A DEFECT. Each client's "Could not find tracking data for
peer 1" names a machine id that is a 5-byte-shifted window over its OWN id (mac own
54:30:08:2E:01:DC:0FA6 vs phantom DC:0F:A6:1D:63:07:F015; rig own 47:08:CF:CA:01:E6:22D0 vs
phantom E6:22:D0:F7:38:83:6C84 - same shape both machines, sharing exactly 3 bytes). This is
the ALREADY-RECORDED `blob[0..7]` vs `blob[5..12]` window pair from p2(89) /
BOOT_BRIEF_p2-87, which the fork already routes around by keying on the account slot instead
of the member key. Non-blocking: both clients released the phantom reservation and continued.
Nothing to change; re-derived here only as independent confirmation of the window map.

p2(93) `9fd3134`: `activity_slice_set_follows_region` (default true, flippable without a
rebuild) makes both publish paths follow the reported region. Settings echo gains
`slice_follows_region`. Brief: BOOT_BRIEF_p2-93.md (gate PASS + L14 literal). Deployed exe
`50d1f4cccbad0a56`; client DLLs UNCHANGED `ae4d41f76b5202a5` both machines. If the roster
lines go to `region=56 slice=56` and the switch task STILL does not appear, the slice set is
not the phase input and the next candidates are named in the brief: the region record's
32-lane transition-token array (kRegionTokenCount, all lanes published as one value) and the
host tail's teleport block (state 3b / token 8b / sliceSet 10b / hash 32b), which this host
has published EMPTY on every boot to date (`teleport_state=0 teleport_slice=-1`, all 482
keepalive lines).

## 20.152 *** GATE MAP + TWO NEGATIVE FLAG EXPERIMENTS: FIELDS 11/12 ARE THE ***
## *** FLAGS (PROVEN), BUT NOT THE GATE'S LEVER - THE RIG CROSSED WITH ZERO FLAGS. ***
## *** THE REAL ASYMMETRY: THE MAC NEVER RECEIVES THE RIG'S WORLD-START STATUS. (2026-08-28 ~21:4x) ***

Lane MS-START-GATE-3 (deep): the message-30 member-row apply is the sibling of the
player apply - writer 0x1417692E0 + data-driven protobuf copier 0x1416e2350 over the
descriptor table 0x141ca68e0, which PROVES member fields 11/12 are the only 1-byte
member fields and the last two (the setup-complete flags). The +0xED/+0xEF puzzle
resolved: the gate accessors read at state+8 while the member array lives at state+0x40,
so those are record bytes +181/+183 - the last two bytes of the 184-byte record, inside
the fork's modeled stride. Map: field 11 (flagA) -> record +181, field 12 (flagB) ->
+183. OPEN: the peer-delta bitstream tail (applied by 0x14176a230) feeding peer-entry
dword @+0xD0 bit 10 - bit-reader not located; the fork's 3 absent tail flags cannot
express it.
TWO EXPERIMENTS, BOTH NEGATIVE on the join: p2(91) fields 11/12=1 on ALL rows and
p2(92) fields 11/12=1 on all rows except the recipient's own BOTH broke the mac's
citizen join ('the session disappeared while we were joining', retry loop; switch-off
restored the join both times - verified-by-execution). Even the HOST row alone flagged
(p2(92) solo phase: members = host + self) broke it. The flags are consumed somewhere
in the join path where nonzero = invalid.
THE OVERRIDING FACT: in the p2(90) boot the RIG CROSSED THE GATE WITH ZERO FLAGS
ANYWHERE. The flags are therefore NOT the gate's lever. Re-reading the p2(90)
differential with the map in hand: the rig received the mac's peer-properties
('fireteam req. peer update ... peer-properties accepted, map status=_precached,
progress=100') and rendered; the mac never accepted the rig's world-start status (its
last world line was the peer fixup for peer '1' in its private session). The mac's
gate waits on the RIG'S world-start status arriving through the managed session's
peer-properties relay - THAT relay is the asymmetry. New primary lead: why does the
mac never receive the rig's peer-properties update (client->server->client relay path
through the group session)? Switch RESTORED TO OFF (baseline = p2(90) state).
Deliverables: RE_output/claims/ms-start-gate3.md (+ -raw.md). p2(91)/p2(92) commits
remain in the tree, switch-gated off. Also: 20.150 was double-assigned (bapdecode
entry + the gate entry now renumbered 20.151) - walk both when citing 20.150.

## 20.150 BAPDECODE.PY: THE PCAP->BAP GENERALIZED DECODER, ORACLE-BEARING.
## Selftest 12/12 (positives + 3 negatives that must fail); --verify re-decodes
## THE 224 GROUND-TRUTH BODIES with 100% field agreement; full --pcap run on
## bap30975_20260828_160741: 224/224 decoded, 0 decrypt failures. (2026-08-28
## ~23:3x, meta/workflow lane; reference implementation = the GAH-REGION-DECODE
## lane's scratch decoders, per the TOOLS.md no-fork rule.)

WHAT IT IS: RE_scripts/bapdecode.py = the registered route-B harvest decoder.
Pipeline: pcap (tshark TCP reassembly) -> BAP outer framing -> svc-25 slot
resolution -> svc-26 key recovery (HMAC-verified) -> AES-128-GCM s2c decrypt ->
svc-9 envelope -> type-12 membership bodies -> region-block bit walk. Modes:
--pcap (full), --streams DIR (pre-split bins), --selftest, --verify.

THE ORACLES (per the "every reader gets an oracle it can fail against" rule):
 - SYNTHETIC: --selftest builds a complete mini BAP session with planted
   token/keys/nonce and a planted type-12 body (bubbles 48/56 advertised,
   planted descriptor + ambassador slots), decodes it, then runs three
   NEGATIVES that must be DETECTED: corrupted svc-26 HMAC -> recovery
   rejected; corrupted GCM tag -> decrypt_fail counted, no ghost body; wrong
   body size -> no shape candidate, decoded=False. (A BitWriter fixture
   builder plants the bits; the positive path asserted exact key/nonce/
   session/descriptor/ambassador values.)
 - REAL: --verify RE_output/scratch/gah_en0 <its decode_report.json> re-decodes
   the lane's 224 ground-truth bodies (streams 4+6, 144+80) and asserts every
   decoded field: 224/224 match, region-56 descriptor head d9aed900ebb92cf2
   intact. Full --pcap against the raw capture: streams 4/6 = 144/80 bodies,
   0 decrypt failures across 1077 sealed frames.

TWO FIXTURE BUGS THE ORACLE CAUGHT DURING DEVELOPMENT (both mine, both in the
TEST not the decoder - which is the oracle working): (1) the fixture sealed
frames ct||tag but the wire format is TAG-FIRST ([0:16]=tag) - the decoder was
right; (2) the size-rejection negative appended a byte at STREAM level, which
never enters the frame payload; rewritten as a direct decode_membership unit
test. Also re-confirmed: NUL-bearing lines sanitize to spaces via
merge_timeline/boot_record's grammar; the envelope +28 (len=size+28) and
BitReader MSB-first layout re-verified bit-exact against 224 real bodies.

INTERPRETER NOTE: bapdecode needs `cryptography` -> miniconda python3 on this
Mac (the /usr/bin/python3 interpreter lacks it; sqlite stays on /usr/bin - the
matrix in ENVIRONMENTS.md now has both directions).

  - the client's established-peers scan, stride 288 / state@+0 = the fork replica's peer
  table geometry, now confirmed from first principles.
- The 4-bit connection-state ladder is NECESSARY BUT INSUFFICIENT: the mac already sees
  the rig at _established, and the gate still does not pass. The state ladder is NOT the
  lever; do not tune it.

  (confirms 20.105) - resolved via the recorded caller-capture RVA 0xC18442 instead.

  timeout path is not fatal; what woke the rig's world post-timeout is part of the
  answer (lane 2's question).
- The missing carrier: a per-peer activity-setup-COMPLETE signal our verbatim-private-
  snapshot public bodies do not publish. Candidate locations: the 8-bit connectionValue
  (replica peer+4, published 0 today) / ~264 unmapped bytes of the 288-byte peer entry /
  a distinct session message.
Deliverables: RE_output/claims/ms-start-gate.md (+ -raw.md). NEXT (lane MS-START-GATE-2,

Fallback if static stalls: caller-capture instrument on 'Waiting for managed-session-
start' + 'Adding player' (client DLL change + boot - LAST resort).

## 20.149 *** p2(90) BOOT: BOTH CLIENTS EST-Y ON THE PUBLIC ROW, PEER CHANNELS ***
## *** ESTABLISHED BOTH WAYS, TOWER PRECACHED - THE MAC'S WORLD-INSTANTIATE ALONE ***
## *** STALLS AT 'WAITING FOR MANAGED-SESSION-START FOR ALL PEERS'. (2026-08-28 ~19:0x) ***

Full boot timeline (p2(90), VERIFIED from both client logs + server log):
1. Mac: private session 200001, claimed region 56, entered a Tower; then citizen-joined
   the public row 200003 (screen black during the world swap - expected); slice-set 56
   loaded, 'public AH instance ready', acked membership 5 on the PUBLIC TARGET AC.
2. RIG: private session 200004 - the p2(90) SEED FIRED on it (200004 seeded 56 from
   200001) and the whole downstream cascade followed: rig acked membership (MEM-4/5,
   the 20.146 ack=0 wall GONE), PUBLIC TARGET joined 200003 EST-Y acked, Tower rendered.
   THE RIG'S LOAD IS CLEAN - the p2(90) fix worked as designed.
3. Peer channels: mac<->rig establishment completed BOTH directions (ids 4 and 9,
   'establishment complete' on both logs). The bdNAT ignore-lines are confirmed harmless.
4. Both dumps: [group_target:50B9AD15:07FB37E5] (same id both sides), peers valid 0x7,
   players valid 0x3. CO-LOCATION HOLDS at the session layer.
THE ONE STALL: the mac's world controller waits at 'Waiting for managed-session-start
for all peers' (t=85286) and never resumes; the rig crossed the same gate. After
'Finished precaching' the mac processed the peer-fixup for peer '1' (machine
54:30:08:2E:01:DC:0FA6, in its private session 200001) then went silent world-wise.
The mac's dump shows the rig's group-session state as _established - the rig's
ACTIVITY-SETUP-COMPLETE status bit never reaches the mac's world controller through the
group-session membership. Root shape: the p2(89) public-row bodies are VERBATIM COPIES
of each account's PRIVATE session snapshot - they do not carry a public-session view
with per-peer activity-setup status. The rig's world-started state has no carrier to
the mac. (The rig rendered because it crossed the gate while its view was already
satisfied - asymmetry consistent with who joined whose snapshot first.)
SECOND OPEN GAP (pre-existing, 20.104): roster UI names PEER xuid=0 on both sides -
'Adding player' fires only for own xuid; same managed-session member-table carrier.
NEXT: static lane - what field/body does the client's 'managed-session-start' gate
read, and how must the public-session membership view publish per-peer setup status;
then shape the public-row bodies (server-side, code we own). Then the roster call site
(same carrier). Then the profile-blob fireteam capture (render milestone).

## 20.147 *** WIRE DECODE OVERTURNS HALF OF 20.146: THE REGION-56 RECORD WAS ON THE ***
## *** WIRE TO THE RIG ALL ALONG (224/224 BODIES). SERVER BODY CONSTRUCTION IS ***
## *** EXONERATED. THE DEFECT IS THE RIG'S OWN REGION NEVER BECOMING 56. (2026-08-28 ~20:3x) ***

Lane GAH-REGION-DECODE (deep, offline). Recovered every membership-carrying connection's
AES-128-GCM key+nonce offline (SignOn wrap keys derive via HMAC-SHA256 from the authored
bootstrap tokens in s1_accept/settings.json; per-boot nonce+sessionKey ride the plaintext
svc-26 envelope, HMAC-verified), then bit-decoded all 224 membership bodies sent to the rig
across both private sessions (20000A/20000B), full window.
VERIFIED: every rig body carries a region-56 record (bubble 7) - descriptor head
d9aed900ebb92cf2 (= the 20.140 session description), online_session_id 0x9EAA300100200003.
Key byte: 759 bit 4 of every rig body (bit 6076 of the 4095-byte body). NEG-B did not fire:
the region-record encoding was located with confidence (per-record region-index self-check
passed on all bodies).
SUPERSEDES 20.146 step 3's claim "the rig's updates 2/4 carry ONLY PRV48.48": the rig's
client processed those records as not-its-own because the region-56 record names the PEER
(the mac, ambassador slot 0, endpoint = public session 200003) - the rig's OWN region
record stayed 48 in every body. The mac's client, by contrast, reported region 56 ~5 s
into its session (server advertise line t=12781867 region=56 source=reported) and its
session binding flipped 48->56 (t=12816757).
REFINED CHAIN: the rig DID initially read as region 56 to the mac (the mac's two earliest
bodies: own=48, peer=56) - then the rig's session churn (re-target own, membership_peer
withdrawn unacked=2, ack=0 forever) dropped it to 48 and it never recovered; after the
churn the region-56 record it receives names only the mac, so its client correctly reads
'public region does not include me' and never citizen-joins. The defect to hunt: WHY THE
RIG'S FIRST-SESSION REGION-56 STATE WAS LOST IN THE CHURN - i.e. (a) the rig's client
never acked its first session's membership (withdrawn), (b) the re-target reset the region
binding to initial 48, (c) nothing re-armed the claim. All three are OUR server's
state machines (ack handling, session re-creation, region re-arm). NOT a client-DLL
defect, NOT a body-writer omission.
Deliverables: RE_output/claims/gah-region-decode.md (+ -raw.md). OPEN in the lane: the
mac's earliest bodies show peer=56 before the rig's own bodies begin (early region churn,
not pinned); join-descriptor bytes beyond the first 16 opaque.
