## 20.145 *** L9 PROFILE BLOCK IS AN OPAQUE, SELF-HASHED BLOB - THE SESSION LAYER ***
## *** CANNOT NAME ITS CONTENTS BY CONSTRUCTION. ROUTE B (CAPTURE) IS THE ONLY ***
## *** GROUNDED SOURCE. THE BLOCK IS ~396 BYTES, NOT 264. (2026-08-28 ~17:0x) ***

Lane L9-PROFILE-LAYOUT (deep subagent, read-only static analysis of
RE_output/destiny2_unpacked_full.exe). Deliverables: RE_output/claims/l9-profile-layout.md
(+ -raw.md). VERIFIED against the binary: the client's player-row apply is fn
0x141781800 (carries hash literal 0xDEAE2F4E + the 0x7060 state size + the
`mov [entry+0x1c],0xffffffff` / `[entry+0x108],0xffffffff` stores in a 424-stride
table) - the fork's session_state.cpp model matches it field-for-field. ONE
profile-present flag (byte +0x21, checked at 0x14178241b) gates ALL of: region A
[+0x1c..+0x108) = 4B header + 232B blob, region B [+0x108..+0x190) = 136B, tail
[+0x190..+0x1a4) = 20B. Region B/tail are raw memcpys; region A is a 9-bit-masked
copy of 8 verbatim sub-chunks carrying its OWN lookup3 integrity hash (initial
0xdeadbfd6, distinct from the session hash 0xdeae2f4e). CONSEQUENCE: the session
layer transports, it does not parse - the bytes are authored in the identity/loadout
system, so a server writer must REPLAY a real blob, not synthesize one (and the
self-hash lets us validate a replayed blob). The decoded (per-update) form totals
8+232+136+20 = 396B of profile payload (CLAIM 5).
OPEN (logged in the deliverable): (1) the wire bit-reader DECODE was not located -
the on-wire encoding of the blob is unverified; anchors to try next listed there.
(2) TABLE-BASE DISCREPANCY: the apply's live-state table sits at count@0x3b58/
mask@0x3b5c/table@0x3b60 - 8B ABOVE the fork's and the diff-fn's 0x3b50/0x3b54/
0x3b58. Entry-relative offsets agree, so the layout stands, but audit the fork's
replica base before ANY profile write that must hash-match. (3) The meanings of the
two 4B headers (state+0x00, state+0x1c) are unnamed - 0xFFFFFFFF/0 when absent.
NEXT ACTION UNCHANGED IN TARGET, UPGRADED IN ROLE: the route B tcpdump is no longer
just the cheapest way to learn the bytes - it is the ONLY grounded source. Instrument
registered: RE_scripts/capture_bap30975.sh (TOOLS.md). Run a CLIENT-HOSTED fireteam
(mac client hosts) and capture tcp/30975; the fireteam self-row pc=1 is the
existence proof of a row that sets the flag.

## 20.144 *** W-A ANSWERED: THE PEER CHANNEL CARRIES A 26-BYTE, ~4 Hz, ENCRYPTED ***
## *** DYNAMIC STREAM AND NOTHING ELSE. APPEARANCE IS NOT ON IT - BY SIZE, NOT BY ***
## *** GUESS. THE STATIC HALF IS GENUINELY MISSING. (2026-08-28 ~15:1x) ***

Instrument: `tcpdump -i en0 -s0 -U -w <pcap> 'udp port 3097 or 3074 or 3075 or 30976'`
on the mac during a live paired dwell, both players MOVING. No sudo needed - this user is
in the `access_bpf` group, so /dev/bpf is directly usable. No client change, no code, no
boot brief. Capture retained: RE_output/captures/l9_peer/peer_20260828_130417.pcap
(122 KB, 1000 packets). THIS IS THE REAL W-A; 20.141's "the capture exists, zero boots"
was wrong (the client log holds only counts, 20.142) and this replaces it.

### THE CHANNEL, MEASURED END TO END
678 packets with src port 3097 AND dst port 3097 (the earlier 1000 included 3097->3074
and 3097->30976, which are NOT the peer channel - filter on BOTH ports).
Two cleanly separated phases:
  t+90.2 .. t+91.3s  ESTABLISHMENT BURST: 52, 209, 116, 116, 130, 226, 130, 50, 50.
      Low entropy = STRUCTURED PLAINTEXT. Readable, and read: the 209-byte body carries
      `d9aed900ebb92cf2` - the session-description D9AED900:EBB92CF2 both clients
      reported (20.140) - plus `c0a80188190c` = 192.168.1.136:3097 and the mac's
      endpoint. Session identity and addresses. NO character or appearance content.
  t+91.4 .. t+158.9s  STEADY STATE: 552 packets of exactly 34 bytes, EVERY ONE DISTINCT,
      281 from the rig and 271 from the mac over 67.5 s = 4.2/s and 4.0/s. Symmetric.
      Header `06 02 be9e`, a 4-byte sequence at offset 4, offsets 16-17 constant, and
      every other byte ~230 distinct values in 281 samples = HIGH ENTROPY.
Plus 116 packets of 29 bytes, 112 of them byte-identical: keepalive.

### THE CONCLUSION, AND WHY ENCRYPTION DOES NOT BLOCK IT
The steady-state payloads are ENCRYPTED (the channel completed DTLS - "advancing to
secured" - and the entropy confirms it), so their contents cannot be read from a pcap.
**That does not matter, because the SIZE settles it.** 34 bytes total, minus a 4-byte
header and a 4-byte sequence, leaves ~26 bytes per tick. Twenty-six bytes cannot carry a
character definition: the group-session player row's own optional identity block is 264
bytes (20.143), an order of magnitude larger, and that is only the identity block. A
26-byte 4 Hz slot is a position/orientation-shaped budget.
=> **APPEARANCE IS NOT ON THE PEER CHANNEL.** The peer channel supplies DYNAMIC state
only. The static half - what the other guardian looks like - is genuinely absent from
every path measured so far, which is exactly what the user sees: co-located, moving,
invisible.
This retires the 20.141 hope ("if the peer channel already carries appearance, none of
this is needed"). It does not, and it cannot.

### WHAT THIS DOES *NOT* ESTABLISH (state it, do not let it drift)
- It does not prove the 26 bytes ARE position. They are opaque. All that is proven is
  that they are too small to be appearance.
- It does not rule out a THIRD carrier nobody has looked at. Measured so far: the peer
  channel (this entry), the queuez/snapshot families (20.142 - root-scoped, no
  root->account resolution exists), and the group-session player row (20.143 - has a
  264-byte slot, we write `profile absent`). A carrier outside those three is unexamined.

### STEP 3 IS NOW UNBLOCKED AND ITS TARGET IS CHOSEN
The group-session player row (20.143) is the carrier to build: same channel both clients
already consume, the identity-block slot already defined in the format and already parsed
by the consumer, and `write_player_delta` already writes the gating flag - as
`kPlayerProfileAbsent`. The BAP root->account route (20.142) stays the fallback.
BLOCKING UNKNOWN, unchanged and now the single next question: WHAT THE 264 BYTES CONTAIN.
Nothing in the fork writes or decodes them. The pcap route cannot source them either -
this capture proves the group-session membership traffic rides BAP (TCP 30975), not the
peer channel, so a UDP pcap will never show one. Sourcing it needs either the retail
client's own encoder (caller capture on the membership dump line, LESSONS 18c +
pdata_bounds.py - a client-DLL change and a boot) or a TCP capture of a client-hosted
session's membership push. DO NOT guess the layout.

## 20.143 STEP 2 (decode `pc=`): THE GROUP-SESSION PLAYER ROW CARRIES AN OPTIONAL
## 264-BYTE IDENTITY/PROFILE BLOCK AND THIS HOST HAS NO WRITER FOR IT. EVERY PLAYER
## ROW WE PUBLISH DECLARES `profile absent`. (2026-08-28 ~15:0x)

Static, no boot. Step 2 of the L9 order (20.142).

### WHAT WAS DECODED, AND WHAT WAS NOT - be exact about which is which
VERIFIED (this pass + prior work): `a=` address, `s=` member state ladder, `j=` joinId,
`amb=` ambassador slot (FINDINGS_2026-08-24), `peers valid` / `players valid` bitmasks.
INFERRED, high confidence: **`o=` is a per-peer link ordinal with -1 for SELF.** Holds on
every row of both sessions and both machines - fireteam self row `o=-1`; group_target
server row `o=0`, rig row `o=1`, own row `o=-1`.
**`pc=` IS NOT VERIFIED.** What it is NOT is now established, which is the useful half:
it is not "players this host published for that member", because it reads 0 on the
client's OWN group_target row while that same client reads `pc=1` on its OWN row in the
fireteam session. A per-member player count would read 1 in both.

### THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE FIELD NAME
`middleware/gameplay/group/session_messages.cpp`, the encoder's own constants:
    /** A clear flag ends a player row after its identity group. The profile block it
     *  would gate has no writer here, so no row carries one. */
    constexpr std::uint64_t kPlayerProfileAbsent = 0;
    /** This host publishes no 264-byte identity block and neither trailing delta-entry
     *  flag. */
    constexpr std::uint64_t kEntryFieldAbsent = 0;
and `write_player_delta` closes every row with
    writer.write(kPlayerProfileAbsent, kFlagWidth)
**THE GROUP-SESSION PLAYER ROW HAS AN OPTIONAL 264-BYTE IDENTITY/PROFILE BLOCK, AND THIS
SERVER HAS NEVER WRITTEN ONE.** Every player we publish arrives with an identity
(playerId, slot, memberIndex, addSequence) and NO profile. A consumer handed a player
with no profile knows that a player exists and nothing about what to draw - which is
precisely the shape of L9's symptom and precisely what the peer channel's dynamic state
cannot supply.
CONSEQUENT HYPOTHESIS for `pc=`, INFERRED and now well-supported but STILL UNVERIFIED:
`pc` = profiles carried on that row. It is 0 on every row of the session where we write
`kPlayerProfileAbsent`, including our own player, and 1 on the client's own row in the
fireteam session, which the client builds LOCALLY from its own profile. That correlation
is exactly what the two constants above predict. It does NOT promote to VERIFIED without
the emitting function (LESSONS 18c caller capture + pdata_bounds.py), which costs a
client-DLL change and a boot - do not spend that yet, because step 1 may make it moot.

### WHY THIS CHANGES STEP 3's DESIGN BEFORE STEP 3 IS REACHED
20.142 scoped the static half as "(a) a root->account resolution and (b) serve one
account's family-0/3 records to another account's connection" - i.e. a BAP-side change to
the queuez/snapshot families. This finding names a SECOND, and much closer, candidate
carrier: the group-session player row we ALREADY publish, on a channel both clients
already consume, with a slot for the identity block already defined in the format and
already parsed by the consumer. If the profile block is what the client builds a remote
character from, the fix is an ENCODER addition in code we own, not a new subscription
route.
NOT ESTABLISHED, and must not be assumed: what the 264 bytes CONTAIN. Nothing in the fork
writes or decodes them, no claims doc covers them, and guessing a 264-byte layout is
exactly the class of guess this project has paid for before (LESSONS: a measured call
outranks a published header). The bytes have to be READ before they are written.

### ORDER, REVISED
1. STEP 1 UNCHANGED and still first: pcap the peer channel (UDP 3097) during a paired
   dwell. If the peer channel already carries appearance, neither carrier is needed.
2. NEW, and cheaper than step 3: source the 264-byte block. Two routes, both offline -
   (a) the retail client's own encoder for a client-hosted session, which DOES write one
   (the fireteam `pc=1` row is the existence proof), reachable by caller capture on the
   dump line; (b) a pcap of a client-hosted fireteam session's membership traffic, which
   needs no client change at all and can ride the same capture as step 1.
3. Only then design the writer. Prefer the group-session player row over the
   root->account/BAP route: same channel, format slot already defined, consumer already
   parsing it.

## 20.142 W-A / W-B RESULTS: THE FOREIGN-ROOT ROUTE IS CLOSED BY CONSTRUCTION - NO
## root->account RESOLUTION EXISTS ANYWHERE. W-A CANNOT BE DONE FROM THE EXISTING
## CAPTURE; A ZERO-CODE PCAP INSTRUMENT REPLACES IT. (2026-08-28 ~14:4x)

### W-A - BLOCKED, AND 20.141 OVERSTATED IT
20.141 scoped W-A as "the capture exists, zero boots". THAT WAS WRONG and is retracted.
The client log records only COUNTS and LIFECYCLE for the peer channel - `packet_loss_stats`
summaries, `channel:establish`, `channel:connect`, DTLS/NAT lines. There is NO payload,
no message type, no size histogram for a single peer packet. Nothing in any capture on
disk says what those ~250 packets/summary contain.
WHAT W-A DID YIELD, from the membership dumps, and it is worth keeping:
    fireteam session rows (steamid:...):        pc=1
    group_target session rows (IP addresses):   pc=0  on EVERY row, BOTH machines,
                                                local row and peer row alike
while the session-level mask reads `players valid: 0x3` and this server publishes
`members=3 players=2` (4x). So the shared session's mask says two players and no PEER
ROW declares one. **`pc=` has never been decoded in this project** - it appears in no
claims doc and no prior finding. It sits directly on the question L9 asks and it is a
free target for the msg12 schema work (msg12-schema-decoded.md).
REPLACEMENT INSTRUMENT, zero code and no client change: both machines are on one LAN and
the peer channel is plain UDP 3097. `tcpdump -i any -s0 -w peer.pcap udp port 3097` on
the mac for one paired run captures the actual peer traffic both directions. That is the
real W-A and it costs one already-planned paired dwell.

### W-B - ANSWERED, AND THE ANSWER IS NEGATIVE (verified-by-reading)
Two independent facts close the "make the client subscribe to the peer's root" route:
1. MEASURED: each client subscribes families 0/2/3/4 to its OWN account root only
   (mac 9EAA300100100100, rig 9EAA300100110100). Neither ever names the other's.
2. VERIFIED BY READING - and this is the one that closes it: **the subscription's
   `familyRootSoid` plays no part in selecting whose data is served.** The snapshot path
   picks its account from the CONNECTION: `state::account_snapshot(before.accountKey)`,
   `prepare_initial(..., before.accountKey)`, `prepare_roster_appearance_refresh(...,
   AccountKey accountKey)`. A grep across all of `state/` and `server/` for any
   root->account resolution (`account_for_root`, `account_for_soid`, `resolve_account`,
   `by_root`) returns NOTHING - no such lookup exists.
   => A client that DID subscribe to a foreign root would be served its OWN records.
   The root is carried on the wire, logged, and ignored for source selection.
So W-B's cheap hope - "the existing family-0/3 encoders may serve a foreign root
unchanged" - is FALSE. Serving a foreign root requires new server code (root->account
resolution) regardless of whether the client can be made to ask.

### CONSEQUENCE FOR THE L9 PLAN
W-C is now the only route for the STATIC half (appearance/character records), and it is
better specified than lane_entity_scope's W1-W8 assumed: what is missing is not the whole
entity pipeline but (a) a root->account resolution, and (b) a decision to serve one
account's family-0/3 records to another account's connection. Both are additive server
changes in code we own. Whether they are SUFFICIENT depends entirely on the real W-A -
if the peer channel already carries appearance, none of this is needed.
ORDER, unchanged in shape but now correctly costed:
  1. REAL W-A: pcap the peer channel during the next paired dwell. Zero code. Decides
     whether the static half is missing at all.
  2. Decode `pc=` in the membership dump (free, static, against msg12-schema-decoded.md).
  3. Only then design the root->account serving path, if W-A says it is needed.
DO NOT flip `physicsHostSession` (20.141: "produces no wire output either way") and do
not open lane_entity_scope W1-W8 on the strength of the 2026-08-19 scoping, which
predates co-location and predates the peer channel ever carrying a packet.

## 20.141 L9 DEEP DIVE: THE PEER CHANNEL IS ALREADY REPLICATING (250 pkts/summary,
## 0 LOST). L9 IS NOT "BUILD THE ENTITY PIPELINE" - IT IS THAT THE PEER'S CHARACTER
## RECORDS NEVER REACH THE OTHER CLIENT. (2026-08-28 ~14:2x)

Static analysis over the 20.140 co-located capture + the fork source. No boot.

### FINDING 1 - THE PEER CHANNEL CARRIES REAL TRAFFIC NOW
    Outgoing packet delivery summary - 242 delivered, 0 lost   (also 241/240/239/169)
    Incoming packet read result summary - 250 ok, 249 ok, 248 ok
On the mac's channel to 192.168.1.136. The SAME lines read `0 delivered / 0 ok` in
p2(88) (20.131 census). The two clients are exchanging hundreds of packets directly.
Peer replication is RUNNING. That retires the framing in FINDINGS_2026-08-22 item 5
("nothing replicates another player") as a description of TODAY: something replicates;
what is missing is narrower.

### FINDING 2 - THE SERVER'S PHYSICS/REPLICATION PLANE IS OFF, AND TURNING IT ON
### WOULD NOT HELP (do not spend a boot on the switch)
~1,800 lines under server/gameplay/physics/{replication,world,interest,host} never
execute: `ev=physics` / `ev=replica` / `ev=world` / `ev=actor` / `ev=interest` all read
ZERO in the live server, including the `result=fail` branches, so `open_world_for` is
never called. Gate: `physics_session.cpp:562`,
`server.activation.physicsHostSession` (default FALSE, absent from settings.json).
**Its own doc comment settles whether to flip it: "It produces no wire output either
way."** It opens, ticks and closes a world server-side and sends clients nothing. A
settings flip here buys a frame-stall on the render thread and no visibility.
Two neighbouring gates are explicitly unwired and must not be mistaken for levers:
  `gameplayExternalBody` - "nothing reads this gate. The codec and receive shadow exist;
   the packet-outcome binding does not."
  `serverDefaultEntity`  - "nothing reads this gate. The allocator and channel-2 codec
   exist offline; a visible class chain and a live outcome path do not."

### FINDING 3 - THE CHARACTER-RECORD PIPELINE IS ROOT-SCOPED, AND THAT IS THE GAP
    stage=subscribe_in family=0 root=9EAA300100100100   (mac, its OWN root)
    stage=subscribe_in family=0 root=9EAA300100110100   (rig, its OWN root)
    ... same for families 2, 3, 4
Each client subscribes ONLY to its own account root. Neither ever subscribes to the
other's, and nothing on this server pushes a foreign root. Families 0/3 are the
character/appearance records (character_record_encoder.cpp; family-3 carries
kPreviewFlagOffsets, family-0 a 24-byte tail - FINDINGS_2026-08-22 ~L988).
MODEL, INFERRED but consistent with every observation: the p2p channel carries the
peer's DYNAMIC state (position/motion - which is why the user once saw movement drive a
phantom, 20.131), while the peer's STATIC identity - which guardian, which armour -
must arrive as character records, and never does. A client handed motion for a player
whose character records it does not hold has nothing to build, which is exactly the
long-standing shape "the phantom is each client rendering its OWN character; identities
never copied" (20.127).

### FINDING 4 - ONE OF THE 08-22 ROADMAP'S FOUR BLOCKERS IS ALREADY GONE
Item 3 ("IDENTITY COLLISIONS: two clients on the same settings.json share character
soids; replication cannot distinguish them - needs per-account soid allocation ranges")
is RESOLVED by the two-account provisioning: measured distinct character soids, mac
0x9EAA300100100101 vs rig 0x9EAA300100110103, and distinct account primaries
(key=0 0x...00100100, key=1 0x...00110100). Do not re-plan per-account soid ranges.
Item 4 ("shared-space placement unproven end to end") is CLOSED by 20.140.

### WHAT L9 ACTUALLY NEEDS - RANKED, CHEAPEST FIRST
W-A  MEASURE WHAT THE PEER CHANNEL ALREADY CARRIES. ~250 packets/summary are flowing
     and NOBODY HAS LOOKED INSIDE THEM. If they already carry appearance, the gap is a
     consumer-side bind, not a missing feed. Zero boots: the capture exists. This must
     come first - every plan below is a guess until it is done.
W-B  DETERMINE WHETHER THE CLIENT ASKS FOR THE PEER'S RECORDS AND IS REFUSED, OR NEVER
     ASKS. `stage=subscribe_in` is an INBOUND request, so the client drives it. If a
     client can be made to subscribe to a foreign root (by the peer appearing in a
     roster/world it consults), the existing family-0/3 encoders may serve it unchanged.
     Static read of the subscribe path + the roster contents. Zero boots.
W-C  ONLY IF W-A AND W-B BOTH COME BACK NEGATIVE: push foreign character records
     unsolicited into the subscriber's snapshot. That is new wire behaviour and needs
     the entity/object pipeline the parked lane_entity_scope W1-W8 covers - item 5's
     "doing entities first pays twice" applies, and that lane is 1-2 weeks of boots.
DO NOT start at W-C. The roadmap was written on 2026-08-19, before co-location existed
and before the peer channel ever carried a packet; its scoping predates both.

## 20.139 *** COCONUT ROOT CAUSE: THE SERVER HAS 4 BAP CONNECTION SLOTS AND A ***
## *** CLIENT NEEDS THREE. p2(89) DID NOT BREAK THE RIG - IT STOPPED THE MAC'S ***
## *** THIRD LINK FROM DYING, WHICH IS WHAT USED TO FREE THE SLOT. (2026-08-28 ~13:5x) ***

Two hypotheses were falsified on the way here and both are recorded so neither is
re-derived: (1) a dirty lobby claim table - FALSIFIED, the table was verified EMPTY before
the run and COCONUT reproduced identically; (2) the published identity - the user's own
hypothesis, that continuously asserting the MAC's member table into the shared activity
host locks the rig out - FALSIFIED by the rig's own log, which never reaches the public
instance at all.

### THE EVIDENCE THAT SETTLED IT WAS ON THE RIG, AND HAD NEVER BEEN READ
This session diagnosed a RIG failure from server-side and MAC-side logs only, for hours.
The rig's own log answers it in four lines:
    world_controller:activity_manager: Waiting for 'PRIVATE CURRENT' activity client
                                       to connect.                          (x5, ~26 s)
    [PRIVATE CURRENT] Timeout 'timed_out_connecting_to_bap' (26001ms / 25000ms + 1000ms)
    world_controller:state:activity_setup: Fatal error 'timed_out_connecting_to_bap'
                                       starting PRIVATE activity instance, aborting to orbit
    [BAP ID 1 desc:OUT FAH - _channel_starting] Fatal '_connection_failure_suicide'
        last status '_channel_starting[192.168.1.164:30975] rx 0.0p 0.0kb tx 0.0p 0.0kb'
The rig fails on its **PRIVATE** activity instance, not the public one, and its FAH link
never starts in EITHER direction (tx 0.0 as well as rx 0.0). That is below every layer
this lane touches.
PROCESS LESSON, and it converts to an EXISTING TOOL rather than to prose: **run
`bash RE_scripts/boot_verdict.sh`** - it is registered in TOOLS.md, it pulls the RIG's log
over the ~/.ssh/cm-rig control socket automatically, and it prints a BOTH-machines verdict.
It was never run once during this entire session while a rig failure was diagnosed from
mac+server logs alone for hours, and the hand-rolled `powershell -NoProfile -Command`
over ssh that finally read the rig reproduced a fraction of what it already does. Its own
header states the reason it exists: the verdict is "easy to eyeball wrong at 2am".
RULE: the FIRST read after any paired boot is boot_verdict.sh, before any grep.
SECOND DEBT, same class, named by AGENTS.md's own trigger ("Digging through logs ->
logq.py over a logindex.py index; any raw sed/grep CHAIN beyond that = conversion debt"):
this session ran dozens of raw grep chains against both logs instead of indexing once with
`logindex.py` and querying with `logq.py --ev/--stage/--aligned`. The cross-machine
`--aligned` view is exactly the instrument that would have put the rig's
`timed_out_connecting_to_bap` next to the mac's deliveries on one clock.
(Rig shell detail worth keeping: it is Windows cmd, POSIX tooling absent, and plain
`cmd /c` mangles the space in "dcv build" - use `powershell -NoProfile -Command` with
single-quoted paths IF boot_verdict.sh is ever unavailable.)

### THE MECHANISM
    client/network/consumer.h: kBapConnectionCount = 4        (was)
    ev=bap stage=arm conn=1 keyfp=6F9FB1C668DEF376   <- mac
    ev=bap stage=arm conn=2 keyfp=6F9FB1C668DEF376   <- mac
    ev=bap stage=arm conn=3 keyfp=6F9FB1C668DEF376   <- mac
    ev=bap stage=arm conn=4 keyfp=7B3DEA2F2DFD8E3D   <- rig, the last slot
A client that reaches a public activity host runs THREE outbound BAP links, named in its
own log: `OUT PRIMARY`, `OUT FAH` (private activity) and `OUT GAH` (public activity). Four
slots hold ONE such client. The rig got one slot and needs two before it can even start
its private activity client - hence a 26 s timeout and the abort to orbit.

### WHY IT HAS NEVER BEEN SEEN BEFORE, AND WHY p2(89) EXPOSED IT
The ceiling was invisible for the whole project because the third link USED TO DIE. Every
~20 s the public link starved (20.137: `rx 0.0` in 60 of 61 fatals) and its slot recycled,
so the mac never held three at once for long and the rig always found room - which is
exactly what it did on p2(88) (`members=3 players=2`, 20.134). p2(89) fixed the
starvation; the link stopped dying; the mac now holds 1/2/3 permanently; the rig is
locked out. **The regression is the fix succeeding.** A capacity sized for connections
that kept crashing is not a capacity.

### FIX
`kBapConnectionCount` 4 -> 12 = kAccountCapacity (4) x 3 links, so every provisioned
account can hold a full set. All three users of the constant are SERVER-side
(`server/transport/internal.h` peers, `server/transport/bap_listener.cpp` wasPending,
`server/bap/internal.h` kSessionCount); it merely LIVES in a client header and no client
code indexes anything by it, so the deployed client DLL `4831be3cb85db735` is unaffected
and must NOT be redeployed. Cost is `Peer` (2 x kBapFrameCapacity) + `Session` per slot;
the 256 KiB scratch buffers are ONE shared instance and do not scale with it.
Builds clean, both targets.

### STANDING / WHAT IS STILL TRUE
Everything measured about the GAHN starvation stands (20.137/20.138) and the p2(89) SOLO
result stands: zero errors, one region rebind, 63 continuous acknowledged deliveries. The
paired half has never actually been tested - both attempts died on this capacity ceiling
before the rig reached any activity layer. p2(89)'s OUTCOMES 1/2/4 remain unconsumed.
PRE-NAMED for the next run: if `ev=bap stage=arm` now shows >=2 distinct keyfps holding
3 conns each and the rig still aborts to orbit, the capacity was necessary but not
sufficient and the rig's FAH failure is its own defect - read the rig log FIRST.

## 20.138 *** THE TWO LINKS DO NOT SHARE A MEMBER KEY: SAME IDENTITY BLOB, TWO ***
## *** WINDOWS (private blob[0..7], public blob[5..12]). THE ACCOUNT SLOT IS THE ***
## *** ONLY KEY COMMON TO BOTH. UPSTREAM'S CLAIM IS FALSE HERE. (2026-08-28 ~13:2x) ***

p2(89) run 1, SOLO MAC. The brief's step-4 gate fired and STOPPED the run before the rig
was booted - the boot was saved, which is what that gate exists for.

### THE READ THAT STOPPED IT
    client: GAHN lines in the whole log: 0.  `rx` never left 0.0.
    server: stage=public_row_gate rowsession=0x9EAA300100200003 activesession=0x0
            private=0x0 member=0x2DF1FE0138FBFC51 budget=65535 armed=0     (2461 pumps)
    server: stage=public_row_gate rowsession=0x0 activesession=0x9EAA300100200001
            private=0x9EAA300100200001 member=0x32E4DCEEB92DF1FE           (3239 pumps)
    server: stage=bind result=public_host_row x6, deliveries: 0
OUTCOME 3 by the brief's own naming, and the `member=` field - shipped for exactly this -
named the mechanism on the first look.

### THE MECHANISM: ONE BLOB, TWO WINDOWS
    private (OUT FAH):  32 E4 DC EE B9 2D F1 FE
    public  (OUT GAHN): 2D F1 FE 01 38 FB FC 51
The public key begins at byte 5 of the private key: the SAME identity blob read at a
window shifted by 5, sharing exactly 3 bytes. That is verbatim the window map already
recorded in BOOT_BRIEF_p2-87's chain marks ("blob[0..7] join identity + blob[5..12]
tracking key/releases", 20.128/20.129/20.130) - the two windows were known, and what is
new is that the two BAP LINKS use different ones.
**RETRACTED: upstream's "The client matches itself by the key at `client+27696`, which is
its machine id and is the same on both links"** (quoted in activity_keepalive_push.cpp's
`publicTarget` branch, and load-bearing in 20.132/20.136/20.137). It is not the same on
both links in this build. Any future design that keys a cross-link lookup on the member
key is dead on arrival; do not re-derive it from that quote.
DO NOT match on the 3-byte overlap either: 24 bits is not an identity, and the joining
window rotates per boot (20.131's map point - blob[0..3] is a per-boot nonce).

### THE FIX: KEY ON THE BAP ACCOUNT SLOT
`core::settings::AccountKey` (uint8, capacity 4) is the same on both of a client's links
and distinct per machine (server startup: `ev=account stage=identity key=0` mac /
`key=1` rig). A four-slot table `g_privateActivitySessions` in bap_connection_publication
is written wherever `session.activitySessionId` is assigned - a link that OWNS an activity
session is that account's private half by definition, since the public half never owns one
(`activesession=0x0` on every row-bearing connection across p2(88) and p2(89)) - and read
by that account's public link. Both call sites already run under the BAP route's exclusive
lock, so the table needs none of its own.
WHY THE PUBLIC LINK'S SLOT IS TRUSTWORTHY (verified-by-execution, not assumed): p2(88)
sealed three bodies to that connection with `state::bap(session.accountKey).sessionKey`
and the client DECRYPTED AND ACKNOWLEDGED them (`membership_ack session=...00200003` x5).
A wrong account slot could not have produced a decryptable frame.
The member-key query added earlier this session
(`session_binding_for_member_excluding`) is REMOVED, not left unused - it cannot work for
the reason above and an unused general-purpose query is a trap for the next session.
The gate line now also prints `slot=`, so the new key is readable the way `member=` was.

### STANDING
Everything in 20.137 survives: all three restart drivers are still one starvation of the
GAHN connection, and the model still predicts the p2(88) solo/paired split. This entry
changes only HOW the source session is found, which was 20.137's fix-half 1. Fix-half 2
(cadence 65535) is deployed and confirmed loaded (`public_row_bodies=65535`).
p2(89) brief's OUTCOMES 1/2/4 and its absence negatives stand unchanged and are still the
reading frame for the next run; OUTCOME 3 has now been consumed and answered.

## 20.137 *** ALL THREE RESTART DRIVERS ARE ONE ROOT CAUSE: THE CLIENT'S SECOND ***
## *** BAP CONNECTION ("OUT GAHN") IS NEVER ANSWERED BY THIS SERVER. rx 0.0 ***
## *** IN 60 OF 61 FATALS. THE HARE/SUICIDE CLASS IS THE SAME DEFECT. (2026-08-28 ~12:4x) ***

Static pass, no boot. Scopes 20.136's three drivers together, as instructed, so a boot is
spent only at high confidence.

### THE CLIENT RUNS TWO OUTBOUND BAP CONNECTIONS, AND ONLY ONE IS SERVED
    BAP DESC: BAP      OUT FAH  _connected[192.168.1.164:30975]   x62   (healthy, all run)
    BAP DESC: BAP      OUT GAHN _connecting / _connected           (dies, every cycle)
FAH is the connection this server has always served. GAHN is the one the PUBLIC activity
client uses, and it is the one that dies. Both name the SAME endpoint,
192.168.1.164:30975 - so this is NOT an address defect (the p2(84) resolver fix holds;
`kLoopbackAddress` in `fill_activity_host` is the parameter body, not what GAHN dials).

### EVERY DRIVER IS THE SAME STARVATION, AT A DIFFERENT STAGE
    Last status '_connecting[192.168.1.164:30975] rx 0.0p 0.0kb tx 0.0p 0.0kb'   x47
      -> Fatal '_connection_failure_failed_to_call_transport_endpoint_async_is_connected'
      -> world_controller: "Waiting for 'X' activity client to be connected, and BAP
         connection died."  -> `error_connecting_to_bap`   (46)
    Last status '_connected[...] rx 0.0p 0.0kb tx 0.7p 0.3kb'                    x14
      -> Fatal '_connection_failure_suicide'
      -> "[PUBLIC TARGET] Lost connection to activity host (possible crash)"
      -> `error_host_crashed`   (14)
    Last status '_channel_starting[...] rx 0.0p'                                 x2
      -> `timed_out_connecting_to_bap`   (2)
**`rx 0.0` in 60 of the 61 fatals with a recorded status.** The single exception reads
`rx 2.0p 24.8kb` - the one GAHN connection this server actually fed, which is p2(88)'s
own doing. The client transmits on GAHN (`tx 0.7p`) and receives nothing back. Three
error names, one mechanism: THE SERVER DOES NOT SEND ON GAHN.

### THE PREDICTION THIS MODEL ALREADY MADE, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT CONFIRMED IT
The model says: never served -> dies in `_connecting` -> `error_connecting_to_bap`;
served then silenced -> `_connected` then starves -> suicide -> `error_host_crashed`.
p2(88) run 3 (SOLO) is exactly that experiment, run before the model existed. We served
the GAHN connection for the first time ever (3 bodies) and then went silent (the
`live_region_session` flip, 20.134). Result, measured: **`error_connecting_to_bap`
67 -> 0**, and `error_host_crashed` present at 7. Connecting failures vanished the
moment we sent anything; starvation failures remained because we stopped. The paired
run then reverted to 46 connecting-failures because the source defect meant the rig's
GAHN was never correctly served at all. The model predicts all three counts across two
runs it was not built from.

### THIS ALSO ABSORBS THE HARE / `_connection_failure_suicide` CLASS (20.127)
64 `_connection_failure_suicide` in this run. 20.127 recorded the HARE dialog with
`Fatal error '_connection_failure_suicide' raised` and theorised (UNPROVEN) that
interacting near the duplicate guardian starved BAP. That theory is superseded: suicide
is what a BAP connection does when it is connected and receives nothing, and the GAHN
connection receives nothing by construction. The duplicate-proximity correlation was
coincidental. Blocker "HARE/suicide" is therefore NOT a separate lane - it is this one.
(The duplicate-guardian HUSK itself remains unexplained and separate - 20.135.)

### WHAT THE FIX HAS TO BE
1. SOURCE (already in code, 20.136): serve each GAHN connection from ITS OWN account's
   private session - `session_binding_for_member_excluding`. Without this, on a paired
   run the rig's GAHN is either unserved or served the mac's table.
2. CADENCE (settings only, NOT yet set): the body budget must not run out. A cap of 8
   guarantees eventual silence, and silence on a connected GAHN is precisely what
   `_connection_failure_suicide` is. `activity_public_row_membership_bodies` must be set
   high enough that the connection is fed for the whole session. No rebuild needed - it
   is a uint16 in settings, which is why it was made a count rather than a bool.
BOTH are needed. Either alone reproduces one of the two failure stages, and this entry's
whole value is that it tells you WHICH failure each omission produces.

### PRE-NAMED NEGATIVES FOR THE NEXT BOOT (L6/L13)
- N1 `error_connecting_to_bap` -> 0 on BOTH machines AND `error_host_crashed` -> 0: the
  model is right and the restart loop should stop. Judge co-location next.
- N2 connecting -> 0 but host_crashed persists: still starving; read the gate line's
  `armed=` and `sent=` against the budget before touching anything else.
- N3 connecting stays high on the RIG only: the per-account source is not reaching the
  rig's GAHN; read `stage=bind result=public_host_row` and the gate `member=` field on
  the rig's connection - it names which member key that connection carried.
- N4 both go to 0 and the region STILL rebinds ~60x: the restart loop has a fourth
  driver not in this run's error census. Re-census `encountered error` before theorising.
INSTRUMENT NOTE: the gate line now prints `member=0x...`, which is what the new query
keys on; N3 is unreadable without it, so it must ship.

## 20.136 STATIC SCOPE OF L8c: THE "0 SLOTS" READING WAS A SYMPTOM, NOT A BLOCKER.
## THE PEER CHANNEL SUCCEEDS 63/63. THE REAL BLOCKER IS THE PUBLIC-TARGET INSTANCE
## RESTART LOOP, AND ONE OF ITS THREE DRIVERS IS OURS. (2026-08-28 ~12:2x)

Static pass over the p2(88) paired capture. No boot spent. Two prior framings of this
front are RETRACTED by it, both of them mine.

### RETRACTION 1 - "the bubble reservation refuses to ask for peer slots"
20.134 read `Updating public bubble reservation peer request ... to '0' slots` (34/34,
rig present) as the blocker. Reading the line IN SEQUENCE falsifies that: it fires
immediately after
    Region 'PUB56.56' ('PENDING') reporting a new session-description
                                   '00000000:00000000 --> 9701F960:BCF10408'
i.e. at the moment the region BINDS a session-description. It is the reservation being
INITIALISED for a newly-bound region, not a refusal to reserve. It has never once been
observed with a non-zero value in any capture, which is consistent with initialisation
and NOT diagnostic of refusal. Do not chase the slot count as an independent lane.

### RETRACTION 2 - "the session-description keeps changing" (considered and killed)
The description is STABLE: one value, `9701F960:BCF10408`, all run. What repeats is the
BINDING: `00000000:00000000 --> 9701F960:BCF10408` x63. The region's session binding is
torn to null and rebuilt 63 times, and each rebuild re-initialises the reservation.

### THE MEASUREMENT THAT REFRAMES THE WHOLE FRONT
    peer join successful, secure connection in 200msec, initial updates in 301msec   x63
    session state is changing from 'peer-joining' to 'peer-established'              x63
    membership dump: peer # 2 a=[192.168.1.136:3097] s=_established o=1
**THE CLIENT-TO-CLIENT PEER CHANNEL SUCCEEDS, 63 TIMES OUT OF 63, IN 200 ms.** The rig
reaches `_established` in the mac's group session with `peers valid: 0x7`. 20.131's
"the next blocker is client-to-client peer connectivity" is now doubly dead: 20.132
showed the channel was killed from above, and this shows it establishes on demand every
single cycle. The peer layer is NOT a blocker and has not been one for two boots.

### WHAT IS ACTUALLY BLOCKING: THE RESTART LOOP
    encountered error 'error_connecting_to_bap'        46
    encountered error 'error_host_crashed'             14
    encountered error 'timed_out_connecting_to_bap'     2
    Stopping transition 'transitioning:normal_z_leg' due to preempted   62
Everything downstream - the reservation, the channel teardown, co-location - is
re-initialised by each of those 62 preemptions. THE LANE IS: stop the public-target
activity instance from erroring. Not the reservation, not the channel.

### ONE DRIVER IS OURS, AND IT IS FIXED IN CODE (not yet booted)
`error_host_crashed` (14) has a named server-side cause from 20.133/20.134: the
public-row service used `live_region_session`, which is WRONG IN TWO WAYS, both
measured. (a) It is process-wide, so on a paired run it hands one machine's member
table to the OTHER machine's public link - all 74 `armed=1` pumps sourced the mac's
0x...00200001, including on the rig's connection. Upstream is explicit that a body
carrying anything but the client's own private snapshot makes the client prune the
member and destroy the player it holds. (b) Once the public client reports a region it
returns the PUBLIC session itself, so the source collapsed onto its own target and the
server went SILENT on the session it had just started serving (`armed=0` for ~1200
pumps; only 3 bodies ever went out solo). A host that goes quiet is a plausible -
STILL UNPROVEN - cause of the client declaring the host crashed.
FIX SHIPPED IN CODE: new State query `session_binding_for_member_excluding(memberKey,
excludedSessionId, output)` (state/activity/runtime.h + activity_session_lookup.cpp).
The member key is the client's machine id, identical across BOTH of that client's links
and different on every other machine, so keying on it with the public session excluded
names exactly one session: that account's own private one. The `live_region_session`
call and the interim LATCH (20.133, never deployed) are both gone; the dead
`activityPrivateSourceSession` field is removed rather than left as a trap.
Builds clean, both targets. NOT DEPLOYED.

### HONEST LIMIT OF THIS FIX
It addresses ONE of three error drivers, and its link to `error_host_crashed` is a
hypothesis, not a measurement. `error_connecting_to_bap` (46, the dominant one, and it
appears ONLY when the rig is present - it was 0 across the whole solo run) has NO
identified cause yet and is the more likely gate. Expect the fix to change the mix, not
necessarily to end the loop. Pre-named negative for the next boot: if
`error_host_crashed` goes to 0 and the loop CONTINUES on error_connecting_to_bap, the
fix was correct and the lane moves to that error with a clean single-driver signal -
which is worth the boot on its own.

## 20.135 RECORD CORRECTION - THE DUPLICATE-GUARDIAN "HUSK" IS NOT A CO-PLAYER
## PHANTOM. IT FIRES SOLO, AND THE BODY THE PLAYER CONTROLS IS THE NEW ONE.
## CAUSE STILL UNKNOWN; TWO CANDIDATE READINGS ARE NOW DISTINGUISHABLE. (2026-08-28 ~11:5x)

### WHAT THE RECORD SAID, AND WHY IT IS NOW WRONG
20.127 recorded the duplicate as "a client-side phantom (built from BAP-layer
knowledge of the rig; the server has no gameplay player row for it)" and the class as
co-presence-driven, with the established shape "black screen -> recover -> duplicate
hunter". 20.131 added the user's observation that moving the RIG's character clears
the mac's black screen and spawns the mac's second hunter, and read that as "rig
movement data reaches the mac WITHOUT the peer channel".
**FALSIFIED 2026-08-28 (p2(88) run 3).** The mac produced the full sequence - black
screen, recovery, duplicate guardian - with the RIG CLIENT NOT BOOTED AT ALL. The
server log independently confirms the rig's absence for that window: every
`stage=identity result=ok tag=8` line carries `addr=0xC0A801A4` (192.168.1.164, the
mac); the rig's `addr=0xC0A80188` does not appear until it joins later in the session.
The duplicate therefore CANNOT be built from BAP-layer knowledge of the rig, and it is
not a co-presence effect. Do not re-derive the co-presence framing from 20.127/20.131.

### THE NEW FACT THAT CHANGES THE SHAPE (user, verbatim, recorded as observation)
"it went black and then the duplicate spawned in, and that's the one i have control
over". The record had it as a second body standing alongside the player. It is the
opposite: the ORIGINAL body is left behind inert and the player is in the NEW one.
Also user-supplied and load-bearing: "it's always been like that" - the sequence
PREDATES p2(88) and predates the public-membership lane entirely. It is not caused by
addressing the shared activity host, and an initial reading here that attributed it to
a container rebind caused by the new body was WRONG and is retracted.

### WHAT THIS LEAVES: TWO READINGS, NOW DISTINGUISHABLE
(a) WORLD-CONTAINER REBIND. The player is re-spawned into a new container and the old
    body is never torn down. Predicts: the husk is the player's PREVIOUS body, is
    inert, and appears at the position held before the black screen.
(b) DOUBLE SPAWN. Two player objects are created from one join and control binds to
    the second. Predicts: the husk stands at the spawn point, not at the pre-blackout
    position.
The two are separated by ONE observation costing no boot and no instrument: WHERE the
husk stands relative to where the player was before the screen went black. Ask for it
the next time the sequence fires.

### WHY THE LOGS CANNOT SETTLE IT TODAY (instrument gap, name it rather than guess)
The render lane leaves no client-side trace - already recorded for the render-death
class ("there is no `ev=render`", FINDINGS_2026-08-25 ~L1531) and it holds here. The
client log carries `Adding player` on managed sessions #0/#1/#3 with a ZERO session
handle (`[{0}-00000000:00000000-0.0.0.0:0]`) and no xuid captured in the funnel, and
20.101's standing rule applies: the bare "Adding player" line proves nothing, the xuid
decides - and this build's funnel does not capture it. `players valid` on the mac ran
0x0/0x1/0x2/0x3 across the run; the 0x2 readings (index 1 valid, index 0 NOT) are
consistent with reading (a) but do not establish it.
INSTRUMENT THAT WOULD SETTLE IT (not built): caller capture (LESSONS 18c) on "Adding
player" for the xuid argument, plus the managed-session index, on a SOLO boot. That is
a client-DLL change and belongs to the render lane, which stays PARKED - it is not on
the critical path to co-location (L8c) and must not pre-empt it.

### STATUS
Cause UNKNOWN. Not a blocker for the multiplayer milestone by itself, but it is
adjacent to the unexplained `_connection_failure_suicide` / HARE class (20.127: nearby
interaction with the duplicate preceded link death, working theory, UNPROVEN), which
IS a stability risk for any long paired session. Keep both parked together.

## 20.134 PAIRED RUN: MEMBERSHIP IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT. THE BUBBLE
## RESERVATION STAYS AT 0 SLOTS WITH THE RIG PRESENT, AND THE SOURCE-SELECTION
## IN THIS LANE IS GLOBAL WHERE IT MUST BE PER-ACCOUNT. (2026-08-28 ~11:4x)

Same server as 20.133 run 3 (5d3a947ea0950bab; the private-source LATCH was built but
deliberately NOT deployed - deploying restarts the server and would have cost both
clients their links mid-run). Rig joined the Tower second, as the run order requires.

### WHAT HELD WITH BOTH MACHINES IN
- Rig present and distinct: `stage=identity result=ok tag=8 addr=0xC0A80188 port=3097`
  (192.168.1.136) against the mac's 0xC0A801A4. Its private AC joined 0x...00200004
  with `row=0 match=0` - correctly NOT a host row, exactly as the mac's did.
- Group session: `members=3 players=2` x20. Both players admitted.
- Shared host served 14x (`public_row_membership result=delivered
  host=0x9EAA300100200003`), 14 binds, and the mac's PUBLIC TARGET AC held MEM-6..9.

### THE PRE-NAMED CONTENT NEGATIVE FIRED (BOOT_BRIEF_p2-88, L6)
`Updating public bubble reservation peer request ... to '0' slots` **34 of 34, WITH
THE RIG IN THE TOWER.** Solo this reading was meaningless (no peer to reserve for);
paired it is decisive. The brief pre-named it: *membership was necessary but not
sufficient; the lane becomes the PAH tabulated-region read, NOT more membership
bodies.* That is now the standing verdict - do not add membership bodies in response
to this. The peer channel behaved exactly as in p2(87): 18 `lost all owners`, 7
`failed to connect`, 3 reaching `connected4`. `error_connecting_to_bap` returned (15)
once the rig was present, having been 0 for the whole solo run.

### DEFECT IN THIS LANE, FOUND BY ITS OWN INSTRUMENT - SOURCE SELECTION IS GLOBAL
`live_region_session(fallback)` is a GLOBAL query ("the newest session with a reported
region"), not a per-connection or per-account one. The gate line's `private=` field
therefore takes every value in the process across the run:
    private=0x0                  1521
    private=0x9EAA300100200001   1081   <- mac private
    private=0x9EAA300100200003  18448   <- the PUBLIC target (20.133's flip defect)
    private=0x9EAA300100200004   7586   <- RIG private
and all 74 `armed=1` pumps sourced `private=0x...00200001` - the MAC's table. Any
delivery made on the RIG's public connection therefore carried the MAC's member
table. Upstream's warning applies directly (quoted in the `publicTarget` branch): "a
body carrying anything else makes the client prune the member and destroy the player
it holds". UNMEASURED whether that fired, but it is no longer a safe construction.
**The LATCH fix built at 20.133 does NOT resolve this** - it latches the first
non-public value of the same global query, which on the rig's connection can still be
the mac's session. The correct source is the private activity session OF THE SAME
ACCOUNT (the connection's `accountKey` / `activityMemberKey`), and that is a
different query than the one this lane has been using. Do not deploy the latch as-is.

### CHAIN MARK
L8b (address the shared activity host) - **verified-by-execution as ACHIEVED**
(20.133: addressed, acknowledged, MEM 0 -> 9, `public AH instance ready`,
error_connecting_to_bap 67 -> 0 solo). It is NOT the last link before co-location.
L8c (NEW, now the front): whatever feeds `public bubble reservation peer request`
must be made to ask for a non-zero slot count. Read the PAH tabulated-region data in
the group-session membership body (`Processing new PAH tabulated region data`,
`Region ... PAH tabulated data now includes us` - note "us", never a peer). SCOPE IT
STATICALLY FIRST, as 20.132 did; that read cost no boot and retired three candidate
lanes in one pass.

## 20.133 *** THE SHARED ACTIVITY HOST HAS BEEN ADDRESSED AND ACKNOWLEDGED. ***
## *** THE PUBLIC ACTIVITY CLIENT LEFT MEM-0 (0 -> 9) AND THE PUBLIC INSTANCE ***
## *** REACHES 'public AH instance ready'. error_connecting_to_bap IS GONE. (2026-08-28 ~11:2x) ***

p2(88), three server iterations in one session (solo mac; rig not yet joined at the
time of this entry). Server hashes in order: 1f2892e5fb001be1 (first behaviour),
c8bc2530aff1d42f (gate instrument), 5d3a947ea0950bab (corrected placement).

### RUN 1 - THE PREDICATE WORKS; 20.132's PREMISE IS CONFIRMED BY EXECUTION
The unconditional `stage=join_target` instrument (the lookup used to sit behind
`!namesOwnSession` in the predicate AND in its own log line, so `row=` printed 0 on
every join ever captured) settled it on the first solo boot:
    join_target result=own session=0x...00200001 handle=0x...00200001 row=0 match=0 region=-1
    join_target result=own session=0x...00200003 handle=0x...00200003 row=1 match=1 region=56
The client's PRIVATE activity session is NOT a host row; the shared PUBLIC one IS.
`stage=bind result=public_host_row host=0x9EAA300100200003 sameid=1` fired for the
first time - the legacy `bindsPublicTarget` gate demands sameid=0, which no client
has ever produced (20.132), and that is why it had never fired in any boot.

### RUN 2 - ROOT CAUSE OF THE FIRST SILENCE: TWO BAP CONNECTIONS, NOT ONE
Run 1 bound the row and then sent NOTHING. The gate instrument, moved out of the
guard it was meant to explain, named it in one line: 14 pumps, every one of them
    public_row_gate rowsession=0x0 activesession=0x9EAA300100200001 sent=0 budget=8
against 2 binds naming 0x...00200003. The bind wrote a Session the keepalive never
read. **Sessions are keyed by CONNECTION (`session_for(request.connectionId)`,
bap_route.cpp:173) and the client runs TWO BAP connections, one per activity
client.** The public half's connection carries NO activity session of its own, so it
bailed at the keepalive's `activitySessionId == 0` test and never reached the body it
was owed. RETRACTED: 20.132's "one BAP link multiplexes both activity clients by the
envelope handle" - the envelope handle is real but the connections are separate. The
ORIGINAL upstream design comment ("the private link keeps its own role, so the pair
stays distinguishable") was correct and this lane's first draft routed around it.
Fix: serve the public row BEFORE that bail, sourcing the member table from the
private session via `live_region_session` - the same helper, for the same reason, the
legacy `publicTarget` branch uses.

### RUN 3 - IT LANDS. THE HEADLINE NUMBERS.
    server: stage=public_row_membership result=delivered host=0x9EAA300100200003
            private=0x9EAA300100200001 bytes=4140/3927/3927 revision=4/6/7
    server: stage=wire_snapshot session=11433003384888623107   <- 0x9EAA300100200003,
            a session id that had NEVER appeared in any capture, in any boot
    server: stage=membership_ack result=ok session=11433003384888623107 x5
    client: [AC PUBLIC TARGET CON-Y EST-Y AH->ID MEM-4..MEM-9]
**The public activity client left MEM-0 for the first time and climbed to 9, and the
client ACKNOWLEDGES the bodies.** Client-side consequences, all new:
- `session state being changed from 'ctng' to 'cntd' due to 'public AH instance
  ready'` x8. The public instance reaches READY. Never observed before.
- `error_connecting_to_bap`: **67 occurrences in p2(87) -> 0**. Gone.
- `Stopping transition ... due to completed` for initial_slice_set AND orbit.

### WHAT REMAINS, AND A DEFECT OF OUR OWN
1. `error_host_crashed` x7, and normal_z_leg transitions preempted x7. NOT NEW - it
   fired 18x in p2(87) too. The dominant failure was converted into the lesser one;
   this is now the blocker.
2. OUR DEFECT, found in run 3's own gate table and fixed but NOT YET BOOTED:
   `live_region_session(fallback)` returns the NEWEST session with a reported region.
   The moment the public activity client reports a region, it starts returning the
   PUBLIC id, so a freshly derived "private source" collapses onto its own target and
   the arm condition goes false - the server STOPS SERVING THE SESSION IT JUST
   STARTED SERVING. Measured: `private=` flips 0x...00200001 -> 0x...00200003 and
   `armed=0` for ~1200 subsequent pumps; only 3 bodies ever went out. A host that
   goes quiet is a plausible - UNPROVEN - cause of `error_host_crashed`. Fix: latch
   the private source once (`Session::activityPrivateSourceSession`) and never let
   the public id overwrite it. Built, not yet deployed at the time of writing.

### NEGATIVE THAT WAS MIS-NAMED IN THE BRIEF (correct it before reading run 3)
BOOT_BRIEF_p2-88's content negative said "MEM climbs while the bubble reservation
stays at 0 slots => membership was necessary but not sufficient". That reasoning does
NOT apply to a SOLO run: with no second machine there are no peers to reserve slots
for, so `0` is the correct answer, not a failure. The reservation reading only
becomes meaningful with both machines in. Solo run 3 read `0` slots x5 (down from
125x in p2(87), which is a churn reduction, not a verdict).

### USER OBSERVATION, and a correction to the first reading of it
Black screen -> recover -> a duplicate guardian, and the DUPLICATE is the one the
player controls. First read here as a container rebind caused by the new body; the
user corrected it: **this pattern predates p2(88) entirely** ("it's always been like
that"), consistent with the established shape already recorded at 20.111/20.127 and
FINDINGS_2026-08-25 line 417. Do not attribute it to this lane. It remains the
render-lane class and is still unexplained.

## 20.132 *** THE PEER CHANNEL IS NOT THE BLOCKER. THE PUBLIC ACTIVITY CLIENT ***
## *** GETS ZERO MEMBERSHIP BODIES BECAUSE `bindsPublicTarget` IS STRUCTURALLY ***
## *** UNREACHABLE - AND A READY-MADE PUBLISHER SITS BEHIND IT. (2026-08-28 ~12:xx) ***

STATIC SCOPE of 20.131's "NEXT LANE", done with no boot, on the p2(87) capture
(mac Game/bin/x64/Sunrise/logs/sunrise.log + server RE_output/s1_accept/.../sunrise.log,
both still on disk from the 10:2x run). 20.131's three candidate shapes (a) client
DTLS, (b) server proxy addresses, (c) gate-not-dependency are all RETIRED by this
read: none of them is the defect.

### VERIFIED 1 - THE PEER CHANNEL ALREADY WORKS. IT IS KILLED FROM ABOVE.
20.131 recorded "DTLS to each other's game ports fails (securing2 -> dead)". That
reading is WRONG and is retracted here. Measured on the mac:
- NAT traversal succeeds both directions: `bdNATTravClient.cpp:295 discovered addr
  for 192.168.1.136:3097`, and the mac sends INTRO REPLY (only sent in answer to a
  received INTRO REQ) - so the rig's intro traffic arrives.
- DTLS ASSOCIATES: `bdSocketRouter.cpp:1042 Creating DTLS conn`, `bdSocketRouter.cpp:888
  New incoming DTLS connection from 192.168.1.136:3097`, then `advancing to secured`.
- Channel state census (mac, whole run):
    server channel 192.168.1.164:30976  88 secured / 88 connecting3 / 88 connected4
    peer   channel 192.168.1.136:3097   51 secured / 51 connecting3 /  3 connected4
                                        + 12 `failed to connect`
- `bad_security_id` reset fires only 9x against 162 `Found bdSecurityID:
  [793f140d f380590e]` lookups. It is teardown noise, NOT the wall. (Pre-named
  negative: if it were the wall, ZERO channels would ever reach `secured`. 51 do.)
- THE ACTUAL DEATH LINE, immediately before every `failed to connect`:
    `channel '...3097#<UNKNOWN PEER NAME>' (1) lost all owners who want the channel
     to stay open, closing and returning to waiting (no non-sim owners: true)`
  preceded by
    `slice_set_transition_manager: Updating public bubble reservation peer request
     for 'group_target:793F140D:F380590E' to '0' slots.`
  **125 of 125 bubble-reservation updates in the run ask for `0` slots.** The game
  never wants peer capacity in the public bubble, so it drops the channel's last
  owner and the channel dies. `error_connecting_to_bap` (67x) is the CONSEQUENCE.

### VERIFIED 2 - THE PUBLIC ACTIVITY CLIENT HAS NEVER RECEIVED ONE MEMBERSHIP BODY.
Client AC census by role (mac):
    [AC PRIVATE CURRENT ... AH->9eaa300100200001 MEM-1..92]  91x `Acknowledged membership`
    [AC PUBLIC TARGET   ... AH->9eaa300100200003 MEM-0]       0x `Acknowledged membership`
Server side, the type-12 encoder's own line names every session it ever built a
membership snapshot for:
    `ev=activity stage=wire_snapshot session=11433003384888623105 (0x...00200001)` 73 + 23
    `ev=activity stage=wire_snapshot session=11433003384888623108 (0x...00200004)` 93
    session 0x...00200003  -> **ZERO, all run**
0x9EAA3001:00200003 is the shared public AH both clients joined (`stage=activityhost
result=queued host=0x9EAA300100200003` 168x; client `Sent join request [AH
9EAA3001:00200003]` 18x, `EST-Y` 18x). The server established both public ACs into a
session it then never spoke to. MEM-0 -> no other citizen known -> 0-slot reservation
-> dead channel. That is the whole chain, and it closes VERIFIED end to end.

### VERIFIED 3 - ROOT CAUSE: A GATE KEYED ON A DISTINCTION THE CLIENT NEVER MAKES.
`activity_keepalive_push.cpp:149` already contains the exact publisher this needs -
"This link owes exactly one membership body", the private link's member table sent
verbatim on the public envelope, with the upstream quote about client+27696 and the
`activity_public_membership` switch (VERIFIED **ON** in the deployed
RE_output/s1_accept/Sunrise/settings.json). It is gated on
`session.activityRole == ActivityClientRole::publicTarget`.
That role is set in ONE place, `bap_connection_publication.cpp:80-87`, from
`plan.bindsPublicTarget`, set in ONE place, `activity_message_route.cpp:233-267`:
    const bool namesOwnSession   = parsed.sessionId == request.accountHandle;
    const bool namesAdvertisedHost = !namesOwnSession && host_session_for_activity(...) && ...;
`request.accountHandle` is the handle the CLIENT wrote in the svc-8 envelope
(`activity_message_request_parser.cpp:43`) - i.e. the AH the activity client is
addressing. A client joining AH X always addresses AH X, so `parsed.sessionId ==
request.accountHandle` is true BY CONSTRUCTION on every join it can emit.
MEASURED, this run: `stage=join_target result=own` 41x for
session=0x...00200003 handle=0x...00200003, 1x for ...0001, 1x for ...0004;
`result=advertised` **0x**; `ev=activity stage=bind result=public_target` **0x**
in the entire server log. Every link is therefore `privateCurrent`, and the public
membership branch has never executed - not once, in any boot.
NOT a p2(87) regression: p2(74)/20.111 already measured `result=own` bit-for-bit
before region binding existed. L4 was closed by the region-forcing route (p2(78)),
which reaches the shared AH WITHOUT ever tripping `bindsPublicTarget` - so the role,
and its publisher, were left stranded behind a door the chosen road does not use.
`activityJoinedForeignSession` (the other early return at
`activity_keepalive_push.cpp:143`) is INERT: no assignment to it exists anywhere in
the tree, and all 1000 `stage=roster` lines read `foreign=0`. One gate, not two.

### VERIFIED 4 - THE SAME-REGION SKIP IS REAL BUT IS NOT THE LEVER (do not start here).
`activity_membership_push.cpp:207` skips the peer's citizen advertisement when
`peerRegion.index == region.index`: measured `stage=peer_advert result=same_region
own_region=56 peer_region=56 own_citizen=1 peer_citizen=0` **158x** vs 8 `result=built`
(the pre-pairing 48/56 pairs). Co-location CREATED this skip's trigger.
Its stated justification is TWO claims; they now differ:
  (i) host-row key collision (`region_machine_id` keys by region index alone, so the
      peer's `request_host_session` retired the local row) - **DISSOLVED by p2(87)**:
      `group_host_sessions.cpp:143-160`, the `regionBound && sameRegion` branch now
      REUSES the row instead of retiring it. This half of the comment is stale.
  (ii) "a region record holds ONE descriptor" - **STILL TRUE, and it is structural**:
      `activity_membership_region_writer.cpp:50-56` picks
      `advertise ? &citizen : (peerAdvertise ? &peerCitizen : nullptr)` - the LOCAL
      citizen wins and the peer's is silently dropped; `replicate_membership.h:124`
      counts same-index advertisements as ONE. Lifting the skip alone therefore
      changes NOTHING on the wire.
  AND it would be a no-op anyway: `gameplay_advertisement.cpp:145-160` builds every
  citizen descriptor from `endpoint::advertised()` - the SERVER's 192.168.1.164:30976 -
  with `join.machineId = host.groupSessionId` and `onlineSessionId =
  region_identity(..., regionIndex)`. In one shared region the local and peer
  descriptors are byte-identical except `ambassadorSlot`. 20.131's shape (b)
  ("publish server proxy addresses so peer channels dial the server") is ALREADY
  what the citizen advertisement does, and has been all along. Shape (b) is closed.

### THE LANE (design decision, server-only, no client DLL, no new transport)
Make the shared public AH session a thing the server SPEAKS TO. The publisher exists;
what is missing is a reachable predicate for "this link is the public half". Candidates,
in cost order - all decidable from state the server already holds:
  1. Re-key the role on the HOST ROW, not on the envelope: this link's join named a
     session whose `HostSessionBinding` is region-bound and already carries another
     link's source (`request_host_session` returned an existing row). That is exactly
     "the client took the bait", expressed in the vocabulary p2(87) introduced.
  2. Frame a membership body with `sessionId = <shared AH>` directly from the
     activity push path, independent of role (`append_notification_frame(scratch,
     activity.sessionId, ...)` already takes the id as a parameter).
  3. Set the role when `stage=activityhost result=queued host=<id>` and the client
     subsequently joins `<id>` - the server queued that parameter 168x and knows the id.
PRE-NAMED NEGATIVES for whatever boots next (L6/L13):
  - `stage=bind result=public_target` still 0 => the new predicate did not fire; the
    lane is the predicate, not the publisher. Do NOT iterate on the body.
  - `bind` fires, `wire_snapshot session=0x...00200003` appears, but the client still
    reads MEM-0 => the body is addressed right and rejected; THEN the member-table
    content (the client+27696 self-match quote) becomes the lane.
  - `bind` fires, MEM climbs, and the bubble reservation is STILL `0` slots =>
    the reservation is fed by something other than AH membership; re-open the PAH
    tabulated-region read (`Processing new PAH tabulated region data`, 179x).
  - The reservation goes non-zero and the peer channel holds past `connected4` =>
    the milestone gate becomes L9 (co-presence render), not networking.
MAP POINT: the mac's `<UNKNOWN PEER NAME>` and `session_id=` (empty) on BOTH channels,
server included, is normal for this build - the working server channel carries it too.
It is not a symptom; do not chase it.

## 20.130 *** ROOT CAUSE: THE ACTIVITY-HOST ROW IS REBOUND TO WHOEVER PUSHED LAST. ***
## 118 ALLOCATIONS / 116 RETIRES IN ONE RUN - THE SHARED ACTIVITY SESSION NEVER
## SURVIVES THE OTHER CLIENT'S NEXT MEMBERSHIP PUSH. (2026-08-28 ~2:5x, static)

With the row drop fixed (20.129), co-location still failed: the mac's PUBLIC
TARGET activity client joined the server's allocated AH (`9EAA3001:00200003`,
result 0) but the rig's public activity client NEVER joined anything (0
`AC PUBLIC TARGET` lines over 290k ticks - not a log-window artifact). The
static chain, end to end:

1. The citizen advertisement's host-session request is SOURCED from the pushing
   client's own activity session (activity_membership_push.cpp:143-153,
   `snapshot_binding(sessionId, source)`), and the retain that would pin the row
   is dropped right after the build (:158-160).
2. `request_host_session` (group_host_sessions.cpp) matches rows by
   groupSessionId alone, then requires same source GENERATION + region. On a
   source mismatch with zero references: RETIRE + REALLOCATE under a new
   activity-session id, copying the new source's destination.
3. Two clients in one region = the sources alternate on every push. MEASURED:
   118 `result=allocated` / 116 `result=retired` in the p2(86) run, allocations
   704 ticks apart at one point. The mac joined one window; every id the rig's
   client learned (00200007, 0020000C - it RESOLVED them to 192.168.1.164:30975,
   so the parameters flowed) was retired before or while its public AC acted.
4. The same churn defeats `find_ready`'s source-liveness check: the first
   source's session record recycles with the client's ~22.8 s cycle, so the row
   ALSO invalidated itself on every recycle even without a competing client.

FIX (p2(87) 6122885, switch `activity_host_region_bound`, default false): the
row belongs to the (group session, region) - reuse on a same-region source
mismatch, and let the row outlive its first source's record (target liveness
still checked). Destination is activity CONTENT (DestinationSelection:
package/activity/bubble/slice-set - not a network address), and both clients in
the Tower carry the same content, so freezing the first source's destination
costs nothing. OFF = source-bound behavior unchanged.

## THE MAP POINT, COMPLETED (20.129's bonus): THE IDENTITY BLOB'S TWO WINDOWS
The 27-byte identity blob in the join request's table has TWO live 8-byte
windows, each with its own layer:
- blob[0..7] = what the client declares as `machine` in its own ACTIVITY JOIN
  identity (`machine 49:A5:E2:7A:01:FE:F12D`, mac) = what p2(86) publishes in
  membership snapshots (LE u64). VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION (20.129: rows survive).
- blob[5..12] = the ACTIVITY TRACKING key: the `ev=activity stage=identity`
  key (mac 0x32E4DCEEB92DF1FE), the machine named in every peer-reservation
  release on BOTH machines (rig's release names the mac as
  FE:F1:2D:B9:EE:DC:E4:32; mac's names the rig as E6:22:D0:F7:38:83:6C:84),
  and the tail of the client's own session-name string
  (`...@FE_F1_2D_B9_EE_DC_E432`). If a future lane authors a machine id into
  any ACTIVITY-plane body, it is THIS window, LE.
Both machines' release lines naming each other by blob[5..12] is also direct
evidence the activity layer now KNOWS both machines - the release is 20.112's
block firing on "no tracking data", not on unknown identity.

## 20.128 THE JOIN REQUEST'S IDENTITY TABLE IS DECODED. BOTH MACHINES' REAL MACHINE
## IDS ARE IN HAND; THE JOINID STAND-IN CAN NOW BE REPLACED. (2026-08-28 ~1:5x)

p2(85) `7d59eb3bd9946b5d` capture run (paired, healthy: 23 connects, 64 joins,
46 `stage=joincapture` lines = 23 unique containers, all 149 bytes, framing
identical). The container the instrument dumps is marker+header+211-bit prefix
(= 237 bits) followed by the identity table; `tail_bits=955` on every capture
located it exactly. THE TABLE, bit-faithful after a 3-bit slice alignment:

    u8   tag = 0x08 (both machines, every capture)
    u32  address, network order = THE SENDER'S OWN IPv4
         (mac C0A801A4 = 192.168.1.164, rig C0A80188 = 192.168.1.136)
    u16  port, LOW BYTE FIRST = 3097 = the sender's source port
         (the descriptor's own NetAddr grammar: address network order, port
         memory order - join_descriptor.cpp read_network_order/read_memory_order)
    24B  filler = 00 FF 00 FF 00 00 x4 (identical all captures)
    u32+u16 address+port AGAIN, byte-identical to the first pair
    27B  identity blob, DIFFERS PER MACHINE, STABLE ALL BOOT:
         mac 3E313EC1 01FEF12D B9EEDCE432528180 36B049B426CB437478B3D5
         rig F01F13A0 01E622D0 F738836C84528180 2BCF11133D13937ED8D3D5
    tail constants `4600200200 20`, zeros, then `001FB0...` with ONE differing
    byte (off 111: mac 0x10, rig 0x40 - unexplained bit-field, logged for later)

Facts that fall out:
1. The table is SELF-IDENTIFYING: entry 1 names the sender's own address, so the
   runtime decoder can validate its parse against the datagram's source endpoint
   (p2(86) does exactly that - mismatch publishes nothing).
2. The identity qword is per-boot per-machine and STABLE across retries (mac: 21
   identical tables including the paired phase). It is the machineId candidate
   the code comment predicted. BYTE ORDER IS INFERRED low-byte-first from the
   descriptor's convention - logging carries both readings (machine + machine_rev).
3. The advertisement descriptor's head8 (mac D9C8F32959E3DF2C, rig
   5EC1C9C022304DCA tonight) is a DIFFERENT per-boot value from the table's
   qword - two identities exist. Which one the consumer matches is unknown; the
   table's is the named lane's source and ships first.
4. JoinComplete.machine= values (group_host.cpp:714) are unique per join across
   23 captures - a per-join nonce. EXONERATED as an identity source.
5. The mac's table NEVER grew when the rig joined (21 identical captures) - the
   join request declares SELF, not "machines I know".

NEXT (p2(86), built + reviewed separately): parse the table at admission with
the self-check, store the machine id per admitted record, publish it in peer
member rows behind `publish_join_machine_ids` (default false; the stand-in is
byte-restorable without a rebuild). Pre-named in BOOT_BRIEF_p2-86: if rows STILL
drop with real machine ids, the machineId suspect is FALSIFIED and the next
hypothesis opens - the consumer's ladder (handbook: reserved 3 -> 5 -> 7 -> 8 ->
9 -> 10, "do not skip edges"; a foreign row appearing instantly at 10 skips every
edge, while self (found by NetAddr) and host (hostMemberIndex) need no creation)
- plus the consumer's row-creation path as the static lane.

## 20.127 *** ROOT CAUSE: OUR RESOLVER TOLD THE RIG IT LIVES AT THE MAC'S IP. ***
## FIX SHIPPED (p2(84) a0e7fd1). TWO RECORD CORRECTIONS: 20.109'S RESERVE-DETOUR
## CALLER-FRAME READ WAS A MISREADING; 20.125'S "LAST SHIM EVENT" LINE WAS WRONG.
## (2026-08-28 ~1:xx)

Claude (Claude Code, separate CLI) diagnosed; I verified L2 against source and shipped
step 1 of its fix. Full chain: RE_output/claims/rig-stall-self-address.md (164 lines).

### THE CAUSE (each link a log or source line)
`egress_resolver_forward.cpp` answered EVERY name lookup with
`client.external_server.host` (= 192.168.1.164 = the mac = the server) and discarded the
requested name. The game resolves its OWN hostname at boot; the rig's client therefore
joined believing its public address was 192.168.1.164 - the very host it was dialing.
Same boot, same join line: mac `our address 192.168.1.164:3097:192.168.1.164:3097`
(true, server is local), rig `192.168.1.136:3097:192.168.1.164:3097` (public half = the
host). The wedge sits between `reserve mach=0` (host slot) and `reserve mach=1` (SELF
slot) - exactly on the record whose public address duplicates the host's. It hid all
project long because on the mac the lie is true.

### THE FIX (p2(84), step 1 of the claims doc; client DLL only)
Self-name exception in all five resolver entries: hostname (up to first dot) or
"localhost" resolves to this machine's own adapter address inside
peer_subnet/peer_prefix_bits - numerically via GetAdaptersAddresses, no DNS leaves the
box. Switch: `client.external_server.resolve_self_locally`, default TRUE; off restores
today's behavior. SAFETY: on the mac it returns 192.168.1.164 - the SAME value as today
- so the proven solo path is unchanged by construction; a changed mac `our address`
line voids the boot.

  entry-0x2F8, so [rbp+0x330] = the core's own 7th stack argument (the wrapper's arg 7).
  The jr reserve detour is ABI-correct (16-slot forward) and is EXONERATED. The
  p2(71/72/73) freezes were real but their documented cause was wrong.
- 20.125's "the last shim event remains seq=3 lobby_join" is WRONG: the last DLL line in
  both rig logs is our own `ev=jr fn=reserve n=1`. And the netprobe "zero winsock calls"
  is at the width of its four probed functions (send/WSASend/WSASendDisconnect are hooked
  but unprobed). The 20.125 conclusion stands at that width.

## 20.124 L8 BUILT + OFFLINE-VALIDATED: RECORDS KEYED (SESSION, ENDPOINT), PER-RECIPIENT
## COMPLETE SNAPSHOTS, BYTE-IDENTICAL SOLO PATH. THE SIZE CENSUS CAUGHT A SILENT-REFUSE
## DEFECT BEFORE THE BOOT. (2026-08-27 ~23:xx)

Server-side only (group_host, peer_transport, one physics caller, NEW middleware composer
snapshot_builder.{h,cpp}). No client DLL change. Both targets build clean. Deliverable:
RE_output/claims/l8-multi-peer.md; tests: RE_output/scratch/l8_regression/.

### THE DESIGN, AND THE ONE THING THE HANDOFF DID NOT SPELL OUT
The consumer CLEARS its own table and rebuilds it from each snapshot (session_messages.h),
so when peer B joins, peer A must RECEIVE an updated snapshot naming B - the old structure
had no path that ever republished to A. New per-record `snapshotOwed` flag + `service()`
retry (250 ms) carry it; the acting record still publishes immediately, so the join ladder
timing is unchanged. Every session-keyed transport call is now (session, ENDPOINT) -
enqueue_reliable / remote_address / application_ready / link_identity / drop - because
find_session_locked's first match would deliver one peer's records to another's link. The
first-match form survives only in the HUD's display-only link_stage.

### THE VALIDATION THAT PAID
- BYTE IDENTITY: the new composer reproduces the pre-change encoder bit-exact (296/310 B)
  across all 4 one-peer state combinations - the proven solo path cannot have drifted.
- SIZE CENSUS (test_sizes.cpp): worst-case body grows ~139 B/member: 3 members = 435 B,
  7 = 988 B, 8 = 1128 B. The reliable path's enqueue_message stages every body into a
  1024-byte buffer - an 8-member session would have failed SILENTLY (refuse -> retry
  forever, no log line). Fixed structurally, not vigilantly: kSnapshotPeerCapacity = 6,
  kMembershipBodyCapacity = kReassemblyCapacity, kAdmittedCapacity = 6 with
  static_assert(kAdmittedCapacity <= kSnapshotPeerCapacity). A wrong size is now a compile
  error, not a mystery silence (the pre-boot rule: make failure impossible to express).
- REBUILD DISAMBIGUATION: measured (p2(81) server log) that all 11 rebuilt connects came
  from the client's SAME source port, so claim() rebinds on (session, endpoint) match and
  only steals a same-session record whose endpoint has no live link (peer::linked).

### NOT DONE HERE (bounded by evidence, not skipped)
- Player slot/addSequence for the SECOND player: rules implemented from the documented
  consumer semantics (slot = lowest free; sequence = 1 + max, first = 0); N>=2 values are
  INFERRED - the boot is their first execution evidence.
- Physics multi-peer world binding untouched: the second peer is skipped there (benign for
  the roster goal); link_identity disambiguation added so the first world's view key cannot
  silently bind the WRONG peer's channel.


## 20.123 STATIC LANE: PARAMETER 21'S ANSWER GRAMMAR IS NOT RECOVERABLE IN ONE PASS. THE

Lane deliverable: RE_output/claims/param21-body-grammar.md; raw:

### VERIFIED (disassembly, addresses in the deliverable)
- [registry+0xB958] IS the managed session (cross-confirmed +0x1AEF8 state, +0x1C7C0 session
  id - matches 20.109).
- Record 21's vtable = 0x141CA7718 (ctor 0x1417B1EC0). Its encode slots tail-call
  0x140E0DC10 = `xor al,al; ret` - THE CLIENT NEVER ENCODES PARAMETER 21. It is a pure
  requester, so there is no client-side encoder to mirror; the grammar must come from the
  decode path alone.
- Record 21's value object holds a 0x300-byte buffer with [0x44C]=-1 (entry count) and
  [0x450]=0 (total): a reservation LIST, not a scalar. The 0-slots log line names a count
  over that list.
- The decode path runs through the 2252-byte dispatcher 0x1417C48F0 into obfuscated helpers
  (0x1417CF0E0, XOR/magic-constant flattened). Not followed to completion.

### CORRECTION TO 20.122 (do not re-derive from the older text)
fieldB (rec+0x24) = 2 on indices 4,5,6,7,8,17,21,23,24 - NOT just 21/23/24 - and = 1 on
9,12; fieldA = 1 on 0..4, = 2 on 24. The "class marker" therefore does NOT by itself mean
"nested body"; 20.122's correlation with kRequestBodyWidths was partial. 21 IS still
nested-root by our own wire-derived widths; that agreement stands.

### BOOT CONSEQUENCE
Do not change the writer. The clear-root answer ("no value, keep your own") is the only
fully-verified body; inventing the set-root walk is the policy-31 fatal class (p2(80) paid
for one already). The ~22.8 s recycle is expected to persist through the next boot; the
