or (b) the publicSessionReservations reflection schema via the project's schema-walker.


## 20.122 STATIC: THE CLIENT'S GROUP-PARAMETER REGISTRY IS BUILT AT 0x1417A9820,
## AND PARAMETER 21 CARRIES A CLASS MARKER (+0x24 = 2) THAT ONLY 21, 23 AND 24
## HAVE. THE BODY GRAMMAR LIVES BEHIND THE SHARED OBJECT AT record+0x18.
## (2026-08-27 ~21:0x)

All static, no boots. Continues 20.121 (parameter 21's request body is empty; the
client is asking us for the value and our answer says "no value").

### THE ANCHOR
Registry builder: **fn 0x1417A9820** (0x1417A9820..0x1417A9D63, 1347 B), called from
0x14178CD4D. Found from the parameter NAME TABLE, whose strings are in the image:
`public-session-reservations` @ 0x141CA8648 (strings_dump.txt:7700), with its
neighbours 0x141CA8610 matchmaking-progress, 0x141CA8628 initial-slice-set-status,
0x141CA8668 clan-lobby-data.

### THE RECORD LAYOUT, READ FROM THE BUILDER
Each parameter gets a record, written in a repeating block:
    [rec+0x08] = registry index          (dword)
    [rec+0x10] = name pointer            (qword, the string above)
    [rec+0x18] = SHARED OBJECT, the same value for every record, loaded each time
                 from [rbx+0xB958]
    [rec+0x20] = field A
    [rec+0x24] = field B                 (only on some records)
    [rec+0x28], [rec+0x2C] = extra, only on index 24
Record pointers are also written to a table at [rbx+0xBA00 + 8*n].

### TWO SHAPES, AND 21 IS IN THE MINORITY
    index 18,19,20,22:  mov QWORD [rec+0x20], rsi          (64-bit zero, no +0x24)
    index 21:           mov DWORD [rec+0x20], esi ; mov DWORD [rec+0x24], 2
    index 23:           mov DWORD [rec+0x20], esi ; mov DWORD [rec+0x24], 2
    index 24:           mov DWORD [rec+0x20], 2   ; mov DWORD [rec+0x24], 2
                        + mov DWORD [rec+0x28], 0x7530 (30000)
                        + mov DWORD [rec+0x2C], 0x2710 (10000)
So **21, 23 and 24 share a class marker the others do not have**, and 24 adds what
read as two timings (30 s / 10 s).
Cross-check that this marker means "multi-record / nested body": our own
`kRequestBodyWidths` independently marks 21, 22 and 23 as "nested root" and 24 as
"single-record request", derived from the wire long before this table was read. 21,
23, 24 line up; 22 does not, and 22 is exactly the index where the two disagree
(below). Treat the marker as CORRELATED with nested bodies, not proven to mean it.

### A DIVERGENCE WORTH RECORDING
The client's own table has **index 22 = `clan-lobby-data`**. The handbook's registry
(p43) lists 22 as `matchmakingData`. The client is the authority for this build;
the handbook's list is otherwise index-for-index identical. Do not "correct" our
names to the handbook's for 22.

### THE NEXT MOVE, NAMED EXACTLY
The body grammar is NOT in this builder - it writes indices, names and class
markers only. Every record shares one object at `[rbx+0xB958]`, stored at
`rec+0x18`. That object is the common owner and is where a codec or vtable would
live. NEXT (static, no boot):
  1. Find what fills `[rbx+0xB958]` in fn 0x1417A9820's prologue, and what rbx is
     (the caller is 0x14178CD4D).
  2. If it is an object with a vtable, read the slots: an encode/decode pair keyed
     by the class marker at rec+0x24 is the shape to expect.
  3. Only then encode parameter 21's answer body. Handbook 16.5 makes a wrong
     nesting a policy-31 FATAL decode, and p2(80) already cost a boot to a guess
     this session.

### AND ONE INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION FROM THE SAME PASS
Handbook 20.4 gives the publication order for a new group join: "1. Finish peer
establishment. 2. EXCHANGE ONE NORMAL CONNECTED PACKET. 3. Publish a complete
membership snapshot. 4. Publish required group parameters." That is exactly the
application-ready gate p2(79) shipped, arrived at from the wire rather than from
this page. The gate is right and should stay.

## 20.121 p2(81): PARAMETER 21's REQUEST BODY CAPTURED. IT IS EMPTY - THE CLIENT IS
## ASKING US FOR THE VALUE, AND WE ANSWER "NO VALUE". ALSO: THE PUBLIC ROUTE ONLY
## RUNS WHEN THE CLIENT ACTUALLY SEARCHES, WHICH IS STATE-DEPENDENT.
## (2026-08-27 ~20:5x)

Solo, mac. Server `72177ecd4083e6b0`, client `3fa02bb785a1cacc`.

### THE CAPTURE
    stage=parameters result=body_capture mask=0x00200000 bytes=256
      hex=0804 0000...00   (256 bytes captured of a ~1005-byte tail; all zero after
                            the first two)
mask names ONLY parameter 21. `kRequestBodyWidths[21] = kNoRequestCodec // nested
request root`, which is why the walk stops there and why `walked=0x00000000`.
READING: the request body is essentially EMPTY. The client is not telling us a
reservation count - it is ASKING for one. Our answer for 21 is
`writer.write(0U, kRootBitClear)`, i.e. "no value, keep your own", so the client
keeps zero and logs "Updating public bubble reservation peer request ... to '0'
slots".
MARK: bytes verified-by-execution; "empty means asking" is INFERRED from the mask
naming only 21 plus the all-zero body.

### THE REAL BLOCKER IS THE ANSWER SIDE, AND IT IS NOT A QUICK PATCH
We now know the request. What we still lack is the ANSWER body layout for registry
21 - what a non-empty `publicSessionReservations` value looks like on the wire.
That is a static RE task on the client's parameter decoder, not a guess: handbook
16.5 records a wrong nesting as a policy-31 FATAL decode, and this session already
spent one boot on a guess (p2(80), 20.120).
NEXT (static, no boot): recover the layout of parameter 21's answer body from the
client's decoder. Anchor: our own `write_parameter_body` is the shape to extend, and
`kRequestBodyWidths` documents the grammar style for the parameters we DID recover
(bias-encoded value fields of 2-9 bits) - 21 is one of six marked "nested root".

### SEPARATE AND IMPORTANT: THE PUBLIC ROUTE IS STATE-DEPENDENT
The boot before this one forced the region public (`result=forced ... slice_set=56`)
and then NEVER SEARCHED: `session_search=0`, `initiate_search=0`, `recv=0`, and the
client froze on spawn-in with the game alive. Its transition manager logged
"Transition to private slice-set" and a "minimum matchmaking time of 10000ms" - a
different route into the Tower than the three preceding boots took.
After `reset_lobby_claims.sh` and a relaunch, the same build searched once, was
answered with our endpoint, dialed (recv=37), and reached the parameter request.
So: A PUBLIC REGION WITH NO SEARCH IS A HARD STALL - the client waits forever for a
host it never asked for. The search firing is not guaranteed by forcing the region;
it depends on server state (claims/presence/host sessions) at the moment of the
transition. Reset between runs is not hygiene here, it is a precondition.
This is the same failure shape as p2(76)'s orbit stall, now seen at the Tower, and
it is the honest cost of forcing the flag: when the rest of the route does not line
up, the client has no fallback.

## 20.120 RETRACTION: THE "IDLE TIMEOUT" WAS MY OWN FILTERED GREP. TRAFFIC FLOWS
## BOTH WAYS EVERY ~250 ms AND THE PEER STILL REBUILDS EVERY ~22.8 s. THE REAL
## LEAD IS REGISTRY PARAMETER 21, publicSessionReservations, WHICH WE CANNOT
## DECODE AND THE CLIENT THEN SETS TO ZERO SLOTS. (2026-08-27 ~20:1x)

Solo boot, mac. Server `4d966c3d8808d1c2`, client `a604e2eed3995b3e`.
Brief: RE_output/claims/BOOT_BRIEF_p2-80.md.

### THE RETRACTION, AND IT IS AN ANALYSIS ERROR NOT A CODE ONE
20.119 claimed "21.5 seconds of COMPLETE SILENCE, both directions" after a settled
join, and built the keepalive on it. That silence does not exist. The grep behind it
listed stages explicitly - join|membership|publish|player|link|connect|parameters|
view - and OMITTED `packet`. `ev=gameplay stage=packet result=ok` arrives every
~250 ms throughout, continuously, in the very window I called silent.
So: an absence I measured with an instrument that could not have shown the thing
present. That is L13 turned on its own author, and the rule it earns is narrower
than "check for absence": AN EXPLICIT STAGE LIST IS A FILTER, AND A FILTER'S
ABSENCES ARE ITS OWN. Census first (`grep -o "ev=gameplay stage=[a-z_]*" | sort |
uniq -c`), then filter - the census was run earlier the same session and would have
shown `packet` at 125 hits.

### CONSEQUENCE: THE KEEPALIVE IS INERT AND THE CYCLE IS UNCHANGED
`ev=gameplay stage=keepalive result=sent`: ZERO for the whole boot. The condition
requires `!acknowledgementOwed && !resendDue`, and since the peer sends every
~250 ms this host always owes an acknowledgement - so it always sends anyway and the
keepalive never fires. (Beware: `grep stage=keepalive` also matches the PRE-EXISTING
`ev=activity stage=keepalive` BAP line, which is what made this look like 20 hits at
first read. Match `ev=gameplay stage=keepalive`.)
The cycle survived exactly as before: connects at t=322699 (rebuilt=0), t=345522
(rebuilt=1), t=368323 (rebuilt=1) - 22.8 s apart - and 6 admits.
p2(80) is therefore a NO-OP that must not be credited with anything, and OUTCOME 2
of its brief is the one that landed: the rebuild is a lifetime the peer enforces
REGARDLESS OF TRAFFIC. Adding more traffic was the wrong instinct and the brief said
so before the boot: "read what the client logs in the second before the rebuild
rather than adding more traffic."

### WHAT THE CLIENT ACTUALLY DOES, PER CYCLE (its own words)
    session state 'none' -> 'peer-creating' -> 'peer-joining' -> 'peer-established'
    idle_peer_creating: [group_target:29A9502E:C95818A9] initiated join to remote
      group session at '192.168.1.164:30976...'
    session:join: remote join to host-address 192.168.1.164:30976
    peer # 0 a=[steamid:...] s= _established o=-1 j=[XXXXXXXX-XXXXXXXX] amb=[X...]
    slice_set_transition_manager: Updating public bubble reservation peer request
      for 'group_target:29A9502E:C95818A9' to '0' SLOTS.
Five full cycles. It reaches `peer-established` every time and then recycles. Note
the membership dump alternates `peers valid: 0x3 ... players valid: 0x0` with
`peers valid: 0x1 ... players valid: 0x1`, and the established peer's join id prints
as the placeholder `XXXXXXXX-XXXXXXXX`.

### THE LEAD, AND IT IS EXACT
Our own server log, every cycle:
    stage=parameters result=request mask=0x00200000 mode=0 names=public-session-reservations
    stage=parameters result=ambiguous walked=0x00000000 STOPPED=21 tail=8039
    stage=parameters result=answered carried=0x00200000 names=public-session-reservations
Handbook p43 lists the group parameter registry, and index **21 is
`publicSessionReservations`** - the exact index our walker stops at, having walked
nothing (walked=0x00000000) with 8,039 bits of body left. We cannot decode it, we
"answer" it with nothing meaningful, and the client then sets its public bubble
reservation to ZERO SLOTS and recycles the session ~22.8 s later.
A public bubble with zero reserved slots has no room for anybody, which is a
coherent reason for the client to tear the session down and try again.

MARK: the chain request -> ambiguous -> "0 slots" -> recycle is INFERRED from
ordering and plausibility. Each individual line is verified-by-execution. Do not
upgrade it without a build that answers parameter 21 properly and nothing else.

### NEXT
Implement group registry parameter 21 (`publicSessionReservations`) in the parameter
path, with a real slot count. Handbook 20.2 is the neighbouring constraint already
known: "The peer needs a membership snapshot AND at least one parameter update", and
`activityHost` must be nonzero. The bubble policy values are documented at 16.4
(max players 9, posse 3, matchmade 6).
DO NOT add more transport traffic. That was this boot's wrong turn and it is
recorded here so it is not repeated.

## 20.119 p2(79): THE APPLICATION-READY GATE WORKS AND THE JOIN NOW COMPLETES
## CLEANLY. THE ~21.5 s CYCLE SURVIVES IT AND HAS A DIFFERENT CAUSE: BOTH SIDES GO
## SILENT AFTER THE JOIN AND THE LINK IDLES OUT. (2026-08-27 ~19:5x)

Solo boot, mac. Server `aa665c071ef68aa6`, client `c6fc5da1f093ff52`.
Brief: RE_output/claims/BOOT_BRIEF_p2-79.md.

### THE GATE DID EXACTLY WHAT IT SAYS
    stage=publish result=held reason=not_application_ready session=0x584CDFAF77FC5C5D
    stage=publish result=released reason=application_ready session=0x584CDFAF77FC5C5D
Held, then released ~1.0 s later (996 ms and 1007 ms on the two cycles). The peer
does send its first established packet about a second after admit, and holding until
then is both necessary and short. Admits fell from 13 to 4.

### AND THE JOIN NOW RUNS TO COMPLETION, IN ORDER
    t=290442 connect result=ok rebuilt=0
    t=290467 link bound / publish HELD / parameters queued / join admit
    t=291463 publish RELEASED
    t=291464 membership built revision=1 members=2 players=0
    t=291701 membership built revision=2 members=2 players=1 + player result=added
    t=291702 parameters request -> answered  names=public-session-reservations
    t=291703 membership built revision=3
    t=291705 join result=completed
    t=291765 player result=properties
That is the whole ladder, in order, with the peer's parameter request ANSWERED -
further than any previous boot. OUTCOME 1 of the brief on every point except the
cycle.

### BUT THE CYCLE SURVIVED, AND ITS CAUSE IS NOT 18.5
    t=291765  (last line of the completed join)
    ... 21.5 seconds of COMPLETE SILENCE, both directions ...
    t=313251  connect result=ok rebuilt=1     <- the PEER rebuilt its channel
So this is OUTCOME 3 for the cycle specifically: handbook 18.5 is EXONERATED as its
cause. The gate fixed what it was for (publishing into a peer that could not
dispatch) and the re-join has a separate, simpler cause.

CAUSE, read from our own code: `peer_transport::service` sends only when
`acknowledgementOwed` or a resend is due. Once the join settles this host owes
nothing, so it sends NOTHING; the peer, having nothing to answer, also sends
nothing; and the link idles out. `stage=ping` count for the whole boot: ZERO -
neither side pings. A real host emits a continuous packet stream and ours emits
none. The peer then tears down and rebuilds (`rebuilt=1`), redoing a join that had
completed cleanly ~21.5 s earlier.

### FIX (p2(80)): A KEEPALIVE ON IDLE CONNECTED LINKS
`kKeepaliveInterval` = 1000 ms. `service` now also sends when a CONNECTED link has
been silent that long, carrying the ack/sequence state it already builds. One packet
per second costs one sequence out of a 1024 modulus, and 1 s is far inside the
~21.5 s timeout. Logged `stage=keepalive result=sent` at debug, because "the link is
being held open" and "the link is merely quiet" look identical from outside (L13).

### WHAT IS NOW TRUE OF THE SINGLE-PEER PATH
Client-side across the cycles: `ev=jr fn=reserve` firing for mach=0 AND mach=1, both
at memb=10 (ESTABLISHED), ZERO hitches, and the client healthy throughout. The only
defect left on the solo path was the idle timeout. If p2(80) holds the link, the
single peer is stable and L8 - one session, many peers - becomes readable, which is
the whole reason the paired run has been deferred.

## 20.118 *** THE DOOR OPENED. *** p2(78) FORCED THE TOWER'S REGION PUBLIC AND THE
## CLIENT DIALED THE GAMEPLAY ENDPOINT FOR THE FIRST TIME: DTLS, ESTABLISH, GROUP
## JOIN, MEMBERSHIP, PLAYER ADDED, JOIN COMPLETED. TWO DEFECTS NOW HOLD IT OPEN
## ONLY BRIEFLY, AND BOTH ARE PRECISELY SPECIFIED. (2026-08-27 ~19:3x)

Solo (mac) then paired (mac + rig). Client `ee8998551caf95f1` both machines, server
`d372d4ea562bf349`. Settings-only change: `region_public_slice_set: 56`.
Brief: RE_output/claims/BOOT_BRIEF_p2-78.md.

### WHAT HAPPENED, AND EVERY ONE OF THESE IS A FIRST
- `ev=gameplay stage=receive` = 64. It has been ZERO on every boot in this project.
- The whole stack ran: dtls ok 30, establish ok 4, packet ok 125, message ok 17,
  `connect result=ok peer=3097`, `link result=bound`, `join result=admit`,
  `membership result=built members=2 players=1`, `player result=added`,
  `join result=completed`.
- Scoping held exactly: 24 and 48 `result=native answer=0`, 56 `result=forced
  native=0 answer=1`. The Tower loaded as **PUB56.56** (was PRV56.56).
- CLIENT SIDE: `ev=jr fn=reserve mach=1 memb=10` - the 20.108 host-side reservation
  firing for a SECOND MACHINE at member state 10 = ESTABLISHED, the exact state
  20.113 proved the game requires.
- `peers valid: 0x3` - two peers in the session bitmask. It has been 0x1 always.
- `[AC PUBLIC TARGET ...]` - the activity client took the PUBLIC TARGET role. Every
  line of every previous boot read `PRIVATE CURRENT`.
- `Could not find tracking data`: 0. `peer-reservation release`: 0. The two lines
  that ended every peer's life (20.112) did not fire once.
- The player id the server added (0xCED4C4530F4A0B9F) equals the one the client's
  own `admit` names. Both sides agree on identity.
L6 and L7 of FRONT_public-host-chain.md move from verified-by-reading to
VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION.

### DEFECT 1 - WE PUBLISH MEMBERSHIP BEFORE THE PEER CAN RECEIVE IT (handbook 18.5)
13 admits, 16 distinct join ids, on a ~21.7 SECOND CYCLE - and the cycle was
present in the SOLO boot too, so it is not a two-client problem:
    t=2794027 join=0xAD20BF0D...  t=2815503 join=0xCEFBA255...  t=2836616 join=0x5AD9F75F...
The peer joins, we queue membership and parameters immediately (`membership=queued
parameters=queued` on the admit line), it reports complete, and ~21.7 s later it
joins again with a fresh join id. Handbook 18.5, which our digest dropped:
    "The establishment exchange alone does not make the peer application-ready. The
     client must also exchange a normal connected packet. Before that boundary, the
     transport can ACKNOWLEDGE reliable records WITHOUT DISPATCHING their group
     messages. Wait for application-ready before you send important membership or
     parameter records."
That is the symptom exactly: our retry logic sees the ack and is satisfied; the
client never dispatches the record; the join never truly lands; it restarts.
GREP CONFIRMS WE HAVE NO SUCH CONCEPT: zero hits for app-ready/applicationReady
anywhere under server/gameplay/. MARK: mechanism verified-by-citation + the cycle
verified-by-execution; the causal link is INFERRED until a build that gates
publication on application-ready changes the cycle.

### DEFECT 2 - ONE ADMITTED RECORD PER SESSION, REBOUND TO THE LAST ENDPOINT
Both clients named the SAME group session `0xC936D593933D90FE`. That is the good
news and the trap. `claim()` (group_host.cpp:147) is keyed by session and, on a
hit, REBINDS `entry.endpoint = peer` - written for one client rebuilding its
channel from a new port ("Admission is what establishes ownership"). With two
clients it means each admit steals the record: the snapshot goes to whoever joined
last, carrying THAT client's join id, and the other client's entry for itself now
names a foreign join id - which group_host's own comments say a peer refuses.
`members=2 players=1` on every one of the 39 snapshots. Never 3, never 2 players.

### THE TWO CLIENTS' END STATES (different, and both informative)
- MAC: alive and healthy in the log - still accepting pushes at t=524954, ZERO
  hitches, `reserve` still firing with mach=0 AND mach=1 at memb=10. Its black
  screen is the RENDER-DEATH class already parked in 20.110/20.111, not a logic
  failure. The user read it correctly as "rendering black, not freeze black".
- RIG: reached `activity:in_world`, then a hitch storm - `hitch detected: mainloop
  world controller state activity:in_world` x18944, still climbing at the tail. Its
  `reserve` only ever saw mach=0; it never got the second machine. Consistent with
  losing the record-rebinding race to the mac.

### WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE GOAL
The road is open and the remaining work is two named, bounded changes in code we
own - not another unknown:
  1. Gate membership/parameter publication on application-ready (one normal
     connected packet after establish). Handbook 18.6 adds the corollary we must
     also respect: "Do not interpret an acknowledgement as proof that the
     application dispatched the record."
  2. Make one group session hold a LIST of peers: `Admitted` gains endpoints,
     `publish_snapshot` builds host + N peers with N players, and each peer
     receives a snapshot whose entry for ITSELF carries its own join id and its own
     address blob echoed byte-exact (group_host.cpp's existing comments state both
     constraints). That is L8, and `kSnapshotMemberCount = 2` is where it starts.
Do 1 BEFORE 2: a stable single peer is the control that makes 2's result readable,
and 1 alone may change the cycle for both clients.

## 20.117 p2(76): THE REGION WENT PUBLIC - FIRST TIME EVER - AND THE CLIENT SAID
## EXACTLY WHAT IT IS WAITING FOR. THE FORCE WAS TOO BROAD: IT LANDS ON ORBIT.
## (2026-08-27 ~18:4x)

Solo boot, mac. Client `54c04cbfba204488`, server `e9b452316976c66c`.
Brief + run log: RE_output/claims/BOOT_BRIEF_p2-76.md.

### THE CONTRACT LINE, EXACTLY AS PREDICTED
    ev=bootflow stage=region result=forced native=0 answer=1 slice_set=24
Two things settle at once, both verified-by-execution:
1. THE SITE IS REACHED. 20.82's "the filtered call site was never reached this boot"
   is now formally refuted, and with it the three findings that cited it forward
   (20.82, 20.111, 20.115). The silence was our own early return (20.116).
2. THE GAME'S OWN ANSWER IS `native=0` - not public - exactly as 20.116 predicted
   from reading the early return. The prediction held.

### WHAT THE CLIENT THEN SAID, IN ITS OWN WORDS
    Starting a new transition of type 'transitioning:orbit' to
      'PUB24.24 (HASH: 0x841b4ef7)'; token advanced to 001
    Started / Finished precaching slice-set 'PUB24.24 (HASH: 0x841b4ef7)'
    Region 'PUB24.24 (HASH: 0x841b4ef7)' is PUBLIC but not yet connected;
      the slice-set-switch task is not possible yet.          [x8]
    Failed to find the Orbit slice-set name for destination 'HASH(0x13a023bc)', so
      we're falling back to a new character (Orbit destination 'HASH(0x59ad083e)',
      slice-set 'HASH(0x841b4ef7)')
The region really did go PUBLIC - the first time in this project - and the client
names its wait precisely: PUBLIC BUT NOT YET CONNECTED. That is the state we have
been trying to reach for twenty boots, and it is now reachable on demand.

### AND THE FORCE WAS TOO BROAD - THE FIRST TRANSITION THROUGH THAT POINT IS ORBIT
`transitioning:orbit`. We forced ORBIT public. An orbit that waits on a public host
never completes, so the boot stalls before the work we wanted to test: ZERO
matchmaking_seeker lines, ZERO server-side `stage=activityhost` / `stage=advertise`
/ `stage=join_target` lines this whole boot. The client never allocated an activity
host at all. Observed by the user as a black loading loop with the director and
inventory still usable - the game is alive and holding, exactly as OUTCOME 4 of the
brief pre-named it ("INFORMATION, not a crash").
This does NOT retract handbook 15.3. Their change was at this decision point for a
DESTINATION route; ours applied to every transition reaching it, and orbit gets
there first. "Change the input at the exact native decision point" also means at the
exact TRANSITION, which our blanket switch did not express.

### WHAT "CONNECTED" MEANS IS NOW THE QUESTION, AND THE WAIT IS PASSIVE
While holding, the client initiated NOTHING: no search, no matchmaking, no datagram
to 30976. So a public-but-unconnected region does not go looking for a host - it
waits to be told it is connected. Whatever sets that flag is upstream of the search,
not downstream of it, and it is the next thing to find. Do not assume it is the
activity-host bind (L4/L5) without reading it.

### CHANGE MADE (p2(77)): SCOPE THE FORCE TO ONE SLICE SET
`client.region_public_slice_set` (int, -1 = use the blanket `region_public`) forces
public for exactly ONE slice set. Orbit is 24 on this account, so naming the Tower's
slice set leaves orbit alone.
WE DO NOT YET KNOW THE TOWER'S SLICE SET, because no boot has ever reached it with a
working region instrument. The FIXED instrument reports EVERY decision now, native
and forced alike, so one ordinary boot harvests the whole map. Both machines are set
to `region_public: false`, `region_public_slice_set: -1` - the pre-p2(76) behaviour -
for that harvest.

### PROCESS NOTE, RECORDED BECAUSE IT NEARLY SHIPPED
The patch adding the setting applied; the patch teaching the hook to READ it did not
(a failed `cd` swallowed it), and the build still succeeded because an unused setting
compiles fine. It was caught by grepping the changed files for the new symbol before
deploying, not by the build. A setting that parses and is never read is exactly the
p2(64) failure class (a relay that returned false into a discarded value): the knob
appears to work and changes nothing. GREP THE CONSUMER, not just the definition,
after any multi-file settings change.

## 20.116 THE SILENCE WE READ AS "NEVER REACHED" WAS AN EARLY RETURN IN OUR OWN
## HOOK. THE NATIVE ANSWER IS ALREADY "NOT PUBLIC", AND THE HANDBOOK RECORDS
## FORCING IT PUBLIC AT THAT EXACT CALL AS THE CHANGE THAT STARTED THE CITIZEN
## AND SEARCH PATH. (2026-08-27 ~18:2x)

### THE INSTRUMENT BUG, AND WHAT IT COST
`region_private.cpp`'s reader was:
    if (!original(sliceSet)) { return false; }        // <- returns BEFORE any report
    caller = _ReturnAddress(); if (caller != site) return true;
    forced = regionPrivate || override_active();
    report(sliceSet, forced);
So a FALSE native answer produced no line at all. Its own `report` doc comment even
says "Only a public bubble reaches here" - the author knew; the readings did not.
20.82 saw zero `result=forced|public` lines on both machines and concluded "the
filtered call site was never reached this boot, so the setting had no effect". That
conclusion is RETRACTED. The correct reading of the same silence is the opposite and
far more useful: the site is reached and THE NATIVE ANSWER IS ALREADY FALSE - the
game itself says this bubble is not public. `region_private` was indeed inert, but
only because it can only force the answer DOWN, and the answer was already down.
20.111/20.115 repeated the bad reading twice more ("the flag remains inert, as 20.82
measured") because the instrument could not distinguish the two cases. L13 exactly:
an absent line meant something, and we assigned it the wrong meaning three times.
FIXED THIS BUILD: both branches report, and the line now carries the native answer
and ours separately - `stage=region result=native|forced native=N answer=N
slice_set=N`. Also moved `_ReturnAddress()` to the first statement, ahead of the
call to the original, per the rule the retail-log observer already follows.

### WHAT THE HANDBOOK SAYS TO DO ABOUT IT (15.3, read from the SOURCE this time)
    "HISTORICAL: Homecoming first stayed on a private or fireteam path. Changing the
     region transition input to public started the citizen and search path. The
     change occurred at the transition-starter call. Forcing a global reader or an
     orbit destination did not identify the correct boundary.
     General rule: Change the input at the exact native decision point. Do not force
     a downstream result globally."
Our hook is already anchored on exactly that boundary: it answers only for the
STARTER's own call (`find_return_site` requires exactly one matching call site in
the starter's body, and rejects the install if there are two). We have had the right
lever in the right place since it was written, wired only in the direction that
loads solo.

### ALSO MINED FROM 15.2, AND IT IS A GATE WE HAVE NEVER CHECKED
    "The public route uses a region activity host, an advertisement, and a citizen
     membership row. The ambassador slot must differ from the local slot...
     THE ADVERTISEMENT HOST IDENTIFIER MUST MATCH THE activityHost GROUP PARAMETER.
     A mismatch causes the client to report public_activity_host_mismatch."
We publish both an advertisement (`build_candidate`, machineId = host.groupSessionId)
and an `activity-host` group parameter (`publish_activity_host`), and NOTHING in this
project has ever checked that those two carry the same identifier. If the region goes
public and the client then reports `public_activity_host_mismatch`, that string is the
next thing to grep for - it is pre-named here so the next boot cannot mistake it for
a new mystery.

### p2(76): ONE CONTRACT
`client.region_public` (new, default false, ON for this boot on both machines) forces
the starter's public-flag input to TRUE at that one call site. `region_private` still
wins if both are set, and a forced destination still loads solo - no public host
serves one. Nothing else changed; p2(75)'s self-host search answer stays on.

## 20.115 p2(75) PHASE 1: THE SEARCH ANSWER LANDED - FIRST TIME EVER - AND THE
## CLIENT CANCELLED ITS OWN SEARCH 30 ms LATER. THE CAUSE IS OUR EMPTY-BUT-PRESENT
## CONFIGURATION, AND THE HANDBOOK DOCUMENTS BOTH THE FAILURE AND THE FIX.
## (2026-08-27 ~18:2x)

Solo control boot, mac. Deployed: server `ab26ade01c671839`, client
`d703d6472914f3dc`. Brief: RE_output/claims/BOOT_BRIEF_p2-75.md.

### THE CHANGE WORKED, EXACTLY AS SPECIFIED
    stage=descriptor where=serve_self blob=128 routable=1
      asif_local=192.168.1.164:30976 asif_public=192.168.1.164:30976
      asif_nat=1 asif_method=0 asif_session=0x6437A1A8BC0D4889
    stage=request kind=session_search served_descriptor=1
      advertisement=0x82BC542310D328C2 encoded=1 bytes=164
First non-empty search answer this project has ever produced for a FIRST searcher
(the relay it replaced returns `result=none self=1` to whoever searches first). The
`asif_` fields are meaningful here and only here: this is OUR blob in OUR layout, so
20.90's foreign-blob rule does not apply. routable=1, naming our gameplay endpoint.

### AND IT CHANGED NOTHING DOWNSTREAM: ZERO DATAGRAMS ON 30976 (still)
`ev=gameplay stage=receive` count: 0. OUTCOME 2 of the brief - but NOT for either
reason the brief pre-named. The topology suspect (loopback vs LAN) is not indicted,
because the client never got as far as evaluating the endpoint.

### WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENED (client clock)
    t=175615  matchmaking_seeker: Starting the search. MM type=0
              qos_threshold_top_results_fraction=0.5 qos_threshold_minimum_of_top_results=4
    t=175616  matchmaking_seeker: new session properties have been set, clearing the current search
    t=175617  matchmaking_seeker: local search index 0, total=1
    t=175619  composition: initiate_search [PRIVATE activity] [1 players]
    t=175632  matchmaking_seeker: new session properties have been set, clearing the current search
    t=175633  matchmaking_seeker: online session search has a currently running task, we must wait
    t=175646  matchmaking_seeker: Ending the search [0].  No results were being tracked.
    t=175648  team_picker: BEGIN posse session 'HOST' team dump, call_site=Exit Matchmaking
The search lives 31 ms and is cleared TWICE by the client's own session-property
change before it ends with nothing tracked. It then exits matchmaking as HOST.

### THE SERVER SIDE OF THE SAME 500 ms
    t=331980  kind=configuration        encoded=1 bytes=2      <- EMPTY BUT PRESENT
    t=332328  kind=advertisement_update
    t=332421  kind=advertisement_update
    t=332448  kind=session_search       served_descriptor=1 bytes=164
    t=332476  kind=advertisement_update      <- the advertisement change
    t=332515  kind=advertisement_update
Seven advertisement_updates this boot, clustered around the one search.

### THE HANDBOOK NAMED THIS, TOO (p33-34, 16.6 - the SECOND time in one day)
    "An empty but present configuration is not neutral.
     HISTORICAL: Zero timing thresholds caused an immediate advertisement change.
     That change also changed the generation and cancelled the active search.
     Use valid nonzero policy values when the field is present."
Our configuration answer is `encode_empty_message(kConfigurationField, ...)` -
2 bytes, present, empty (matchmaking_response_encoder.cpp:58-59). The observed
sequence is the documented one: empty configuration -> advertisement change ->
generation change -> active search cancelled -> "No results were being tracked".
MARK: the CAUSAL link between our empty configuration and the property change is
INFERRED (the handbook's mechanism fits the observed ordering exactly). The ordering
itself, and the cancellation, are verified-by-execution. Do not upgrade the mark
without a boot that changes the configuration and nothing else.

### THE FIX, WITH VALUES BUT WITHOUT A LAYOUT (16.4)
The handbook states what a working configuration carries:
  lane policy   - service configuration 1; search-only interval 60 s;
                  desperation interval 60 s; one present provider-policy entry
  bubble policy - maximum players 9; maximum posse size 3; maximum matchmade 6;
                  service configuration 1
It gives VALUES, not FIELD NUMBERS. Recovering the svc-43 field-4 layout is the next
piece of real work, and 16.5's warning about the search result applies here too:
"a result with one incorrect nesting level caused a policy-31 fatal decode."

### PHASE 2 NOT RUN, DELIBERATELY
A paired boot adds nothing while the search dies this way: the cancellation is
driven by the searching client's own property churn, not by what the other client
published, and the foreign relay this build replaced is no longer in the path.
Spend the paired run after the configuration answer is real.

### WHERE THIS LEAVES THE CHAIN (FRONT_public-host-chain.md)
Step 2 of 20.114's chain is now HALF fixed: we name the right host, and the client
never gets to consider it because step 1.5 - the configuration that governs whether
a search survives at all - is empty. The chain is unchanged below that point.

## 20.114 *** THE UPSTREAM CAUSE. *** THE SEARCH ANSWER IS THE WRONG HOST. WE HAND
## EACH CLIENT THE OTHER CLIENT'S STEAM-IDENTITY BLOB INSTEAD OF OUR OWN GAMEPLAY
## ENDPOINT - AND THE FUNCTION THAT BUILDS THE RIGHT ONE IS DEAD CODE.
## (2026-08-27 ~19:1x)

All static, plus a re-read of the p2(74) server log. This is as far upstream as the
code goes: everything in 20.111/20.112/20.113 is downstream of this one answer.

### THE ORACLE WE ALREADY OWNED (U1 - and it had been sitting here since 08-22)
FINDINGS_2026-08-24 closed with "the only remaining unknown is ONE protobuf shape -
the layout INSIDE svc-43 field 3, which upstream does not implement either". The
community handbook answers it verbatim (p33-34, RE_output/dumps, digested 08-22):
    HISTORICAL: a working descriptor-bearing search result used
      root field 3 / repeated field 1 / field 1 descriptor message /
      field 1 exact 128-byte descriptor
    "A result with one incorrect nesting level caused a policy-31 fatal decode."
OUR ENCODER ALREADY MATCHES IT (matchmaking_dynamic_response.cpp:153-185: field 3
SearchResults -> field 1 SearchResult -> field 1 DescriptorWrapper -> field 1
bytes[128], plus an IdPair at field 6). The shape was never the gap.
LESSON, and it is the expensive one: a question we called "the honest next front"
and carried for three days was answered in a document already in this repo, digested
by us, cited in our own doc map. U1 says fetch the reference; it does not say check
the one you already have. It does now.

### WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENS, MEASURED (p2(74) server log)
    stage=descriptor where=publish blob=128 ... asif_machine=0x16FFF2121B989B4E   x9
    stage=descriptor where=publish blob=128 ... asif_machine=0x24D2E4E887092FF1   x5
    stage=descriptor where=raw off=0 hex=...737465616D69643A3736353631313938373736373538...
    stage=foreign result=none  self=1 sibling=0      <- FIRST searcher: nothing to give
    stage=foreign result=found self=0 sibling=0
    stage=request kind=session_search served_descriptor=0 advertisement=0x0 bytes=2
    stage=request kind=session_search served_descriptor=1 advertisement=0x1 bytes=146
Both clients publish a 128-byte descriptor. ONE search was answered WITH a
descriptor - 146 bytes, correctly nested - and still no gameplay datagram ever
followed (zero packets on 30976, 20.111).

### WHY THAT ANSWER CANNOT WORK
The raw hex of a published descriptor decodes as ASCII `steamid:765611987767538..`.
That is foreign-blob truth (20.90's rule: only machineId + offset hex + raw dumps
are facts about a client; every `asif_` field is our layout guessed onto their
bytes, and `routable=0` on a client blob is NOT a claim - do not cite it).
So the client publishes a STEAM-IDENTITY join descriptor, structurally unlike the
IP-endpoint descriptor `descriptor::build` produces. Handing client A that blob
points A at B over Steam networking - which our shim stubs and which never runs:
`get_certificate` returned 0 once and `send_rendezvous` has never fired (20.111).
We answered the search with an address to a road that does not exist.
Note also the RACE: the FIRST client to search always gets `result=none self=1`,
because no foreign advertisement exists yet. Peer-relay answering is order-dependent
by construction.

### THE FIX IS WRITTEN AND UNUSED
`build_search_descriptor` (matchmaking_route.cpp:185) builds a 128-byte descriptor
naming THIS SERVER's gameplay endpoint, via the same constructor the citizen
advertisement uses. Its own comment states the road-C premise outright: "It points
at THIS server's gameplay endpoint, which is the host of every instance here - so
there is nothing to reverse-engineer".
IT IS NEVER CALLED. Grep returns exactly one hit: its definition. The live path
(matchmaking_route.cpp:238) serves `foreign_advertisement` only.
It was written under the 20.34/20.35 reading - "a client handed its own session back
has nothing to join" - which is CORRECT for peer-to-peer and WRONG for road C. Under
road C the server is the host, both clients are guests, and the descriptor every
searcher should receive is ours. That reading is now retracted for this call site.

### THE COMPLETE CHAIN, TOP TO BOTTOM (every link now has a mark)
  1. client searches once at setup:matchmaking and takes the answer as final [V-R
     20.34 + the encoder comment]
  2. we answer with the other client's Steam-identity blob, or with nothing at all
     if it searched first [V-X, this log]
  3. no gameplay association forms; zero datagrams reach 30976 [V-X, 20.111]
  4. each client hosts its own managed session, member table = self [V-X, 20.110/111]
  5. no peer ever reaches member state 10 = ESTABLISHED [V-R 20.113]
  6. the activity client's tracking table therefore holds only self [V-R 20.113]
  7. a BAP membership push naming a peer misses that table -> warn -> gate false ->
     PEER-RESERVATION RELEASE -> latched [V-R 20.112, V-X 20.111]
  8. no roster, no second guardian, ever - whatever we encode at step 7's layer.
Twenty boots were spent between steps 6 and 8. The lever is step 2.

### NEXT (one code change, then the first boot that can succeed)
Serve `build_search_descriptor` as the sessionSearch answer - our own gameplay
endpoint - in place of, or ahead of, the foreign relay. Small, local, and it is the
first change in this project's history aimed at step 2 rather than step 7.
CALIBRATION, kept honest: the handbook's 16.7 warns a valid search proves ONLY
matchmaking state - not that an association exists, not that the group session
joined. And their working topology advertised LOOPBACK 127.0.0.1 while ours
advertises a LAN address; that difference is untested and is the first thing to
suspect if the client still does not dial.

## 20.113 *** THE END-TO-END PIPELINE. *** THE ACTIVITY CLIENT DOES NOT TRUST BAP
## MEMBERSHIP: IT ENUMERATES THE GAME'S OWN MANAGED-SESSION MEMBER TABLE AND ONLY
## TRACKS PEERS THAT REACH MEMBER STATE 10 (ESTABLISHED). EVERYTHING WE HAVE PUSHED
## OVER BAP FOR TWENTY BOOTS IS DOWNSTREAM OF THAT. (2026-08-27 ~18:3x)

All static. No boots. This joins the two halves of the project: the session layer
mapped in 20.108/20.109 and the activity/BAP layer every boot since 20.28 has aimed
at. They meet in ONE function, fn 0x140C17E40, and the session layer wins.

### THE PROOF THAT rbx IS A MANAGED SESSION (independently derived offsets meeting)
Inside fn 0x140C17E40, before any peer work:
    mov ecx, dword ptr [rbx + 0x1AEF8]   ; 20.109: "State dword at +0x1AEF8 (6..9 = live)"
    lea eax, [rcx - 4] / cmp eax, 5 / ja out    ; state must be 4..9
    lea eax, [rcx - 6] / cmp eax, 3 / ja out    ; AND 6..9
    mov rcx, rbx / call 0x14177A240 -> r13      ; the member container
and 0x14177A240's own entry (fn 0x14177A1E0) opens with
    mov ecx, dword ptr [rcx + 0x1C7C0]   ; 20.109: "session id at +0x1C7C0"
Two constants recovered months apart from a different lane land exactly here. This
is not a resemblance; it is the same object.

### THE MEMBER WALK (fn 0x141779230 / 0x141779710, read in full)
    r8d = [container + 0x30]         ; 32-bit occupancy bitmask
    base = container + 8             ; member array, STRIDE 0x120, 32 slots
    for i in 0..31:
        if !bit(r8d, i): continue
        if [member + 0x1750] != 3: return i     ; state 3 is SKIPPED by the iterator
    return -1
and the collection loop in 0x140C17E40 keeps a member ONLY when
    cmp dword ptr [member + 0x1758], 0xA / jne next-member
0xA = 10 = ESTABLISHED. Anything short of it is walked past in silence.

CONVERGENCE WORTH NOTING: the handbook digest's member ladder, from a wholly
independent source, reads "3 reserved ambassador -> 5 connected -> 7 joined ->
8 waiting -> 9 ready -> 10 established (do not skip edges)". This code enforces
exactly that ladder: it skips 3 and it collects only 10.

### THE FULL CHAIN, TOP TO BOTTOM
For a peer to survive a BAP membership push instead of being thrown out, ALL of
this must already be true, in this order:
  1. the peer occupies a slot in the client's own managed-session member table
     (bitmask at container+0x30, stride 0x120);
  2. its member state at +0x1750 is past 3 (reserved ambassador);
  3. its field at +0x1758 reads 10 (established) - else no record is built;
  4. resolver 0x1404DD640 matches the record's key against a 32-slot, 0x2AC0-stride
     container (occupancy bitmask at +0x5924C, key at entry+8);
  5. fn 0x1404F4980 then inserts the peer into the tracking table
     (client+0x602BC, stride 10) - 20.112;
  6. ONLY THEN does a type-12 membership push naming that peer find tracking data.
Fail any of 1-5 and 20.112's block runs: warn "Could not find tracking data" ->
gate 0x1404F7710 false -> SEND PEER-RESERVATION RELEASE -> set the released latch.

### WHAT THIS SETTLES, AND IT IS THE WHOLE FRONT
**Declaring a peer over BAP can never work by itself.** The activity client does not
take our word for who is present; it reads the game's own session member table and
tracks only members that reached ESTABLISHED. Our type-12 bodies, peer rows, citizen
advertisements and roster pushes are all downstream of a table we have never put
anybody into. 20.110/20.111 measured that table's contents directly on both
machines: `add_candidates` always cnt=1 with the OWN xuid, `poll cand=0` all boot.
The session member table contains self and nothing else, on both machines, always.

That is why twenty boots of wire-shape work produced nothing, and it retires the
whole class: no trailing-field value, no bitmask, no reason byte, and no roster
push can substitute for session membership. It also explains 20.53's freeze lead -
naming a foreign peer in the roster without a reservation - as the same law seen
from the other side.

### AND IT IS EXACTLY WHAT ROAD C BUILDS
The only route to member state 10 is the session/peer layer: connect -> join ->
reserve -> admit -> adoption (20.108/20.109). Our server ALREADY implements the
host side of that ladder: group_host.cpp publishes `MemberState::ready` and
promotes to `established` on join-complete, with three static_asserts pinning the
peer's own bar. What is missing is not code - it is that no client has ever dialed
the gameplay endpoint (L6, zero datagrams, 20.111). Road C was chosen on cost; it
turns out to be the ONLY road. A and B are now formally dead:
  - B (in-process injection at reserve/admit) would have to forge session
    membership AND drive it to established, which is road C with no server.
  - A (peer-native Steam rendezvous) still ends at the same ladder.

### NEXT (still static, then one boot)
The chain above is a CHECKLIST for the next boot, not a new question. Read, in the
fork, how far our group host actually drives the ladder and whether it reaches
`+0x1758 == 10` on the client for a real peer. The first boot that matters is the
one where a client dials 30976 - link L6 - and the instruments for reading the
result already exist (`ev=jr fn=add_candidates` with a FOREIGN xuid is the single
line that proves the whole chain).

## 20.112 STATIC PASS ON p2(74)'s RVAs: THE PEER RELEASE IS A TWO-LINE DECISION,
## AND 20.105's "SELF-HEALING WARNING" IS A RELEASED-ALREADY LATCH. THE
## same_region SKIP IS DELIBERATE AND ROAD C ANSWERS THE QUESTION IT PARKED.
## (2026-08-27 ~18:0x)

All static. No boots. Inputs: the six caller-capture RVAs from 20.111.
Tool changes, both generalisations rather than forks (TOOLS.md):
- `pdata_bounds.py` now follows UNW_FLAG_CHAININFO. TWO of the six RVAs sat in
  FRAGMENT entries, whose begin address is NOT a function start: the composition
  function is really 0x1417E22F0 (not 0x1417E2366) and "waiting to connect to AH"
  belongs to 0x140C09010 (not 0x140C090B0). An xref scan on either wrong address
  would have returned nothing and read as a dead end.
- `xref_scan.py` gained `call rel32` / `jmp rel32` forms, so a function address now
  yields its CALLER census from the same tool (the 20.106 method, no longer inline).
  Oracle still passes.

### THE RELEASE DECISION, READ END TO END (fn 0x140C17E40 @0x140C18442)
The captured RVA for "Could not find tracking data" IS the return address of that
warning's own log call. What follows it, in ONE basic block:

    0x140C1843D  call 0x14035D860        ; emit "Could not find tracking data..."
    0x140C18442  mov rdx,r14 / mov rcx,r15
    0x140C18448  call 0x1404F7710        ; THE GATE
    0x140C1844F  jne  0x140C185A1        ; gate true -> skip the release entirely
    0x140C1845B  call 0x1404F8250        ; SEND PEER-RESERVATION RELEASE
    0x140C18462  je   0x140C18477        ; release failed -> skip the latch
    0x140C18464  mov r8b,1
    0x140C1846D  call 0x1404FC2E0        ; set the flag TRUE for this peer

The release function 0x1404F8250 does NOT decide anything: its log line sits at its
head (offset 0x1D7 is the funnel return), so it executes a decision made here.
Two call sites total: 0x140C17C0C and this one.

### THE GATE IS A TABLE LOOKUP (fn 0x1404F7710, 85 bytes, read in full)
    count = [client + 0x602B8]
    for i in 0..count:  if [client + 10*i + 0x602BC] == *key: index = i; break
    return (byte)[client + 10*index + 0x602C5]        // entry + 9
Entry stride 10: {u64 key (the peer MACHINE ID), u8 @+8, u8 @+9}. On a MISS index
stays -1 and the read lands on the byte just below the table - a sentinel slot,
not a bug.

### WHO WRITES THAT FLAG (immediate scan on 0x602B8 / 0x602C5, 19 + 5 sites)
- INSERT sites 0x1404F4983 and 0x1404F6FEB: write the key, then set BOTH +8 and +9
  to ZERO, then `inc dword [client+0x602B8]`. A freshly inserted peer therefore
  reads FALSE at the gate.
- THE SETTER is fn 0x1404FC2E0 (`mov byte [rbx+rcx*2+0x602C5], r8b` @0x1404FC330),
  and its ONLY call in this block passes r8b = 1 - immediately after a successful
  release.

### THEREFORE: 20.105 IS CORRECTED, PRECISELY
The +9 byte is a RELEASED-ALREADY LATCH, not a "peer is valid" mark. First
membership update naming an untracked peer: gate false -> release sent -> latch
set. Every later update: gate true -> release skipped, silently. That is exactly
the "self-healing" 20.105 observed - the WARNING stops repeating - but the peer
was released on the first pass and stays released. The warning is not benign at
this site; it is one instruction block upstream of the release.
20.105's underlying claim (the warning sits in a per-peer loop and does not repeat)
stands. Its CONSEQUENCE was read wrong, and boots #10-#13 were read against the
wrong consequence. The release is real and it is the roster's exit door.

### THE QUESTION THIS PROMOTES ABOVE ALL OTHERS
WHY is there no tracking data for the peer? The failing lookup lives higher in fn
0x140C17E40 (2225 B), above the log-line construction. That single lookup decides
whether a peer survives its first membership update. Reading it is the next move
and it costs no boot.

### THE same_region SKIP IS OURS, DELIBERATE, AND NOW ANSWERABLE
20.111 result 5 asked whether `peer_advert result=same_region ... peer_citizen=0`
is a gap. It is not an oversight - activity_membership_push.cpp:183-190 states the
reason: a region record holds ONE descriptor, the local advertisement owns the
record for the local region, and `region_machine_id` keys the host-session row by
region index alone, so a peer advertisement on the same index COLLIDES and retires
the row the local call just built (20.78 defect 3). Skipping makes the collision
unreachable by construction.
Its parked question is: "whose endpoint belongs in a shared bubble" (20.79 gap 5).
ROAD C ANSWERS IT: neither client's - the SERVER's. Both clients are guests of one
host. That is what makes the same-region case resolvable now when it was not in
20.79, and it is the first place road C pays for itself in code we already own.

### AND THE ADVERTISEMENT ALREADY CARRIES EVERYTHING
Read while chasing the above, and it sharpens L4 rather than softening it:
`build_candidate` (gameplay_advertisement.cpp:109-163) puts the SERVER's endpoint
(192.168.1.164:30976) in the join descriptor, `host.groupSessionId` as machineId,
and `host.target.sessionId` - the very id `host_session_for_activity` keys on - in
`candidate.onlineSessionId`. The region writer encodes all 128 descriptor bytes AND
that 64-bit session id onto the wire (activity_membership_region_writer.cpp:77-81).
So the client is HANDED the address, the machine id, and the joinable session id,
in its own region record, and still aims its join at its own session. L4 is not a
missing-data problem. Do not "fix" the advertisement.

## 20.111 p2(74) ROAD-C BOOT: L4 IS VERIFIED - NEITHER CLIENT EVER AIMS AT THE
## ADVERTISED HOST. SIX DECIDING FUNCTIONS NAMED. THE PRIVATE/PUBLIC LABEL IS A
## `<= 0x1FF` RANGE CHECK. THE PEER RELEASE IS BORN ON THE MEMBERSHIP PUSH.
## (2026-08-27 ~17:3x)

Instruments: six new caller-capture targets in the retail log funnel (LESSONS 18c,
target strings only - mechanism unchanged) + `ev=activity stage=join_target` on the
server. Both observation-only. Brief and full run order:
RE_output/claims/BOOT_BRIEF_p2-74.md. Chain: FRONT_public-host-chain.md.
Deployed: client `bf791db513362b41` both machines, server `8870459b31b87cc2`.

### LIVENESS AND CROSS-CHECKS (read these before the results)
- Control target `Adding player` reported rva=0x17922C2 on BOTH machines, identical
  to p2(73). Mac base 0x140000000, rig base 0x7FF756D00000 - ten targets, ten

- `pdata_bounds.py` (new, TOOLS.md) resolves `Adding player`'s RVA to fn
  0x141792080, which is `add_candidates`, derived independently in 20.108 from the

- PRE-NAMED CONTENT NEGATIVE FIRED LITERALLY AND IS REFUTED ON THE EVIDENCE. Two
