  the shape that hides a bad instrument - the discriminator here is the five
  distinct functions, not the argument.

### RESULT 1 - LINK L4, VERIFIED BY EXECUTION ON BOTH ROLES
    server: stage=join_target result=own session=0x9EAA300100200001 handle=0x9EAA300100200001 row=0
    server: stage=join_target result=own session=0x9EAA300100200004 handle=0x9EAA300100200004 row=0
Session id EQUALS account handle, bit for bit, on both machines. Zero
`result=advertised`, zero `result=unknown`, zero `stage=bind result=public_target`.
The client never aims at the advertised host session. It is not refused - it never
asks. L4 moves from assumed to verified-by-execution; OUTCOME 1 of the brief.

### RESULT 2 - THE SIX DECIDING FUNCTIONS (RVA -> .pdata bounds)
    join request to AH              0x140C0CF30..0x140C0EA4A  (6938)  <- the target chooser
    waiting to connect to AH        0x140C090B0..0x140C0998C  (2268)
    activity_host_changed           0x1404F4A10..0x1404F4E47  (1079)
    initiate_search + gatherer      0x1417E2366..0x1417E24CB   (357)  <- both lines, one fn
    Could not find tracking data    0x140C17E40..0x140C186F1  (2225)
    Sending peer-reservation release 0x1404F8250..0x1404F88B2 (1634)
    (controls: Adding player 0x141792080, Submitting player add 0x14179D460,
     creating Steam lobby 0x140A46E50)

### RESULT 3 - `PRIVATE` VS `PUBLIC` IS A NUMERIC RANGE CHECK, NOT A FLAG
In fn 0x1417E2366, at 0x1417E2417:
    cmp    dword ptr [rdi + 0xC], 0x1FF
    lea    rcx, [0x141C1A008]      ; "PUBLIC"
    mov    eax, dword ptr [rdi + 0x28]   ; the "[N players]" count
    lea    r9,  [0x141C1A000]      ; "PRIVATE"
    cmovbe r9, rcx                 ; unsigned <= 511 -> PUBLIC
Strings read out of the image at those two addresses to fix which is which
(0x141C1A000 = "PRIVATE\0PUBLIC\0\0CURRENT\0TARGET\0"). We log PRIVATE on every
line of every boot, so `[rdi+0xC] > 511` always holds for us.
CAUTION, and it is the whole value of the finding: this is the source of the
LABEL, not proof it is the causal switch. The next static step is the writer of
[rdi+0xC], not an attempt to force the comparison.

### RESULT 4 - THE PEER-RESERVATION RELEASE IS BORN ON A MEMBERSHIP PUSH
Identical on both machines, three milliseconds wide, on receipt of a type-12
membership body (mac shown; rig identical at t=54657):
    t=1015137  push svc=9 type=12 msg=membership size=4112
    t=1015139  "Could not find tracking data for peer '1', for activity membership
                '5', machine id 'E6:22:D...'"        <- fn 0x140C17E40
    t=1015140  "Sending peer-reservation release ..."  <- fn 0x1404F8250
    t=1015140  "Sent peer-reservation release"
    t=1015169  "Processing new PAH tabulated region data in membership update '5'."
Both clients reach MEM-5 on their OWN activity host, both classify PRIVATE CURRENT,
and both release the other's reservation on the same membership update.
DOES NOT RETRACT 20.105. 20.105 established that the tracking-data warning
self-heals inside a per-peer loop; that still stands. What is new is that the same
membership push that produces the warning also produces the release, from a
DIFFERENT function 1 ms later. Sibling effects of one input, not proven cause and
effect. Whether 0x1404F8250 branches on the same failed lookup is a disassembly
question, and it is the next one to answer.

### RESULT 5 - THE REGIONS CONVERGE, AND THE PEER CITIZEN IS DROPPED WHEN THEY DO
    stage=peer_advert result=built       own_region=56 peer_region=48 own_citizen=1 peer_citizen=1
    stage=membership_peer result=withdrawn variant=packed_masks unacked=2
    stage=peer_advert result=same_region own_region=56 peer_region=56 own_citizen=1 peer_citizen=0
The two clients DO converge on region 56, and the moment they do, our own
`same_region` branch stops carrying the peer's citizen advertisement
(peer_citizen=0). Whether that is correct (retail has ONE PAH broadcasting one
instance's advertisement) or is the gap itself is UNKNOWN and is now a named,
cheap, server-side read. It was invisible before this boot because the two
machines had never been in the same region at the same time in an instrumented run.

### RESULT 6 - `region_private` STAYS INERT
Zero `stage=region result=forced|public` lines on either machine, again, with the
mac now explicitly false. 20.82's measurement holds. Both machines are aligned on
the key for the first time; the divergence is closed.

### INCIDENT - RENDER DEATH, AND IT FOLLOWS CO-PRESENCE, NOT ENTRY ORDER
The MAC lost render (black screen) when the RIG entered the Tower second. The game
stayed ALIVE and kept logging normally for at least 50 s afterwards (BAP connected,
pushes accepted, t=1065327 well past the release at t=1015140), so this is a render
death, not a hang, and it left NO client-side trace: there is no `ev=render` or
graphics event in either log.
This CORRECTS 20.110's reading of the same gap. There the rig went black entering
second, which read as "the joiner dies". Here the machine that entered FIRST died
when the second arrived. The correlate is the co-presence event itself, not the
entry order. Still the render lane, not the roster lane - but the next time it is
investigated, the hypothesis to test is co-presence, not joiner context.

## 20.110 OBSERVATION BOOT COMPLETE (p2(71)->p2(73)): SELF-PIPELINE VERIFIED ON
## BOTH MACHINES; JOIN STALLS AT THE CLIENT-TO-CLIENT CONNECT LAYER; TWO DETOUR
## FREEZE BUGS FOUND AND FIXED. (2026-08-27 ~15:5x)

Instruments: join_roster observer group (join_request 0x16E0460, join_process
0x17806C0, reserve, admit 0x1777EC0, add_candidates 0x1792080) + candidate-table
count poll (global RVA 0x31E1118) + settings switch join_roster_observer.
Full run log: RE_output/claims/BOOT_BRIEF_p2-71.md (incl. incident addenda and
the preserved p2(71) freeze log in RE_output/incidents/).

### EXECUTION-VERIFIED RESULTS
1. Self-pipeline on BOTH machines and BOTH roles: at world entry, admit(mach=0)
   -> add_candidates(cnt=1, own xuid) fire within milliseconds; the pipeline
   20.108 mapped runs for SELF exactly as read. Mac xuid ...EC5, rig ...EC6.
2. Face-to-face join activates under co-presence: BOTH machines logged
   "face_to_face_join: Caching destination 'HASH(0x13a023bc)'" - the SAME
   destination hash, cached on each side within the co-presence window. The
   join subsystem discovers the peer, arms the destination, and waits.
3. The join request NEVER fires: join_request/join_process silent both sides,
   candidate table cand=0 all boot, no foreign admit. The blocker is UPSTREAM
   of the type-0x0A handler: the client-to-client connect layer (connect-
   request 5 / establish 8 must precede 0x0A per 20.109) does not exist in the
   fork - everything transits the standalone server (20.99).
4. Neither client stalls in clean co-presence on p2(73): both self-host their
   own sessions (20.102's two-solo-sessions pattern, re-confirmed).

### THE TWO FREEZE BUGS (both ours, both fixed, both instructive)
- p2(71) MAC freeze at character select: detour bodies declared a 5-arg ABI;
  reserve takes >=7 args (wrapper writes 3 outgoing slots) and admit ~10
  (caller writes 6 slots) - slots 6+ were read from our body's frame garbage
  on the pass-through. FIX p2(72): forward 4 register + 12 stack slots
  bit-exact (over-declaration safe, under-declaration fatal).
- p2(72) RIG freeze (paired AND solo): the reserve CORE 0x1417692E0 reads its
  caller's frame at [rbp+0x330] (= entry+0x238 with rbp=entry-0xF8) - beyond
  ANY arg-forwarding depth. A core detour puts body-frame garbage there and
  session creation hangs. Machine-dependent manifestation (mac benign, rig
  hang) - timing/garbage luck. FIX p2(73): reserve site moved to the WRAPPER
  0x141769230 (simple frame, same call path, core frame left intact).
- Attribution discipline that worked: the settings switch (join_roster_observer
  false) bisected live - knob off = rig reaches Tower, knob on = freeze - which
  localized the fault to the attach-time group, and the static map localized it
  to the one site whose window contained no logged call.
- CAVEAT: with the wrapper hook, the rig's own reserve path goes through
  DIRECT core callers (0x141772440 / 0x141781800 / 0x1417ABC40 family) that
  bypass the wrapper - reserve is unobserved on the rig, and admit proves
  registration happened. On the mac the wrapper IS on the path.

### FORK GAPS SURFACED (each a future lane)
- Presence entries NEVER expire on client death: a quit/crashed client keeps
  advertising connect + steam_player_group; the next client to enter the world
  wakes face_to_face_join against a ghost and can stall (observed: rig solo
  freeze with the mac quit but its keys still served). Server-side TTL or
  keepalive expiry needed.
- Rig render death under joiner context: rig (real Windows) black render,
  game alive, on entering the Tower second. Did not occur on p2(70)-era
  paired boots; not investigated (render lane, not roster lane).
- The face-to-face trigger condition is still unknown: co-presence arms the
  destination but nothing pulls the join in-Tower. Retail trigger likely a
  destination transition or a social action.

### WHERE THIS LEAVES THE ROAD
The join plane cannot speak until the client-to-client connect link exists.
Decision pending: option A (build the peer connect relay per
RE_output/claims/transport-relay-design.md, then the whole 20.109 flow is
game-native) vs option B (in-process injection on the host at the reserve/
admit boundary, bypassing the connect layer - fastest to a shared roster).
All the instrumentation to validate either choice is deployed and proven.

## 20.109 INJECTION SEAM MAPPED END TO END: TYPE-0x0A JOIN REQUEST IS THE FRONT
## DOOR; CANDIDATE TABLE IS A GLOBAL; PUMP IS SINGLE-THREADED. (2026-08-27 ~15:4x)

All static, no boots. Complements 20.108 (same address conventions).

### STEP 1 - THE SEAM (who feeds the join-request handler)
- Dispatcher 0x14175E520 is called ONLY from network subsystem tick
  0x1416D2320 (@0x1416D239C). That tick is called from pump 0x140B58F80
  (chain: 0x140B58220 <- 0x140B525C0-region) whose SCHEDULER ENTRY above
  0x140B525C0 is VMP-VIRTUALIZED (jmps into .vmp0 0x1437xxxxx). Do not chase
  the scheduler further; everything needed is below it.
- Pump guard: single-thread lock (thread id global 0x1427E4B90, lock cmpxchg),
  enable flag 0x143050824, re-entrancy flags 0x143050826/827.
- CONFIRMED: the session tick state machine 0x14178C126 runs INSIDE the same
  pump: 0x1416D2320 -> 0x1416CCDCC -> 0x140C276C0 -> 0x14178C0D0/0x14178C126
  -> 0x1417A6EA0. One thread runs all of it.

### STEP 2 - THE LOCAL TRIGGER (how 0x14178DE60 is reached)
The dispatcher does NOT decode a message to reach the join handler. Per tick it
builds a bitmask r12d: bits 1/2 from flag bytes [rbp+0x4D2..4D4] and
[rbp+0x5AA/5AB]; then for session slots 0/1/2 (getters 0x140C265C0/0x140C26490):
  if session.state ([sess+0x1AEF8]) in 6..9 AND 0x14178D3F0(sess)==false
  -> r12d |= 8 / 4 / 0x20 respectively. If mask != 0 -> 0x14175C7C0(mask).
0x14175C7C0 then works per set bit: session state must be in 6..8 (two range
checks), 0x1417A22E0(sess)==false, per-slot flag dwords at 0x1431E10A0[6],
and iterates THE JOIN-CANDIDATE TABLE (below), calling 0x141768BA0(sess,
entry, entry+0x20 ptr, code 7) and 0x14175DF90(entry) on matches, and for
mask-bit-3 entries 0x141751A40 first. VERIFIED EDGES: 0x14175E520 -E8->
0x14175C7C0 (@0x1417600C3, with the mask in ecx); 0x14175C7C0 -> 0x14178DE60
(via 0x14175CAA6, through fn 0x14175C7C0); 0x14178DE60 calls reserve
0x141769230 (@0x14178EA73, gate: test al,al) then admit 0x141777EC0
(@0x14178EB92).

### THE JOIN-CANDIDATE TABLE (the injection datum)
- GLOBAL, STATIC: base 0x1431E1120, entry stride 0x1828, count dword at
  0x1431E1118, per-session-slot flag dwords at 0x1431E10A0[6] (checked via
  0x141A83C00 equality helper). Cleared (count=0) by 0x141758574 after
  processing; trimmed by 0x14175863D.
- Entry fields seen: +0x18 (key, compared via 0x141A83C00), +0x20 (ptr passed
  to 0x141768BA0), +0x1530 state byte (values 1/2/3/4/5 drive behavior),
  +0x1531 byte, +0x1548/+0x1558 per-state payloads, +0x1820 float timer.
- Creators (count++): 0x141751D4D (tail of fn 0x141751B80 family, which also
  per-entry calls 0x1417E1D50 with string "local" @0x141BE9168 and entry
  +0x1548/+0x1558), 0x1417557DE (two sites). Callers of creators:
  0x141755738 (fn 0x141755540 - also rejects with code 4) and 0x14175C99E
  (inside 0x14175C7C0). DECREMENTS: 0x1417587CC (fn 0x14175866D),
  0x14175CB3E (fn 0x14175C7C0), 0x14175E019 (dispatcher).
- 0x141768BA0(sess, entry, ptr, code) = per-candidate action; codes seen:
  4 (reject), 7 (process), 0xA, 0x1B (refuse/log).

### STEP 3 - THE WIRE: TYPE 0x0A IS THE JOIN REQUEST (connection layer)
- RECEIVE SWITCH (jump table, NOT the 0x40-stride descriptor registry):
  head at 0x1416E0940: bound r9d <= 0x2A; byte map at 0x1416E0A88 -> slot
  index; dword RVA table at 0x1416E0A44 (base 0x140000000); stubs load args
  then jmp handler. Types 0x1E..0x29 are DEFAULT here (the L3 session
  messages are dispatched elsewhere - do not conflate the two spaces).
- CONNECTION-LAYER NAMES (F1 registration, base id 5): connect-request=5,
  connect-response=6, connect-refuse=7 (string @0x141C9A778),
  connect-establish=8, connect-closed=9. F2: ping/pong/broadcast-search/
  broadcast-reply/packets-discarded. IDS 10..29 ARE UNREGISTERED (no name
  strings) yet handled by the switch - a nameless platform family.
- TYPE 0x0A (10) -> stub 0x1416E099C -> HANDLER 0x1416E0460:
  rcx=connection ctx, rdx=connection obj, r8=packet record (word[+0] must
  equal 0x1416C1260() instance nonce; +0x10/+0x18 qwords; +0x10 block used
  as name in logs).
  - If a session exists via [ctx+0x28] lookup 0x14177A0B0 AND its state is
    6..9: -> 0x1417806C0(sess, conn, pkt) THE JOIN PROCESSOR: checks request
    member count [pkt+8] against computed capacity (0x1416C8440 outs), flags
    [pkt+4]; on fit -> candidate creation via 0x141780D0B/0x141751B80 family
    (three 0x141768BA0 call sites with codes); on overflow -> refuse path
    with code 0x1B.
  - Else: REFUSES: 0x1416E3D80(..., r9=0x18, [rsp+0x40]=4 <- reason 4) then
    0x1416E3470 + 0x1417D67C0 (close). This is the no-session/no-reservation
    refusal.
- So: joiner connects (connect-request 5/establish 8) then sends type 0x0A
  with its member record(s); HOST's tick machinery then does reserve -> admit
  -> adoption -> +0xC8 -> roster, all per 20.108.

### STEP 4 - WHAT AN IN-PERFORMING INJECTION NEEDS (all resolved)
- THREAD: everything above runs on the pump thread (0x140B58F80 single-thread
  lock). Any shim-side call must be made from game-thread context we already
  own (the log funnel / interface methods called by the game), never from
  another thread.
- NETMGR SINGLETON: global ptr 0x143051E58 via getter 0x1416C0840 (which has
  obfuscated xor checks - call the getter, do not read the global directly).
- SESSION OBJECTS: 0x140C26490(idx 0/1/2) -> base 0x140C03430() + idx*0x39148,
  returns slot + 0x18 + [slot+0x10]*0x1C8A0. State dword at +0x1AEF8 (6..9 =
  live), session id at +0x1C7C0, self-join staging +0x1C828.
- RESERVATION TABLES: base = 0x1416C0840() + 0x3548; lookups 0x1417C40F0 /
  0x1417C3480 (must return -1 for a new machine), commit 0x1417C35F0/
  0x1417C4200; bitmap at [tbl+0x30], count +0x24, capacity +0x38.
- MEMBER RECORD (stride 0x1A8, array at netmgr+0x3B60): +0x4 xuid qword,
  +0x18 membership byte, +0xC machine idx, +0x10 member idx, +0xA0 address
  block (0x30 bytes), +0xD0 flags word (bit0/2/3/4), +0xD2=0xFF, +0x108=-1.
  (0x141769ACD reads the same records via base r15; treat 0x3B60-vs-r15 as an
  implementation-time confirm.)
- PEER SLOT (stride 0xB8): +0xC8 machine id qword, +0xE4 count, +0xE8 list
  count, +0xF0+[i*4] member indices, +0x48=0x56 + 0x54-byte address block at
  +0x50 written at reserve time.
- LOCAL JOIN RING: netmgr+0xE7E8 stride 0x118, head +0xEC48, records carry
  machine id +8, member id +0xC, address block +0x98, flags byte +0xC8.

### INJECTION OPTIONS, RANKED
A. WIRE-LEVEL (game-native): have the joiner actually speak connect 5/8 then
   type 0x0A to the host over an established transport connection. Requires
   the transport between the two clients (L5 works) + nonce handshake. Most
   faithful; untested whether our transport carries the connection layer.
B. IN-PROCESS (host-side shim): on the HOST, after the peer exists as a
   candidate (from our existing pairing), call the tick-side pieces directly
   from game-thread context: create a candidate-table entry or call
   0x1417806C0 with a synthetic packet record, then let the per-tick
   machinery (0x14175C7C0 -> reserve -> admit -> adoption) do the rest.
   Needs a correct 0x1828 entry or a correct synthetic pkt record.
C. OBSERVATION FIRST (mandatory before either): logging stubs on
   0x1416E0460 / 0x1417806C0 / 0x1417692E0 / 0x141777EC0 / 0x141792080
   (args + returns) to see which of these the game itself fires during a
   normal two-machine paired boot. This answers whether any join traffic
   already flows and calibrates B without a behavior change.
DO-NOT: do not call 0x1416C0840-adjacent internals from non-game threads;
do not write the candidate table without pinning +0x1530 state semantics
per state (1/2 vs 3/4/5 drive different 0x141768BA0 codes).

## 20.108 L3 DISPATCH TABLE FOUND; PLAYER-ADD WIRE SHAPE DECODED; THE FULL
## HOST-SIDE JOIN GATE MAPPED. (2026-08-27 ~14:3x, static, no boots)

All verified-by-reading (capstone + .pdata + .rdata strings). Nothing executed.
RVAs at image base 0x140000000; add 0x140000000 for VAs.

### THE L3 MESSAGE TABLE (Phase 1)
- Vocabulary strings cluster in .rdata 0x141CA39F0..0x141CA3BB0:
  connect-request/establish/closed/response, ping, pong, broadcast-search/reply,
  packets-discarded, membership-update, peer-properties, delegate-leadership,
  desired-leader-address, boot-machine, boot-peer-address, player-add,
  player-refuse, player-remove, player-properties.
- Three registration functions call a common registrar 0x1416E2C50:
  F1 0x141743690 (connect-*), F2 0x141743810 (broadcast-*), F3 0x141743990
  (the eight session messages). All three called from network init
  0x1416C2405 at ~0x1416C265A-0x1416C2672 with rcx = netmgr+0x14B8 (the
  message registry object).
- REGISTRAR LAYOUT: descriptor[stride 0x40] indexed by message id:
  +0x00 present byte, +0x08 name ptr, +0x14/+0x18 dword sizes,
  +0x20 ENCODE fn, +0x28 DECODE fn, +0x30/+0x38 extra fns.
- MESSAGE IDS: membership-update=0x1E (size 0x7980), peer-properties=0x1F
  (0x198), delegate-leadership=0x20 (0x5E), boot-machine=0x21 (0x64),
  player-add=0x22 (0x18!), player-refuse=0x23 (0x18), player-remove=0x24
  (0x18), player-properties=0x25 (0x120).
- player-add encode 0x14173EC90 / decode 0x14173C600. WIRE SHAPE (bit-packed,
  LSB-accumulate writer, MSB-first per field):
    1 bit  (dword@struct+8; width from global dword 0x142048AC0 = 1)
    64 bits qword@+0xC  <- THE JOINING XUID
    32 bits dword@+0x14
    2 bits  dword@+0x18 (enum, decode enforces <=3)
    then optional fields via 0x1416D3B00 (+0x20) and 0x141710AC0 (+0x108,
    two dwords = 64-bit address key), likely conditional on the leading bit.
- An id->name getter for a WIDER id space (0..0x43 incl. our 0x1E..0x25)
  exists at ~0x140DEFFxx (cmovne string chain) - logging only.
- CAUTION: descriptors are runtime-built (netmgr heap); no static dispatcher
  switch was found for RECEIVE. Static id-cmp scans hit only log formatters.

### THE WRITER OF session+0xC8 (closes 20.106's OPEN question) (Phase 2)
- 0x141792080 ADD-CANDIDATES is the sustained writer. Args: (session_id ecx,
  xuid array rdx qwords, flagsB array r8 bytes, flagsA array r9 bytes, count
  arg5). Per entry: find free/ matching slot in candidates[32]@session+0xC8,
  set key@+8 = xuid, word@+0 |= 1 (present), |=2 from flagsB[i], |=4 from
  flagsA[i]; then sets session dirty flags: [sess+4]|=0x400, [sess+0xA]|=0x300,
  [sess+4]&=~4, [sess+0x18]=0, [sess+0x24]=-1. The second half of the fn is
  the "Adding player [xuid=..]" log spam (obfuscated log table 0x141CA2FC0).
- EXACTLY TWO CALLERS:
  1. 0x1417A8178 in SESSION TICK 0x1417A6EA0 (states 6..9): adds SELF from
     the local join-intent ring (netmgr+0xE7E8, stride 0x118, head +0xEC48;
     intents recorded by 0x141769D20-family writer 0x141769DF0). Matches
     20.101's "own xuid only, every boot".
  2. 0x141769CFC in the PEER-ADOPTION path: walks peer slots (stride 0xB8),
     collects each peer's advertised member records (stride 0x1A8; address
     block 0x30B at rec+0x3C00, flags nibble at rec+0x3C30 bit4->flagsA,
     rec+0x3B78 membership byte -> flagsB), upserts them into +0xC8. THIS is
     the sustained source for peers.

### THE HOST-SIDE JOIN GATE, END TO END (Phase 2)
    dispatcher 0x14175E520 -> 0x14175C7C0 -> JOIN-REQUEST handler 0x14178DE60:
      per member of the request:
        0x141769230 -> 0x1417692E0 REGISTER/RESERVE, the gate:
          - 0x1417C40F0 must return -1 (machine NOT in table A)
          - 0x1417C3480 must return -1 (machine NOT in table B)
          - commit: bts bitmap@[r12+0x30], count++ @+0x24, capacity vs
            [r12+0x38] (max_allowed_join_reservation_count per .rdata
            0x141BFB531), peer slot init ([slot+0xC8]=machine id qword,
            +0x48=0x56, 0x54-byte address block at +0x50), member slot via
            0x1417C35F0/0x1417C4200 -> [slot+0xE0EC/E0F0]
        then admit 0x141777EC0: dup-check rec+0x3B64, record join intent
          (0x141769D20), fill member record +0x3C00 address block and
          +0x3C30 flag bits (bit0,2,3,4 from four request bools).
      adoption (above) then feeds candidates +0xC8; the reconcile loop
      (20.106) does the rest.
- Third admit caller 0x141771060 (tick): processes LOCAL join intents from
  the ring, then 0x1417AF360(...,0x1FF,...) (broadcast?).
- RESERVATION VOCABULARY in .rdata (no direct xrefs - obfuscated log path):
  request_peer_reservation 0x141BE9A75 / release_peer_reservation 0x141BE9A95
  (side by side -> method names), no-ambassador-reservation 0x141C9E38B,
  no-reservation 0x141C9E463, too-many-player-and-reservations 0x141CAE93C,
  "live_players: %d, total_players_and_reservations: %d, max_allowed_j..."
  0x141C9CE54 (a format string IS in the binary - exception to the usual
  no-log-strings rule), reservations_failed 0x141BE9E60.

### WHAT THIS MEANS FOR PHASE 3
The gate is not "send player-add". The joining machine must be REGISTERED
(reserved) on the host's session object first - table A/B membership + the
+0x30 bitmap + capacity counter - and then its members admitted, and only
then does adoption feed +0xC8. The reserve step is reached from the join-
request handler 0x14178DE60; what selects that branch in dispatcher
0x14175E520 (which message id / which transport event) is the remaining
open question - as is how the request packet reaches it (which is where the
shim/server would inject). DO-NOT: do not attempt direct +0xC8 writes
(reconcile loop removes them, 20.106); do not chase the id->name getter.

## 20.107 FULL UPSTREAM->DOWNSTREAM MAP OF THE JOIN ARCHITECTURE, AND THE LAYERS
## WE HAVE NEVER TOUCHED. (2026-08-27 ~12:4x)

All verified-by-reading (disassembly + .pdata + the 20.104 string tables). Nothing here
has been executed. RVAs at image base 0x140000000.

### THE LAYERS, OUTERMOST FIRST

L1 STEAM SHIM - where the whole of 2026-08-27 was spent.
   friends (3 slots ever called), matchmaking (create/join lobby, lobby ids).
   VERDICT: THE PLAYER PIPELINE NEVER TOUCHES STEAM. Read end to end, pump -> submit
   -> add-players -> append all operate on the managed-session object and make no
   Steam call. Everything shipped today (shared lobby id, LobbyChatUpdate, persona
   name, presence relay) is upstream of nothing in it.

L2 MANAGED SESSION / PLATFORM PLANE - 0x1768000..0x17B0000, and it is a SUBSYSTEM:
   274 direct callers of session_by_index (0x179AF00), which indexes TEN slots.
     0x17A46A0  per-tick pump: for slot 0..9, if byte[session+4]&1 -> submit
     0x179D460  submit: walks count at +0x948, logs "Submitting player add"
     0x1792080  add-players: logs "Adding player [xuid=..]"
     0x17923E0  APPEND-ADDS   = candidates(+0xC8) MINUS added(+0x608)
     0x1792610  APPEND-REMOVES = added(+0x608) MINUS candidates(+0xC8)
   Object: candidates[32] @+0xC8 (stride 0x10: flags byte, key qword at +8);
   added[32] @+0x608; xuid out[32] @+0x808; flagA @+0x908; flagB @+0x928;
   count @+0x948. Ranges close exactly (0xC8+0x200=0x2C8, 0x608+0x200=0x808).
   THIS IS A RECONCILE LOOP, NOT AN INJECTION POINT - and that matters more than the
   gate itself. A peer forced into +0xC8 once is computed as a REMOVAL by 0x1792610 on
   the next tick unless whatever populates +0xC8 keeps asserting it. There is no
   one-shot write that survives.
   OPEN: the writer of +0xC8 is NOT locatable by displacement scanning - the append
   walks it by pointer (lea then +0x10 per step), so the writer almost certainly does
   too and never names 0xC8 in an operand. Precise scan of the whole module found
   exactly one non-indexed candidate and it belongs to a different object.

L3 NETWORK SESSION MESSAGES - almost certainly what writes +0xC8 (20.104):
   player-add / player-refuse / player-remove / player-properties, beside
   membership-update, peer-properties, delegate-leadership, boot-machine.
   We have never sent any of them.

L4 ACTIVITY PLANE - where our type-12 emission lives.
   0xC17E40 is a THREE-PASS outer loop (`inc edi; cmp edi,3`) around a per-peer inner
   loop; the missing-tracking-data case self-heals (20.105) and is not a failure.
   peers and players are separate arrays with separate valid masks.

L5 BAP / TRANSPORT - working, not implicated.

### WHAT WE HAVE NEVER TOUCHED (the answer to "what is in a different layer")
1. RESERVATIONS. `no-reservation` and `no-ambassador-reservation` are join-refuse
   reasons; nothing in this project has ever made a reservation. A reservation looks
   like a PREREQUISITE to a player add, and it is the most likely reason a peer never
   becomes a candidate in the first place.
2. THE PARTY LAYER. `_join_remote_state_party_start_join -> party_join_host ->
   party_join_clients -> party_join_complete` is a distinct plane from both the Steam
   lobby and the managed session. Joining is a PARTY operation with phases.
3. MEMBERSHIP PROTOBUF. `network session membership protobuf encoding/decoding` -
   a protobuf path for session membership that is NOT the bit-packed type-12 schema
   every prior boot has been aimed at.
4. TEN SESSION SLOTS. The pump iterates ten; the logs show msi 0 AND msi 1 per client.
   Every plan to date has reasoned about one session.
5. `network_session_check_properties`, with the literal strings "IN SESSION WITH DUDES"
   and "ALONE" - the game's own predicate for whether it is with anyone.

### THE HONEST READ
The operator's prior stands: player-add alone will not be enough. It is one step of a
reconcile loop fed by a message plane we have never spoken, and gated upstream by a
reservation concept we have never implemented. The next unit of work is L3 - locate
the player-add message handler and read what it requires - NOT another shim binding.
DO-NOT: no further Steam-interface work for this goal. No one-shot writes into +0xC8.

## 20.106 STATIC PASS ON BOTH LANE-B CANDIDATES: THE PLAYER-ADD PIPELINE IS FULLY
## MAPPED, AND THE LEVER IS THE CANDIDATE ARRAY AT session+0xC8. (2026-08-27 ~12:2x)

All addresses are RVAs at image base 0x140000000, from p2(70) caller capture (20.105)
plus .pdata function bounds. Everything below is verified-by-reading (disassembly);
nothing here has been executed.

### THE PIPELINE, END TO END
    0x17A46A0  per-tick pump: for slot in 0..9, get session (0x14179AF00), and if
               byte[session+4] & 1, call submit for that slot. Called from 0x16D1FBE.
    0x179D460  SUBMIT: iterates count at +0x948 over the xuid array, logging
               "Submitting player add for xuid ..." per entry (log at 0x179E279,
               loop `inc esi; add r15,8; cmp esi,[rdi+0x948]`).
    0x1792080  ADD-PLAYERS: logs "Adding player [xuid=..]" inside a loop (`inc r12`).
    0x17923E0  THE APPEND, and the gate. This is the one that matters.

### THE OBJECT LAYOUT (derived from the append routine)
    +0x0C8  candidate members[32], stride 0x10: flags byte at +0, key qword at +8
    +0x608  already-added players[32], stride 0x10 - the dedupe set
    +0x808  xuid out[32] (qwords)     +0x908  flagA[32]   +0x928  flagB[32]
    +0x948  count
  0xC8 + 32*0x10 = 0x2C8 and 0x608 + 32*0x10 = 0x808, so the ranges close exactly.

### THE APPEND GATE (0x17923E0)
    r8 = session + 0xC8; for each of the candidate entries:
      if (byte[r8] & 1) == 0            -> SKIP          <= presence bit
      scan session+0x608 (32 entries): if entry valid and [r8+8] == [rcx+8] -> SKIP
      else: out[count] flags come from (byte[r8]>>2)&1 and (byte[r8]>>1)&1,
            then inc count
  So a player reaches the submit ONLY IF it is present in the candidate array at
  session+0xC8 with bit 0 of its flag byte set, and is not already in +0x608. The two
  flag bits are the `as_private` / `online-enabled` pair the log prints.

### CANDIDATE 1 (post-peer-loop, 0xC185B8) - read, and it is not a lever
  The tracking-data routine 0xC17E40..0xC186F1 is an OUTER LOOP OF 3 passes
  (`inc edi; cmp edi,3; jl 0xC17EA0`) each running the inner peer loop. After the
  inner loop it calls 0x1404F7B10(session) - which gates on byte[session+0x131] & 1 -
  and then a conditional `if (A(obj) && !B(obj)) C(obj, ebx)` on [rsp+0x58]+0xF9D8.
  Nothing here writes the candidate array; it is bookkeeping around the self-heal
  already described in 20.105.

### WHAT THIS MEANS
  Every mechanism we shipped today (shared lobby, membership event, persona name,
  presence relay) is upstream of NOTHING in this pipeline. The pipeline reads ONE
  structure: the candidate array at session+0xC8. The peer has never appeared there.
  The next question is what writes +0xC8 - and 20.104 already named the likely source:
  `player-add` is a network session MESSAGE TYPE, sitting beside membership-update and
  peer-properties. That is the write we have never made.
DO-NOT: do not add more Steam-interface bindings for this - the pipeline never touches
Steam. Do not target the tracking table (session+0x602BC); the client self-heals it.

## 20.105 CALLER CAPTURE LANDED, AND IT RETRACTS THE PREMISE WE HAVE HELD SINCE
## 20.82: "Could not find tracking data" IS A SELF-HEALING WARNING INSIDE A PEER
## LOOP, NOT THE RELEASE. (2026-08-27 ~12:0x)

p2(70) captured return addresses out of the retail log funnel. All four resolved, one
image base (0x140000000), all in normal .text despite the .vmp0 section:
    Adding player                 rva 0x17922C2
    Submitting player add         rva 0x179E279
    Could not find tracking data  rva 0xC18442
    creating Steam lobby          rva 0xA46F4B

1. THE RELEASE SITE IS NOT A RELEASE. Disassembly at 0xC18442 shows the log call is
   immediately followed by the game's OWN repair sequence, then a loop continuation:
     call 0x1404F7710(session, peer)  -> lookup; jne = already present, done
     call 0x1404F8250(session, peer)  -> the ADD (big frame, 0x6A0 stack)
     call 0x1404FC2E0(session, peer, r8b=1) -> finalize when the add succeeded
     else: throttled error log, gated on a 1000 ms (0x3E8) interval
   and the "done" target 0xC185A1 does:
     call 0x1404DF4B0 -> next peer index; cmp eax,-1; jne 0xC18280 = LOOP BACK
   So the whole thing is a per-peer iteration, and the missing-tracking-data case is a
   warning followed by the client ADDING THE ENTRY ITSELF and carrying on.
   => 20.82 link 4 and everything downstream of it framed this line as the moment the
   peer is released. THAT FRAMING IS WRONG. Boots #10-#13 were read against it.
2. THE TRACKING TABLE IS NOW ADDRESSED: count at session+0x602B8, array at
   session+0x602BC, entry stride 10 bytes, key = the entry's first qword compared
   against the peer's first qword.
3. "Adding player" (0x17922C2) is emitted INSIDE A LOOP over an array in r12 (`inc r12`
   right after the log), reading a byte pair from r12 and a field at r15+0x57C. So the
   add-players routine iterates a list; ours has exactly one entry, which is the same
   fact as `Starting add operation on '1' xuids` seen from outside.
4. WHAT THIS CHANGES: the peer not joining is NOT explained by this log line, and no
   amount of feeding the tracking table will fix it if the client already self-heals
   that table. The open question moves to what happens AFTER the peer loop exits
   (0xC185B8: call 0x1404F7B10(r15), then 0x14178DAB0/0x14178DA50 on [rsp+0x58]+0xF9D8)
   and to the actual player-add path at 0x179E279.
DO-NOT: do not treat "Could not find tracking data" as a failure signal. Do not build
anything further aimed at populating tracking data - the client does it itself.

## THE STEAM LOBBY. managed_session CREATES ONE PER SESSION AND EACH CLIENT MAKES

saw the peer (`peers valid: 0x1`), release fired as always at t=202364. Contract was

1. FRIENDS CENSUS - COMPLETE, AND IT IS TINY. Across 256 logged slots on BOTH
   machines the client called exactly: slot 3 (1-2x), slot 5 (3x), slot 43 (3x).
   NOTHING ELSE, and nothing above 79.
   => THE OUT-OF-BOUNDS FREEZE THEORY IS RETIRED. The vtable audit's statically
   measured call sites at 0x330..0x690 DO NOT EXECUTE. 20.97's step-1 resize was
   worth doing for safety but it was never the freeze cause, which matches p2(61)

   in the record are now closed as wrong; the p2(62)/p2(63) cause remains UNKNOWN

2. SLOT 64 IS NOT SetRichPresence - REFUTED BY EXECUTION. The argument guard fired
   on both machines instead of faulting:
     mac  arg1=0x0000000000000001  arg2=0x0000000000000114
     rig  arg1=0x000002D647070001  arg2=0x7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFC
     rig  arg1=0x0000000000000001  arg2=0x7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFC
   Neither argument is a string on either machine, and arg2 differs across machines,
   so it is not a constant. No string-pair setter is called on the friends interface
   AT ALL. The '/connect:' strings of 20.96 were found in memory, not intercepted on

   for it in p2(62)..p2(66) is dead weight EXCEPT the presence transport (20.99),
   which is a general cross-machine key/value store and is reused below.
3. THE ACTUAL MECHANISM, straight out of the mac log:
     managed_session: [#0] Creating managed session as host. session_type=fireteam
     managed_session: [#0][..] Adding player [xuid=0x110000130aa9ec5] .. to session.
     managed_session: [msi 0] creating Steam lobby for new session
     managed_session:steam: [msi 0] creating Steam Lobby for new session
     managed_session: [#0][..] Starting add operation on '1' xuids.
     managed_session: [#0][..] Submitting player add for xuid '0x110000130aa9ec5'.
     managed_session: [#0][..] Player add completed successfully.
   THE MANAGED SESSION'S MEMBERSHIP IS A STEAM LOBBY. "Adding player [xuid=..]" -
   the very line BOOT_BRIEF_p2-62/65 named as step 4 of the success chain - ALREADY
   HAPPENS, on every boot, and always for exactly ONE xuid: the client's own
   ("operating on '1' xuids"). It was never going to name a peer.
4. EACH CLIENT CREATES ITS OWN LOBBY, so the two sessions are disjoint by
   construction:
     mac  lobby=0x010900000CC46DB7 (msi 0), 0x01090000EA0CF3EE (msi 1)
     rig  lobby=0x010900000CC46DB4 (msi 0), 0x01090000EA0CF3ED (msi 1)
   And OUR SHIM INVENTS THOSE IDS: create_lobby computes
   `kLobbySteamIdPrefix | (account ^ (call * kLobbyCallStride))` locally and queues

   calls join_lobby; join is driven by obtaining a connect target", now with the

6. The client never enumerates lobby members: ZERO matchmaking stubs (table=1) this
   boot, on either machine. Reading: no member-change callback ever fired, because
   each lobby only ever had one member. Our shim owns the callback queue
   (`queue_callback`, already used by create_lobby), so we can fire one.
7. REGRESSION, MINE (p2(66)): `presence_worker` never started - 0 lines. The only
   paths to ensure_worker() were the three slots this build deliberately unbound,
   plus set_rich_presence, which bails at the argument guard BEFORE reaching it. So
   the relay half went untested this boot. The server's three `presence stage=
   snapshot bytes=0` lines were the operator's own curl probes at 10:0x, not client
   traffic. Fix: start the worker from a path that actually runs (create_lobby).

NEXT (lobby lane, all of it evidence-backed): publish own lobby id + activity to the
server on create_lobby; on the second client, return the ALREADY-ADVERTISED lobby id
from create_lobby instead of inventing one, so both managed sessions name the same
lobby; then fire a LobbyChatUpdate for the peer and serve the member-enumeration

LESSON CANDIDATE (converts on acceptance): "A measured call is worth more than a
published header. When runtime evidence and a reference disagree about an interface,
the reference is a hypothesis and the measurement is the fact." Same family as U1
(reference = fresh-fetch oracle) but pointing the other way, and it is the rule

## 20.99 p2(64) WAS DEAD ON ARRIVAL: ITS PRESENCE RELAY NEVER LEFT THE CLIENT
## PROCESS. FIXED AND PROVEN ON THE WIRE AS p2(65). ALSO: THE "CLI-LAUNCH WEDGE"
## IS THE PRESS-TO-START SCREEN, NOT A WEDGE. (2026-08-27 ~10:1x)

Deliverables: RE_output/claims/BOOT_BRIEF_p2-65.md (gate PASS, 7 literals
checked); fork 3f7e9d2 = p2(65).

1. THE DEFECT. friends.cpp relayed and fetched through
   `client::network::consume_http`. That call does not reach the network and does
   not reach the standalone server: the ONE in-process consumer the Client DLL
   registers is `server::http::consume` (server_http.cpp), whose first statement
   is `if (request.url.find("/SignOn") == npos) return false;`. So
   - every `relay_to_server()` returned false, and the return was DISCARDED
     (`static_cast<void>`), so the drop was silent;
   - every `fetch_peer_values()` returned false and early-returned, so no peer
     row could ever exist.
   The `/presence` and `/presence/store` routes existed ONLY on the standalone
   server's TLS listener (8443), which the client never dials for this and whose
   handshake fails anyway (SEC_E_UNSUPPORTED_FUNCTION 0x80090302, seen in the
   server log at t=9603). Cross-machine presence was structurally impossible.
   CONSEQUENCE HAD WE BOOTED: step 1 of BOOT_BRIEF_p2-62 goes green (the store is
   shim-local), step 2 stays dark, and the brief's pre-named branch sends the next
   pass to "friend-enumeration slot mapping wrong" - a road with nothing on it.
   The brief marked this link "verified-by-reading (this build)". The reading was
   wrong; only execution caught it. L16 marks are only as good as the read.

2. THE FIX (p2(65)). Routes moved to the PLAINTEXT admin listener (8099), which
   was already bound, already reachable both ways, and needs no TLS:
   - admin_http.cpp: GET /presence, POST /presence/store?xuid=<hex>&key=&value=.
     The value rides as a query parameter because `serve_connection` performs
     exactly one recv() - a single-segment request then needs no body reassembly.
     Both routes log (ev=presence stage=store|snapshot).
   - friends.cpp: a real HTTP/1.1 socket client to externalServer.host:8099,
     driven by a DEDICATED WORKER THREAD (1 s cadence, 1.5 s socket timeouts).
     The interface methods now do ZERO I/O - they only read the local table. The
     vtable audit (20.97) measured 33 calls at one friends offset alone; a
     blocking connect on that path is exactly the pre-title freeze class that
     already cost two boots. Structurally excluded now rather than tuned.
   - egress: no interference. peerSubnet is 192.168.1.0/24 on both machines, so
     `redirect_ipv4` returns early on `is_peer_address` and the destination is
     reached AS ADDRESSED; the fail-closed gate allows it.

3. VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, BEFORE SPENDING A BOOT (this is the point):
   mac POST /presence/store -> 200; rig POST /presence/store -> 200; and then
   BOTH machines' GET /presence returned BOTH rows -
     76561198776753861 connect /connect:c59eaa3001001001
     76561198776753862 connect /connect:c69eaa3001001001
   Chain link 3 of the boot brief is now verified-by-execution, not by reading.
   Server restarted afterwards so the boot starts from an EMPTY table (the store
   is in-memory) - a leftover row would let step 2 pass falsely.

4. INSTRUMENTS ADDED so the brief's branches are distinguishable (L13). p2(64)
   could log `rich_presence_store result=ok` and nothing else; a never-asked
   question and an empty answer were indistinguishable. Now:
   presence_worker (lane ran at all), rich_presence_relay (http status per key),
   presence_fetch (rows/peers merged), peer_rich_presence (friend/key/value/
   ok|empty), friend_count (count), friend_by_index (the id handed to the Client
   as a join target).

5. THE "CLI-LAUNCH WEDGE" IS NOT A WEDGE (corrects 20.98 item 4). In a known-good
   boot, `bootflow:start` starts tasks ENUM(0) and ENUM(3); ENUM(3) finishes in
   27 ms and ENUM(0) finishes after 15765 ms, immediately followed by
   `platform_user: Assigning primary controller to '-1'` -> "Signing user in to
   machine" -> platform_signin. ENUM(0) is the press-to-start gate and those
   15.7 s are a human pressing a key. In BOTH overnight CLI runs ENUM(3)
   completed normally and ENUM(0) never did - the title is simply sitting there
   with nobody to press anything. The 02:xx run had a fully successful STUN
   exchange with a live server first (valid 16-byte response, external address
   assigned), so it is not the server-down artifact either; the 08:55 run's STUN
   failure is separately explained (server was down until 09:18).
   REVISED RULE: the operator's GUI launch is required because a CLI launch has
   no input, NOT because the launch context corrupts the build. Nothing is wrong
   with the binary or the prefix.

6. HOUSEKEEPING. Two orphaned winedevice.exe processes on the game bottle
   (Aug 21 and Aug 26 22:33, no wineserver, ~2.5% CPU each, 249 and 19 minutes
   of CPU burned) killed before the boot. Rig reachable via the ControlMaster;
   its ICMP is firewalled, so `ping` is not an aliveness test - the socket is.

DO-NOT: do not reintroduce `consume_http` for anything that must reach the
standalone server - it only answers /SignOn. Do not put friends-path I/O back on
the calling thread. Do not read `ping` failure to the rig as the rig being down.

was the END state only. The correct statement: **there is a live search window
on every boot** (mac t~85532-85553, rig t~53125-53141 in boot #12 client logs),
which closes by timeout to advertise. The public-bubble path aborts before ever
running PUBLIC search.

Consequences:
1. No need to force searching to make clients look - they already do. The
   open question is what happens INSIDE the window: who receives the query,
   and why the answer is empty.
2. Roll parity note: both rolled role [1]; gather chance was 0.13, so identical
   roles twice is plausible by chance but worth one cheap look at whether role
   derivation is seeded identically across our two clients.

### DESIGN PRIOR (from GDC 2015 Truman "Shared World Shooter"; prior, not
### measurement - D1 architecture, details may have drifted by Arrivals)
- Three host roles: Physics Host = a player machine (P2P); Mission Activity
  Host + Bubble Activity Host = cloud. Public spaces (Tower) are bubble
  instances served by cloud Bubble Hosts; strangers co-presence needs NO
  fireteam.
- Bubble matchmaking is transition-triggered and time-boxed (~15s precache /
  10s search start / ~5s budget then abort and self-host a standalone bubble).
  Our observed self-hosting matches this documented abort path.
- Implication, unverified: type 54 bubble_host_table may be the bubble-host
  DIRECTORY rather than a side lever. Feeds Phase-4 lane (schema-key hunt).
- Halo Reach host-migration black-screen anecdote is a candidate explanation
  for the Mac black-screen symptom. Testable later; do not spend a boot on it.

### RE-PRIORITIZATION (supersedes nothing closed; de-prioritizes 20.86 NEXT-1)
1. Phase 1: correlate the search-window timestamps above against THIS boot's
   server log filtered by session (...0014 mac / ...0018 rig; log spans four
   boots). Pre-named outcomes: (i) query arrived and we answered empty/late ->
   pure server fix; (ii) nothing arrived -> search rides a transport we do not
   expose (names the message family; feeds the schema-key lane).
2. Serve the gatherer to the searcher during the live window (one boot).
3. Only if windows miss each other: force complementary roles via the
   string-anchor sites (site=140/141 region of code).
4. Schema-key hunt / type-54 runs offline as background lane (spawned
   2026-08-26 ~21:2x, deliverable RE_output/claims/schema-key-hunt-2026-08-26.md).
DE-PRIORITIZED: the type-14 send-path hook (20.86 candidate #1) - reason-1
reproducibility plus the constant-candidate makes interrogating the refusal
letter less informative than attacking tracking-table population frontally
via the client's own retail-shaped matchmaking flow. Parked: fireteam/posse
target fabrication (private path; wrong door for Tower co-presence).

DO-NOT:
- Do not treat "the client only asks once" as established - refuted above;
  it asks, searches 10-25s, then flips roles.
- Do not force-role-hook before Phase 1 correlation - the window may already
  overlap; that boot would be spent answering a question the logs can answer.
- Do not cite the GDC block as project-measured fact; label every use PRIOR.

## RIG SEARCHED LATE, WAS HANDED MAC'S LIVE ADVERTISEMENT ON THE WIRE, AND
## TRACKED NOTHING. THE GAP IS BETWEEN WIRE DELIVERY AND SEEKER ACCEPTANCE.
## (2026-08-26 ~21:45)

Sources: `RE_output/captures/20260826_203518_p2-58_reason_hook/{server,
mac_client,rig_client}_sunrise.log`, boot #12 only (server clock t>5.28M).

### VERIFIED SEQUENCE (boot #12)
Per-client arc, both machines identical in shape - each searches exactly
ONCE per activity transition, all under matchmaking set 0xE8ABA41B:
```
config request -> valid config response (~32 ms)          [site 122/124]
publish advertisement (mac=id 5, rig=id 6)                [site 130/138]
ONE "Sending session search request"                      [site 156]
   PRIVATE search starts, Search[ACTIVE]                  [t~85.3k mac / 53.1k rig]
+10s/+25s later: advertise flip, "No results tracked"     [site 167, both]
```
Clock attribution against the server log (whose t spans four boots - filter):
- t=5310462 mac publishes ad 5 (steamid ...861)
- **t=5310603 mac's session_search -> EMPTY, `result=none self=1 sibling=0`**.
  Correct-at-the-time: rig's BAP identity did not land until t=5401872
  (~91 s later). No sibling advertisement existed yet.
- t=5401872 rig BAP conn=3 identity lands
- t=5432397-42 rig publishes ad 6 (steamid ...862)
- **t=5432556 rig's session_search -> FOUND**: mac's live 146-byte ad selected
  (`where=serve machine=0xC31525645A50F848`), sealed outbound 154 B
  (svc=42 rsp=43, `dir=seal ... bytes=154`) within 1 ms.
- Rig client nevertheless ends its search with `Ending the search [0].
  No results were being tracked.` and NEVER logs receiving a search response
  (contrast config: explicit "Received valid config response").

### VERDICT
The pre-named branch (ii) "nothing arrived over any transport" is REFUTED -
search requests reach us over svc=42 both ways; serving computes, seals, and
ships a real foreign advertisement. Branch (i) split: mac got a correct empty;
rig got a correct-looking serve. **The open gap is single: the seeker did not
ACCEPT the served candidate.** Role forcing (old phase 3) is UNNECESSARY as a
first move - complementary roles occurred NATURALLY in boot #12 (mac gathered/
advertised early, rig searched late into a live ad).

### TWO NAMED SUSPECTS (evidence attached; either or both may hold)
S1 - CANDIDATE SUITABILITY: every descriptor logged this boot reads
`routable=0 ... method=6`. join_descriptor.cpp::read() returns unroutable for
any `method != kDirectMethod(0)` (its header documents "Methods 6 and 7 would
select the relay instead"), and every client-published ad carries method=6.
Rig log showed "clearing unsuitable sessions" during search setup. If the
retail seeker applies the same direct-path test to candidates, we handed it a
candidate flagged unroutable-by-construction. NOTE: our relay transport exists
in design (transport-relay-design.md) but has never carried real traffic.
S2 - RESPONSE COMPOSITION SHAPE: the served 146-byte body is the PUBLISHER'S
RAW advertisement body (off=0 hex begins machine-id then literal
"steamid:76561198776753861#..."; offset-64 tail decodes oddly - see the
route-file comment on '957860'), wrapped in our hand-built svc-43 transaction
layout. It is plausible the retail search-response composition wraps candidate
descriptors differently (or expects composed fields beside them). Nothing has
ever validated our composition against consumer behavior.
DISCRIMINATOR SET (pre-named): (a) OFFLINE FIRST - walk the fork's svc-43
response encoder against the in-process DLL's proven behavior; a divergence
census here needs no boot. (b) if census finds nothing wrong, ONE instrument
at the client's seeker-insertion point (matchmaking_seeker "tracking" site,
between wire arrival and "No results were being tracked") reports WHY each
candidate is dropped.

### CORRECTIONS TO PRIOR ENTRIES
20.87's phase-2 as written ("make sure the server actually serves the
gatherer to the searcher") is MOOT - §20.74-style serving plus today's boot
evidence show serving happens. Keep 20.87's verified arcs and GDC labels; its
recommendation order below the correlation step is superseded by this entry.

DO-NOT:
- Do not spend a boot on role forcing; boot #12 proves complementarity occurs
  unprompted.
- Do not call the serve "delivered": seal+write is transport egress, not
  application receipt. The client logged nothing of receipt.
- Do not treat S1/S2 verdicts as exclusive; the discriminator census resolves
  both before any boot.

## `reason_name` WAS NEVER CALLED. THE ABSENCE NEGATIVE LANDED EXACTLY AS
## PRE-NAMED, AND IT RETIRES THE NAMING ROUTE WITH A MECHANISM.
## (2026-08-26 ~20:40)

Capture: `RE_output/captures/20260826_203518_p2-58_reason_hook/`.
DLL `9bba721d961c0869` both machines; server exe UNCHANGED `1991b3da6f518165`.
**This boot's sessions are `...0014` (Mac) and `...0018` (rig); the server log
now spans FOUR boots - filter by session id or t>5,200,000.**

### THE RESULT, and both halves matter

```
mac  ev=peerlink stage=reason result=ok        <- installed
rig  ev=peerlink stage=reason result=ok        <- installed
     ev=peerlink stage=reason value=...        <- ZERO, both machines
```

**The instrument was live** (the signature matched; the install line is
unconditional) **and the system under test ran**:

```
t=5315913  handle=...0014  payload=0000000000000001 E622D0F738836C84   Mac releases rig
t=5437766  handle=...0018  payload=0000000000000001 E97375DB5FAA526F   rig releases Mac
mac/rig    "Could not find tracking data for peer '1' ... adding it to fixup"
mac/rig    "[AC ...] Sending peer-reservation release for machine '...'"
```

The exact refusal we are studying occurred on both machines, and the accessor
was never invoked. That is the ABSENCE negative BOOT_BRIEF_p2-58 pre-named, and
it is a real result rather than a wasted boot **only because the boring-path
liveness line and the system-ran evidence were both required in advance.**

### WHY - the mechanism, not just the observation

The release log line carries **no reason**:

```
[AC PRIVATE CURRENT CON-Y EST-Y AH->9eaa300100200014 MEM-1]
    Sending peer-reservation release for machine 'E6:22:D0:F7:38:83:6C84'.
```

Machine only. So there is nothing on this path that needs a reason NAME, and
`reason_name` - which exists to turn a reason into a string for a log line -
is correctly never called. **The reason byte goes straight onto the wire.** We
have only ever seen it because OUR SERVER decodes the type-14 payload; the
client never prints it.

That retires the whole naming/logging route to the decision. It also explains
why 20.85's five printer sites all format a machine id: they are machine-centric
diagnostics, and the reason argument they take is used for OTHER peer-link
messages, not this one.

### A CANDIDATE THIS RAISES - marked, not claimed

If the release is emitted directly from the "could not find tracking data ->
fixup" path, the reason byte at that call site is plausibly a **constant** -
"I hold no record of this peer" - rather than the outcome of a comparison. That
would explain three things at once: why reason 1 survives every change we make;
why 20.83's gate-table correspondence had to be retracted; and why the Mac moved
5 -> 1 when the lobby ids changed (a different upstream path was taken to the
same fixup).

**CANDIDATE. Not verified.** It would mean there is no "comparison" to find on
this path at all, and the question "what does the client compare" is the wrong
question for the release - the right one being "what populates tracking data",
which is 20.82 link 4 and has been the answer for four entries now.

### REPRODUCIBILITY GAINED

Boot #11 and boot #12, both machines, all four releases: **reason 1**. The
Mac's 5 -> 1 shift after the unique-lobby-id fix (20.84, recorded as
unexplained) is now confirmed STABLE across two boots rather than a one-off.
Whatever it means, it is a property of the current build, not noise.

### WHAT THE p2(58) HOOK IS WORTH KEEPING FOR

It stays installed and costs nothing: it is silent unless a reason is NAMED, so
the moment any other peer-link path does name one, the deciding site is reported
for free. Its five printer sites are on other messages we have not yet
provoked - a `could-not-connect`, a `join-timed-out` or a `session-full` would
each fire it.

### NEXT, for whoever picks this up

The naming route is closed. Two live options, in the order I would take them:

1. **Hook the type-14 SEND path** in the activity-client module
   (`0x1404Fxxxx`; `activity_client_send_message` is referenced at
   `0x1404FB7D4`, the send wrapper at `0x1404FB790` reads `[rsi+0x65BC0]` and
   `[rcx+0x6C18]` then calls through `[r10+0x28]`). Capture the message type and
   body at the moment of send, and the reason's origin is in the caller's frame.
   This tests the CONSTANT candidate above directly.
2. **Go at 20.82 link 4 instead** - what populates the peer tracking table -
   which is where four consecutive entries have now pointed, and which the
   constant candidate would make the *only* remaining question.

### DO-NOT
- Do not re-run the `reason_name` hook expecting the release to fire it. Proven
  not to, with the mechanism understood.
- Do not read reason 1 as a verdict about identity. It survives changes that
  demonstrably altered identity (20.84), and may be a constant.
- Do not read this capture's server log without filtering; it now spans four
  boots.
## 20.85 THE TYPE-14 EMITTER LANE: THE PEER-LINK DIAGNOSTIC SUBSYSTEM IS
## LOCATED AND THE REASON ENUM IS NOW VERIFIED BY A SECOND, INDEPENDENT ROUTE.
## THE DECISION SITE IS **NOT** REACHED - THE STATIC WALK ENDS IN AN OBSERVER
## FAN-OUT. THE CHEAP ROUTE IS A LIVE HOOK. (2026-08-26 ~20:30)

Method: `RE_scripts/xref_scan.py` (built in 20.83, self-tests against a known
xref) plus pointer-table search, on `RE_output/destiny2_unpacked_full.exe`.

### WHAT IS NOW ESTABLISHED

**1. The reason-name table - `.data 0x142037510`, 35 entries, stride 8.**
Index equals the reason value exactly: `privacy-mode` sits at `+0x28` = index 5,
`tried-to-join-self` at `+0x08` = index 1.

**This independently verifies 20.64's enum reading.** 20.64 derived the indices
from a *contiguous string run* under the assumption "contiguous, so index ==
order". That assumption is now discharged by a different mechanism - an explicit
indexed pointer table - and it holds. Two of this project's expensive mistakes
(20.61's node+0x14, 20.83's gate correspondence) were models fitted on the
evidence that suggested them; this one survives a second, independent route.

**2. `reason_name(int)` at `0x1416E1620`:**
```
cmp ecx, 0x23          ; 35 entries, matching the string count exactly
ja  0x1416E1634        ; out of range -> fallback string
movsxd rax, ecx
lea rcx, [rip+...]     ; -> 0x142037510
mov rax, [rcx + rax*8]
ret
```

**3. A SECOND, previously unrecorded name table** - `.data 0x142037630`, ELEVEN
entries, accessor `0x1416E1600` (`cmp ecx, 0xa`). A smaller enum in the same
subsystem, identity unknown. Recorded so the next lane does not mistake it for
the 35-entry one; both tables begin with `no-reason-given`, which is exactly the
kind of coincidence that produces a wrong reading. A third pointer to that same
string sits at `.data 0x142048A80` (referenced from `0x141743678`), so there are
at least THREE parallel registries - consistent with the note in 20.49.

**4. The peer-link diagnostic module is located.** `reason_name` has exactly
five call sites, all inside `0x14176xxxx..0x1417Axxxx`:
```
0x141768CFC   0x141783EDD   0x1417860CB   0x141789D98   0x1417A5DB1
```
Each formats a MACHINE ID alongside the reason - the template
`00:00:00:00:00:00:00:00` at `.rdata 0x141CA7F80` is lea'd ~40 bytes before each
call. These are the printers behind
`Could not find tracking data for peer ... machine id 'E6:22:D0:...'`.

**5. The reason is a PARAMETER at those sites**, not computed there:
`0x1417860C9  mov ecx, edi` immediately precedes the call. They log; they do not
decide.

**6. The call chain above one of them:**
```
0x141785E90                      the printer
  <- 0x1416E1250                 guards on a state field:
       mov ecx,[rax+0x1AEF8]; add ecx,-6; cmp ecx,3; ja skip
       i.e. it reports ONLY while that state is in {6,7,8,9}
    <- 0x1416DFA4F               one of TWENTY-SEVEN identical arms, each
                                 `cmp [rdx+0x1D18], 5 / jne / call <handler>`
```
Those 27 arms are an observer fan-out gated on one mode field being 5 - a
notification layer, not a dispatch on message type. **That is where the static
walk stops being productive**, and it is the point at which this entry stops
rather than guessing.

**7. The AC log line and the reason naming come from DIFFERENT modules.**
`[AC %s CON-%s EST-%s AH->%llx MEM-%d]` (`.rdata 0x141BEA6D8`) is formatted at
`0x1404F5B06`, and `activity_client_send_message` (`.rdata 0x141BEA8E0`) is
referenced at `0x1404FB7D4` - both in `0x1404Fxxxx`, while every reason printer
is in `0x14176..0x1417A`. So "[AC ...] Sending peer-reservation release" is
assembled by the activity client, and the reason value reaches it from the
peer-link subsystem. Anyone hunting the emitter must not assume one module.

The send wrapper at `0x1404FB790` reads `[rsi+0x65BC0]` and `[rcx+0x6C18]` then
makes a virtual call through `[r10+0x28]`. `0x6C18` sits in the self-key cluster
at `0x6C30` that opencode's statics already named (msg12-parser-read.md), so the
activity-client object is the same one that front has been reading.

### WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED - stated plainly

**What the client compares before choosing the reason byte.** That was the
question, and the static route did not answer it. The decision sits above an
observer fan-out, the log format strings are absent from both the image and the
08-15 dump (20.82), and several functions in this range tail-jump into `.vmp0`.
Continuing to walk it by hand is the shape of work this project has repeatedly
found unprofitable.

### THE CHEAP ROUTE, and it should be next

**Hook `reason_name` itself.** It is a five-instruction leaf with a distinctive
signature (`cmp ecx,0x23 / ja / movsxd / lea / mov / ret`), it is called on
exactly the path we care about, and its argument IS the reason byte. A detour
that logs `(reason, _ReturnAddress())` converts an expensive static walk into a
runtime read that names the deciding call site directly - and from there the
comparison is one disassembly away, with the address handed to us instead of
searched for.

This is the same move that has worked repeatedly on this project: the shim
already installs signature-scanned detours (`region_private.cpp`,
`composition_check.cpp` are the pattern), and lesson 2 says the instrument comes
before the intervention. It also satisfies the pre-boot checklist trivially: the
liveness line fires on EVERY reason lookup, including the boring ones we already
understand.

Estimated shape: one new file under `client/hooks/`, ~80 lines, no server
change, no protocol risk. It answers 20.84's ADDRESS CANDIDATE for free.

### DO-NOT
- Do not confuse the 11-entry table at `0x142037630` with the 35-entry one at
  `0x142037510`. Both start with `no-reason-given`.
- Do not read the five printer sites as decision sites. The reason arrives in
  `edi`/`ecx` as a parameter.
- Do not resume the hand-walk above `0x1416DFA4F` without the hook's answer;
  27 sibling arms and a `.vmp0` boundary are what is up there.
## 20.84 BOOT #11 (p2(57)): THE FIX WORKED AND MY PREDICTION WAS WRONG. IDS ARE
## NOW PROVABLY UNIQUE; THE MAC MOVED FROM `privacy-mode` TO `tried-to-join-self`
## AND BOTH MACHINES NOW SAY REASON 1. THE GATE-TABLE CORRESPONDENCE IS
## **RETRACTED**. (2026-08-26 ~20:05)

Capture: `RE_output/captures/20260826_195738_p2-57_unique_lobby/` (server 9,183
lines - **it spans boots #10 AND #11 because the server was never restarted;
this boot's sessions are `...000A` and `...000D`, t>3,000,000. Filter or the two
boots blend.**) DLL `63f5fbda6b83e0b4` both machines, server `1991b3da6f518165`,
fork `dd6ac7d`.

### WHAT LANDED: the ids are unique, byte for byte as predicted

```
MAC  lobby=0x010900000CC46DB7 account=0x30AA9EC5
     lobby=0x01090000EA0CF3EE
RIG  lobby=0x010900000CC46DB4 account=0x30AA9EC6
     lobby=0x01090000EA0CF3ED
```

Every one matches BOOT_BRIEF_p2-57's predicted value exactly, and the change
propagated into the managed sessions:

```
MAC  [#0]{74590869042523575}-294E0ADD:4C174D6F-steamid:...861#ef2d1a97595d7e43
RIG  [#0]{74590869042523572}-EFC96410:13BCE3A4-steamid:...862#f6f266a896ddac16
```

Distinct platform ids, distinct internal ids, distinct steamids, distinct
tokens, distinct published machine ids (`0x443619E053DCD0DF` vs
`0xE7B69DCE3CD364F8`). **Nothing at the platform-identity layer is shared any
more.** The latent bug 20.83 found was real and is fixed.

### WHAT DID NOT LAND: the prediction, and it fails cleanly

Predicted: *the rig stops saying reason 1 and starts saying reason 5.*
Observed - the opposite move, on the other machine:

```
handle=...000A (MAC, joined t=3069460)  reason 1  key 0x846C…  = releases the RIG
handle=...000D (RIG, joined t=3202893)  reason 1  key 0x6F52…  = releases the MAC
```

**The MAC moved 5 -> 1. Both machines now say `tried-to-join-self`** - after a
change that made their identities strictly *more* distinct. That is backwards
for a self-check, and it is the CONTENT negative the brief pre-named.

Session-to-machine mapping verified, not assumed: keepalives show
`key=0x6F52AA5FDB7573E9 slot=0` (account key 0 = Mac) and
`key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 slot=1` (rig); `...000A` joined first.

### THE INSTRUMENT WAS INDICTED FIRST, AND IT SURVIVED

Before blaming the system: the reason enum was re-read from
`.rdata 0x141C9E2F8` end to end. 20.64's anchor and ordering are **correct** -
`[0] no-reason-given, [1] tried-to-join-self, ... [5] privacy-mode`. The decode
is sound, so the result is real.

### RETRACTED: the gate-table <-> reason-enum correspondence (20.83)

20.83 proposed that peer-link reason 1 reports gate [28]
`target_fireteam_is_not_ours` and reason 5 reports [27]
`target_fireteam_privacy_is_not_closed`. It was marked INFERRED. **The negative
landed: retract it.** Making the fireteam platform ids unique did not clear
reason 1 - it spread it.

The better reading, from the client's own sequencing: the release is emitted
~1-2 ms after `Could not find tracking data for peer '1'`, which is unchanged
this boot on both machines -

```
MAC  ... machine id 'E6:22:D0:F7:38:83:6C:84'  (the rig's member key)
RIG  ... machine id 'E9:73:75:DB:5F:AA:52:6F'  (the mac's member key)
```

**The reason code is a symptom of the tracking-data miss, not an independent
gate verdict.** A peer the client holds NO record for cannot be evaluated
against gates that read that record, so whatever the reason byte reports, it is
chosen on a degenerate path. Why it moved 5 -> 1 for the Mac is UNEXPLAINED and
recorded as such; it proves only that the byte is sensitive to platform identity.

### EVERYTHING ELSE IS UNCHANGED

```
peers valid: 0x1     both clients, every dump      (never 0x3)
TARGET lines: 0      both clients                  (no group target, ever)
tracking data miss:  both clients, same message
peer bodies:         4095 / 4095 / 3882  (MAC)  and  3967 / 4095 / 3882  (RIG)
peer_advert:         result=built ... peer_citizen=1  x13
```

The delivery half keeps working perfectly. 20.82's links 4-7 are untouched.

### THE ONE THING STILL IDENTICAL - candidate, NOT a claim

Every citizen advertisement we publish carries
`join.address/port = endpoint::advertised()`, and the server binds **one**
gameplay endpoint: `stage=endpoint result=ok mode=embedded port=30976`. So each
client receives a peer whose join address is byte-identical to its own.
`join.machineId` does differ (it is `region_machine_id(regionIndex)` and the two
sat in regions 48 and 56 all boot), which leaves the ADDRESS as the last shared
field in the descriptor.

Marked CANDIDATE and nothing more. I have just had one confident prediction
refuted on this exact question; per lesson 2 the next move is an instrument that
says what the client compares, **not another fix guess**. Writing "the address
is the cause" here would repeat the mistake this entry exists to record.

### NEXT - stay on path (A), and instrument before intervening

1. **Locate the emitter of the peer-reservation release** and read what it
   compares before choosing the reason byte. `xref_scan.py` now exists; the
   release strings themselves are absent from both the image and the 08-15 dump
   (20.82), so approach it from `activity_client_send_message` /
   the type-14 send path rather than from the log text.
2. That read settles the address candidate for free, and it settles what a
   "known" peer must look like - which is the same question as link 4.
3. Only then decide whether a per-session advertised endpoint is worth building.

### WHAT p2(57) WAS WORTH, honestly

It fixed a real latent bug: two machines publishing one platform session id
would have blocked any join attempt the moment one was made, and it would have
been extremely hard to see later. It also removed a confound from every future
capture. **It was not the unlock, and the entry that proposed it over-claimed
the mechanism.** Both facts belong in the record.

### DO-NOT
- Do not cite the gate-table/reason correspondence. Retracted here.
- Do not read the Mac's 5 -> 1 move as progress or regression. It is unexplained.
- Do not filter this capture's server log without `t>3000000`; boots #10 and #11
  share the file.
## 20.83 PATH (A) FIRST RESULT: THE CLIENT'S JOIN-GATE TABLE IS READ, AND IT
## NAMES OUR BUG. BOTH MACHINES PUBLISHED THE **IDENTICAL** FIRETEAM PLATFORM
## ID, SO `target_fireteam_is_not_ours` COULD NEVER PASS. p2(57) DEPLOYED.
## (2026-08-26 ~19:50)

The user chose path (A) - map the retail mechanism rather than fabricate a
slice of it. One evening of (A) produced the mechanism AND a root-cause bug.

### TOOLING ADDED: `RE_scripts/xref_scan.py`

Generalises the byte-pattern pass that `lane_svc43_xref_scan.py` and
`hashtable_xref_scan.py` each forked privately. Finds rip-relative LEA/MOV/CALL
references from `.text` into any VA range. **It ships with `--self-test`**: the
type-name table at `.data 0x141F91AD0` is known to be read by the getter at
`0x1404DC070`, and the scan must find that pair or it refuses to be trusted.
It does: `0x1404DC073 -> 0x141F91AD0 [lea r64]`.

### THE RETAIL MECHANISM, READ OUT OF THE CLIENT

**1. The join-gate table** - `.data 0x141FECDE0`, 33 char* entries, indexed.
Found by pointer-table search after a direct xref scan returned only
mid-string false positives (the names are held in an array, not lea'd):

```
[ 0] all_controllers_signed_out        [17] target_activity_is_unlocked_for_fireteam
[ 1] booted                            [18] target_activity_is_possible
[ 2] failed_to_start                   [19] target_activity_is_unlocked

[15] target_fireteam_join_not_closed_script                  [32] outgoing_joins_closed_in_active_guided_game
[16] target_activity_is_possible_for_fireteam
```

**Entries [27] and [28] are our two observed refusals.** The peer-link failure
enum's reason 1 `tried-to-join-self` and reason 5 `privacy-mode` correspond to
`target_fireteam_is_not_ours` and `target_fireteam_privacy_is_not_closed`.
INFERRED (the correspondence is by meaning, not by a read of the evaluator),
but it is the only reading under which both observations have a cause.

**2. The activity-client property vocabulary** - `.rdata 0x141BEA7D0`:

```
activity_host_fireteam_is_connected        activity_host_group_target_is_connected
activity_host_last_fireteam_session_id     activity_host_group_target_session_id
activity_host_group_current_is_connected   activity_client_send_message
activity_host_last_group_current_session_id
```

and the format string for the status line we read every boot:
`[AC %s CON-%s EST-%s AH->%llx MEM-%d]`, whose fields resolve against
`.rdata 0x141C1A000`: `PRIVATE|PUBLIC` then **`CURRENT|TARGET`**.

So the activity client tracks a group CURRENT *and a group TARGET*, and
`activity_host_group_target_session_id` is literally the AH a guest joins.
Every line we have ever captured says `CURRENT`. **No client has ever held a
group target.**

**3. The bubble_host state machine** - `.rdata 0x141C283E8`:

```
bubble_host:connect_to_proxy      bubble_host:load_world
bubble_host:start_investment      bubble_host:in_world
bubble_host:idle                  bubble_host:cleanup...
bubble_host:connect_to_posse_activity_host   <-- THE GUEST STEP
```

`connect_to_posse_activity_host` sits between `idle` and `load_world`. That is
the guest path in one string: a client with a posse activity host connects to it
instead of starting its own. Ours go `idle -> load_world` every time.

Also present: `starting public AH` / **`joining public AH`** / `public AH
instance ready` (.rdata 0x141C288A1), and `PRIVATE activity` / `PUBLIC activity`
/ **`PUBLIC activity target`**.

### THE BUG THE MAP EXPOSED - AND IT IS OURS

`target_fireteam_is_not_ours` compares the target fireteam against your own.
The managed session's platform id IS its Steam lobby id (`{74590868828323842}`
= `0x109000000000002`, 20.82). And `create_lobby` built that id as:

```cpp
const std::uint64_t lobby = kLobbySteamIdPrefix | call;   // call = next_api_call()
```

`next_api_call()` is a **per-process counter**. Both machines start it at the
same value, so both invent the same ids. Verified across the boot #10 capture:

```
MAC  [#0]{74590868828323842}-B1F73462:B2ED6203      fireteam
RIG  [#0]{74590868828323842}-8954314B:5E1013E6      fireteam   <- IDENTICAL
MAC  [#1]{74590868828323843}-599A4769:CFCB1502      posse
RIG  [#1]{74590868828323843}-2CBD073C:C5DC8F43      posse      <- IDENTICAL
```

Only the internal session ids differ. **The platform identity - the one the
client compares - was byte-identical.** `target_fireteam_is_not_ours` could
never pass, and the rig said `tried-to-join-self` about the mac in every capture
we hold. It was right.

**This is 20.40's bug one layer up.** That entry made the USER identity
per-instance; `get_steam_id` even carries the comment "both machines presented
this same user, so no client could rendezvous with the other". Every id DERIVED
from a process counter was left alone.

### p2(57), BUILT AND DEPLOYED TO BOTH MACHINES

The lobby's account-id field now mixes the authored per-instance `steamId` with
the call number:

```cpp
const auto account = static_cast<std::uint32_t>(settings.steam.user.steamId & 0xFFFFFFFF);
const auto identity = account ^ (static_cast<std::uint32_t>(call) * kLobbyCallStride);
const std::uint64_t lobby = kLobbySteamIdPrefix | identity;
```

**XOR against an odd stride, not ADD, and the reason is worth keeping**: the two
authored accounts differ by exactly 1 and consecutive call numbers differ by
exactly 1, so `account + call` collides across machines on the first pair it
sees (mac call 3 == rig call 2). Verified offline: 78/78 distinct over two
machines x 39 calls. Predicted ids -

```
mac  call 2 -> 0x010900000CC46DB7    rig  call 2 -> 0x010900000CC46DB4
mac  call 3 -> 0x01090000EA0CF3EE    rig  call 3 -> 0x01090000EA0CF3ED
```

Deployed: DLL `63f5fbda6b83e0b4` on BOTH machines, hash-asserted after copy on
the machine that runs it, with the literals `stage=lobby_create` and
`account=0x%08X` confirmed present in each deployed file. The rig push went over
the existing ssh master with no password. Server exe UNCHANGED
(`1991b3da6f518165`) - the steam shim compiles into the DLL only.
Fork @ `dd6ac7d`.

### WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, STATED PLAINLY

Gate [27] `target_fireteam_privacy_is_not_closed` is untouched. The session is
created `as_private=1` with `priv=0/pub=12/friend-only=1`, and the mac's
refusal was reason 5 `privacy-mode`. **The predicted outcome of the next boot is
therefore that the rig STOPS saying reason 1 and starts saying reason 5** - both
machines converging on the privacy gate. That is a falsifiable prediction and it
is the whole point of changing one variable.

Also untouched, and still on the critical path: no group TARGET is ever set, and
`send_rendezvous` is a log-only sink (20.82 links 6-8).

### DO-NOT
- Do not add the call number to the account id. See the collision note above;
  the naive form fails on the exact pair of machines we own.
- Do not read the gate-table/reason-enum correspondence as verified. It is an
  inference from meaning; the evaluator itself was not located (a direct xref
  scan of the table returned only mid-string false positives, so the consumer is
  likely virtualised).

Capture: `RE_output/captures/20260826_191159_p2-56_peer_row/` - now complete
