### FIRST: THE RIG CONFIRMS THE MAC, SYMMETRICALLY

```
rig  t=51203  Could not find tracking data for peer '1', for activity membership
              '1', machine id 'E9:73:75:DB:5F:AA:526F', adding it to fixup the
              situation
rig  t=51203  [AC PRIVATE CURRENT CON-Y EST-Y AH->9eaa300100200004 MEM-1]
              Sending peer-reservation release
```

`E9:73:75:DB:5F:AA:52:6F` is the MAC's member key `0x6F52AA5FDB7573E9`
byte-reversed. **Both machines fail the identical lookup on the other's key.**
20.81 called the reason-enum asymmetry (1 vs 5) a reporting difference rather
than a mechanism difference; that was an inference and it is now VERIFIED BY
EXECUTION. One failure, two enum values.

### THE CHAIN, EVERY LINK MARKED (lesson 16)

| # | link | mark |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | we publish a peer in the type-12 roster, named by machine id | verified-by-execution |
| 2 | the client parses and accepts the body | verified-by-execution (`result=ok accepted=1`) |
| 3 | it resolves that machine id against **tracking data** | verified-by-execution (the log line names the id) |
| 4 | tracking data comes from **managed-session** membership + player-properties | verified-by-reading the logs; the only producer of per-machine records in evidence |
| 5 | the managed session holds ONE member | verified-by-execution |
| 6 | to gain a member, a client must TARGET and JOIN another's session | verified-by-reading (the client's own gate table, below) |
| 7 | **no target is ever set and no join is ever attempted** | verified-by-execution (`send_rendezvous`=0, both machines, every boot) |
| 8 | nothing in our server ever names another client's session as joinable | verified-by-reading our tree |

Link 7 is the discovery of this pass. We have spent six boots asking why the
client REFUSES a peer. It refuses because it has never been introduced - and
**it never even tries to make contact.** Zero rendezvous, zero P2P sends, zero
join attempts, on both machines, in every capture we hold.

### WHAT THE MANAGED SESSION ACTUALLY IS

```
[#0] Creating managed session as host. session_type=fireteam,
     session_class=system-link-demonware, private_slots=0, ...
[#0] Adding player [xuid=0x110000130aa9ec5][online-enabled=1][as_private=1]
[#0] Modifying slot count from priv=0/pub=0/friend-only=0
                            to priv=0/pub=12/friend-only=1
[#0][{74590868828323842}-B1F73462:B2ED6203-steamid:76561198776753861#bd35816eee627a31]
```

- **It is a Demonware "system-link" session**, not a bare Steam lobby. The
  Demonware lineage AGENTS.md names is the right frame for this layer.
- **It has vacancy**: 12 public slots plus 1 friend-only. Nothing is full.
- **It is addressed by SteamNetworkingIdentity** - `steamid:<id>#<token>` - the
  same form as the matchmaking descriptor (20.78 gap 1).
- **`{74590868828323842}` = 0x109000000000002**, the Steam lobby id our shim
  invented. So the lobby id IS the managed session's id, confirming the
  container identification in 20.79 gap 1 from a second direction.
- The Steam identity IS wired here: `xuid=0x110000130aa9ec5` matches
  `ev=steamnet stage=identity result=resolved id=0x0110000130AA9EC5`.
- Slot #0 = fireteam, slot #1 = posse, both created locally, both solo.

Caveat recorded: `networking:logic:session: local user 0 set platform account
id=**unknown platform account id**`. The platform account does not fully resolve
at this layer even though the xuid does. Unexplained; may or may not matter.

### THE CLIENT'S OWN JOIN-GATE TABLE (new artifact, .rdata 0x141C84768+)

A contiguous string run, read straight out of the image - the same shape as the
peer-link failure enum that has paid off twice:

```
target_public_sessions_have_vacancy
target_activity_session_has_vacancy
target_fireteam_session_has_vacancy_for_activity_type
target_fireteam_session_has_vacancy_for_orbit
target_fireteam_join_not_closed_in_matchmaking
target_fireteam_join_not_closed_loading
target_fireteam_join_not_closed_lifetime_sensor
target_fireteam_join_not_closed_script
target_activity_is_possible_for_fireteam
target_activity_is_unlocked_for_fireteam
target_activity_is_possible
target_activity_is_unlocked
target_activity_loadout_requirements_not_met
target_activity_is_synchronizing
have_permission_to_join_friends_only_fireteam
have_permission_to_join_invite_only_fireteam
have_permission_to_join_clan_and_friends_only_fireteam
target_fireteam_join_not_closed_solo_mode
```

Every one is a predicate about a **TARGET**. This is the checklist a join runs
before it proceeds - and it is dead code for us, because no target is ever set.
When one is, this table is the map of what will refuse us next, and three of its
entries (`friends_only`, `invite_only`, `clan_and_friends_only`) are privacy
predicates that our stubbed Steam friends interface cannot satisfy - the client
calls Friends slots 3 (count), 5 (persona state) and 43 (unknown predicate)
three times each per boot and gets our silent stub every time.

### TWO LEADS KILLED THIS PASS - both would have cost a boot

1. **`client.region_private` is NOT the cause of PRIVATE.** The hook forces
   `slice_set_is_public` to false, and its own comment explains the trade
   ("a public region holds its slice-set switch until a public activity host
   connects... it must load solo"). It looked like the whole answer. **It never
   ran.** Its decision path emits `result=forced` or `result=public` with a
   budget of 8, and BOTH clients emitted ZERO such lines - only the install line
   `stage=region result=ok`. The filtered call site was never reached this boot,
   so the setting had no effect and PRIVATE comes from elsewhere.
   **HYGIENE BUG FOUND WHILE CHECKING**: the Mac's settings carry
   `"region_private": true`; the rig's settings.json **has no such key at all**
   and therefore defaults false. The two machines have been differently
   configured on this switch. Irrelevant this boot because the hook never fired,
   but it must not stay divergent.
2. **Web-service opcodes 701/702 are not the fireteam seam.** They are the two
   highest-volume calls of the boot (34 and 29) and have no codec in our tree,
   which made them look like a lesson-17 gap. `web_service_runtime.cpp:137`
   already tags them: "the 701/702 subclass-swap". Prior work, not a new lead.

### HOW WE GET PAST IT - the fork, stated honestly

The blocker is **the directory at the session layer**: something must hand
client B the fact that client A's fireteam session exists, its id
(`8954314B:5E1013E6`), and its address (`steamid:76561198776753862#...`), as a
join TARGET. Two ways:

**(A) FIND THE RETAIL MECHANISM.** In retail the fireteam is formed through the
Bungie backend before the activity - that is why the activity roster assumes the
peer is already known. Census the BAP/web services for the one that carries a
fireteam join target. Honest cost: this is RE with no located anchor yet, and
20.80 established we cannot yet read arbitrary message schemas (only type 12's
key is known). Slow, correct, and it teaches us the real protocol.

**(B) FABRICATE THE TARGET IN THE SHIM.** Our server already knows both
sessions' ids and steamid addresses - the clients publish them in
`advertisement_update` and we log them. The client DLL could be handed the
foreign session as a join target directly, skipping retail discovery entirely.
This is the same move Lane V designed for the friends roster (20.46) and never
built. Faster, in the established spirit of the shim, and it lands on the
`target_*` gate table above - which is exactly the kind of failure we can read.

**Behind BOTH sits the transport** (20.79 gap 2, unchanged): once a target
exists, the join travels by Steam P2P, `send_rendezvous` is a log-only sink, and
the inbound callback cannot be delivered through a 32-byte ring. That work is
required for either path and is now firmly on the critical path rather than
speculative.

### NOT READY FOR A BOOT, AND SAYING SO

There is nothing to boot yet. `send_rendezvous`=0 means no instrument on the
current build can produce new information - the next fact requires code that
sets a join target, not another run of the code we have. The pre-boot
checklist's item 2 cannot be satisfied: there is no line that would appear
whatever the outcome, because the outcome is already known to be silence.

### DO-NOT
- Do not spend another boot on the activity/membership plane. Links 1-3 are
  verified and the gate is at link 7.
- Do not re-open `region_private` as a cause without first proving its decision
  line fires.
- Do not treat 701/702 as unexplored.
- Do not read the reason-enum asymmetry as two mechanisms. One lookup, two
  labels, now verified on both machines.
## 20.81 BOOT #10 (p2(56)): THE PEER ROW WAS DELIVERED, THE CLIENT ACCEPTED AND
## PARSED IT, READ THE PEER OUT OF IT - AND REFUSED HIM 2 ms LATER BECAUSE IT
## HAS NO **TRACKING DATA** FOR HIM. THE DELIVERY-GAP THEORY IS REFUTED BY
## EXECUTION. THE REAL GATE IS NAMED FOR THE FIRST TIME.
## (2026-08-26 ~19:20)

Capture: `RE_output/captures/20260826_191159_p2-56_peer_row/`
(server 5,274 lines; mac client 1,531). Server exe `1991b3da6f518165`, fork
`f0059b5`, pin 0 / cap 2. **Rig client log NOT captured** (needs the ssh
password); every rig-side claim below is read from the SERVER's view of it.
Grep with `-a`.

### THE FIX IS PROVEN BY EXECUTION

```
encode_fail        0      (boot #9: 4 out of 4)
body_capture       4      (boot #9: 0)
type-12 body=4095  4      peer row + BOTH endpoints - FIRST TIME EVER
peer_advert result=built ... own_citizen=1 peer_citizen=1   x7
```

The new `stage=peer_advert` instrument also paid for itself immediately,
separating three causes that were previously one silence: `built` (7),
`same_region` (2), `build_failed` (1). Without it the `body=3882` bodies at
t=493482 and t=531306 would have been unattributable.

### THE CAUSAL CHAIN, AT MILLISECOND RESOLUTION

The Mac's own log, correlated to the server by the framed push size (4095 body =
4112 framed) so no clock arithmetic is involved:

```
t=369063  handle_message push type=12 size=4112 result=ok accepted=1
t=369065  world_controller:activity: Could not find tracking data for peer '1',
          for activity membership '5', machine id 'E6:22:D0:F7:38:83:6C:84'
t=369066  [AC PRIVATE CURRENT CON-Y EST-Y AH->9eaa300100200001 MEM-5]
          Sending peer-reservation release
t=369066  ... Sent peer-reservation release
t=369095  world_controller:slice_set_transition_manager: Processing new PAH
          tabulated region data in membership update '5'.
t=379115  ... Acknowledged membership '5'.
```

Every step of that is new information.

1. **`result=ok accepted=1`.** The client PARSES the four-field, two-descriptor,
   peer-bearing body. The wire shape is not the problem and can be retired as a
   suspect.
2. **It READS our peer row.** It names `peer '1'` and prints the rig's key as a
   machine id - `E6:22:D0:F7:38:83:6C:84` is the byte-reversal of
   `0x846C8338F7D022E6`, the rig's member key, exactly as we published it.
3. **It refuses on a lookup, not on content.** The peer is rejected because the
   client cannot find **tracking data** for that machine id in a table it keeps
   elsewhere. Two milliseconds later it releases the reservation.
4. **It still processes our region block** and acknowledges the revision. The
   body is not discarded; only the peer is.

### THE DELIVERY-GAP THEORY (20.74.4) IS REFUTED

That entry's claim was: the peer arrives with no joinable endpoint, so the host
fixup-releases it. We delivered both endpoints this boot - `peer_citizen=1`,
`body=4095`, regions 48 and 56 in separate records - and **the release fired
anyway, 2 ms after the body, naming a lookup failure and not an address.**

Recorded as refuted-by-execution. p2(55)/p2(56) are still correct and stay in
(they fix three real defects and the body now encodes), but the theory that
motivated p2(54) is dead.

### THE REASON ASYMMETRY SURVIVES - AND NOW IT MEANS SOMETHING

Both type-14 payloads decoded (`{u64 reason BE, u64 memberKey LE}`):

```
t=481320  handle=...0004 (rig)  0000000000000001 E97375DB5FAA526F
          reason 1 = tried-to-join-self,  key = 0x6F52AA5FDB7573E9 = THE MAC
t=483011  handle=...0001 (mac)  0000000000000005 E622D0F738836C84
          reason 5 = privacy-mode,        key = 0x846C8338F7D022E6 = THE RIG
```

Same split as boot #8 - but boot #8's was measured against a peer whose account
had collapsed onto the other's. This one is measured with keys AND accounts
separated, endpoints delivered, and the body accepted. **The asymmetry is real
and it is not an artifact.** The two clients fail the same lookup and report it
through two different enum values.

### THE REAL GATE, NAMED

`world_controller:activity` resolves a peer through a **tracking table keyed by
machine id**, and the membership message does not populate it. Something else
does, and on both machines that something is empty:

```
networking:session:membership: [fireteam:B1F73462:B2ED6203] player-properties
                               accepted for player #0
networking:session:membership: [posse:599A4769:CFCB1502]    player-properties
                               accepted for player #0
```

**Player #0 only, on both machines, all boot.** The posse and fireteam sessions
each hold exactly one player. 20.72 recorded that they were solo and read it as
a symptom; this boot shows it is the CAUSE. The activity-membership roster and
the posse/fireteam session are two different registries, and the client will not
admit a peer into the first unless it already knows him from the second.

This supersedes gap 5's framing. The host/guest question (types 8 -> 9/10) is
still open and still real, but it now sits BEHIND a nearer gate: a peer we name
in the activity roster is not a peer the client has ever heard of.

### THE MAC'S BLACK SCREEN - CORRELATION AND A NAMED SUSPECT, NOT A CAUSE

The user reported the Mac going black as the rig landed. The rig's
`join_result` is server t=476260 and the Mac's peer body is client t=369063
(server t=482971), so the window matches.

**The Mac did not crash.** The process stayed alive and kept logging network
traffic for ~3 more minutes (last line client t=554720). Only the retail-side
line density collapsed. This is a renderer/present hang with a live sim thread,
not a lockup.

**PRIME SUSPECT, and it is checkable.** Compare the two region-data updates:

```
t=138080  Processing new PAH tabulated region data in membership update '4'.
t=138080  Region 'PRV56.56 (HASH: 0x82ae25b4)' -- PAH tabulated data NOW
          INCLUDES US.
...
t=369095  Processing new PAH tabulated region data in membership update '5'.
          <- NO "includes us" line follows. No transition starts.
```

Update 4 (solo, one advertisement) put the client in its own region. Update 5
(two advertisements, the peer's in a DIFFERENT region record) processed the same
way and **did not conclude that the client is in its region**. Publishing a
second region record is the only thing that changed between them.

Marked INFERRED. It is a correlation with a mechanism, not a proven cause, and
the boot brief's graphics-delta section said "zero new models", which was true
and is not what happened - the suspected fault is region bookkeeping, not shader
compilation. **The next peer boot must pin this**: publish the peer row WITHOUT
the peer's advertisement (`same_region` already produces exactly that body,
3967) and see whether the "includes us" line returns.

### WHAT STAYS TRUE

- `peers valid: 0x1` in every client dump. No peer ever entered the table.
- Both clients kept working: acks at revisions 4, 5, 6, 7; no freeze of the
  networking stack; the rig ran to the end.
- Retry cap 2 behaved exactly as designed - two unacked peer bodies per session,
  then withdrawal, then a solo body the client acknowledged. No storm.

### NEXT, in order

1. **Find what populates the peer tracking table.** It is the nearest gate and
   everything else is behind it. The posse/fireteam session
   (`networking:session:membership`, ids `fireteam:B1F73462:B2ED6203` /
   `posse:599A4769:CFCB1502`) is where a second player would have to appear.
   This is a fresh RE target and it is NOT the activity plane we have been
   working for six boots.
2. **Pin the black screen** with the no-peer-advertisement body (free - the
   `same_region` path already builds it).
3. Types 8 -> 9/10 and the schema-key xref lane stay open but drop below (1).

### DO-NOT
- Do not re-argue the wire shape of type 12. `result=ok accepted=1` settles it.
- Do not resume the delivery-gap line. Endpoints were delivered; nothing changed.
- Do not read the reason asymmetry as noise. It is reproducible across two boots
  under different conditions.
## 20.80 p2(55) LANDED: THE THREE p2(54) DEFECTS ARE FIXED AND THE GATE NOW
## CATCHES THEM. THE TYPE->KEY HUNT IS OPEN - ONE HYPOTHESIS REFUTED, THE
## DESCRIPTOR ARRAY LOCATED, THE ROUTE NAMED. (2026-08-26 ~19:05)

### PART 1 - p2(55), the fix (task closed)

Fork `upstream-gameplay-scoped` @ `5e475cf`. Built clean.
  server exe `6db65e1671458158`   steam_api64.dll `6e461d3901363c71`

1. **`advertisement_count(snapshot)`** is new in `replicate_membership.h` and is
   now the ONLY place the "one descriptor per region record" rule is written.
   Both `meaningful_bit_count()` and `region_block_end_bit()` read it, so the
   two can no longer disagree with the writer or with each other. It returns 2
   only when both advertisements are present AND name different regions.
2. **The `wire.peerCitizen.present` override is deleted**
   (activity_membership_push.cpp). `build_advertisement`'s own verdict stands.
3. **The peer advertisement is built at the PEER's region**
   (`effective_region(peerBinding.sessionId)`), and the same-region case is
   skipped outright rather than resolved. That makes the `region_machine_id`
   key collision - which retired the local host session on every peer push -
   unreachable by construction, not merely unlikely. Whose endpoint belongs in
   a SHARED bubble is the host/guest question (gap 5) and is deliberately not
   guessed at here.

**THE GATE, and it was seen to fail first.** `run_membership_wire_test` grows
from 2 cases to 6, four of which populate descriptors:

```
solo               bytes=3754   solo_citizen  bytes=3882
peer               bytes=3839   peer_citizen  bytes=3967
peer_two_adverts   bytes=4095   peer_shared_region bytes=3967
```

With the p2(54) bug reinstated in both size functions and rebuilt, the gate
reports exactly the production failure, offline:

```
ev=wire_test stage=check result=fail case=peer_two_adverts what=bit_count
              got=31729 want=32753 descriptors=2
ev=wire_test stage=check result=fail case=peer_two_adverts what=encode_refused
ev=wire_test stage=done result=fail failures=1
```

`got=31729` is 30,032 + 673 + 1,024 - the arithmetic 20.78 predicted from
reading, now reproduced by execution. With the fix: `result=ok failures=0`.
The case also carries an INDEPENDENT descriptor count passed in by the caller,
because a size rule checked against itself passes while it is wrong - which is
precisely how p2(54) shipped.

Deploy gates re-run on the staged candidate from `RE_output/s1_accept`:
`--cache-check`, `--membership-sweep-test`, `--membership-wire-test`,
`--local-account-test` all rc=0. `--s1-test`, `--equip-diff` and
`--selection-version-test` return rc=1 **before the cache restamp**, which is
deploy step 3 and precedes the gates in `deploy_p2d6_gameplay.sh`; the same
three pass on the currently-deployed exe. NOT DEPLOYED - the deploy stops the
live server and drops clients, so it belongs to the boot window.

### PART 2 - the type->key hunt: refuted, and what stands

**THE HYPOTHESIS, AND ITS REFUTATION.** `0x141FA4180` - the global 20.61 read
the type-12 key through - sits inside a stride-8 array of pointers to 0x20-byte
schema-descriptor objects. Taking base = `0x141FA4180 - 12*8` produced a clean
table of 59 keys, descending by 1 with a handful of outliers at exactly the
semantically distinct types (17, 22, 23, 26). It looked convincing.

It is wrong, and it is wrong in the way AGENTS.md already names: **the base was
FITTED on type 12 and then "verified" on type 12.** The independent check
refutes it outright -

```
0x808086AB  ("type 9")   -> 1 field, a bare 32-bit scalar
0x80808680  ("type 54")  -> 1 field, a bare 32-bit scalar
0x808086A9  ("type 11")  -> the 31-bit ARRAY ELEMENT already documented as
                            type 12's member roster (msg12-schema-decoded.md)
```

A descending block of keys will produce a plausible table from ANY base. The
block around `0x141FA4180` is type 12's own schema FAMILY, not a per-type index.

**WHAT STANDS, all verified-by-execution against the dump:**

1. **The descriptor object holds its packed key at `+0x00`.** This is a
   different structure from the registry node, which holds its self-key at
   `+0x08` (W1 CLAIM 1). Reading `+0x08` on a descriptor object returns a
   pointer's low half and looks like a garbage key - it cost this session one
   false refutation before the layout was read out properly.
2. **The descriptor array is located and enumerable**: `0x7FF6443B4EE0` ..
   `0x7FF6443D2560`, stride `0x20`, **3,765 entries, all keys unique**, in the
   `0x8080xxxx` band. (Module base in `dump_healthy_inproc.dmp` is
   `0x7FF641BF0000`, not the unpacked exe's `0x7FF6AF7F0000` - they are
   different captures and the bases are not interchangeable.)
3. **The unpacked exe cannot answer these questions.** `.data` holds the
   pointers but the heap they address is absent from
   `destiny2_unpacked_full.exe`; every read into a descriptor object returns
   None there. The minidump is required. A static-image census of this
   structure will silently produce nulls.
4. **A shape classifier now exists with an oracle**:
   `RE_scripts/schema_root_census.py` resolves every descriptor to
   (struct|array, field count, presence width) and refuses to print results
   unless `0x808086A8` comes back `('struct', 9, 1000)`.

**WHAT THE SHAPE CENSUS DOES NOT SHOW.** It finds 60 structs with >=5 fields
against the client's 59 message types, which is tempting and worth nothing: the
threshold is what produces the number. >=4 fields gives 90 and >=6 gives 43.
Recorded explicitly so nobody re-derives the coincidence and calls it a mapping.

**THE ROUTE, named but not walked.** Type 12's key was found by xref - a handler
reading its descriptor pointer out of a named `.data` global. That generalises:
each message type's parse/encode path reads one `.data` slot in the pointer
array, so disassembling the per-type handlers and recording which slot each one
touches pins type -> descriptor -> key, one type at a time, with no fitting.
`RE_scripts/disasm_fn.py` is the tool. Types 9, 10 and 54 are the three worth
doing first; 17 and 45 follow.

### CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PLAN

**Gap 4 (fill type-54 `bubble_host_table`) is BLOCKED on this**, and that is a
change from 20.79, which called it the cheapest lever on the grounds that its
wire slot is already proven. The slot is proven; the CONTENT still needs the
schema, and the schema needs the key. It is not cheap until the xref lane runs.
Same for gap 5's 9/10 pair. Neither is guessable - the trailing-field episode is
what guessing a wire layout costs.

### DO-NOT
- Do not resurrect the type-indexed-array reading of `0x141FA4180`. It is
  refuted by three independent shape checks.
- Do not read a descriptor object's key at `+0x08`; that offset belongs to the
  registry node, not to this structure.
- Do not run any descriptor census against `destiny2_unpacked_full.exe`. The
  heap is not in it and every answer will be a null.
- Do not count "roots by field-count threshold" as a type mapping.
## 20.79 THE GAP CENSUS BEYOND p2(54): SIX MISSING PIECES, IN DEPENDENCY ORDER.
## THE INTRODUCTION PATH IS BROKEN IN THREE INDEPENDENT PLACES AND THE
## DIRECTORY WORK TOUCHES NONE OF THEM. (2026-08-26 ~18:40)

Prompted by "beyond the fix, what else is missing". Every line below is marked
per lesson 16. Fixing 20.78's three defects makes the peer body ENCODE; this
entry is what stands between an encoded body and two guardians in one tower.

### GAP 1 - THE STEAM LOBBY IS THE INSTANCE CONTAINER, AND OUR SHIM GIVES BOTH
### CLIENTS THE SAME MADE-UP LOBBY [verified-by-execution]

New this session, from the boot #9 client logs. Both machines:

```
ev=steamnet stage=interface_acquired version=SteamNetworkingSocketsSerialized003
ev=steamnet seq=1 stage=get_certificate      result=0
ev=steamnet seq=2 stage=relay_ticket_count   result=0
ev=steamnet seq=1 stage=lobby_create type=3 max=32 result=2 lobby=0x0109000000000002
ev=steamnet seq=2 stage=lobby_create type=3 max=32 result=3 lobby=0x0109000000000003
```

Three facts fall out of those five lines.

1. **`max=32`.** The client creates its lobby with a 32-member cap - the same 32
   the type-12 roster declares (0x808086A9, 32 fixed slots, 20.62). The lobby
   and the membership roster are two views of one container. That is corroboration
   from a completely independent surface that the roster's shape was right.
2. **The lobby id IS the published session id.** `create_lobby` builds
   `kLobbySteamIdPrefix | next_api_call()` (steam/interfaces/methods/matchmaking.cpp:77),
   and `ev=matchmaking stage=descriptor where=publish` carries
   `session=0x0109000000000003` on BOTH machines. The advertisement names the
   lobby.
3. **`next_api_call()` is a per-process counter**, so both machines invent the
   SAME two ids. Each client believes it owns lobby `...0002` and `...0003`.
   There is no cross-machine lobby registry anywhere in the shim.

So the container both clients must share is, today, two disjoint local fictions
wearing the same number.

**And the client has no Steam networking credentials**: `get_certificate=0`,
`relay_ticket_count=0`, which is exactly why every published descriptor reads
`routable=0`.

### GAP 2 - THE INTRODUCTION PATH IS BROKEN IN THREE PLACES [verified-by-reading]

20.78 named the shape (we built the directory, not the introduction). Here is
the introduction path itself, end to end, with every link marked:

| link | state |
|---|---|
| client publishes `steamid:<id>#<token>` in its advertisement | **works** [verified-by-execution] |
| outbound `send_rendezvous` | **log-only sink.** `serialized_send_rendezvous` (serialized_networking.cpp:146) prints a line, dumps the blob, and returns. It sends nothing anywhere. [verified-by-reading] |
| inbound `RecvP2PRendezvous_t` (528 B) | **cannot be delivered.** `kEventPayloadCapacity = 32` (steam/runtime/callbacks/callback_registry.h:22) - still 32, unchanged since Lane T priced it. [verified-by-reading] |
| SDR credentials | **absent.** certificate 0, relay tickets 0. [verified-by-execution] |

Three independent breaks. None of the six peer boots touched any of them. This
is the honest reason `send_rendezvous` has "never fired": there has never been a
path for it to fire ON, and if it fired the bytes would go to a log file.

### GAP 3 - THE CLIENT ASKS FOR PEERS ONCE PER BOOT, AT THE WORST MOMENT
### [verified-by-execution]

`kind=session_search` appears exactly ONCE in the boot #9 server log, at
t=1914549 - **143 seconds before the second client sent its first
`configuration` request** (t=2057986). Answered `served_descriptor=0
encoded=1 bytes=2`: an empty list, which was the correct answer, because nothing
foreign existed yet.

Everything after that is `advertisement_update` (13 of them). The client
publishes continuously and asks once. We have no mechanism that makes it ask
again, and 20.44 already proved a served result sits filed without being acted
on. Whatever re-opens matchmaking is not a message we answer - it is a client
state transition (`setup:matchmaking`, step 17) that runs once per activity
setup.

**This is why "boot the Mac first, then the rig" can never work as a sequence.**
The searcher must be the SECOND client, and by the time the second client exists
the first has already searched and stopped.

### GAP 4 - THREE DIRECTORY CHANNELS, ALL NAMING NOBODY [verified-by-reading]

We have now built or inherited three separate channels whose job is to say who
is in the instance, and all three are empty or self-only:

1. **svc-43 `session_search`** - answered empty (gap 3).
2. **type-12 membership roster** - the peer row, which has never successfully
   shipped (20.78).
3. **type-54 `bubble_host_table`** - and this one is new to the record:
   `kEmptyBubbleHostState` is a **single zero byte**
   (activity_message_push.cpp:28), pushed **once, inside the join burst**
   (line 106), and **never again**. Two push sites exist in the whole tree and
   both are that one call. The client received exactly one type-54 in boot #9.

Channel 3 is the one that names which bubble is hosted by whom, and the two
players were standing in different bubbles all boot (regions oscillating between
48 and 56 on both sessions). We have never put a single row in it. Per lesson 17
that is a missing requirement, not a spare - and unlike types 9/10/17/45, this
one we already EMIT, so the wire slot is proven and only the content is absent.

### GAP 5 - THE UNANSWERED REQUEST/RESPONSE PAIRS ARE STILL UNANSWERED
### [verified-by-reading, current as of this session]

`protocol-census-2026-08-25.md` stands unchanged; re-verified against
`activity_message_route.cpp` today. `kAcceptedMessages` still carries 12 rows
and its comment still claims they "carry no work for this host".

| request | our handling | its response | our handling |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8 `request_activity_host` | ACCEPT-NOOP | 9 / 10 | never sent |
| 13 `request_peer_reservation` | logged only (p2(48b)) | grant, 45 `reservations_failed` | never sent |
| 16 `keepalive_request` | dispatch | 17 `keepalive_response` | never sent |

**8 -> 9/10 is the guest-role mechanism the last three boot briefs kept calling
for.** With two players in one instance somebody must be told to host the
activity and somebody must be told not to. We accept the ask and drop it.

### GAP 6 - THE SCHEMA LEVER'S INPUT IS MISSING, AND STATE OVERSTATED IT
### [verified-by-reading]

STATE has claimed since 20.61 that "any other message type is now one command
away" via `schema_walk.py --tree <key>`. **The walker works; the keys do not
exist.** Type 12's key `0x808086A8` was obtained from a specific pointer chain -
`mov r9d,[rax]` @ 0x1404F73D9 off global 0x141FA4180, the membership
orchestrator's own chain (msg12-schema-decoded.md:22). No equivalent chain has
been found for types 9, 10, 17, 45 or 54, so none of their keys is known.

The registry is fully enumerable (6,765 self-verifying nodes, buckets
1024-1028), and `schema_walk.py --find <bits>` searches by wire width, so this
is bounded work - but it is WORK, not a command. Correcting the claim because a
plan built on "one command away" will budget wrong.

### THE DEPENDENCY ORDER

Gaps 3 and 5 gate everything: if the client only asks once, and nobody is ever
told to host, then a correct roster arrives at a client with nothing to do about
it. Gap 4 is the cheapest unexplored lever (proven wire slot, empty content).
Gaps 1 and 2 are the transport, and they are the expensive half - but they are
also the half that has never been touched, and gap 1's `max=32` is the first
positive evidence in weeks that we have been reading the right container.

Suggested order, all of it offline until the last step:

1. 20.78's three defects + the wire-gate case. Makes a peer body encodable.
2. Fill type-54 `bubble_host_table` (gap 4). Proven slot, no schema hunt needed
   beyond its own layout, and it speaks directly to the host/guest question.
3. Answer 8 -> 9/10 (gap 5). Needs the schema; start the key hunt (gap 6) here,
   width-first, since 9/10 are the guest-role pair.
4. Decide gap 1/2 (the transport) against the above with real numbers. A
   cross-machine lobby registry in the shim is a small, well-understood piece of
   work compared to the SDR path, and gap 1 says the lobby is the container.

### DO-NOT
- Do not describe the schema walker as "one command away" again (gap 6).
- Do not plan a boot sequence that depends on the second client searching
  (gap 3) until something re-opens matchmaking.
- Do not treat `send_rendezvous` silence as evidence about the client. It is a
  log-only sink with no transport behind it (gap 2).
## 20.78 WHAT THE BOOT ACTUALLY MEASURED, AND THE THREE DEFECTS IN p2(54)
## THAT MADE IT UNMEASURABLE. THE ENCODER REFUSED EVERY PEER-BEARING BODY.
## (2026-08-26 ~18:22, validation pass over the boot #9 capture)

Capture read end to end: `RE_output/captures/20260826_175432_peer_advert_test/`
(server_sunrise.log 3,179 ev-lines; mac 1,321; rig 5,323 / rig_now 11,423).
NOTE FOR EVERY GREP ON THESE FILES: they contain NUL bytes, so plain `grep`
reports "binary file matches" and returns nothing. **Use `grep -a`.** A whole
census can read as zero otherwise - lesson 13 with a new face.

### THE HEADLINE FACT: ZERO PEER-BEARING TYPE-12 BODIES WERE TRANSMITTED

Four peer inclusions were attempted. All four were immediately refused by the
encoder:

```
t=2064208 stage=membership_peer result=included key=0x6F52AA5FDB7573E9 ... peer_row=1
t=2064208 stage=membership result=encode_fail
t=2065625 stage=membership_peer result=included key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 ... peer_row=1
t=2065625 stage=membership result=encode_fail
t=2074304 stage=membership_peer result=included key=0x6F52AA5FDB7573E9 ... peer_row=1
t=2074304 stage=membership result=encode_fail
t=2075667 stage=membership_peer result=included key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 ... peer_row=1
t=2075667 stage=membership result=encode_fail
```

Four inclusions, four `encode_fail`, **zero exceptions**. Corroborated three
ways, each independent of the others:

1. **Every type-12 that shipped is a SOLO body.** 15 pushes, all `body=3882`
   except one `body=3754`. 3,882 = the p2(47) solo body with one citizen
   descriptor; a peer-bearing body must be at least 673 bits (85 bytes) larger
   and none is.
2. **`stage=body_capture` fired ZERO times** (grep -ac = 0). That instrument is
   gated on `encoded && snapshot.peerPresent` (activity_membership_push.cpp:378)
   and was 20.76's own expected-line #1. Its silence is the encoder's refusal
   seen from the other side.
3. **`peers valid: 0x1`** in all 13 client membership dumps on both machines.
   Never 0x3. No peer row ever reached either client.

### THEREFORE STATE's BOOT #9 HEADLINE IS VOID (see 20.77)

The acked revision 5 STATE cites is the **post-withdrawal solo body**:

```
t=2085700 membership_peer result=withdrawn variant=packed_masks unacked=2 session=...105
t=2085700 push result=ok type=12 soid=0x9EAA300100200001 body=3882   <- solo
t=2085741 membership_ack result=ok revision=5                        <- the "41 ms" ack
```

The 41 ms is real and the ack is real. The body it acknowledges carries no peer.
Revision 5 had already been riding keepalives since t=2065626, unchanged through
t=2080683, because every attempt to advance it past a peer body was refused.

"Zero fixups, zero type-14s, no privacy-mode" is likewise true and likewise
means nothing: those are the client's reactions to a foreign row, and no foreign
row was delivered. **A null from an input that was never sent.** This is
AGENTS.md lesson 13 for the third time in this project, and the second time this
week - and it is exactly the shape the PRE-BOOT CHECKLIST's item 3 (the ABSENCE
negative) exists to catch. The checklist was not applied to the READ.

### DEFECT 1 (the direct cause) - the bit accounting never learned about peerCitizen

`replicate_membership.h` `meaningful_bit_count()`:

```cpp
std::size_t bits = kMeaningfulBitCount;
if (snapshot.peerPresent)     bits += kPeerRowExtraBits;
if (snapshot.citizen.present) bits += kDescriptorBitCount;   // <- peerCitizen absent
return bits;
```

`region_block_end_bit()` has the identical omission. p2(54) added a SECOND
1,024-bit descriptor to the region writer
(activity_membership_region_writer.cpp:51-55, 76-80) and updated neither size
function. The writer is handed `output.first(encoded_size(snapshot))`, so it
runs out of buffer 1,024 bits early; `write_region_block`'s own
`writer.bit_count() == region_block_end_bit(snapshot)` assertion (line 135)
fails on the same axis. `encode_replicate_membership` returns false. Every time,
deterministically, for any body with a peer row and a peer citizen.

### DEFECT 2 (what makes defect 1 fire even though the peer build FAILED)

`build_advertisement` for the peer **skips**, and the push code overrides its
verdict:

```
t=2075667 ev=gameplay stage=advertise result=ok   region=56 ambassador_slot=1  <- local
t=2075667 ev=gameplay stage=advertise result=skip reason=no_host_session region=56  <- peer
```

(`ambassador_slot(localMemberSlot)` returns 1 for slot 0 and 0 for slot 1, so
the `ok` line is provably the LOCAL call and the `skip` the peer call. Every
peer timestamp carries this pair.)

`build_candidate` opens with `candidate = {}` and returns on skip, so
`peerCitizen` comes back fully zeroed - `present=false`, **`regionIndex=0`**,
descriptor all-zero. Then activity_membership_push.cpp:258-259:

```cpp
wire.peerCitizen = peerCitizen;
wire.peerCitizen.present = peerSessionId != 0;   // overrides the builder's failure
```

`peerSessionId` is set from `session_binding_for_member`, which succeeded. So a
**zeroed advertisement is marked present with regionIndex 0**, the region writer
matches it at bubble 0 (`0 == 0`), and writes 128 zero bytes plus a zero
onlineSessionId there - the second descriptor that overflows the buffer. Had
this body ever encoded, it would have advertised a null join endpoint in region
0 to a client standing in region 48 or 56.

### DEFECT 3 (the design error under both) - one host-session row, two claimants

The peer's advertisement is requested at the SAME key as the local one:

- `region_machine_id(regionIndex)` = `region_identity(endpoint::identity().machineId, regionIndex)`
  - a function of **our machine id and the region index only**.
- Both calls pass `region.index` = `planned_region(mutation, sessionId)` - **this
  session's** region, not the peer's.

So `request_host_session` is called twice with one groupSessionId and two
different `source` bindings. The local call retains and the push code releases
its generation immediately (line 158-160), so by the peer call `references == 0`
and the else-branch **retires the row it just built and re-binds it to the peer's
source**, returning `pending` -> `Skip::noHostSession`. The log shows exactly
that churn, timestamp-aligned to every peer attempt:

```
t=2064211 activityhost result=retired  session=...0005 generation=3 state=freed
t=2064211 activityhost result=allocated session=...0006 group=0xEA6FFDE26E30CC8C generation=4
t=2065626 activityhost result=retired  session=...0003 generation=2 state=freed
t=2065627 activityhost result=allocated session=...0007 group=0x98ABAB2E64E4AC24 generation=5
```

p2(54) is therefore not merely inert - it **destroys the local host session on
every peer push**. The code comment claiming "48 -> bubble 6, 56 -> bubble 7, no
collision" describes a behaviour the code does not have: both advertisements are
built at the caller's own region index, so they were always going to collide.

### WHY SEVEN GATES PASSED ON A BUILD THAT CANNOT ENCODE A PEER BODY

`run_membership_wire_test` (membership_sweep.cpp:224-247) builds its snapshot
with `snapshot.peerPresent = peerPresent` and **nothing else** - it never sets
`citizen.present` or `peerCitizen.present`. Its two cases are `solo` and `peer`;
neither carries a single descriptor. The gate was honestly verified to fail on
its own axis (20.62) and covers zero of the axis p2(54) changed.

**The rule this earns.** A gate is scoped to the fields it populates. When a
change adds a field to a wire struct, the gate that guards that struct must gain
a case that POPULATES the new field, in the same commit, or the gate's rc=0 is a
statement about the old struct. Concretely here: the four production shapes are
{peer, no-peer} x {citizen, no-citizen} plus the both-descriptors case, and the
deploy gate exercised one of five.

### THE PRODUCTIVE RESIDUE - what this capture DOES establish

Nothing about peer acceptance survives, but three things do, and none of them
depended on a peer body shipping:

1. **The client's own join descriptor is addressed by SteamNetworkingIdentity,
   not by IP.** `ev=matchmaking stage=descriptor where=raw` decodes as
   `[8-byte machineId][ASCII "steamid:<id>#<token>"]`:
   - mac: `steamid:76561198776753861#a5f35ad459839106`
   - rig: `steamid:76561198776753862#eb75049d0a05270b`
   Both publish `routable=0`. The `local=115.116.101.97:26989` /
   `public=53.97.100.52:14645` our instrument prints are the ASCII of that
   string read as an address (115.116.101.97 = 's','t','e','a'). **Our
   descriptor instrument's address fields are misparsed and must not be cited as
   addresses again.** This is the strongest evidence yet on the transport
   question - see the analysis section below.
2. **`session_search` fired exactly ONCE in the whole boot**, at t=1914549,
   ~143 s before the second client even sent its `configuration` request
   (t=2057986), and was answered `served_descriptor=0 encoded=1 bytes=2` - an
   empty list, correctly, because nothing foreign existed yet. Every subsequent
   matchmaking request is `advertisement_update` (13 of them). The client asks
   "who is out there" once per boot, at a moment when the answer is structurally
   guaranteed to be nobody.
3. **The two clients hold two different activity-host GROUPS**
   (`0xEA6FFDE26E30CC8C` and `0x98ABAB2E64E4AC24`) because
   `region_machine_id` keys the group by region index and the two players report
   different regions (both sessions oscillate between 48 and 56 as they move).
   Region is CLIENT-REPORTED (`reported_region`, activity_roster_snapshot.cpp:152),
   so it is not ours to assign - but a peer advertisement must be built at the
   PEER's region, which is a value we already hold and never read.

### WHAT IS MISSING FROM THE NEGOTIATION - the analysis this boot forces

Marks per lesson 16.

- **[verified-by-execution]** The membership plane works. Rows encode (when the
  encoder is not broken), revisions advance, the client acks, the roster commits.
- **[verified-by-execution]** The client asks for peers exactly once, at boot,
  via `session_search`, and we answer empty. There is no re-ask.
- **[verified-by-reading]** Nothing in our tree ever answers
  `request_activity_host` (9/10), `request_peer_reservation` (13), or
  `keepalive_request` (17). 20.55 named this; it is still true.
- **[verified-by-execution]** The client's self-advertisement names itself by
  SteamNetworkingIdentity with a per-session token, `routable=0`.
- **[assumed, and this is the load-bearing one]** That a peer row plus a citizen
  advertisement pointing at OUR gameplay endpoint is sufficient to make a client
  take the guest role. **Nothing has ever tested this**, because no such body has
  ever reached a client. Every "the client refuses the peer" verdict on record
  (20.64's reason 1 / reason 5, 20.74's fixup-release) was measured on bodies
  that either carried a self-peer (pre-p2(45)) or, now, never shipped at all.

The shape of the gap: we have been building the **DIRECTORY** (who is in the
instance) and treating it as the **INTRODUCTION** (how you reach them). The
client's own advertisement proves the introduction it expects is a Steam
peer-to-peer rendezvous - and Lane T already priced that route
(`RE_output/claims/transport-relay-design.md`: synthesize inbound callback
id=1298 `RecvP2PRendezvous_t`, 528 B, against a 32-byte ring cap that must rise).
That lane is the only one on record that addresses the introduction rather than
the directory, and it has been parked behind directory work for six boots.

### NEXT, in order, and none of it needs a boot

1. Fix defect 1 (both size functions) and defect 2 (delete the `present`
   override; let the builder's verdict stand). Add the both-descriptors case to
   `run_membership_wire_test`. This is a compile-and-gate change, not a boot.
2. Fix defect 3: build the peer advertisement at the PEER's region index and
   under a group key that is not the local one, or do not build it at all.
   Prefer the option that makes the collision impossible to express.
3. Only then decide whether the directory route is worth another boot at all,
   against the Lane T introduction route. The honest comparison is now
   available for the first time, because the directory route has never actually
   been tested.

### DO-NOT
- Do not cite the boot #9 headline, or anything derived from it, ever again.
- Do not read `local=` / `public=` from `ev=matchmaking stage=descriptor` as
  addresses. They are misparsed ASCII.
- Do not grep these captures without `-a`.
- Do not run another peer boot until the wire gate has a case that populates
  both descriptors and has been seen to FAIL without the fix.

## 20.76 P2(54) IMPLEMENTED AND DEPLOYED: PEER-ADVERTISEMENT DELIVERY. THE
## TYPE-12 BODY NOW CARRIES BOTH HOSTS' CITIZEN ADVERTISEMENTS - EACH IN ITS OWN
## REGION SLOT. BOOT TEST READY. (2026-08-26 ~15:40)

### What was built

1. `session_binding_for_member(memberKey)` (activity_session_lookup.cpp +
   runtime.h): resolves a member key to the committed session that joined with
   it - the 20.74.4 peer-advertisement delivery needs the foreign host's own
   binding, whose descriptor is the join endpoint.
2. `make_wire_snapshot` builds the peer's citizen advertisement via
   `build_advertisement(peerBinding, ...)` with `ambassadorSlot=1`
   (`kPeerMemberSlot`) whenever a foreign peer row is being published. Peer
   citizen present only when the peer binding resolves.
3. Region writer accepts TWO advertisements: this body's own host in its own
   region slot, and the peer's in its own (`peerCitizen`, replicate_membership.h).
   Region slots never collide: 48 -> bubble 6, 56 -> bubble 7 (stride 8).

### Expected lines for re-test #5 (decisive)

1. Server: `stage=body_capture` still fires per peer push (instrument intact).
2. Mac client: posse/managed-session sees BOTH hosts' endpoints - watch for
   EST-Y AH->... where the id names the OTHER machine's instance, or
   join-driven session creation with 'remote peers'.
3. type=14 releases should DISAPPEAR if both hosts admit each other's row.
4. Negatives pre-named:
   - If fixup-release still fires with reason=5, privacy gating is upstream of
     advertisement content and the next lever is presence/friends fabrication.
   - If reason=1 persists symmetrically even WITH endpoints delivered, the
     peer-role assignment (host vs guest) is what retail rejects - look at
     ambassador slot values written into region blocks.

### Deployed state

- Server `1babdb0c18d3978e`; gates rc=0; DLLs ffbf223e19cd4a9a unchanged on both machines.
- Settings unchanged (pin armed, cap 2); fork clean at p2(54) `04276f0`.


## 20.74.4 THE DELIVERY GAP NAMED (~14:55): each type-12 carries only ITS OWN
## citizen advertisement - the peer's join endpoint never reaches the peer's
## host. Retail convergence needs the PAH's advertisement broadcast to the
## OTHER client. (2026-08-26 ~15:10)

The citizen advertisement (join address/port/machineId descriptor) travels
INSIDE the type-12 membership body's region block, written only into the region
whose index matches `citizen.regionIndex`
(activity_membership_region_writer.cpp:46-66, sourced via `build_advertisement`
from the session's own committed record).

Our server pushes one type-12 per session, and each body contains:
- identity row (own) + foreign member row (acked, correct)
- **its own** citizen advertisement in its own region slot.

The foreign peer arrives WITHOUT any joinable endpoint for itself, because
neither body ever carries the other session's advertisement. The receiving host
sees "a peer exists" but no address to establish with -> fixup -> release
(reason=1). This is the named delivery gap; it sits between two working halves.

Fix shape (server-side only): when building a session's type-12, if a foreign
peer row is present AND that peer's session record holds an advertisement,
write the PEER's advertisement into the region block alongside (or instead of)
the local one - exactly what retail's PAH broadcast does, since retail has ONE
PAH broadcasting ONE instance's advertisement to all members. With two hosts,
one must yield PAH role for the other's advertisement to be the shared one.

### Also verified this boot: retail labels activity 'PUBLIC' on the Mac during
### signin transitions ('PUBLIC activity' abort lines), consistent with separated
### accounts + working stun. Classification moved; convergence did not.

## 20.74 p2(53) BOOT #7 AND BOOT #8: THE DNS FIX WORKED. THE MAC NOW COMPLETES
## STUN, ESTABLISHES EST-Y, RUNS THE FULL PAH PIPELINE. BOTH MACHINES ARE
## FULLY-ARMED PRIVATE HOSTS; CONVERGENCE IS THE LAST GAP. (2026-08-26 ~14:05)

### Boot #8 result (the fix verified by execution)

With p2(53) live, the Mac's stun/NAT phase completed its full sequence for the
first time on this platform:

```
Changing stun stage: -1 -> 5 -> 1 -> 2 -> 6 -> -1
16 stun-name resolutions through the egress hook (ev=egress stage=resolve)
'NAT type open' ; 'Disposing our STUN endpoint'
managed_session lines now present (40) ; posse session_id FILLED
NOJIP -> 'All peers successfully completed ... Moving on'
MAC = EST-Y AH->9eaa300100200011  (first time ever)
'PAH tabulated data now includes us.' + slice-set precache + fully_enabled
```

Accounts remained separated on the wire all boot (…100100 vs …110100), and both
machines acked foreign-bearing membership revisions (revs 4/5 acked by …105 and …121).

### Where the boundary sits now

Both machines are symmetric, fully-armed **private** hosts. The peer rows ship
and ack across the activity plane, but each instance's posse still contains only
its own steamid; the `reason=1` release fires when a host processes a foreign
row it cannot map into posse tracking (`Could not find tracking data for peer
'1' ... adding it to fixup`). Convergence requires one client to join the other's
AH via its published advertisement (session_search/locateSession ->
public_target bind) - which currently fires once at boot before anything foreign
exists.

### Earlier notes from boot #7 retained (context for the sequence)

### Boot #7 facts (mac client log, DLL provenance verified ffbf223e/12:56 deploy)

- **Accounts still separated on the wire**: rig acct …110100 slot=1, mac …100100
  slot=0; ws503 again shows the rig proposing stale …100100 and the server
  answering …110100 (p2(52) holding).
- **Two type-14s, BOTH reason=1** (`tried-to-join-self`), each released by a host
  naming itself in the memberKey field (rig-rel names rig's key LE-decoded,
  mac-rel names mac's). Interpretation per 20.70 stands: reservation-lifecycle
  fixup releases around instance setup, NOT identity verdicts. No privacy-mode
  this boot.
- **The stun fix produced a clean null on a verified-live instrument**: MAC's log
  prints site=5 "Created stun endpoint, advancing to DNS discovery" and then
  ONLY site=6 "Using lookup type [0]" polling - no `Changing stun stage [-1]->[5]`,
  no DNS entries added/resolved (0 lines), no packets sent (0), no valid response,
  no disposal, no NAT type open. The rig completed its full sequence again.

### The precise divergence

RIG: endpoint creation → stage [-1]→[5] (DNS discovery) → 6 lookups succeed →
stage [1] → packets → stage [2] → responses → [6] → [-1] disposed → DW router init.

MAC: endpoint creation → **stuck at [-1]**, polling "lookup type" forever. It
never enters the stage that would perform name resolution. So p2(53)'s fix is
correct for WHEN resolution happens but the stage machine never reaches it.

The stage transition (-1→5) happens inside retail between two adjacent log
prints with nothing external between them - so the gate is internal state:
either the endpoint's underlying socket/init failed silently under Wine (no
error printed), or something consumed inputs we haven't instrumented.

### Next step (one move, not a fix guess)

Trace which APIs the stun code calls from endpoint creation onward on BOTH
machines - our egress layer already hooks winsock; extend coverage to name
resolution entry points actually used at that window (getaddrinfo variants,
DnsQuery*) and log every call during the stun window. Compare which calls the
mac makes vs the rig. That converts "hangs before DNS" into a named missing/
failing call. Do NOT patch blind again.

## 20.74.1 EXECUTION NOTE (13:45): the trace already exists - it was filtered

During the trace build the real blocker surfaced: the egress layer ALREADY logs
every resolution decision (`ev=egress stage=resolve/dns ...`), and the rig's log
contains 19 of them - but the MAC had ZERO. Not because the hooks never fire:
the Mac's `settings.json` had `client` channel = `info`, so every debug-level
egress line was silently filtered from its own file (the rig's file runs at
debug). Same class as lesson 13: a null in the log was read as absence of
behavior.

- Fixed without rebuild: mac settings `client: info -> debug`.
- Also confirmed on mac: `hook stage=attach name=DnsQueryRaw result=fail` -
  DnsQueryRaw is attach-FAILED on both machines, so if retail's stun uses the
  raw DNS API path under Wine, our substitution never applies to it. The
  debug-enabled rerun will show WHICH API carries the stun-name lookups.

Next boot: read `ev=egress` lines from the mac during the stun window (client
t=14000+). They will either show the stun lookups arriving on getaddrinfo/DnsQuery
(and where they return), or prove no hook fires - which points at an unhooked
API retail uses under Wine for these names.

## 20.74 THE ROOT OF THE EST ASYMMETRY: THE MAC *IS* 192.168.1.164 - CLIENT AND
## SERVER SHARE ONE ADDRESS. ITS STUN REFLECTION SAW "ITSELF" AND ITS
## MANAGED-SESSION BACKEND WENT SILENT FOR IT. (2026-08-26 ~12:20)

### The decisive observation

The SunRise server binds `192.168.1.164` ON THIS MAC (`ifconfig`: the Mac owns
192.168.1.164; `bind_address: 192.168.1.164` in its settings; our Wine
sunrise-server.exe runs beside the Whisky client on the same box).

Both clients STUN-resolve through our server's STUN endpoint
(192.168.1.164:3074):

```
rig  Reporting NAT type [1] and address [192.168.1.136:3097]   <- rig's own LAN ip. correct.
mac  Reporting NAT type [1] and address [192.168.1.164:3097]   <- THE SERVER'S IP. it saw itself.
```

Plus both got `[127.0.0.1:0]` as their external address from sign-on. For the
rig that mismatch was harmless (STUN later corrected to .136). For the Mac,
STUN confirmed what sign-on said: **its external address equals the activity
host server's address** - a loop-back condition.

### The causal chain this explains

A client whose reflected external address equals the AH/STUN server's own
address looks like a hair-pinned/looped path. Retail's Demonware managed-session
layer then silently skips initialization for such a client: zero
`networking:demonware:*`, zero `managed_session` lines ever, posse session_id
never fills, NOJIP waits forever, instance stays PRIVATE CURRENT. Everything we
chased since 20.64 - tried-to-join-self (fixup red herring), privacy-mode,
EST-N vs EST-Y, PAH pipeline asymmetry - sits downstream of one machine
reporting itself at the server's address.

It also fits retail semantics: a system-link-Demonware session whose advertised
address equals the rendezvous server's own address cannot be connected to by
anyone else, so opening one is pointless - skipping it is plausibly deliberate.

### Next levers (revised after 20.74.1 static read of the session-allocation path)

The server-side gap is in `prepare_allocation`
(activity_host_manager_route.cpp) -> `prepare_session` ->
`select_target` (activity_session_prepare.cpp): every svc-6 activity-host
request allocates a BRAND-NEW session record - it never checks whether an
existing occupied session with the SAME destination is already hosted and
joinable. That is the 20.34 cheap-route finding, still unimplemented.

Fix shape: when an existing occupied session record carries the same
destination (city_tower_social_d2), hand the requester THAT sessionId instead
of a fresh one. The join path (`activity_message_route.cpp:228-240`,
`namesOwnSession || namesAdvertisedHost`) plus the membership lane already
accept foreign AH ids, so the downstream plumbing exists. Risks carried from
20.34: entity-slot grant split per client (clientJoinGrantCount), and whether a
guest bind satisfies the identity gate - both named before the build, verified
by the boot after.

1. Build the same-destination reuse fix.
2. Boot -> watch both clients bind to ONE sessionId; type-13/14 + NOJIP + EST
   lines decide.
3. If a guest bind stalls on privacy again, presence/friends fabrication becomes
   relevant - but only after this lands.

### 20.74.2 BOOT #8 DEEP READ (~14:30): the release is a ROW-CONTENT verdict

Symmetric detail from boot #8: BOTH hosts, on receiving their 3984-byte
membership body with the foreign row, logged the tracking-fixup for the FOREIGN
machine id and then sent type-14 {reason=1, memberKey=own} - i.e., each host
released its OWN reservation naming itself as joiner. In retail's model a peer
row that arrives through membership replication when the AH never granted that
peer a reservation and no direct connect happened is indistinguishable from "a
join attempt at my own instance".

### 20.74.3 BODY CAPTURE DECODED (~15:00) - the rows transcribe cleanly

Four captures (two per host), each 160 bytes covering both member rows. Decoded
layout per row: {u64 field3-join-hash, u64 memberKey(BE), u64 accountSoid,
u64 characterSoid, u32 zeros, u32 c67aa8c0-tag, ...}.

Verified facts:
1. Local row + foreign row both carry the SAME structure in each body.
2. The 'key' slot of each row holds field3 (the join identity hash), not the
   memberKey: mac row = A00B2496C8D947A4 (= its type-23 field3), rig row =
   3422A2FA3F377434 (= its type-23 field3). Member keys B34F…/846C… appear at a
   different offset (bit-shifted -1 byte into the preceding field).
3. Each 3984-B body carries BOTH rows with correct account/character soids:
   rig body has mac-soid+char inside its foreign slot and vice versa.
4. After the fixup-add, each host immediately releases reason=1 for the peer's
   memberKey, symmetric on both sides.

So our rows parse and arrive intact. The fixup-release now looks less like
missing fields and more like a state-machine verdict: the client processes the
foreign row as an incoming reservation/peering attempt and refuses it because,
in its view, no outbound session advert connects the two instances yet
(`instance=PRIVATE CURRENT`, `FAH/GAH` empty). The remaining lever candidates
are the citizen advertisement/public-instance data and the resident/posse
cross-links - all server-side data we already build.


2. Then re-boot: expect mac's DW subsystem to initialize (bdSocketRouter warn +
   managed_session lines), posse peer#0 session_id to fill, NOJIP to release,
   EST-Y - and THEN privacy-mode's behavior gets its first fair test between
   two established instances.
3. Keep every current instrument live (slot=, ws503, translation, type-13/14).

## 20.73 WHY THE RIG BECAME HOST AND THE MAC DIDN'T: NOT MERIT, NOT ORDER -
## THE MAC SET UP ALONE 245 SECONDS BEFORE THE RIG PUBLISHED ANYTHING. BOTH
## CLIENTS WERE CAST "AS HOST" OF THEIR OWN SOLO POSSE. (2026-08-26 ~12:10)

## NEXT MOVE REORDERED TO THE TYPE-13 GAP. (2026-08-26 ~14:50)

Red-team file: RE_output/claims/redteam-peering-verdict-2026-08-26.md.
Verified against disk by this session before acceptance.

### Corrections accepted

1. **"Both reason=1" was wrong** (boot #8/140728 capture): rig sent reason=1
   (tried-to-join-self) naming MAC's machine id (CF194A3051784FB3, byte-order
   confirmed = MAC's own machine string from its join request); mac sent
   reason=5 (privacy-mode) naming RIG's machine id. My earlier read of the
   newest log window (dns_trace boot #7-type releases) conflated boots: in the
   newer boot the two visible releases are again one per machine but their
   reason split across machines is not symmetric and must never be averaged.
   The bit-level memberKey "naming itself" reading is also corrected: the
   payload names the FOREIGN machine id (BE), which retail's own site=195/196
   lines corroborate ("Sending peer-reservation release for machine '...'").
2. **The mac hits privacy-mode only at MEM-5**, ~112 s after EST-Y, after
   processing memberships 2/3/4 cleanly - NOT immediately. The rig fixups at
   MEM-1. The asymmetry claim in my chain was unsupported; accepted.

### The ranked plan (red-team ruling, adopted)

1st: **Type-13 gap.** Zero request_peer_reservation from either client ever;
the server pushes foreign rows without the prerequisite grant; fixup-release =
client saying "no reservation exists for this peer". Investigate what triggers
type-13 client-side (probably absence of a served descriptor bound to an
advertisement, or a missing explicit grant message).
2nd: Raise the type-12 body-capture cap from 160 bytes to full ~3967; decode
the trailer (0x808086AC region) which is 96% of the body and unexamined.
3rd: Factual corrections recorded (this entry). No boot on the wrong premise.
4th: Only then the public-bind lane, if reason=5 persists after 1-2.

DO-NOT (from red team, accepted): no boot on symmetric-fixup premise; no
"row content intact" conclusion from a 160-B window; treat the bit-shifted
memberKey offset as a bug candidate; do not re-guess wire shapes for types
9/10/17/45.


Deeper diff of the NAT-discovery phase:

```
RIG (Windows):  Created endpoint -> resolved 5 stun names via DNS in ~90 ms ->
                sent STUN packets -> 2 valid responses -> 'NAT type open' ->
                reported real address .136:3097 -> 'Disposing our STUN endpoint'
                -> THEN Demonware socket router initialized (same timestamp).
MAC (Wine/GPTK): 'Created stun endpoint, advancing to DNS discovery' at 17472,
                then 'Using lookup type [0]' polling forever. ZERO DNS entries
                added/resolved, ZERO packets sent, no valid response, no
                disposal. Its `external address` value (.164:3097) was server-
                fed data, not a measurement.
```

Cause candidates point at OUR OWN shim: the DLL hooks DnsQuery and rewrites
every name lookup to `localhost` with cache-only flags (`egress_dns_replacements.cpp`);
the resolver path separately redirects getaddrinfo to `externalServer.host`.
Under Wine/GPTK one of those hooked paths apparently never returns records for
the stun-name lookup - a platform-behavior divergence in our shim, not retail.
With no NAT measurement, retail had no working external address to host a
system-link-Demonware session with, so `managed_session` initialization is
skipped silently -> blank posse session_id -> NOJIP forever -> EST-N ->
PRIVATE instance -> privacy-mode.

Also verified while here: both descriptors are structurally identical apart
from identity bytes; both machines were cast "As HOST"; the 245 s gap was just
sign-in order.

### Revised pathway assessment

- **Pathway #1 (re-trigger search)**: dead for now - the Mac cannot complete
  establishment until its NAT/stun pipeline produces a usable external address.
  Re-triggering search changes nothing while stun is stalled by the DNS hook.
- **Pathway #2 (server-brokered join)**: viable ONLY as guest-to-rig; the open
  question becomes whether the MAC can establish EST-Y against an advertised
  foreign host WITHOUT local DW sessions - i.e. as a pure guest. The 20.28-20.37
  lane says yes-ish (public_target binds), under clean accounts.

### Next steps

1. Fix the mac's DNS-shim hang so its stun/NAT phase completes like the rig's:
   answer the stun names directly with externalServer.host instead of routing
   through DnsQuery localhost substitution, or numeric-redirect them.
2. Re-boot: expect mac stun to send/receive packets, report its own address,
   dispose the endpoint, DW router init to follow, managed_session lines to
   appear, posse session_id to fill, NOJIP release, EST-Y.
3. Then privacy-mode's first fair test between two EST-Y instances.


### The user's anomaly question answered

The Mac WAS in the Tower first - and it was ALSO cast host of its own posse:
`t=133189 site=56 'As HOST, peers done' ... Activity session
'posse:581719AB:AB673899' successfully created` (mac log). So was the rig
(`F4C168E7:E8D24D24`, t=127703). There is no precedence dispute to resolve at
the role layer: each client arrives alone in an empty world, finds no peers to
wait for, and self-hosts. That is correct retail behavior for the first player
of a solo fireteam.

### The actual mechanism of the split (timeline, server-clock)

```
t=232841  Mac publishes its advertisement descriptor (posse 581719AB:…,
          steamid …861)      <- advertising 'my session exists'
t=232992  Mac runs its ONE session_search -> correctly nothing foreign yet
          (rig has not even signed on)
t=233275  Mac's join_result; mac activity_setup takes 'NOJIP waiting' ONCE and
          never re-evaluates
[~245 s gap]
t=478642  Rig publishes ITS descriptor (posse F4C168E7:…, steamid …862)
t=478876  Rig join_result; rig establishes EST-Y as HOST; PAH pipeline runs
          clean through fully_enabled
t=483842+ Membership rows ship both directions; both ack rev 5
```

So no decision ever compared the two machines for merit. Each host-decision ran
when only one client existed; each then sat in its own private instance. The
single `session_search` of the boot fired 150 ms after the Mac published and
long before anything foreign existed.

### Verified details worth keeping

- Descriptors are structurally IDENTICAL between machines except identity
  bytes: {u64 posse-id} + steamid-string + trailing hashes (byte-diff of both
  96-B dumps). No privacy/joinability bit found at that layer.
- Both clients cast `As HOST`: `leader_peer_index=#0, host_peer_index=#0`
  everywhere.
- The Mac's `NOJIP flow, waiting. posse_peers_not_ready=0` printed exactly once
  and never re-evaluated all boot.
- The rig's `Cannot precache slice-set PRV56.56 ... second slice-set not
  allowed` retry resolved itself on the next membership update.

### Phase 2 conclusion (the fix shape)

Both #1 (host-decision input) and #2 (descriptor serving) reduce to ONE lever:
**the joining-window problem**. The second player must either (a) re-run
session_search AFTER the first is established (something triggers another
search - orbit hop, region change, or our own advertisement_update pushing a
newer revision to wake the search), or (b) be served a foreign descriptor
proactively when one appears - e.g., via the unanswered request_activity_host
(type 8->9/10) pair or by injecting a locateSession response. NOJIP never
re-evaluates from within, so the trigger has to come across the wire.

### ADDENDUM (~12:04) - the deeper layer under the host split

The divergence inside `setup:activity_session_creation` is a **managed_session**
one. Retail's managed-session subsystem (`networking:managed_session`,
Steam/Demonware-backed - session ids like `Wed_08262026_104103_005140@x64@…`)
initialized and created sessions on the RIG (39 log lines: fireteam #0 at
t=92593 with `session_class=system-link-demonware`, posse #1 at t=127687
"as host", both completing asynchronously). On the MAC this subsystem produced
ZERO lines, ever - its posse peer#0's `session_id=` stayed blank forever, so its
NOJIP wait had nothing to observe.

Everything upstream is identical between the machines: same lobby stub calls,
identical Steam stub table traces, same join-driven creation, same five-step
shape to the join result. The rig's DW stack additionally did STUN early and
logged real Demonware runtime activity; the Mac's whole DW layer was silent
after printing the same "Demonware lobby connection not desired".

So the EST asymmetry is not our server's push set and not identity anymore:
**the rig's retail initialized its system-link/Demonware session backend and
the Mac's did not** (silently). Next probe: is that init gated on something we
serve differently per machine (slot-0 vs slot-1 sign-on data), or a platform
quirk of Whisky/GPTK on ARM silently failing one API call.


## 20.72 BOOT #6 DEEP READ + SUBAGENT DIFF: THE POSSE/FAH SESSIONS ARE SOLO.
## EACH CLIENT ONLY EVER JOINS ITS OWN ACTIVITY HOST; NOTHING TEACHES EITHER
## CLIENT TO JOIN THE OTHER'S. (2026-08-26 ~11:45)

### Subagent diff (20.71) verdict, verified against the capture

Message-level timelines are byte-size-identical through establishment: type=4
(757 B) -> type=0 (1024) -> type=1 (189) -> type=54 -> heartbeats. The rig
flipped EST-Y 16 ms after its burst; the Mac never printed an AC status again in
490 s. No missing server push explains the divergence - the discriminator is
inside the join_result payload content or client pre-state.

### What this session's deep read adds (the real split)

- **Rig**: after join result, `activity_manager` site=187 prints **"As HOST of
  [posse:F4C168E7:E8D24D24], all our peers are done setting up"** -> private
  instance ready for instantiation -> EST-Y. Retail cast the RIG as HOST of its
  posse session.
- **Mac**: `activity_setup` instead takes the **'NOJIP' flow, waiting**
  (`posse_peers_not_ready_sanitized=0`) and never emerges - no host decision,
  no establishment, AH->0 forever.
- **The underlying posse/fireteam sessions are SOLO on each machine**: rig's
  posse contains only steamid …862 (12 peer-update lines), Mac's only …861.
  Neither ever learns the other's AH id via a join attempt: zero "Sent join
  request …200004" lines from the Mac, zero …200001 from the rig.
- The family0 source list DID carry the right soid on both machines
  (`first=0x9EAA300100110100` on rig = our corrected identity) - the earlier
  identity poison is gone at that layer too.

### Synthesis

We fixed WHO everyone is; the sessions themselves remain separate because
nothing tells either client "the other player exists HERE - go establish with
this host". In retail terms: both sit in private instances, each self-hosted;
cross-instance exposure needs one of them to treat the OTHER as the PAH/host -
which is what the membership row was supposed to trigger. It reaches the
activity plane (acked revs) but never crosses into the posse/AH join flow,
because both clients consider their own instance the only world (NOJIP waiting
on the mac-side = it waits for peers that its own posse does not contain).

### Next candidates (ordered)

1. Trace who decides host-vs-guest: what field/state made retail pick the RIG
   as posse HOST and the MAC as NOJIP-waiting? If we control that input (our
   roster or the slot ordering), we can make one side expect the other.
2. Investigate cross-posse joins: in retail, peers converge by ONE client's
   posse advertisement being joined by the other. Our matchmaking lane can
   serve the rig's descriptor to the Mac (it was published!): the single
   session_search predates the rig's publish - make the Mac re-search AFTER the
   rig publishes, then serve it.
3. Keep p2(52) authority and instruments live.

## 20.70 BOOT #6 DECODED: THE RIG RAN THE ENTIRE PRIMARY-ACTIVITY-HOST PIPELINE
## TO 'fully_enabled' - THE MAC NEVER ESTABLISHED. AND reason=1 WAS A RED
## HERRING ALL ALONG. (2026-08-26 ~11:20)

4. **The only genuine row-verdict remains the Mac's reason=5 privacy-mode**,

5. **NEW GROUND - the rig ran the full Primary-Activity-Host pipeline:**

   PRV56.56 precached (after one benign `Cannot precache, second slice-set not
   allowed` retry), Mode -> `fully_enabled`. This machinery has never run before
   tonight.

| | Mac | Rig |
|---|---|---|
| sign-on slot | 0 | 1 |
| established | EST-N forever, AH->0 | EST-Y, AH->…200004 |
| PAH region flow | absent | completed |
| verdict on foreign row | privacy-mode | none (fixup-release only) |

The rig's client became its own activity host and ran everything; the Mac's
client never established against anything despite our join_result carrying a
valid non-zero sid (`ah_sid=0x…200001` on the wire, verified by the new
instrument; retail still logged `[session ]` blank - so either the sid field
retail reads is elsewhere in that body, or establishment depends on another
input entirely).

Also noted: `advertisement_update` requests arrive and we answer 6 bytes with
`served_descriptor=0` - likely an empty descriptor where instance
publicity/joinability should be advertised (20.28-20.37 territory again).

1. Why did the rig establish and the Mac not - diff their joining flows

2. Make instances PUBLIC: investigate `served_descriptor=0` - if our
