## 20.69 PHASE 1 VERDICT: privacy-mode IS THE PEER-REFUSAL FACE OF THE PRIVATE
## INSTANCE. THE CLIENT'S OWN WORDS: "PRIVATE session unable to get session
## keys", "'PRIVATE activity' Starting search". (2026-08-26 ~11:00)

### What the enum walk showed

The failure strings are at .rdata 0x141c9e2f8, contiguous, index = order
(0 no-reason-given ... 5 privacy-mode ...). NO direct references to string
STARTS exist for indices 0..5 (scanned every rip-relative displacement in
.text); the only genuine lea clusters target entries 30+ (session-message-type
names - a different enum) and runtime-built descriptor arrays. Conclusion: the
reason STRINGS hang off a runtime-built table, so chasing the formatter was the
wrong corridor; the PRODUCER of reason 5 does not need the string at all.

### The decisive evidence is in the clients' own logs

Rig client, this boot, BEFORE any family traffic of ours:

```
world_controller:session_manager: PRIVATE session [posse:00000000:00000000] unable to get session keys.
world_controller:matchmaking_manager: 'PRIVATE activity' Starting search. [ACTIVE]
```

Mac client:

```
[AC PRIVATE CURRENT CON-Y EST-N AH->0 MEM-0] Sent join request to AH 9EAA3001:00200001 ...
Received join result from AH [result 0][session id                    ]   <- BLANK
activity_host_changed: instance=PRIVATE CURRENT, ah-sid=<blank>
[FAH --------:--------][GAH --------:--------]                          <- no public host known
```

Every session classifies itself as a PRIVATE activity client: the AH accepts
the join (result 0) but returns NO session id, ah-sid stays blank, and a private
member refuses cross-exposure -> reason 5 on the peer row. This is the same wall
20.28-20.37 mapped (blank ah-sid / instance stays PRIVATE / zero public AH), now
confirmed as the CAUSE of privacy-mode rather than an adjacent curiosity.

### Why this re-opens Lane 20.31-20.37 instead of starting something new

The public-instance path was already BUILT back then: citizen advertisement ->
public_target bind -> public_membership push, with `stage=bind result=public_target`
as its success line. Tonight's boot produced ZERO such lines - the clients never
took the advertised-host branch. But that lane stalled when the client ignored
the advertisement - under SAME-ACCOUNT chaos. The accounts are separated NOW,
which removes the confounder that tainted every earlier run of that flow.

### Next (phase 2)

1. Re-run the public-instance flow as-built: Mac-only control, then rig; watch
   `stage=bind result=public_target`, activity_host_changed flipping OFF
   PRIVATE CURRENT, "public AH instance ready" client-side.
2. If still ignored, instrument the AH join-result bytes client-side (the
   received sid), because then the question narrows to exactly one body.
3. Friends/presence fabrication (Lane V) stays parked - privacy-mode traced to
   instances makes friends secondary until instance publicity lands.

## 20.68 ACCOUNT SEPARATION LANDED - AND THE CLIENT'S NEW VERDICT IS
## `privacy-mode`. THE FIRST GENUINELY FOREIGN PEER ROW SHIPPED AND WAS REFUSED
## ON A DIFFERENT AXIS THAN IDENTITY. (2026-08-26 ~10:30)

### The chain that got here (all verified by execution across four boots)

20.66 root-caused why p2(49) was inert: `core_runtime` seeded the client from
`initialAccount` only, so slot 1 never existed client-side; keyed reads clamped
to slot 0. 20.67 proved provisioning alone insufficient too: p2(50) provisioned
BOTH slots (rig log showed key=1 = …110100) yet retail STILL published …100100
and proposed it back over svc/ws - because RETAIL paths read the ACTIVE slot,
so selection had to happen at seed time (p2(51): provision exactly the chosen
account WITH its own bootstrap token -> rig signed on `matched=slot1`).

Then the ws503 instrument caught the last overwrite red-handed:
`t=484199 ev=ws503 stage=adopt slot=1 origin=client soid=0x9EAA300100100100` -
the client proposed its stale learned identity and our handler OBEYED
(`set_primary_soid`), destroying the correct provisioning mid-boot. The DB was
verified CLEAN (both accounts, correct bands) - the poison came over the wire,
not from disk.

### p2(52): the server owns identity

ws503 no longer adopts proposals. It answers the calling slot's provisioned
SOID unconditionally and logs `proposed/answered/origin`. A disagreeing client
proposal is recorded, not obeyed.

### Boot #5 result: SEPARATION AT LAST

```
Mac  stage=identity key=0xB34F7851304A19CF acct=0x9EAA300100100100 character=0x…100101 slot=0
Rig  stage=identity key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 acct=0x9EAA300110100 character=0x…110103 slot=1
```

Distinct accounts, distinct characters, right slots - GOAL 1 RE-PROVEN at the
activity layer after 20.64's refutation. Then:

```
t=539052 membership_peer result=gained
t=539053 membership_peer result=included key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 variant=packed_masks peer_row=1 first=0x00020002 second=0x00000003
t=539092 type=14 payload=0000000000000005 E622D0F738836C84   <- {reason=5 BE, memberKey LE}
t=544053 included again (cap attempt 2)
t=549084 withdrawn variant=packed_masks unacked=2            <- cap worked, stopped hammering
```

The first GENUINELY foreign peer row ever accepted-for-shipping was refused by
the client with **reason 5 = `privacy-mode`** (the client's own enum, .rdata
0x141c9e2f8) - not tried-to-join-self, not a wire-format complaint. The identity
battle is over; the gate has moved to presence/visibility semantics.

User report corroborates: rig saw only itself; Mac went black on entering the
Tower (first time the Mac is the one receiving a foreign-name row while its own
identity differs - unchanged rendering set, no art delta involved).

### What privacy-mode plausibly gates, and the next levers

The refusal fires on the PEER'S VISIBILITY status, not on the body format -
which makes Lane V's designed fallback directly relevant: a fabricated
friend/presence roster whose CSteamIDs match the machines (persona state 2..6,
non-private) may flip the verdict without touching membership at all. Also open:
whether OUR own sign-on publishes a "private" flag the peer's client reads.

Next steps recorded for the next session:
1. Decode reason 5's exact trigger client-side (enum xref walk).
2. Fabricated friends roster (Lane V shape: count getter + by-index id +
   persona-state triple) presenting BOTH machines as non-private friends.
3. Keep the type-14 instrument live - it remains the client's verdict channel.

## 20.67 RE-TEST #2: BOTH SLOTS PROVISIONED, RIG STILL PLAYED ACCOUNT 0 AND
## PRESENTED SLOT 0'S TOKEN. RETAIL READS THE ACTIVE SLOT - SO SELECTION MOVED
## TO SEED TIME (p2(51)). (2026-08-26 ~01:00)

### What boot #2 showed, verified

The rig's own client log proves p2(50) worked as coded - BOTH slots provisioned:

```
state t=47 ev=account stage=identity key=0 primary=0x9EAA300100100100 characters=3
state t=47 ev=account stage=identity key=1 primary=0x9EAA300110100 characters=3
```

Yet on the wire the rig still published `acct=0x9EAA300100100100` and -
new signal this boot - presented **slot 0's** bootstrap token at svc-25
(`matched=slot0 served=0`; last night it matched slot1). `reason=same_account`
again, five snapshots. Different towers explained: the same-account guard
refuses every peer body, so no shared instance forms.

### Root cause, one level deeper than 20.66

p2(50) made our keyed reads (`account_snapshot(local_account())`) able to see
slot 1. But the IDENTITY message and the SIGN-ON TOKEN presentation are built by
RETAIL code paths that read the ACTIVE slot / first token without knowing our
selector. Provisioning a second slot nothing retail reads cannot change what the
client publishes or which token it presents.

### The fix (p2(51), fork 2239831)

Selection moved to seed time: `state::initialize_selected(module, defaults)`
provisions EXACTLY ONE account into slot 0 - the entry `local_account_key`
names, WITH its own bootstrap token. Every reader - ours (which clamp to slot 0
when only one slot exists) and retail's (which read the active slot) - now sees
the same account. Mac behavior is unchanged: key defaults to 0, accounts[0] IS
the legacy initialAccount block.

Also noted while reading the parser: state_settings.cpp has a duplicated,
unreachable `accounts` branch (lines ~199-221) - merge debris that compiles.
Cleanup deferred; it does not affect behavior.

Deployed: server `6285c48f7589d913` + DLLs `21711767879fe135` both machines
(on-machine hash asserts; seven gates rc=0). Settings untouched.

### Expected values for re-test #3

- Rig sign-on: `matched=slot1 served=1` (its own token now travels with the
  selected account).
- Rig identity: `acct=0x9EAA300110100`, character from ITS band.
- `tried-to-join-self` AND `same_account` both gone. If the client refuses the
  peer row anyway, the NEW reason code is the deliverable of the boot.
- Absence negative: no identity line => DLL not loaded; re-hash first.

## 20.66 THE ACCOUNT FIX DID NOT SURVIVE CONTACT: p2(49) CHANGED THE READERS,
## BUT THE CLIENT STILL SEEDED ITS STATE FROM initialAccount - SLOT 1 WAS NEVER
## PROVISIONED IN THE CLIENT AT ALL. FIXED AS p2(50). (2026-08-26 ~00:35)

### The re-test boot (Mac control + rig) and what it showed

One-machine control PASSED clean: Mac `acct=0x9EAA300100100100`, zero peers, no
refusals, new DLL signs in fine.

Then the rig joined with `local_account_key: 1` set and verified in its
settings file - and STILL published `acct=0x9EAA300100100100`:

```
t=2222658 stage=identity key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 acct=0x9EAA300100100100 character=0x9EAA300100100103
t=2222619..2234890 wire_snapshot ... peer=0 reason=same_account   (x5)
```

The p2(49) server guard worked exactly as designed and refused every peer body
(`reason=same_account`, the build 20.64 asked for), which is why both machines
sat in DIFFERENT tower instances - no peer row ships between same-account
sessions. The guard is load-bearing; the identity fix underneath it was not.

### Root cause - one call site

`core_runtime.cpp:142` seeded the client's whole state with
`state::initialize(module, settings.initialAccount, ...)`. `initialAccount` is
the authored `state.account` block (…100100 on BOTH machines' files).
p2(49) changed every account READER to `account_snapshot(local_account())`,
but the runtime only ever provisioned ONE slot from `initialAccount`, so
`slot_account(1)` clamped to slot 0 (`g_accountCount == 1`) and every read got
account 0 no matter what the setting said. The rig's accounts[] array was
parsed (sign-on matched slot 1 - tokens come from it) but never became the
played account.

The server already provisions correctly (`server_main.cpp:459` calls
`initialize_provisioned`; that is where the startup
`ev=account stage=identity key=0/1` lines come from). The client was the last
legacy-path caller.

### The fix (p2(50), fork d17cc62)

core_runtime now calls `state::initialize_provisioned(module, defaults)` when
the parsed settings provision any accounts, keeping the legacy single-account
path only for files that author none (normalize_accounts guarantees count >= 1
whenever a valid state section exists, so the Mac's behavior is unchanged:
count==1, slot 0 = initialAccount byte-for-byte).

Deployed: DLL `18e3af58e7fcc576` on BOTH machines (hash asserted on-machine,
literal pre-checked); server redeployed byte-identical `bd8a5150f06571a4`
(core_runtime is not in the server link), seven gates rc=0.
Settings unchanged: rig `local_account_key: 1`, pin stays ARMED at 0, cap 2.

### Expected values for the next boot (rule 9)

- Mac alone: `acct=0x…100100`, zero peers (unchanged control).
- Rig joins: `stage=identity ... acct=0x9EAA300110100` - the fix's whole point.
- `reason=same_account` must be GONE. If a peer row now ships and the client
  still refuses, expect a DIFFERENT reason code from its enum - record it; that
  is the next real signal.
- Absence negative: if the rig logs NO identity line at all, suspect the DLL
  did not load - re-hash before theorizing.

## 20.64 THE PEER BOOT: THE CLIENT NAMED ITS OWN REASON FOR THE FIRST TIME AND IT
## IS NOT THE TRAILING FIELDS. IT IS `tried-to-join-self` - BOTH MACHINES ARE
## PLAYING THE SAME ACCOUNT. GOAL 1 IS REFUTED. (2026-08-26 ~03:30)

### The boot did what it was built to do, and then some

The designed negative fired cleanly on both sessions - `membership_peer
result=withdrawn variant=packed_masks unacked=2`, exactly two peer bodies each,
the client kept running on the solo body that followed and acknowledged it. The
retry cap change (6 -> 2) worked as specified.

Both clients still froze/black-screened. But for the first time in this project
**the client told us why, in its own vocabulary**, because p2(46)/p2(48b) stopped
discarding message 14.

### The type-14 payload, decoded

Sixteen bytes, and both captures decode exactly:

```
handle 0x...0004  payload=0000000000000001 CF194A3051784FB3
handle 0x...0001  payload=0000000000000005 E622D0F738836C84

layout: { u64 reason (big-endian) , u64 memberKey (little-endian) }
```

The key halves are byte-exact reversals of the two identities this boot logged -
`0xB34F7851304A19CF` (Mac) and `0x846C8338F7D022E6` (rig) - so the structure is
not a guess.

The reason codes index the client's own peer-link failure enum, read from the
image at `.rdata 0x141c9e2f8` (contiguous null-separated strings, so the index IS
the order):

```
  1  tried-to-join-self     <- the RIG, about the MAC
  5  privacy-mode           <- the MAC, about the RIG
```

**The rig refused our roster because it believes the peer we named IS ITSELF.**

### Why it is right to refuse

```
Mac  stage=identity key=0xB34F7851304A19CF acct=0x9EAA300100100100 character=0x9EAA300100100101
rig  stage=identity key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 acct=0x9EAA300100100100 character=0x9EAA300100100103
                                           ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^ THE SAME ACCOUNT
```

`acct` is `parsed.accountSoid` - the CLIENT's own claim out of its type-23
identity, not our label for it. Both machines publish the same account soid and
differ only in which character of it they are playing. The member keys differ,
which is why our peer guard let them through; the accounts do not, which is why
the client says `tried-to-join-self`.

And the server knows two accounts:

```
ev=account stage=identity key=0 primary=0x9EAA300100100100 characters=3
ev=account stage=identity key=1 primary=0x9EAA300100110100 characters=3
```

**Account 1's soid band `0x9EAA30010011....` appears exactly ONCE in the whole
boot log - in that startup line. It was never served to anybody.** Both machines
played account 0, characters 1 and 3.

### What this refutes

**STATE's goal 1 - "Separate accounts - working, identity-complete" - is
REFUTED at the activity layer** and must not be relied on again until re-proven.
The separation is real at the layers below and fails at the one that matters:

| layer | separated? | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Steam identity | YES | `steamid:...861` / `steamid:...862`, both present |
| BAP svc-25 sign-on | YES | `matched=slot0 served=0` / `matched=slot1 served=1` |
| **published activity identity** | **NO** | both `acct=0x9EAA300100100100` |

Something between "svc-25 served account 1" and "the client publishes its
identity" collapses the rig onto account 0's third character. That is the open
question and it is now the front. **Not investigated yet:** whether account 1's
provisioned characters were ever created in its own band, whether the rig's
client caches a character selection across accounts, or whether the character
list served to slot 1 is account 0's.

### And this voids the trailing-field result, for the third time

`packed_masks` was refused - but so would every other reading have been, because
the refusal happens on the peer's IDENTITY and never reaches the question of what
`peer_and_player_counts` means. This is the same trap as 20.54 (verdicts measured
against a self-peer) in a new costume: **a peer that is not genuinely foreign
cannot test anything downstream of being foreign.** The sweep is not wrong and
does not need changing; it needs a real second account to run against.

Do not re-pin and re-run the sweep. It cannot produce a valid result yet.

### Our own guard has the matching blind spot

`foreign_member_identity` (activity_session_lookup.cpp:130) excludes a candidate
that shares the caller's `memberKey` - the p2(45) self-peer fix - and checks
nothing else. It does NOT compare `accountSoid`. Two sessions of one ACCOUNT on
two machines therefore sail straight through and get published to each other.

Adding that check would have converted tonight's double freeze into a log line
(`reason=same_account`) before a single peer body shipped. That is the cheap
build, and it is the shape of the p2(45) fix one level up: 20.37 fixed it for
matchmaking on session id, p2(45) fixed it for membership on member key, and the
account is the level neither reached.

### Chain marks after this boot (lesson 16)

| link | mark |
|---|---|
| type 14 = `{u64 reason BE, u64 memberKey LE}` | **verified by execution** - both captures, keys byte-exact |
| reason 1 = `tried-to-join-self`, 5 = `privacy-mode` | **verified by reading** - the client's own enum in the image |
| both machines publish the same account soid | **verified by execution** - two identity lines this boot |
| account 1 was never served | **verified by execution** - its band appears once, at startup |
| the shared account CAUSES the refusal | **inference**, strong - it is the literal meaning of the enum entry, and the identities are literally the same account. Not yet proven by a controlled test. |
| a genuinely separate account fixes the peer path | **unknown** - the next boot, once there is one |
| the trailing fields mean anything in particular | **untestable until then** |

### Deployed state (unchanged - nothing was rebuilt for this entry)

```
server exe   c6e65a3dbc71f671  (p2(48b))
fork         upstream-gameplay-scoped @ 845c278, clean
settings     membership_sweep_pin: 0 (packed_masks, ARMED), retry cap 2
```

**Disarm the peer row before the next casual boot:** `membership_sweep_pin: 5`.

## 20.63 p2(47) BOOT: PASS. THE CLIENT ACCEPTS THE FOUR-FIELD TRAILER - ACKED
## THREE TIMES, LOADED INTO THE TOWER, STEADY AT REVISION 4. THE SCHEMA READ IS
## CONFIRMED ON THE WIRE. (2026-08-26 ~02:00)

### The result

```
ev=activity stage=membership_ack result=ok revision=2 bytes=4 session=...107
ev=activity stage=membership_ack result=ok revision=3 bytes=4 session=...107
ev=activity stage=membership_ack result=ok revision=4 bytes=4 session=...107
ev=activity stage=roster result=ok dest=city_tower_social_d2 state=3 region=56 slice=48
ev=activity stage=keepalive result=ok membership=1 revision=4 advert=2   (x34, steady)
```

No `encode_fail`. No hang. The client loaded fully into the tower and held
revision 4 for the rest of the run.

### Provenance of the body itself, not just of the binary

The push line reads `type=12 body=3882` on every membership body. That number is
the proof, because it can only be produced by the new encoder:

```
p2(47)  (30,032 + 1,024) / 8 = 3,882      <- observed, every body
p2(46)  (29,968 + 1,024) / 8 = 3,874
```

(1,024 bits = `kDescriptorSize` 128 B; a citizen advertisement was present all
run - `advert=2`.) The wire moved by exactly the +8 bytes the change predicts, so
the client acknowledged a body that demonstrably carried four trailing fields.
Deployed hash `c8f6bde2a8f16303` == built hash, checked before the boot.

### What this does and does NOT establish

**Established:** the client accepts a type-12 body carrying all four declared
trailing fields, at 3,882 bytes with a citizen descriptor. The 20.62 reading of
the schema - four presence-flagged 32-bit fields at presence indices 996-999 - is
confirmed against the running client, and the boot's decisive negative (bodies
sent, none acknowledged) did not occur.

**NOT established, and this is the honest limit (lesson 11 - the exoneration is
only valid in the direction it was tested):** that the client READS fields [6]
and [7]. They sit at the END of the body, so a client that parses only the fields
it needs and stops would accept the two-field and four-field forms alike. What
the boot rules out is REJECTION - a length check, a trailing-bit check, or a
parse that runs off the end. It does not prove consumption. Consumption only
becomes visible when the fields carry values that differ from each other, which
requires a second member, which is the next boot.

Note also that all four fields carried the value 1 by design, so **no semantics
were tested**: with one member a slot mask, a member count and packed counts are
the same number. That was the point.

### The residual: launch 1 acknowledged nothing, and it is not explained

The user launched twice. The first session (...105) DID reach the tower
(`stage=roster ... dest=city_tower_social_d2 state=3`), took four membership
bodies over ~10 s, ran its revision 0 -> 1 -> 2, and sent **zero** type-38
acknowledgements before the client went away at t=302734. The second (...107)
acknowledged three times, the first within 3.3 s of its opening body.

Read as a quit, not a refusal: every line for ...105 stops at the same tick,
including the BAP crypt lines, then 122 s of silence and a FRESH pair of svc-25
identity handshakes with a new session id. A frozen-but-connected client would
have kept receiving pushes every ~5 s and the log would show them.

But the asymmetry is real and is NOT fully explained, so it is recorded rather
than argued away. Candidates, none tested: the client may not acknowledge until
some later stage that launch 1 was quit before reaching; or the opening
revisions 0/1 may be applied without an acknowledgement. `membership_ack` is one
boot old, so there is no baseline to compare it against - the next boot gives one
for free and should be checked against this.

### Deployed state (unchanged by this entry)

```
server exe   c8f6bde2a8f16303   (p2(47b)), SIX gates rc=0
fork         upstream-gameplay-scoped @ baf5b07, clean
settings     membership_sweep_pin: 5  (solo - NO peer row), retry cap 6
client DLLs  eb893d2b1b6d8534  UNCHANGED on both machines
```

### Next

The peer boot is now the front, and it is a two-machine test that has hard-frozen
the Mac before. What it tests: whether a peer-bearing four-field body is accepted,
and - for the first time - what the trailing fields MEAN, because a slot mask (3)
and a member count (2) finally differ. The sweep machinery for that already
exists (`membership_sweep_pin` selects one of six readings) but it only drives
the FIRST TWO fields; extending it to four is the build that precedes that boot.

## 20.62 node+0x14 IS THE PRESENCE BITMAP, NOT THE WIRE WIDTH - AND WITH THAT
## FIXED THE TRANSCRIPTION LANDS A CONCRETE GAP: THE CLIENT DECLARES FOUR
## TOP-LEVEL TRAILING FIELDS AND WE HAVE ALWAYS SHIPPED TWO. p2(47) DEPLOYED.
## (2026-08-26 ~01:10)

### First, correcting 20.61 (mine, one entry old)

20.61 said `node+0x14` "states the wire width outright" and rebuilt `--find` on
it. **Wrong.** Our own type-12 body is 29,968 bits; the largest value at `+0x14`
anywhere in the registry is 8,029, and **92% of all 6,765 nodes read zero there**
- no message width can be zero. A `--find 29968 +/-800` across every live bucket
returns nothing.

What it actually counts, and this reproduces:

```
node+0x14 == sum over fields of (1 if presence else 0)
             + (a nested field's own node+0x14, whether flagged or not)
```

**6,709 of 6,765 nodes match exactly.** It is the size of the schema's PRESENCE
BITMAP, and the per-field dword at `+0x28` is that field's INDEX into it - which
is why type-12's fields read 0, 992, 996, 997, 998, 999 and the roster's element
reads 31. `presence_bits()` replaces the misnamed `total_bits()`; the walker's
`--tree` now prints `pidx=` rather than `bits=` so the number cannot be misread
as a wire offset again.

The residual is named, not hidden: **56 nodes under-predict by 1-4**, 13 of them
inside the type-12 tree and every one of those in the region subtree - never the
roster or member-row spine, which are exact. Some field kind contributes a bit
this rule does not model. Read the stated value; the rule is a cross-check only.

Note what went wrong: 20.61 checked the model against the four numbers it was
derived from (1000, 992, 31, 3) and shipped. The whole-registry check that
refuted it costs one pass and should have run first. **A model fitted on four
points and tested on the same four points has not been tested.**

### The transcription: schema vs. our encoder

Our encoder and the client's schema AGREE on the roster, exactly:

| | client schema | `replicate_membership.h` |
|---|---|---|
| roster slots | ARRAY of **32** (`0x808086A9`) | `kMemberCount` = 32 |
| member row | `0x808086AF`, **3** flagged fields | absent slot costs **3** bits |
| row content | identity block + 28-bit block + 5-bit field | 676-bit populated row |

An absent member row costing exactly 3 bits IS the row's three presence flags.
Two independent derivations of the same structure - one from a dump, one written
months ago from wire observation - agree. The member-row shape is **not** the
problem, which is what the six-shape sweep concluded the expensive way.

### The gap, at the top level

```
0x808086A8  struct, 9 fields, 1000 presence bits
  [0] pidx 0    type 9  presence 0  w32     <- unconditional     (revision)
  [1] pidx 0    type 9  presence 0  w32     <- unconditional     (epoch)
  [2] pidx 0    type 1  presence 0  -> 0x808086A9   roster[32]
  [3] pidx 992  type 1  presence 1  -> 0x808086AC   region block
  [4] pidx 996  type 9  presence 1  w32     <- WE SEND THIS
  [5] pidx 997  type 9  presence 1  w32     <- WE SEND THIS
  [6] pidx 998  type 9  presence 1  w32     <- ABSENT-BIT, ALWAYS
  [7] pidx 999  type 9  presence 1  w32     <- ABSENT-BIT, ALWAYS
  [8] pidx 0    type 2  presence 0
```

**Four** flagged 32-bit fields. Our encoder wrote
`write(1,1) write(first,32) write(1,1) write(second,32) write(0,1) write(0,1) write(0,1)`
- two published, the rest absent.

Lane M's parsed-struct field registry names four consecutive fields in exactly
that position: `peer_and_player_counts, peer_updates, player_updates,
player_seq_number`. Four declared, four named. **[4] and [5] are the two we fill;
`player_updates` and `player_seq_number` have never been sent at all.** Lesson 17:
that is a missing requirement, not a spare.

This also sharpens the standing count-vs-mask question rather than answering it.
If [4] is `peer_and_player_counts` then every body we have ever shipped has put a
slot MASK in a COUNTS field - invisible while solo, because with one member mask
and count are both 1, and divergent the moment a peer appears (mask 3, count 2).
That remains inference and this boot does not test it.

### p2(47): publish all four (deployed)

Wire delta +64 bits: 29,968 -> **30,032** meaningful bits, 3,746 -> **3,754**
bytes, still byte-aligned; a peer body goes 3,839.

The two new fields default to the SAME historical value as the other two, so a
solo body carries 1 in all four - where mask, count and packed counts are the
same number. **The boot therefore commits to no semantics and tests only whether
the client accepts the four-field shape.**

### Two instruments, because neither existed

1. **`--membership-wire-test`, a sixth deploy gate.** The only prior check on this
   encoder was `static_assert(kMeaningfulBitCount == kEncodedSize * 8)` - a
   constant against a constant, which passes just as happily for a wrong pair.
   The gate encodes real solo and peer bodies, seeks to `region_block_end_bit`,
   and checks each field returns the distinct value it went in with.
   **Verified to FAIL (rc=1)** against a deliberately reverted two-field encoder
   before being trusted - an instrument nobody has seen fail is not an
   instrument.
2. **`ev=activity stage=membership_ack`** (p2(47b)). The type-38 acknowledgement
   is the client's ONLY positive verdict on a membership body and it wrote
   nothing to the log, so every boot to date inferred acceptance from the absence
   of symptoms - the exact shape lesson 13 exists to stop. Reports `ok` /
   `not_staged` / `parse_failed`, so silence now means "no type-38 arrived".

### Deployed state

```
server exe   c8f6bde2a8f16303   (p2(47b)), SIX gates rc=0, built == deployed
fork         upstream-gameplay-scoped @ baf5b07, clean
settings     membership_sweep_pin: 5  (solo - NO peer row), retry cap 6
client DLLs  eb893d2b1b6d8534  UNCHANGED on both machines
```

Boot brief with both negatives pre-named: **`BOOT_BRIEF_p2-47.md`** at the root.
It is the ONE-MACHINE CONTROL - one client on the Mac, rig stays off.

## 20.61 THERE IS NO DUMP-TIME-STATE WALL. THE WALKER'S ARITHMETIC WAS WRONG IN
## TWO MORE PLACES; WITH THEM FIXED ALL 31 KEYS RESOLVE OUT OF THE 08-15 DUMP AND
## THE ENTIRE TYPE-12 MEMBERSHIP SCHEMA IS DECODED. NO LIVE CAPTURE IS NEEDED.
## (2026-08-25 ~23:40)

### Retracting 20.60's conclusion (lesson 13, applied to an offline instrument)

20.60 concluded: "the resolver formula, the keys, and the table are each
individually confirmed - and mutually incompatible", and specified a gated live
boot to capture the registry pointer. **That conclusion is wrong and the boot is
not needed.** All three WERE individually right. The thing between them - the
Python walker - was wrong, and a null it produced was read as a fact about the
game rather than a fact about the instrument.

This is lesson 13 ("a null result indicts the instrument before the system") in a
place the lesson had not been applied before: it was written for deployed
instruments and a boot, and the same failure shape happened in an offline script
against a static file. The countermeasure generalises with it - **the walker
needed an oracle it could fail against**, and it had none. It has one now: every
node stores its own packed key at `+0x08`, so a resolution can be checked instead
of merely looking plausible. That check is now in `node_for` and every claim
below is gated on it.

### Bug 1 - the sign fill in `bucket_of` was six bits too wide

20.59 correctly identified that `sar esi,0xd` fills from the KEY's sign bit and
fixed the wrong-value test. The replacement mask was still wrong:

```
sar esi, 0xd     ; result bits 18..0 = key bits 31..13
                 ; result bits 31..19 = 13 copies of the sign bit
```

`key >> 13` in Python already puts the surviving bits at 18..0, so the sign fill
is `0xFFF80000`. 20.59 used `0xFFFFE000`, which fills from bit **13** and so
overwrites bits 18..13 - six real key bits - before the mask is applied:

```
key 0x808086A8   20.59 -> bucket 9220     asm-faithful -> bucket 1028
key 0x80806AC0   20.59 -> bucket 9219     asm-faithful -> bucket 1027
```

Buckets ~9000 read zeros. Buckets 1024-1028 are live, populated, and hold exactly
the keys we were looking for. The "keys land in high buckets that read zeros"
result in 20.60 measured the bug, not the dump.

Verified against the real bytes twice: `FUN_1404c1930`, and the identical
sequence at `FUN_1404c72e0+0x39`, which is where the type-12 walk actually enters
(`RE_scripts/disasm_fn.py 0x1404c72e0`). The membership call site is unchanged
from 20.60: `mov r9d,[rax]` @ `0x1404F73D9` off global `0x141FA4180`, key
`0x808086A8`.

Note also - and this kills a claim in 20.59 and one in `--find` - `movzx eax, si`
keeps only the low 16 bits, so key bits 31..29 never reach the bucket. **The
bucket is not an invertible encoding of the key**, and `(bucket << 13) | idx`
does not reconstruct one. `--find` printed such a value as `key=`. It now reads
the key back from `node+0x08` instead.

### Bug 2 - the pointer arithmetic never wrapped

```
imul eax, edi                     ; 32-bit multiply
and  rcx, qword ptr [rax + 8]
sub  rax, rcx                     ; 64-bit MODULAR subtract
```

The stored adjustment at `slot+8` is routinely larger than the slot address, so
the subtract borrows. Python has arbitrary-precision integers and produced a
**negative VA**, which `read_va` reported as "not mapped" - indistinguishable, to
the caller, from an absent node. Every node in every live bucket looked absent
for this reason alone. Both `node_at` and `node_for` now mask to 64 bits, and the
index scale to 32.

### Bug 3 - the field count was reading the message's bit size

`node+0x14` is the schema's TOTAL BIT SIZE (1000 for type-12), not a count.
Walking `count+1 = 1001` records off a 9-record array is what produced the pages
of garbage field rows. `FUN_1404bd2a0`'s prologue gives the real rule:

```
movsxd rdx,[rcx+0x18] ; test edx,edx ; setg al   ; kind flag
mov rax,rdx ; jne .keep ; mov rax,[rcx]          ; limit
```

### Bug 4 - and that flag means THERE ARE TWO KINDS OF NODE

The loop branches on the same saved flag:

- **flag 0 - STRUCT.** `lea rax,[rbx+rbx*4]` / `[r14+rax*8+0x31]`: one 0x28-byte
  record per field at `node + 0x28 + i*0x28`. Count is `*(u64*)node`.
- **flag 1 - ARRAY** (branch `0x1404bd525`). ONE element descriptor at a fixed
  offset, whose dwords at `+0x20/+0x24/+0x28` are `imul`-ed by the loop index.
  `node+0x18` is an ELEMENT count.

Reading an array node as a struct walks the index-scaling dwords as field records
and yields noise. That is precisely what made `0x808086A9` - a 32-slot member
array - print as "32 garbage fields".

### The result: 31 of 31 keys resolve, each self-verified

Every key from 20.60's pointer-chain table now resolves in the 08-15 dump and
each lands on a node whose `+0x08` equals the key asked for:

```
resolved 31 / 31  (failed 0)
0x141FA4180  key 0x808086A8  bucket 1028 idx 0x6A8  node 0x7FF6454AA438  9 fields  1000 bits
0x141FA3EC8  key 0x80808716  bucket 1028 idx 0x716  node 0x7FF645402210 16 fields    10 bits
0x14209ED28  key 0x80805BD0  bucket 1026 idx 0x1BD0 node 0x7FF64541FB90 14 fields   945 bits
...  (buckets 1024, 1026, 1027, 1028 - the live ones)
```

### The type-12 membership schema, decoded

Full tree in **`RE_output/claims/msg12-schema-decoded.md`**, with per-link
evidence marks. The shape:

```
0x808086A8  struct, 9 fields, 1000 bits
  [2] -> 0x808086A9  ARRAY of 32 x 31 bits    <- the roster: THIRTY-TWO fixed slots
           elem -> 0x808086AF  struct, 3 fields, 31 bits   <- THE MEMBER ROW
                     [0] @0  -> 0x808086BC  identity block (7 fields, two 8-byte blobs)
                     [1] @1  -> 0x808086B1  28 bits, 4 nested
                     [2] @30 type 3 width 5
  [3] @992 -> 0x808086AC  trailer, 3 bits, incl. a 64-entry array 0x808086AD
  [4..7] @996..999  four 32-bit fields, presence-flagged
```

The project swept six member-row shapes blind and filed the row under DEAD ENDS.
The client's own answer is now readable: a **fixed 32-slot** roster of **31-bit**
rows. The trailer `0x808086AC` is where the counts-vs-masks argument has been
happening.

**One thing here is inference, marked as such** (lesson 16): the meaning of the
per-field `bits` dword. Reading it as a bit offset closes two independent budgets
exactly - 992 + 4 + 4 = 1000 for the top level, and 1 + 28 + 1 + 1 = 31 for the
member row. That is suggestive, not proof; it has never been checked against a
captured wire message. Do not change the encoder on it alone.

### Two more corrections that fell out, both of them to our own instruments

**The bucket census in 20.60 was backwards.** It reported buckets 0-23 as "the
only genuinely populated ones" and everything above as heap false positives. A
census that counts only SELF-VERIFYING nodes - a node whose `+0x08` key
re-derives the very slot it was found in - gives:

```
bucket 1024   315 nodes        buckets 0..1023   0 nodes
bucket 1025  1339 nodes
bucket 1026  1354 nodes        total in 0..1199: 6,765
bucket 1027  2024 nodes
bucket 1028  1733 nodes
```

The registry is **6,765 schema nodes in five buckets**, and buckets 0-23 hold
none of it. `--find`'s default range was `24`, i.e. exactly the region with
nothing in it.

**`--find` was reconstructing a number the node states.** `expand()` sums the
per-field dword at `+0x28` as though it were a per-field size. It is not: for a
struct it is the field's bit OFFSET, for an array the element stride. Summing
offsets is meaningless - an EXACT `--find 1000` over the bucket that contains
the 1000-bit type-12 schema returned **zero matches**. `node+0x14` states the
number directly. `--find` now reads it, filters on the self-verifying key, and
returns exactly one hit:

> **CORRECTION (20.62): `node+0x14` is NOT the wire width.** This entry calls it
> "the wire width" here and below; that is wrong and 20.62 measures what it
> actually is (the size of the schema's PRESENCE BITMAP). `--find` is still
> correct - it matches on the stated value - but read it as "find a schema whose
> presence-bit count is N", never as a byte/bit size of the message.

```
MATCH bucket=1028 idx=1704 key=0x808086A8 node=0x7FF6454AA438 struct(9) 1000 bits
```

`expand()` is kept (find_diag.py calls it) but marked superseded in the source.
The 20.59 note that "re-running `--find 29968` is now the cheapest calibration
path" is therefore void - the search it describes could not have worked.

### Tooling

`RE_scripts/schema_walk.py` carries all four fixes, the self-verifying key check,
`node_shape()`/`field_count()`/`elem_bits()` for the two node kinds,
`total_bits()` (badly named - see the 20.62 correction), a corrected `--find`,
and a new `--tree <key> [depth]` that
expands nested walks. `--table`, `--node`, `--key` all still work. The dump is
LOCAL (`RE_output/content/dump_healthy_inproc.dmp`) - 20.58's note that this must
run on the rig is not true for the Mac tree, and none of the above touched the
rig or the deployed server.

### What this does NOT change

Nothing is deployed and nothing was booted. Server stays `fcc65072fdfcf200`
(p2(46)), `membership_sweep_pin: 5`, client DLLs `eb893d2b1b6d8534`. The peer
front's blocker is still the unanswered request/response pairs from the 20.55
census; what changed is that the schema needed to build them is now readable
offline, on demand, for every message type - and the gated live-capture boot
specified at the end of 20.60 should not be run.

## 20.60 THE PACKED KEYS WERE FOUND - ALL OF THEM - AND THE DUMP'S REGISTRY
## REFUSES EVERY ONE. THE OFFLINE ROUTE IS WALL-ED AT DUMP-TIME STATE, NOT AT
## KEY DISCOVERY. (2026-08-25 ~21:53)

### First, a correction to 20.59 (lesson 11 applied to myself)

20.59 concluded the 0x808xxxxx values are "hashes of another domain, never
keys". WRONG DIRECTION. This session read 31 REAL runtime keys out of the dump
through the exact pointer chains the client's own code dereferences, and every
one is 0x808xxxxx. They ARE packed keys; the sar/sign-fill path in the resolver
exists precisely to serve them. 20.59's arithmetic claim ("bucket = key[13..23]
for bit31-clear keys") stays true but is irrelevant: real keys are not
bit31-clear.

### The keys, verified

`RE_scripts/keysource_sweep.py` found **72 call sites** of the four walk
entries (byte-level E8 scan; a linear capstone sweep stalls at ~6k insns on
embedded data - do not repeat it). 41 sites classify cleanly and share ONE key
delivery pattern:

```
mov rax, qword ptr [rip + d]     ; ptr-global in .data
mov ecx|r9d, dword ptr [rax]     ; THE packed schema key
call c74b0 | c72e0 | ...
```

The 31 distinct ptr-globals (list in RE_scripts/keys_from_globals.py) resolve
through the dump to 31 distinct keys, all `0x8080_0007..0x80809c42`. The
membership orchestrator 0x1404F7240's own site (global **0x141FA4180**) yields
**key 0x808086A8** - the type-12 membership walk key, read from the same
chain the parse uses (`mov r9d,[rax]; call 0x1404C72e0` @ 0x1404F73DC).

W1 CLAIM 2's DAT_14204e7b8/ee38 turned out to be POINTER globals whose targets
read ZERO in this dump - not direct keys.

### The wall

With corrected arithmetic (20.59's fix), every one of the 31 keys computes a
bucket in the ~9000s, and the dump's registry reads ZEROS there:

  - full-range census (table_census.py): only buckets 0..23 are genuinely
    populated; "live" entries beyond are heap false-positives (502 raw hits,
    implausible strides/bases).
  - probe_idx.py swept all live buckets at idx 0x6A8 (the membership key's
    index half): no plausible node anywhere - the hits are the known
    empty-array noise pattern (uniform 0x38480 spacing, count=0).
  - needle_scan.py searched the ENTIRE dump for two key values. Hits:
    the expected heap cells, the image-side {ptr,key,pad,ptr} records
    (static ~0x141FAF818 for a8; 0x14206F0F0 for ac0 = the table 20.59
    found), and small .vmp0-range descriptor structs `{0x8080010A, N, 0,
    KEY, ...}` - NO registry entry anywhere maps these keys to nodes.

So the resolver formula (asm-faithful), the keys (runtime-verified), and the
table (dump-read) are each individually confirmed - and mutually incompatible.
The strongest remaining hypothesis: **DAT_142439C70's registry instance is
phase-dependent** - repointed/rebuilt as subsystems load - and the 08-15 dump
froze an instance that predates or postdates activity-parse registration. The
31 keys prove registration HAPPENED in that boot (their cells hold coherent
values); the table just does not serve them at dump time.

### What this sets up: the live capture is now one boot, fully specified

Route 3 (Lane M's) was "capture the key live". The sharper instrument, given
tonight: our DLL reads, once per activity-message parse window,

  1. *(qword)(module_base + 0x2439C70)   -> registry object R
  2. *(qword)R                           -> table T      <- compare vs dump
  3. T + b*64 raw 0x40 bytes for bucket_of(0x808086A8)

plus a hook logging r9d at 0x1404C72E0's entry. If T differs from
0x18CDA4AE380-class addresses per boot (expected), the repointing hypothesis
is CONFIRMED and the SAME boot can then dump the LIVE table + the resolved
membership node straight through our own log - permanent artifact, no more
dump archaeology. Everything downstream (transcribe fields, compare against
our encoder, build types 9/10/17/45) is already built and waiting (20.57).

## 20.59 ROUTE 2 CLOSED AND IT WAS DOUBLE: THE WALKER'S SIGN SEMANTICS WERE
## INVERTED *AND* OUR TWO CANDIDATE KEYS WERE NEVER KEYS - THEY ARE HASHES OF A
## DIFFERENT DOMAIN. TASK 4a SHIPPED AS p2(46). (2026-08-25 ~21:25)

### The bucket arithmetic bug (route 2) - verified against the real bytes

Disassembled FUN_1404c1930 fresh from the local unpacked PE
(`RE_scripts/disasm_fn.py`, capstone):

```
sar   esi, 0xd        ; hi = (int32)KEY >> 13 - sign-fill comes from the KEY's bit 31
movzx eax, si         ; (uint16)hi
mov   ecx, esi        ; rcx = ZERO-extended hi (32-bit pattern)
or    rcx, 0xffc0000
shr   rcx, 0x12       ; logical
and   rcx, rax        ; bucket
```

`schema_walk.py`'s `bucket_of()` tested bit 31 AFTER its own logical shift and
then subtracted `1<<32`. That sign-checks the wrong value: x86 `sar` fills from
bit 31 of the ORIGINAL key. For any key with bit31 set the two disagree
completely. Smoke test (`old vs new`):

```
key 0x80806ac0  old-> bucket 3     new-> bucket 9219
key 0x80808635  old-> bucket 4     new-> bucket 9220
key 0x00000123  old-> bucket 0     new-> bucket 0    (low keys unaffected)
```

**Both candidate keys ever probed have bit31 set.** Every "node absent" they
produced was measured in the wrong bucket. Fixed in `bucket_of()`
(`hi |= 0xFFFFE000` when the KEY's sign bit is set).

### The second, deeper finding: those values are not packed keys

For any key whose bit31 is clear, `(hi | 0xFFC0000) >> 18 & (uint16)hi`
reduces to exactly **bits 13..23 of the key** - so the packing is

```
packed_key = (bucket << 13) | index      bucket <= 0x3FF, index <= 0x1FFF
```

and a well-formed packed key never has bit31 set. `0x80806AC0` therefore is not
a resolvable key AT ALL; it belongs to some other identifier space (a hash).
Route 1's hypothesis ("a schema hash may not BE a packed key") is confirmed by
arithmetic alone. Note `0x80806AC0 & 0x1FFFFFF` decodes to bucket 3 / idx
0xAC0 - which is precisely where the OLD buggy math looked and failed. The old
null result was overdetermined: wrong arithmetic AND wrong key domain.

### Consequence: --find needs no key, so neither does calibration

`--find` enumerates `(bucket, idx)` slots directly via `node_at()` and never
touches `bucket_of()`. Since the packing is now known, any hit yields its own
packed key `(bucket << 13) | idx` - `--find` now prints it per match.

**The recursive-expansion fix to `expand()` was landed after the last recorded
`--find 29968` run and that search has never been re-run.** Re-running it
against dump_healthy_inproc.dmp is now the cheapest calibration path and needs
no live capture. If the fully-expanded type-12 total (29,968 bits) matches a
node, we hold the key AND the layout, offline.

### New evidence recorded (do not re-derive): a hash->pointer table in stored .data

`RE_scripts/find_u32.py` (new) located both constants in stored sections:

- `.data 0x14206ED00..0x14206FCF0`: **255 records** `{u32 hash, u32 pad=0,
  u64 runtime-ptr}`, 16 B stride, hashes CONSECUTIVE DESCENDING
  0x80806B2B -> 0x808069F5, and 0x80806AC0 sits among them. Pointers resolve
  to static ~0x1432xxxxx - past the .data cut, 0/255 stored.
- Consumers (via `RE_scripts/hashtable_xref_scan.py`, 321 hits): one large
  registrar function ~0x1418413D5 LEAs nearly every record individually; two
  readers (`0x140EE043A`, `0x140EE3673`) load the descriptor global at
  0x14206ECF0 (u32 at +0, pointer at +8). No .text immediate references to
  0x80806AC0 exist - hashes are computed at runtime, consistent with 20.58.
- Purpose UNRESOLVED. Recorded so the next session starts from the addresses.

## 20.58 SCHEMA WALK, STOPPING STATE: THE REGISTRY IS OPEN AND THE GRAMMAR IS
## VERIFIED, BUT NO KEY RESOLVES YET. LANE M's LIVE CAPTURE WAS NOT SUCCESSFULLY
## SKIPPED. (2026-08-25 ~22:10)

Recorded at a session boundary so the next session does not repeat any of it.

### Verified, and safe to build on

  - `TABLE = *(*(0x142439C70))` - **two dereferences**. One level yields a struct
    whose +0x30 is zero, which makes every bucket look empty.
  - Two DIFFERENT strides, and conflating them silently misreads everything:
    **registry entries are 0x40 apart** (`shl rcx,6` in FUN_1404c1930);
    **field entries are 0x28 apart** (`lea rax,[rbx+rbx*4]` with `[r14+rax*8]`
    and `inc rbx` in FUN_1404bd2a0).
  - Field offsets (+0x28 bits, +0x30 type, +0x31 presence, +0x34 sub-key,
    +0x3C width) are relative to `node + idx*0x28`, so the entry BODY starts one
    stride in. That is why they appear to overflow a 40-byte record and do not,
    and it reconciles W1 CLAIM 3's "0x28-B entry" with its own offset list.
  - Against dump_healthy_inproc.dmp: registry 0x18CDA4F69B8 -> table
    0x18CDA4AE380, **24/24 buckets live** with plausible bases and strides
    (64, 32, 284, 320, 160, 24, 8, 72, 648).

### NOT working: no packed key resolves

Every key tried returns `node absent (relative pointer 0)`:

  - `0x80806AC0` - our own `world_population_schema_hash`, known-good for this
    client
  - `0x80808635` - a same-family value sitting at 0x141f91ab8, just before the
    type-name table

And probing buckets directly is meaningless: the bucket is DERIVED from the key.
Bucket 2's array turns out to hold `{u16 index, 0xFEFE, ..., u32 hash}` records
at 0x40 stride - not schema nodes at all.

Also ruled out: **no walk call site loads its key as an immediate.** A scan of
both .text spans for `mov ecx, imm32` within 0x40 bytes of a call to
FUN_1404c74b0 / FUN_1404c1930 / FUN_1404c74d0 / FUN_1404c72e0 found ZERO. Keys
travel in data or registers.

### The honest conclusion

I tried to skip Lane M's step (a) - capture the packed key live at
0x1404c72e0's entry - by finding a schema through its bit-total signature
instead. That requires resolving nodes, resolving nodes requires a key, and no
key has been obtained. **The live capture was not successfully skipped.**

What the dump DID retire is the claim that statics cannot reach the registry:
the registry is open and readable offline. What it did not retire is the need
for one key.

### Candidate routes for the next session, cheapest first

  1. A schema HASH (0x8080xxxx) may not BE a packed key. The packed key is split
     `index = key & 0x1FFF`, `hi = key >> 13`, so a valid key's low 13 bits are
     an index into a bucket array. Look for where a hash is TRANSLATED into a
     packed key - that translation is the missing step, and it is probably a
     lookup we can read.
  2. Verify the bucket arithmetic on signed 32-bit semantics. `sar esi,0xd` is
     arithmetic and `movzx eax,si` takes the low 16 bits of the SHIFTED value;
     the Python implementation in schema_walk.py mirrors this but has never been
     checked against a known-good key, because there is not one yet.
  3. Lane M's original route: capture the key live at 0x1404c72e0's entry, one
     boot with a small hook. Everything downstream of the key is already built
     and verified.

---

## 20.57 THE SCHEMA REGISTRY IS OPEN. RESOLVER FORMULA READ FROM THE
## DISASSEMBLY, REGISTRY WALKED LIVE OUT OF THE 08-15 DUMP - 24/24 BUCKETS
## POPULATED. CALIBRATION NOT YET LANDED. (2026-08-25 ~21:40)

### The resolver formula, verbatim from FUN_1404c1930

W1 CLAIM 1 left it as a placeholder ("the exact resolver formula"). Read off the
disassembly of static 0x1404c1930:

```
index    = key & 0x1FFF                                  low 13 bits
hi       = key >> 13                                     arithmetic
bucket   = ((hi | 0xFFC0000) >> 18) & (uint16)hi
entry    = TABLE + bucket*0x40                           64-byte registry entries
resolved = *(entry+8) + *(entry+0x30)*index              base + stride*index
resolved-= *(resolved+8) & (int32)*(entry+0x34)
node     = (resolved+0x48) + *(resolved+0x48)            RELATIVE; 0 = absent
count    = *(uint32*)(node+0x14) + 1
```

**TABLE IS TWO DEREFERENCES DEEP**, and this is the trap: `mov rax,[rip+X]`
loads the CONTENTS of 0x142439C70, then `add rcx,[rax]` dereferences again. So
`TABLE = *(*(0x142439C70))`. Reading one level gives a struct whose +0x30 is
zero, which makes every bucket look empty - a 4096-bucket scan reported zero
live entries before this was corrected.

### Verified live against dump_healthy_inproc.dmp

```
registry 0x18CDA4F69B8 -> table 0x18CDA4AE380   (destiny2 base 0x7FF641BF0000)
bucket  0  base 0x18CDA4AE380  stride 64   mask 0
bucket  1  base 0x18CDB776C80  stride 32   mask -1
bucket  7  base 0x18CDB826080  stride 284  mask 0
bucket  8  base 0x18CDB829600  stride 320  mask 0
bucket 23  base 0x18CDC55B680  stride 648  mask 0
```

24 of 24 buckets live, with plausible per-bucket strides. The registry Lane M
could not reach is open, offline, from a file that already existed.

### Tooling landed

  - `RE_scripts/minidump_reader.py` - parses the module + full-memory streams of
    a minidump and resolves absolute or destiny2-relative VAs. Runs on the rig
    (Python 3.13 present), so 5.7 GB never crosses the network.
  - `RE_scripts/schema_walk.py` - the formula above, plus `--table` (registry
    census) and `--find <bits>` (locate a schema by total width).

### What has NOT landed, and why

Calibration. The plan was to find the type-12 schema by its known total of
29,968 bits and check the walker reproduces our own layout before trusting it
anywhere else. `--find 29968` across 12 buckets returns nothing, and the reason
is structural, not a bug: CLAIM 3 field type 1 is a NESTED WALK through the
sub-key at entry+0x34, so a schema's own entries do NOT sum to the message
size. 29,968 is the FULLY EXPANDED type-12 body; the top-level node sums to far
less.

Next, in order:
  1. Make the bit-sum recursive - expand type-1 fields through their sub-key
     before comparing. Then re-run the 29,968 search.
  2. That match IS the calibration. Only after it reproduces our known layout
     should any unknown schema be transcribed.
  3. Then transcribe the types we cannot currently build: 9, 10, 17, 45 and
     whatever grants a reservation.

Do not skip step 2. The trailing-field episode is what transcribing without
calibration costs.

## CAPTURE. (2026-08-25 ~21:05)

The user asked whether the rig already held a dump before we spent a boot making

5.73 GB  ...\destiny-preservation\RE_output\content\dump_healthy_inproc.dmp
3.70 GB  ...\destiny-preservation\RE_output\content\dump_failing_external.dmp

THAT ARTIFACT. They are not true of these dumps.

So the schema read needs **no live hook, no breakpoint, no boot**. It is now

## 20.55 PROTOCOL CENSUS: WE ACCEPT REQUESTS AND NEVER ANSWER THEM. THE CLIENT
## RELEASED A PEER RESERVATION 56 ms AFTER WE NAMED A PEER, AND WE THREW THAT
## MESSAGE AWAY. (2026-08-25 ~20:40)

Deliverable: RE_output/claims/protocol-census-2026-08-25.md. Client type table
read from the dump (59 types, 0..58); our coverage taken from the dispatch
chain, the kAcceptedMessages table, and - for what we emit - the OBSERVED types
arriving at the client, because grepping our tree for the client's names
produced two wrong censuses first.

### Coverage

17 dispatched, 14 accepted-with-no-work, 22 with no handling at all. We emit
six: 0, 1, 4, 5, 12, 54.

### The shape of the gap

Not "types we skipped" - **requests we accept and never answer**:

  8  request_activity_host   -> 9/10 start_activity_host(_response)  never sent
  13 request_peer_reservation -> grant / 45 reservations_failed      never sent
  16 keepalive_request        -> 17 keepalive_response               never sent
  (3 join_request -> 4 join_result IS answered; that pair is fine.)

`kAcceptedMessages` carries the assumption in its own comment: *"The Client
senders that carry no work for this host. Each is one-way, so accepting is the
whole contract."* Types 13 and 14 sit in that table. We decided peer
reservations were one-way and they are not.

This is Universal Lesson 4 - "the reply envelope IS part of the contract" -
wearing the costume it wore on the equip front, where the missing piece was a
completion token and five correct payloads all failed without it.

### What the client actually said

In the first-foreign-peer capture, the client's only activity traffic besides
heartbeats:

```
first peer-bearing body   t=449666
release_peer_reservation  t=449722   (+56 ms)
release_peer_reservation  t=451294   (+1.6 s)
```

**56 milliseconds after being told it had a peer, it released a peer
reservation.** We accept that message and discard it. The client has been
telling us its verdict in its own vocabulary the entire time.

It never sent `request_peer_reservation` (13), so a reservation is not something
it asks us for at that moment - whatever grants one, it is not a request we owe
an answer to. Mechanism NOT established; do not build on the guess.

### Honest limit, and why the next step is not a boot

The census says WHERE the gaps are. It does not say what goes in them: there is
no wire format in hand for types 9, 10, 17 or 45, and no confirmed shape for a
reservation grant. Filling them by guessing is exactly what the trailing-field
episode cost - three boots, two black screens and a hard freeze, all measured
against a self-peer.

So the next step is the schema read (Lane M's runtime route), not another
wiring-and-boot cycle. What CAN be built without any new format: stop
discarding type 14. Logging its payload turns the client's own verdict into
evidence, and it needs no wire format we do not already have.

## 20.54 THE SECOND GUARDIAN WAS THE MAC SEEING ITSELF. foreign_member_identity
## EXCLUDED ONLY THE CALLER'S SESSION ID, AND ONE CLIENT HOLDS SEVERAL.
## 20.53's MILESTONE IS RETRACTED. (2026-08-25 ~19:55)

The user reported two guardians in the Tower **with the rig not booted at all**.

### Verified by execution, one machine only

  - the Mac's own member key, from 21 keepalives: `0xB34F7851304A19CF`
  - the peer key in every body we published to it: `0xB34F7851304A19CF`

The same key. We were telling the client it was its own fireteam member. The
admin state shows the single machine holding TWO sessions (id=1 with a real
root, id=2 with root 0x0), which is the whole mechanism: one client opens
several BAP connections, so it owns several sessions.

`foreign_member_identity` skipped candidates on `record.sessionId ==
ownSessionId` and nothing else. A sibling session of the same client passes that
test, so the caller is handed its own identity.

**This is the same mistake FINDINGS 20.37 fixed for matchmaking's
foreign_advertisement, in the same shape, and it was never fixed here.** 20.37's
own text: "each BAP connection acquires its own matchmaking context, and one
client holds several connections - so a searcher was being handed its own
session back from its sibling link, which is nothing it can join."

### What 20.53 got wrong

Everything about it being a second PLAYER. The clone was named "You" because it
WAS the user; both were Hunters because it was one character; there was no peer
connection because no second machine was ever involved; the apparent correlation
with the rig's arrival was session churn, not causation.

What survives: **the client will render a body from a membership row.** That is
still true and still new - the roster does create a slot. It just filled that
slot with the local player, which is exactly what it does when handed the local
player's identity.

Also now suspect for the same reason: the trailing-field results. mask_mask
(3/3) and packed_mask (0x20002/3) were both "refuted" against a SELF-peer row.
Those verdicts are void - the client's refusal of a roster naming itself twice
says nothing about counts versus masks. The whole trailing sweep must be re-run
once the peer is genuinely foreign.

### The fix (p2(45))

Skip any candidate whose member key equals the caller's own. Two sessions of one
client share a member key; two machines do not - verified across both runs (one
machine: a single key across all keepalives; two machines: `0xB34F7851304A19CF`
and `0x846C8338F7D022E6`). New reason code `same_client`, surfaced as
`wire_snapshot ... reason=same_client`, so a self-peer can never again be
silently published.

### Method note

Three boots of trailing-field results are void because nobody checked the most
basic property of the peer we were publishing: that it was somebody else. The
check that caught it was the user booting ONE machine and seeing two guardians -
a control nobody had thought to run. A single-client boot is now the cheapest
falsifier available for anything peer-shaped, and it should be run FIRST.

---

## 20.53 A SECOND GUARDIAN RENDERS IN THE TOWER. IT IS A CLONE OF THE LOCAL
## PLAYER, WITH NO PEER CONNECTION ANYWHERE - THE ROSTER MAKES A BODY EXIST,
## THE PEER LINK WOULD MAKE IT THEM. (2026-08-25 ~18:41)

Server exe `d5d9c823fe4163e1` (p2(42)), both clients `eb893d2b`. Capture
preserved at RE_output/captures/2026-08-25_first-visible-peer/ (server + both
client logs + provenance).

### Verified

The user reached the Tower on the Mac; the rig took an unusually long ship load,
arrived, and went to a black screen. On the MAC, a SECOND HUNTER was then
visible in the Tower, named "You". Confirmed by the user to be a copy of their
own Hunter.

  - Both sessions' identities are distinct and correctly crossed server-side:
    Mac `soid 0x9EAA300100200001 / player 0x…0101 / key 0xB34F7851304A19CF`,
    rig `soid 0x9EAA300100200004 / player 0x…0103 / key 0x846C8338F7D022E6`.
    Every body carried the OTHER session's key. We are NOT publishing the local
    player as the peer.
  - **Zero `send_rendezvous`. 15 `ev=steamnet` lines, all sign-in scaffolding.**
    No peer-to-peer contact of any kind occurred.
  - The rig logged 110 `networking:session:membership:` lines with connectivity
    ratings (`nat=1, ratings: conn=0, hst=0, cli=0, ctvty=0001`) - a surface that
    has never appeared in any prior boot.

### What this corrects

**20.38 was half wrong, and so was 20.51.**

20.38 said visibility is peer-authored and needs the peer link. The server alone
just put a body in the world with no link at all. The correct split: the ROSTER
creates the slot; the PEER LINK supplies who occupies it. Appearance, position
and animation are still peer-authored, which is exactly why the body is a clone
that presumably does not move.

20.51 said "key_only and full_mirror behave identically, so the row is not
read." **full_mirror never shipped in that run.** The p2(41) sweep opened on
key_only, advanced once to key_account, and the acknowledgement ended it. I
conflated that with the p2(38)-era runs where the full row shipped under a STALE
revision and was dropped unread. A complete row under a fresh revision had
therefore never been sent until p2(42).

Three runs, one consistent account:

  | run | row | revision | result |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | p2(38) | full | stale, dropped unread | nothing |
  | p2(41) | minimal only | fresh | acked, nothing visible |
  | p2(42) | full, every body | fresh | **a guardian renders** |

### The confound, and the test now running

p2(42) changed TWO things at once: the row became always-full AND the trailing
32-bit pair began sweeping. Either could be responsible.

The sweep is now OFF in settings (no rebuild: with the sweep off the code
already publishes the full row with the historical 3/3 pair and the revision
still advances on peer gain). That is the single-variable test - if the second
guardian still appears, the trailing pair was a red herring and the full row
under a fresh revision is the whole recipe.

### Why the clone, precisely

The roster message names a member and carries an id. It carries nothing about
class, armour, shader, emblem or position, and the client has no character data
for that member - so it draws the only model it has loaded, the local player's,
and falls back to "You" for the name. The `foreign=` field in our roster log is
NOT a foreign-object count: it is `activityJoinedForeignSession`, a boolean that
is always false (the dead field flagged for retirement). There is no
server-side foreign-character path in the roster today.

### Leads for making the body actually be them

From Lane M's type table, two message types we do not implement at all:
`[13] request_peer_reservation` and `[14] release_peer_reservation`. The
peer-failure enum contains `no-reservation` and `no-ambassador-reservation`.
A reservation handshake we are skipping is a candidate for why the peer is a
ghost rather than a real member. Also unimplemented: `[22]
client_authoritative_data_update`.

Not yet investigated. Recorded so the next session does not re-derive it.

---

## 20.52 WHY STATICS KEEP FAILING ON SCHEMA QUESTIONS: OUR DUMP STORES ONLY 7%
## OF .data. THE BLOB IS READABLE, THE RESOLVER IS NOT - AND THAT IS A PROPERTY
## OF THE DUMP, NOT OF THE SCHEMA. (2026-08-25 ~18:00)

Lane M closed with "statics cannot produce the entry bytes - three lanes have
converged on that." The conclusion is right and the reason is more specific,
which matters because the stated reason ("the schema objects are runtime-built")
implies nothing offline can ever work, while the real one names a fixable gap.

### Verified from the PE headers of RE_output/destiny2_unpacked_full.exe

| section | VSize | RawSize stored | |
|---|---|---|---|
| .text | 28,898,816 | 28,898,816 | FULL |
| .rdata | 3,667,968 | 3,667,968 | FULL |
| **.data** | **23,849,392** | **1,746,432** | **TRUNCATED - 7%** |
| .pdata / .vmp0 / .tls / .rsrc / .reloc / .idata / .text(2) | - | - | FULL |

Every section is complete EXCEPT `.data`, which stores 1,746,432 of 23,849,392
bytes. Anything past offset 1,746,432 into `.data` is simply absent from the
file, and reads there return nothing - which is what `pe_reader.off()` reports
as unmapped.

Where the schema globals fall:

  - `DAT_141f44e20` (schema blob)      offset   212,512 -> **READABLE**
  - `DAT_142439c70` (resolver global)  offset 5,409,904 -> NOT IN FILE
  - `DAT_142742fb0` (W1 live-read)     offset 8,593,328 -> NOT IN FILE

So the two globals every schema lane has needed are past the cut, while the blob
they derive from is fully present, as is all of `.rdata` and `.text`.

### What this changes

Not Lane M's route - the live read stays valid. What it changes is the SHAPE of
the problem: this is a dump-coverage gap, not an unknowable. A full-memory dump
of a running client would contain the resolver global, the runtime-built nodes
and their 0x28-byte entries, and it would be a PERMANENT artifact - every future
schema question answered offline instead of costing a boot each.

`comsvcs.dll` is present on the rig (verified), so the built-in path needs no
install:

    rundll32.exe C:\Windows\System32\comsvcs.dll, MiniDump <pid> <out.dmp> full

Also worth naming: **our client DLL is already inside the process.** Reading
`module_base + 0x2439c70` and walking from it is a memory read, not a function
detour - simpler and lower-risk than hooking 0x1404c72e0's entry, IF the type-12
node can be located without capturing the packed key. If the key is genuinely
needed, the hook stands.

### A tension in the leading hypothesis, recorded so it is not lost

Lane M section 6.3 proposes that our post-epoch bits are being consumed as
`peer_and_player_counts`, causing rejection before any row is parsed. That fits
key_only == full_mirror exactly.

But the SOLO body has the same post-epoch layout - root, revision, epoch, then
the slot-0 presence bit and the local row - and it is accepted and acknowledged.
If those bits were being misread as a counts field, the solo body should
misalign too. The lane covers this with "single-player tolerance or a different
schema path (W1 OPEN #3's two dialects)", which is the weakest link in an
otherwise well-cited chain.

The live read settles it either way, so this is not a blocker - but if the
transcribed entry list shows no counts field ahead of the member table, that
hypothesis dies and the divergence is elsewhere. Do not let the hypothesis
survive a read that refutes it.

---

## 20.51 THE APPLIED ROW DRIVES NOTHING. THE CLIENT ACKS A PEER-BEARING BODY,
## TOUCHES NO STEAM SURFACE, AND NEITHER GUARDIAN IS VISIBLE. THE ROW SHAPE IS
## ANSWERED AND CLOSED; THE PRIME SUSPECT IS NOW A COUNT FIELD.
## (2026-08-25 ~17:43, follow-up to 20.50)

### The discriminator 20.50 asked for

Both players stood in the Tower, at the shared spawn, for the whole window.
NEITHER SAW THE OTHER. The user's words: "we spawn in the same place so i
would've definitely seen it."

That alone proves less than it looks - 20.38 established that visibility is
peer-AUTHORED, so a roster entry could be correct and still show nobody until
the peer link forms. What closes the gap is the client's own networking trace.

### Verified by execution

Mac client, whole run: **13 `ev=steamnet` lines total**, all sign-in scaffolding
- `interface_acquired`, `identity`, `get_certificate`, `relay_ticket_count`,
two `lobby_create`, seven `stub`. **Zero `send_rendezvous`, ever.** And after
the peer bodies landed at t=230910: **zero ev=steamnet lines of any kind.**

So the client acknowledged a body carrying a remote member row and then touched
no Steam surface at all. Applying the membership row triggers NOTHING.

This weakens 20.44's reframe substantially. "Retail clients do not self-start
joins from search results; the SERVER declares membership and the client then
contacts the peer" - we declared it, the client accepted it, and no contact
followed. The declaration is evidently not the trigger, or not sufficient.

### The row shape is closed

`key_only` and `full_mirror` produced identical client behaviour because THE
ROW WAS NEVER READ. memberKey alone is sufficient to be accepted (20.50) and no
field addition changes anything. Shape was never the variable. Do not sweep it
again.

### The prime suspect, and why it fits everything

Lane M's field registry (RE_output/claims/msg12-parser-read.md 1b) names the
parsed structure:

  session_id, update_number, incremental_setup, **peer_and_player_counts**,
  peer_updates, player_updates, player_seq_number, leader_peer_index,
  host_peer_index, slot_counts, party nonce, checksum

with the lane's own note: *"a SEPARATE peer_and_player_counts field exists
upstream of them - our body does not currently distinguish counts from
valid-masks."*

Our encoder (activity_replicate_membership_encoder.cpp:12,28,35-36) writes
`masks = 0b11` twice - the two valid-masks - and nothing that is plainly a
count.

Hypothesis: the count still says one member, so the client parses our slot-1
row into a slot it then ignores. This fits EVERY observation at once - the body
parses, the revision acks, the peer never appears, the shape is irrelevant, and
no connection is attempted because there is no peer to connect to.

Corroboration that the row does reach the client: the Mac's type-12 receipts
grow 3891 -> 3976 bytes when the peer row is present, +85 bytes against
kPeerRowExtraBits = 673 bits (84.125 B, byte-padded to 85). The bits ship and
parse; they are simply not counted.

### Second lead, from the same lane

The peer-link failure-reason enum, ~44 entries at .rdata 0x141c9e2f8 - the
client's own vocabulary for why a peer add failed (`not-joinable`,
`no-reservation`, `address-invalid`, `player-count-zero`, `session-full`, ...).
Three parallel registries, no direct code xrefs (double indirection), flagged
by Lane M as a follow-up. Surfacing which reason fires would have the client
name our cause outright instead of us inferring it.

### Next

Both leads are disassembly, in the lane that was paused for this sweep. Resume
it with this boot's evidence added: ack succeeds, zero Steam surface activity
afterwards, no visual. That is information the lane did not have, and it points
at the count rather than the row.

---

## 20.50 THE SHAPE WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM - THE REVISION WAS. BOTH SESSIONS
## ACKNOWLEDGE A PEER-BEARING BODY, AND memberKey ALONE IS ENOUGH.
## (2026-08-25 ~17:38, server exe 427766a2456f493a, opencode paused)

The sweep, fixed to give each shape its own revision (p2(41)), walked for the
first time. Both sessions then applied a body carrying a remote member row.

### Verified by execution

Sweep progression (server log, both sessions interleaved):

  session A 0xB34F7851304A19CF: key_only x4 -> `membership_sweep result=retired
    variant=key_only next=key_account` at t=470966 -> key_account x3, last at
    t=477745
  session B 0x846C8338F7D022E6: key_only x3, last at t=477326

Both stop there. The log runs on to t=579647 - ~100 s - with BOTH clients
connected (232 and 250 frames, two key fingerprints) and `membership=1` on all
48 subsequent keepalives, so neither `hasMembership` nor the connection ended
the loop. Revisions during the window: 4 -> 5 (peer gained, opencode's p2(39))
-> 6 (the sweep's retire republish).

  - **Session A acknowledged revision 6, which carried `key_account`.**
  - **Session B acknowledged revision 5, which carried `key_only`.**

### What this settles

The bisect premise was wrong, and usefully so. No field was missing: `key_only`
- memberKey and nothing else - is sufficient for the client to apply a body
carrying a remote member row. What blocked every earlier attempt was that the
peer row reused a revision the client had already applied, and the client
applies one update per revision and drops every repeat (Lane M, opencode's
p2(39)). Shape variation was never being evaluated.

This also retires 20.49's reading: that run's apparent "key_only refused,
key_account acked" was the same artifact one level down - all its shapes shared
revision 5, so only the first was ever read.

### What is NOT settled, and why waiting cannot settle it

Whether the applied row makes the peer VISIBLE. Neither client has emitted a
membership dump since long before the peer bodies arrived:

  | | last membership dump | peer bodies received |
  |---|---|---|
  | Mac | t=91529 | t=220907, 225904, 230910 (size=3976) |
  | rig | t=54172 | - |

Every `peers valid: 0x1` reading on both machines predates the peer bodies by
minutes. `peer # 1` count of zero is ABSENCE OF MEASUREMENT, not measurement of
absence - the same trap as 20.49, caught the same way.

Nothing else on the client reports peer state: after t=230910 the only retail
sites still firing are 77/78/79 (`investment_bap_manager:drought`), 81, 85, 73.

Two readings remain, and the boot record cannot separate them:
  (a) the client dumps membership only during session-formation phases;
  (b) the client dumps on membership CHANGE, and applying our body changed
      nothing - it acked the revision and discarded the row.

Evidence against a simple orbit-trip probe: the Mac's transitions at t=116542
and t=122588 produced NO dumps, so a region change is not obviously a dump
trigger. Do not spend a boot on that assumption without a better reason.

### Next

The cheapest discriminator is not a log at all: both players were in the Tower
during this window, so ASK WHETHER EITHER GUARDIAN WAS VISIBLE. A yes settles
everything; a no leaves (a) and (b) open but costs nothing to obtain.

Failing that, the honest path is a client-side instrument on the membership
table - our own DLL - which needs the table located first, and that is Ghidra
work in the paused lane.

## 20.49 THE SWEEP'S FIRST RUN TESTED TWO OF SIX SHAPES - AND SETTLED THE ONE
## BRANCH THAT MATTERED MOST: ACKNOWLEDGEMENT IS CONTENT-GATED. SOLO IS ACKED
## ON FIRST TRANSMISSION; full_mirror SURVIVES NINE REPUBLISHES UNACKED.
## (2026-08-25 ~17:01)

### Verified by execution

Peer-bearing bodies ran t=450034 -> t=477222 on session ...108 (the rig; the
Mac's session ...105 logged peer=0 throughout its solo window).

  - `full_mirror`: NINE bodies across 27 s, `ack=0` on every one.
  - `solo`: ONE body at t=477222. No membership body ever again.

The session did NOT end there. Traffic continued to t=833319 - another ~6
minutes, both key fingerprints present (4EE55ACA.. and FDEEB5CE..), svc=8
polls and keepalives flowing. `publishesMembership` is
`hasMembership && (regionChanged || !acknowledged(sessionId))`, and no region
changed, so the only thing that stops the loop is the acknowledgement landing.

Corroborated by size: the rig's membership-carrying keepalive at t=477223 is
`bytes=4754`; afterwards keepalives drop to the small no-body form (`bytes=835`
at t=477915). Note `membership=%u` in that line reports `hasMembership`, NOT
whether a body shipped - checked at activity_keepalive_push.cpp:295 rather than
assumed, because reading it as "a body shipped" would have inverted this whole
entry.

### What this settles

**Acknowledgement is content-gated, and the ack path works mid-session.** This
was the pre-named third branch and the one that would have been most expensive
to be wrong about: it was inferred from earlier boots that the client acks our
solo body, and that inference had been promoted to fact without ever being
tested under the current build. It now holds by execution. 20.48's reading
stands - the peer ROW is refused; the acknowledgement is not broken.

### The instrument bug that cost the other four shapes

`sweep_variant` anchored its phase to the first membership body of the BOOT
(t~325466, the Mac signing in alone), not to the first peer-bearing one. By the
time the rig joined at t=450034 the clock had already advanced
(450034 - 325466) / 30000 = 4.15 steps, so the sweep OPENED on index 4,
`full_mirror`, stepped to `solo`, and terminated. `key_only`, `key_account`,
`key_account_join` and `key_account_join_opaque` never shipped.

The 27-second peer window was not the user quitting and not a short-lived peer
condition - it was the sweep landing on its own terminal control almost
immediately.

Fixed in p2(40) with three corrections: anchor to the first PEER-BEARING body;
advance on a body COUNT rather than a wall clock, so a shape is measured by how
many transmissions carried it; and keep a time floor as well, because bodies
arrive in bursts (three inside one second at t=450034/450099/450919) and a pure
count would skip shapes the client never saw settle.

### Still open

Which field, or whether any second row is structurally acceptable at all. The
next run opens on `key_only` and walks. Pre-named: a peer variant acking names
the field; reaching `solo` again means no peer-bearing shape is acceptable and
the Ghidra lane should target table-level validation rather than field
semantics; `full_mirror` appearing as the FIRST variant line means the
anchoring is still wrong and the run is void.

## 20.45 P2(35): THE PEER IS NOW PUBLISHED AS MEMBER SLOT 1 - DEPLOYED AND
## OFFLINE-VERIFIED. NEXT BOOT READS peers-valid/players-valid IN THE CLIENT'S
## OWN DUMP. (2026-08-25 ~15:30)

Implemented and deployed without a boot; every claim below is verified by
execution unless marked.

### The change (commit cae9ca9)

1. **State**: `foreign_member_identity(ownSessionId, out)` scans the one shared session
   table for another OCCUPIED+JOINED record with a published identity in the SAME
   destination (reuses `same_destination`) and copies its `MembershipState.identity`.
2. **Wire** (type-12 replicate-membership): member slot 1 now carries that identity as a
   full populated row when present; both top-level 32-bit masks set bit 1. THOSE MASKS ARE
   THE FIELDS THE CLIENT DUMP PRINTS as `peers valid` / `players valid` - the named success
   signal since 20.27 ("peers valid: 0x3 with a peer # 1 row").
3. **Layout math, derived then probed**: member table spans bits 65..834 = 769 bits =
   local row + 31x3 absent => populated row = 676 bits => peer delta = 673 (my first hand
   count said 674/677; the offline probe caught it - see below). New bodies: peer 3831 B,
   peer+citizen 3959 B (scratch is 256 KiB).
4. **Liveness + loudness**: slot-1 inclusion logs
   `ev=activity stage=membership_peer result=included key=0x...`; an encode failure logs
   `ev=activity stage=membership result=encode_fail` instead of vanishing.

### Offline verification (throwaway probe, all four snapshot shapes)

solo 3746 B and solo+citizen 3874 B reproduce BYTE-EXACT (no regression on known-good
bodies); peer 3831 B and peer+citizen 3959 B match the derived sizes exactly; encoding is
deterministic; the masks byte differs between solo and peer bodies. Probe source kept at
the opencode temp dir (membership_probe.cpp), compiled from the four middleware TUs.

### Deployed

exe `1cf5243e2fa1c398`, four harness gates rc=0, UDP 30976 bound, backups
*.bak_p2d6_20260825_152354. Client DLLs UNCHANGED (a248109eaeeedc71 still current on both
machines) - this front is server-only.

### What the boot tests, and its negatives

FALSIFIABLE CLAIM: two clients in the Tower produce, in EACH client's log,
`peers valid: 0x3` and a `peer # 1` row naming the other machine's identity.
- Dumps stay 0x1 => the membership the dump reads is not driven by our table shape =>
  Ghidra lane on the client's membership/join path becomes the front.
- Peer row appears but no Steam-surface or UDP activity follows => the row lacks whatever
  the dialer needs (likely platform identity) => next variable.
- ANY contact attempt fires => our three-table cameras catch it and hand us the transport.
NOTE: even full success here is the IGNITION, not the engine - the rendezvous relay
(Lane T, running) is what moves bytes once a client dials.
