## 20.44 THE P2(34) BOOT CLOSES THE STEAM QUESTION: ALL THREE SURFACES ARE
## NOW INSTRUMENTED AND NONE CARRIES JOIN INITIATION. THE CLIENT FILES A
## SERVED RESULT WITHOUT ACTING ON IT. THE JOIN DRIVER MUST BE DECLARED
## MEMBERSHIP. (2026-08-25 ~15:05)

Both machines booted on p2(34) (DLL-only deploy, server untouched). Verified by execution:

1. **A cross-client result was served AGAIN this boot** (server t=5319374,
   session_search served_descriptor=1 advertisement=0x4 bytes=146) - and STILL no contact
   attempt. Combined with 20.42: twice now, a client holding another client's descriptor
   has done nothing through any Steam surface.
2. **Identical choreography on BOTH machines, solo or not**: friends-table polls
   (table=0 slots 3 -> 43 -> 5, recurring; slot 3 re-polls minutes later), then exactly TWO
   create_lobby calls each (type=3 max=32, invented ids prefix|2 then prefix|3 - matching
   the descriptor's lobby field). This is sign-in/session scaffolding, NOT join behaviour:
   the sequence does not differ between a solo client and one that was just handed a peer.
3. **Zero chat_send. Zero chat_read. Zero lobby_join.** Nobody sends anything through any
   lobby. Serialized networking stays sign-in-only (cert->0, relay count->0) for the third
   boot running.
4. No peer # 1 / peers-valid 0x3 anywhere on either machine.

### What this eliminates

Every Steam-facing surface this shim exposes is now camera'd: serialized networking (p2(33)),
lobby methods + every unimplemented matchmaking slot, friends slots (p2(34)). Holding a
passive search result triggers NOTHING on any of them. The client-side join decision either
requires a trigger we do not provide, or runs on internal state we have not fed.

### The inference that reframes the front (marked as inference)

Retail D2 clients do not self-start joins from search results: the SERVER declares
membership ("you are in a fireteam/posse with X"), and the CLIENT then contacts X using
whatever identity/transport the platform offers. Our own logs show the receiving end of
that machine exists and is readable: `networking:session:membership:dump` with leader/
host peer indexes, peers-valid masks, and `[fireteam:*]` / `[posse:*]` structures - always
peer # 0 only. Upstream's in-process mod could poke that state directly; WE own the wire
equivalent already: the server pushes family rosters/membership tables today (the type-12
pushes carry member tables verbatim).

NEXT FRONT (proposed): make the standalone server declare BOTH players members of ONE
fireteam/posse in the pushed rosters - each client's own steamid string is already known -
and watch all three instrumented surfaces. If peer # 1 appears in the dumps, whichever door
the client knocks on hands us the transport contract. Pre-named negative: rosters updated +
dumps still 0x1 => the membership source the dump reads is client-authored, not
server-pushed => straight to the Ghidra lane on the client's join path.

### Reference notes

- The client's SteamFriends017 vtable layout does NOT match the public SDK header ordering
  (our table already places overlayNeedsPresent at 49 vs SDK 26) - slot NUMBERS are the
  record until names are proven; do not map by SDK intuition.
- Searches recur mid-session (two empties then a serve last boot; one empty then a serve
  this boot) - whether a serve happens depends on who has entered the Tower first.

- The join decision either never fired despite a served result naming another client, or it

- NEW PRIME SUSPECT LANE: the descriptor names a LOBBY, and our matchmaking.cpp
  create_lobby/join_lobby/send_lobby_chat are UNTRACED. The client demonstrably calls
  create_lobby (descriptor lobby = prefix|call#). Peer signalling may flow through lobby
  chat, which we neither trace nor relay - invisible to this boot's instrument.

Upstream tip's serialized_networking.cpp is BYTE-IDENTICAL to our old stubs (same comments,
cert returns 0, rendezvous dropped), and their common.cpp hardcodes the SAME kLocalSteamId.
Their committed tree is authoritative ONLY for "what their code does": it cannot explain how
their two-player demo worked. What it DOES suggest: whatever their demo used, it is not in
this layer of committed code - so do not expect "finish the shim" alone to be the answer.

### Next instrument (not built yet)

Trace the matchmaking/lobby table with the p2(33) pattern (create_lobby, join_lobby,
send_lobby_chat, get_lobby_chat_entry + args). If the client exchanges anything through

and it uses only contracts already proven (create_lobby successfully queues callbacks).

## 20.41 P2(33) DEPLOYED ON BOTH MACHINES: PER-INSTANCE STEAM IDENTITY +
## THE FULL SERIALIZED-NETWORKING TRACE. THE BOOT ANSWERS WHETHER
## send_rendezvous FIRES AND NAMING WHOM. NEITHER CHANGE MAKES MULTIPLAYER WORK.
## (2026-08-25 ~13:10)

### What landed (commit cae191a on upstream-gameplay-scoped)

1. **`kLocalSteamId` deleted as a hard constant.** `get_steam_id` now answers
   `core::settings::get().steam.user.steamId` (`steam.user.steam_id` in settings.json,
   default `0x0110000130AA9EC5`, which reproduces the historical behaviour exactly).
   Validation: only individual-account SteamID64 shapes accepted (high 32 bits ==
   `0x01100001`, low word nonzero); anything else falls back to the default WITH an early
   line `ev=settings stage=steam_id result=fallback authored=0x.. default=0x..`.
   Adding the key needs no settings-version raise ("a missing key already takes its default").
2. **The whole `ISteamNetworkingSocketsSerialized` table now traces.** All eight methods log
   `ev=steamnet seq=<N> stage=<method> ...args`; `send_rendezvous` additionally dumps
   `remote=`, `conn=`, `size=` and the message bytes in hex (96 B/line chunks, 1 KiB cap,
   then a `truncated=1` line). The sequence number carries CALL ORDER.
3. **Liveness lines** (lesson 13): `get_generic_interface` emits
   `ev=steamnet stage=interface_acquired` when it hands out the table; `get_steam_id`
   emits `ev=steamnet stage=identity result=resolved id=0x...` once per process. A boot
   record with ZERO ev=steamnet lines means the table was never acquired - that is an
   instrument/acquisition finding, NOT data about rendezvous.

| Artifact | Hash | Check |
|---|---|---|
| server exe | `e2f139ccd37d599a` | deploy script asserts deployed == build output; 4 gates rc=0; UDP 30976 bound |
| client DLL (Mac) | `f516203a039dfab2` | `deploy_client_dll.sh` (new) asserts hash equality post-copy + greps all three literals IN THE DEPLOYED FILE |
| client DLL (rig) | same full sha256 verified via certutil | findstr finds all three literals in the deployed file |

Identity authoring: Mac unchanged (default = old constant, so its presented soid stays
byte-identical to everything already persisted/proven). Rig authored
`"steam_id": 76561198776753862` (= `0x0110000130AA9EC6`) in
`dcv build\bin\x64\Sunrise\settings.json`. Edit validated programmatically: only the
`steam` block changed. Rollbacks: rig `Sunrise/settings.json.bak_p2d7`,
`steam_api64.dll.bak_p2d7_*`; Mac DLL backup `bak_p2d7_20260825_130700`.

### Corrections made while working

- **The handoff's "the Steam shim compiles into both" is imprecise**: the SETTINGS layer
  compiles into both targets, but `sunrise-server`'s source list contains ZERO
  `src/steam/**` files - the interface methods exist only in steam_api64.dll. My first

### What this boot does NOT settle

Distinct identities remove one hard blocker. Whether the client can broker a real join
through our stubbed lobby/certificate layer remains untested and probably negative. The
load-bearing unknown stays what it was in 20.40: what the rendezvous message blob carries.
If it holds candidate addresses we have a relayable signalling channel; if it is
Steam-signed, peers may refuse without valid certificates. Do not call this front "days"
or "a wall" until that blob is read.

- L825 bootflow join_ready forced; L826 t=128031 site=186 "'[AC PRIVATE CURRENT CON-Y EST-Y
  AH->9eaa300100200004 MEM-0]' is ready for instantiation!" -- established +16 ms after the

### 20.71.2 Diff verdict: FIRST DIVERGENCE IS POST-MESSAGE, CLIENT-SIDE

Server-side push windows, normalized and diffed (server_sunrise.log): MAC's join_result
window is server t=233275 (join_result push L583-584, correlation=1343361195,
ah_sid=0x9EAA300100200001); RIG's is t=478876 (L2719-2720, correlation=1446451939,
ah_sid=0x9EAA300100200004). Both windows contain the identical push set with identical body
sizes: type=4 body=757, then type=0 body=1024, type=1 body=189, type=54 body=1, followed by
svc=8 heartbeats, type=5 auth_sense rosters, type=12 membership_replication. VERIFIED-BY-
READING (unified diff over t=[233200..235400] vs [478800..481000]).

THEREFORE: Q3 answer = NO missing message pre-establishment. The rig's establishing sequence
contains no message the Mac did not receive (at size/order fidelity). INFERRED (bounded):
bodies could still differ in content invisible to these logs; only sizes verified.

FIRST DIVERGENCE POINT = immediately AFTER the identical join-result burst: rig's next
activity_manager pump reports EST-Y / "ready for instantiation" (rig_client L826); Mac never
reports an AC state again for the rest of its run (grep over whole file, above). The EST
commit happened inside the client's join-result processing on one side and not the other,
from byte-size-identical inputs. This indicts the join_result PAYLOAD CONTENT (or client
local pre-state) as the discriminator -- exactly where a follow-up instrument must look.

One real content-level asymmetry was found, but it postdates establishment by ~5 s: first
type=12 membership_replication after join_result = body 3882 to MAC (+4993 ms, server L669)
vs body 3967 to RIG (+4966 ms, server L2816). The +85-byte delta persists across the early
updates: rig's roster carried BOTH machines (rig_client L864 site=194 "Could not find
tracking data for peer '1' ... machine id 'CF:19:4A:30:51:78:4FB3'" = the MAC), while Mac's
carried only itself until a single 3967-body push at rel=+254598 ms (near-coincident with
the reservation-release exchange below; association INFERRED, causality unproven).

Lesson-17 corroboration: type=13 request_peer_reservation occurs ZERO times in the server
log (grep count=0); type=14 release_peer_reservation occurs twice, both initiated BY THE
RIG (server L2826 payload 0000000000000001 CF194A3051784FB3 against handle 0x...200004;
server L2933 payload E622D0F738836C84 against handle 0x...200001). Mac's log contains no
peer-reservation lines at all. VERIFIED-BY-READING.

### 20.71.3 What preceded each decision (Q4)

RIG EST-Y preceded by: startup-response sub-state machine visible in retail prints
("Waiting for activity host startup response", "Received ... after '33ms', moving on",
sub-state 22->26, "waiting for join response from AH") then the five-step commit
(join result -> host_changed blank -> 4 pushes -> join_ready forced -> EST-Y). All cited
above. Its membership counter then climbed MEM-0 -> MEM-1 at rig_client L865 when the
two-machine roster landed.

MAC EST-N preceded by: same five-step shape up through join_ready forced (L410), but ZERO
occurrences anywhere in its 899-line log of the strings "moving on", "Waiting for 'PRIVATE
CURRENT' activity host", or "waiting for join response" (grep counts: mac=0, rig>=3). Caveat
recorded honestly: same DLL does not guarantee same per-machine config/verbosity filters,
so absence of those wrapper prints is STRONG but not PROOF of a sub-state path divergence.

OPEN ITEMS (stop-and-write discipline):
1. Dump/diff the two type=4 join_result BODIES (757 bytes each) next capture -- the divergence
   must be inside the payload or client pre-state; nothing else distinguishes the flows.
2. Explain why only the RIG emits activity_manager sub-state prints at EST-N time (same DLL;
   check per-machine sunrise config/verbosity before treating it as path proof).
3. Why did only the rig issue peer-reservation releases? Check whether release issuance is

   may be per-client FAH ids generated elsewhere.

## 20.168 *** THE WEDGE MECHANISM IS CAUGHT: AT setup:orbit THE CLIENT RUNS DEMONWARE ***
## *** NAT TRAVERSAL AGAINST THE PEER - AND DIALS THE IDENTITY STRING AS IP ADDRESSES. ***
## *** SIX INTRO REQs AT STRIDE 6 ARE ASCII WINDOWS OVER "steamid:...#...". ALSO: THE ***
## *** FOUR STATIC READER CANDIDATES NEVER RAN. (2026-08-29 ~12:0x, boot p2(106)) ***

BOOT p2-106 per BOOT_BRIEF_p2-106.md (GATE PASS; mac armed, rig control, both on the
new build 7df1d0e9aff01861 with the reader census; server p2(96), claims reset). Mac
restarted once mid-setup by the user (log clean, single install line). The inject
reproduced a FIFTH time (t=71583, rec1=0x45A3620 now in the line), roster named the
peer (census t=73251), wedge at setup:orbit t=102684.

RESULT 1 - THE FOUR PROBES ARE LIVE AND THE STATIC CANDIDATES ARE DEAD. The install
attached all four; ADMIT (0x1777EC0) logged a miss sample at t=71548 - liveness proven
(L13) - and 0x1771060 / 0x178FE00 / 0x17A0B60 produced NO LINE AT ALL across the whole
boot, which under the first-2-misses design means they NEVER RAN. ADMIT ran exactly
once, for SELF, at session setup (rcx = the arena base = record 0), BEFORE our inject.
The pre-named CONTENT NEGATIVE 1 fired: none of the four static candidates reads the
forged record. Instrument gap owned: the online classifier matches pointers INTO the
field span and misses base-pointer args (ADMIT's rcx WAS the arena); offline analysis
of the logged registers covers it, and miss samples make "never ran" provable. The
wedge consumer is none of the four - it reads through a shifted base, another
structure, or not at all.

RESULT 2 - THE WEDGE MECHANISM (VERIFIED-BY-LOG, the biggest fact of the boot). Three
seconds before the wedge, at t=99379..99381, the mac runs Demonware NAT traversal -
`bdNATTravClient.cpp`: "Request timed out. Retrying. (stage 1)", "Public Addr:
49.56.53.51:12853", then SIX "sent INTRO REQ to A.B.C.D:port" lines. Those addresses
are NOT addresses:
```
  115.116.101.97 = 'stea'  offset  0 of "steamid:76561198776753862#6403185352"
  100.58.55.54   = 'd:76'  offset  6
  49.49.57.56    = '1198'  offset 12
  54.55.53.51    = '6753'  offset 18
  50.35.54.52    = '2#64'  offset 24
  49.56.53.51:12853 = '1853' + port '25'  offsets 30/34
```
Stride EXACTLY 6 bytes = 4-byte address + 2-byte port: an ARRAY OF BINARY ENDPOINT
STRUCTURES whose contents are the INJECTED PEER IDENTITY STRING, parsed as endpoints.
Even the client's own "Public Addr" is ASCII (offset 30 region). So at activity setup
the client believes it must establish the peer connection, pulls a peer endpoint list,
and the list contains identity-string bytes where a real peer would carry real endpoint
bytes. The INTROs necessarily time out, the retry loop runs (stage 1), and setup:orbit
never completes. THE CLIENT-INTERNAL WAIT IS A NAT-TRAVERSAL RETRY LOOP AGAINST
GARBAGE ENDPOINTS.

CLAIM - WHERE THIS LEAVES THE ROAD. The forged record is adopted (roster names it) and
then feeds a CONNECTION-ESTABLISHMENT path that expects the peer's real transport
endpoint. The identity string reaches that path from somewhere we wrote or the game
copied: the slot's +0x50 identity-string field (a5's landing place, verified 20.162),
or a record field. For a REAL peer this either carries real endpoint bytes (written by
whomever fills +0x3c10/+0x3c20 - ADMIT's movups, never executed for us) or the DTLS
session description (20.144's plaintext establishment, absent this boot - the wire
carried no machine ids) replaces the dial entirely. The fix is therefore NOT more
record forging: it is naming who builds the endpoint array and what a real entry's
bytes are.

NEXT INSTRUMENT (pre-named, cheap, no new memory writes):
1. Caller capture on the retail log funnel for the "sent INTRO REQ" line
   (retail_log_enqueue_observer, LESSONS 18c) - names the dialing function's RVA.
2. A one-shot hexdump at the dial site of the endpoint array base (the INTRO target
   pointer is an argument at that call) - shows how many entries the array holds and
   what a whole entry looks like.
3. field_xref on the endpoint-array base offset once (2) names it - who WRITES it.
The page-guard VEH from the brief's content negative 1 stays the fallback if (1)
stalls; it is not needed yet - the log funnel already gives us the caller for free.

ARTIFACTS: mac log archived RE_output/captures/p2-104_peer_admission/mac_log_p2-106.log.
Mac disarmed post-boot (admission_inject=false). Both machines on 7df1d0e9aff01861.
NOTE: the reader instrument stays in the tree - its miss-sample design proved "never
ran" cheaply, and the counter-dump gap (hit/miss totals not in the log) should be
closed if it is reused.

## 20.169 *** THE WEDGE IS FULLY EXPLAINED: bdNAT DIALS IDENTITY-STRING BYTES BECAUSE ***
## *** THE FORGED PEER HAS NO REAL TRANSPORT ENDPOINT. THE UNARMED RIG DIALS REAL ***
## *** ADDRESSES FROM THE SAME LAYER. THE FORK IS NAMED. (2026-08-29 ~12:5x, boots p2(108)/(109)) ***

Two runs. p2(108): the nat_probe cap burned on type-0 shim calls (suppressed bdNAT
startup logging) before the dial - instrument corrected in p2(109) (dump only types
1..3, the shim's three logging arms; cap 16). p2(109): clean capture, wedge reproduced
(hitch asserts in setup:orbit), user swapped characters by accident (warlock/hunter -
noted, irrelevant: the wedge predates spawn and the inject touches no loadout).

RESULT 1 - THE SHIM IS GENERIC. fn 0x1409E3230 is the bnetd log emitter for the whole
bd stack (bdSocketRouter, bdQoS, bdPlatformLog, bdNAT...), dispatching on a type
selector in edx; r9 carries the static file/function/message context. Its xref trail is
obfuscated (zero direct calls), so the dialing logic stays unreachable statically.

RESULT 2 - THE DIAL CAPTURED (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION). Calls 11-16 = the six INTRO REQs;
all carry r8 = 0x6B837F50, a "message/bdSocket/nat" object holding COUNT=6 and the
bdNAT trav client state. The r8-object value 0x141D4EBC0 resolved to an .rdata ERROR
STRING ("Not enough room...") - a dead end, recorded so it is not re-chased.

RESULT 3 - THE DECISIVE DISAMBIGUATION (the same boot, both logs):
```
MAC (armed, forged record):  NAT Type: **UNKNOWN**
  "Starting NAT trav ... 5 Local Addrs Found:
     Local Addr 0 : 115.116.101.97:26989   <- 'stea' + port 'mi' (LE) = offset 0
     Local Addr 1 : 100.58.55.54:13877     <- 'd:76' + '56' = offset 6
     Local Addr 2 : 49.49.57.56:14135      <- offset 12
     Local Addr 3 : 54.55.53.51:13880      <- offset 18
     Local Addr 4 : 50.35.50.56:...        <- offset 24
   => ALL FIVE are stride-6 windows over an identity string."
  121 INTRO REQs, retry stages 1 and 3, wedge at setup:orbit.

RIG (unarmed, same session):  NAT Type: BD_NAT_OPEN
  "Starting NAT trav ... 5 Local Addrs Found:
     Local Addr 0 : 192.168.1.164:30976    <- THE MAC'S REAL PEER-CHANNEL ENDPOINT
     Local Addr 1..4 : 0.0.0.0:0"
  7 INTRO REQs to real addresses. Reached in_world normally.
```
The bdNAT address table is populated PER MEMBER from that member's transport endpoint.
On the rig, the mac is a REAL member (server-announced + real DTLS establishment,
20.144's plaintext session-description) so its table holds the mac's real
192.168.1.164:30976. On the mac, the forged peer's endpoint field holds the identity
STRING (exactly what the creator writes at slot+0x50, verified 20.162) and is NEVER
updated with real bytes, because the real update rides the establishment exchange,
which keys on the peer's real per-session identity - and the forged slot's a7 is a
stale constant (the 20.166 stale-id mechanism, now with a consequence attached).

CLAIM - THE FORK, NAMED. The forge creates the ROSTER entry but cannot create the
TRANSPORT identity; the wedge is the transport layer correctly refusing to traverse
toward garbage. Two roads forward, in cost order:
  (a) REPLAY-WITH-SUBSTITUTION AT THE ENDPOINT: after the creator writes slot+0x50,
      overwrite the endpoint region with the RIG'S REAL endpoint bytes
      (192.168.1.136:3097; the exact binary form is the one open question - the 6-byte
      windows say 4-byte IP + 2-byte port, and the rig's own table holds a real entry
      we could read once via rig_dll_helper.py before writing anything).
  (b) MATCH THE REAL EXCHANGE: make the forged slot's per-session identity match what
      the establishment announces, so the REAL path fills the endpoint itself. Cleaner
      but depends on reading the (now-encrypted) establishment, or on finding the
      plaintext form upstream (the session-form ids DID travel in plaintext in
      20.144's era).
Both roads reuse the switch-gated inject; neither needs new memory-write classes.
CAVEAT (L16): the exact field bdNAT reads (slot+0x50 vs the member-record address
block) is INFERRED from the byte windows, not pinned by a write-site capture. Road (a)
must dump a REAL entry's bytes first (rig_dll_helper or a rigged self-session dump) so
the substitution copies the true format, not a guessed one.

INSTRUMENT NOTES: the nat_probe stays (cap now keyed to logged types). The p2(108)
"cap burned before the dial" miss and the p2(106) classifier gap are both recorded.

7849e28a58d40539 (re-aligned).

BOOT p2(110) per BOOT_BRIEF_p2-110.md (GATE PASS). ZERO DELTAS: no rebuild, no deploy,

`7849e28a58d40539` and DISARMED; server `b9b0f3823f74d1bf` (p2(96)) restarted fresh

### RESULT 0 - THE RECORD CORRECTION THAT MADE THIS BOOT POSSIBLE (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION)
`192.168.1.164:30976` IS THE SERVER'S GAMEPLAY ENDPOINT, NOT THE MAC CLIENT.
```
  server log : ev=gameplay stage=endpoint result=ok mode=embedded port=30976
  lsof       : sunrise-server.exe / wineserver bound UDP *:30976
  mac client : grep -ac 30976 <mac client log>  ==  0   (whole boot, every boot)
  mac client's own socket is 192.168.1.164:3097
```
CONSEQUENCES - two entries are corrected, neither is deleted:
  - 20.167 RESULT 3/4 ("flow: mac 192.168.1.164:30976 <-> rig 3097 ... the client sits
    20 ms from a live encrypted channel to the REAL rig") measured the RIG-CLIENT <->
    SERVER channel. The stalled mac client was never on it. Its D3 conclusion ("the

## 20.171 *** THE PEER ROW IS PUBLISHED AND THEN PERMANENTLY WITHDRAWN ~40 ms LATER: ***
## *** THE RETRY BUDGET COUNTS BODIES, NOT TIME, AND THE BURST SPENDS IT IN ONE ***
## *** FRAME. ALSO: `foreign=` IS A DEAD FLAG AND MY 20.170 READING OF IT WAS WRONG. ***
## *** (2026-08-29 ~13:4x, SOURCE + p2(110) LOG ANALYSIS - NO BOOT) ***

No boot. Source read of the deployed server tree (RE_build/Sunrise-fork-inventory,
the tree that built `b9b0f3823f74d1bf`) against the p2(110) server log.

### RESULT 1 - `foreign=` IS A DEAD FLAG. RETRACT 20.170's READING OF IT.
20.170 RESULT 4 presented `foreign=0` as evidence that "the activity roster never lists
a foreign player". THAT READING IS WRONG and is retracted here. The field is not a count:
```
  activity_roster_report.cpp:43   session.activityJoinedForeignSession ? 1U : 0U
  internal.h:101                  bool activityJoinedForeignSession{};
      /** Set when this link's first binding came from joining a session it did not
          allocate. Such a link carries the keepalive alone. A roster or membership
          push on it stalls the load. */
```
AND IT IS NEVER ASSIGNED. `grep -rn "activityJoinedForeignSession *=" Sunrise/src/`
returns NOTHING. Seven references in the whole tree: one declaration, six READS
(roster_snapshot:202, roster_report:43, keepalive_push:87/97/243/281), ZERO writes.
CONSEQUENCES:
  - `foreign=0` is a compile-time constant in practice and carries NO information about
    peers. Do not use it as a peer diagnostic. 20.170's OTHER evidence for the same
    conclusion - neither client runs `Adding player` for the other's xuid - is
    UNAFFECTED and still stands; it was always the stronger half.
  - `if (session.activityJoinedForeignSession)` at keepalive_push:281 is DEAD CODE.
  - The joined-link region path at roster_snapshot:202 (`live_region_session`) is never
    taken; every link resolves its region from its own `activitySessionId`.
  - Whether the flag SHOULD be set for the second client to join a shared instance is a
    separate open question. It is NOT the milestone blocker (RESULT 2 is). Recorded so
    the dead branch is not mistaken for live behaviour by the next reader.
DOC DEFECT, same area: settings/server/definition.h:104 documents the sweep pin order as
`(mask_mask, count_mask, packed_mask, count_count, packed_packed, solo)`, but the actual
`TrailingVariant` enum (membership_sweep.h:44) is
`packedMasks, countMasks, packedMasksSeq, countMasksSeq, allMask, solo`. The deployed
`membership_sweep_pin = 0` therefore selects `packedMasks`, which MATCHES the log
(`variant=packed_masks`, `first=0x00020002 second=0x00000003`). The comment is stale;
the behaviour is correct. Fix the comment, not the code.

### RESULT 2 - THE REAL GATE (VERIFIED-BY-READING + VERIFIED-BY-LOG)
The server FINDS the peer and PUBLISHES a peer row, then withdraws it permanently.
`ev=activity stage=membership_peer` fired 11 times in p2(110); the same shape three
times on three sessions:
```
  t=607478  included  key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 peer_row=1 first=0x00020002 second=0x3
  t=607518  included  key=0x846C8338F7D022E6 peer_row=1          <- 40 ms after the first
  t=607535  WITHDRAWN unacked=2 session=...107                    <- 17 ms later
  t=607589  membership_ack result=ok revision=1 session=...107    <- client answers, +54 ms

  ...105:  included 611588, included 616606, WITHDRAWN 621645, ack 621682  (+37 ms)
  ...108:  included 762541, included 762596, WITHDRAWN 766678, ack 766706  (+28 ms)
```
THE MECHANISM (activity_membership_push.cpp:277-320):
```c
  if (retry != nullptr && state::activity::membership::acknowledged(sessionId)) {
      retry->unackedPeerBodies = 0;          // budget resets only on an ack
  }
  bool publishPeer = havePeer && values.peerPresent
                     && (retry == nullptr || !retry->peerWithdrawn);
  if (publishPeer && ... retry->unackedPeerBodies >= settings.membershipPeerRetryCap) {
      retry->peerWithdrawn = true;           // ONE-WAY LATCH, per session
      publishPeer = false;
  }
  if (publishPeer) { apply_peer_row(...); ++retry->unackedPeerBodies; }
```
`peerWithdrawn` is a DELIBERATE one-way latch and the code says why:
  "The withdrawal flag is NOT cleared here: after a withdrawal the solo body gets
   acknowledged, and clearing on that would re-add the peer and restart the storm the
   cap exists to stop."
Once latched, `publishPeer` is false for the life of that session. The client therefore
never receives a peer row, never instantiates the peer player, and `Adding player` never
fires for the other xuid - which is exactly what 20.170 RESULT 4 measured on BOTH
machines. THE CHAIN FROM HERE TO "NO SECOND GUARDIAN" IS NOW CLOSED END TO END.

### RESULT 3 - THE DEFECT, NAMED: THE BUDGET IS COUNTED IN BODIES, SPENT IN MILLISECONDS
```
  source default   membershipPeerRetryCap{6}    (settings/server/definition.h:114)
  DEPLOYED VALUE   membership_peer_retry_cap = 2   (s1_accept/Sunrise/settings.json)
```
The peer appears during the LOAD BURST, when bodies go out ~40-55 ms apart
(607478->607518 = 40 ms; 762541->762596 = 55 ms). A cap of 2 therefore gives the client
a ~50 ms wall-clock deadline to acknowledge. That is shorter than the observed
client response time in every one of the three cases.
SCOPE / HONEST CAVEATS (L16, and they bound the fix):
  - The ack that lands after a withdrawal may be acking the peer-LESS body, since the
    withdrawal changes what is sent. "The client acked the peer body" is NOT established.
    What IS established: the client was responsive within 28-54 ms, and the budget was
    spent in ~50 ms of wall time.
  - Session ...105 is NOT the same case: included 611588 and 616606 are 5 s apart and the
    client sent NO ack for 15 s (last ack rev4 t=606594, next rev5 t=621682). Raising the
    cap plausibly fixes ...107 and ...108 and plausibly does NOT fix ...105. Do not
    pre-claim a full fix.
  - `havePeer` is true throughout: `foreign_member_identity` resolves, and the keepalive
    path still logs `membership_peer result=gained` AFTER each withdrawal (t=607602,
    t=763557). The server's STATE keeps the peer; only the PUBLISH is latched off.

### THE FIX SPACE, IN COST ORDER (none implemented - p2(111) tests only the first)
  (a) SETTINGS ONLY, no rebuild: raise `membership_peer_retry_cap` 2 -> 6 (the source
      default) and restart. This is BOOT_BRIEF_p2-111.md.
  (b) Make the budget TIME-based (or body-count AND elapsed-time) so a burst cannot spend
      it. Code change; the right fix if (a) shows the cap is directional but insufficient.
  (c) Clear `peerWithdrawn` on ack. The code comment explicitly warns this reopens the
      127-body storm that blocked a Tower load. Do NOT do this without a separate storm
      guard.
  (d) `activityJoinedForeignSession` never being set (RESULT 1) - a real defect, separate
      front, not on the milestone path.
DO NOT set the cap to 0: definition.h:111 records that disabling it is how the 127-body
storm became possible.

The evidence was `Adding player [xuid=...]` firing only for each machine's OWN xuid.
That line belongs to `networking:managed_session` sessions [#0] (fireteam) and [#1]
(posse) - sessions where ONLY self is ever expected. The SHARED session is [#2]
(`group_target`), which never runs `Adding player` for ANY player, local included.
The instrument could not have shown a peer no matter what happened. STATE's own hard
rule (20.101) says read WHICH xuid; the rule I missed is read WHICH SESSION.

### RETRACTION 2 - "BOTH CLIENTS SIT AT EST-N / MEM-0, THE 20.132 WALL". WRONG, AND
### IT WAS MY REGEX. Both clients REACH:
```
  [AC PUBLIC TARGET CON-Y EST-Y AH->9eaa300100200003 MEM-5]     mac AND rig
```
I censused with `AH->[0-9]*`, which cannot match hex letters, so it returned only the
pre-join `AH->0` states and hid every success. This is EXACTLY Universal Lesson 1
corollary 2 ("an explicit stage list is a FILTER, and a filter's absences are its
own; CENSUS FIRST, then filter") - the corollary was written for this project after
the same mistake, and I made it again. There is NO 20.132 wall and NO regression.

### RETRACTION 3 - "THE PEER RETRY CAP IS THE GATE" (20.171). WRONG, AND p2(111)
### PROVED IT HARMFUL. Both clients reached MEM-5 / EST-Y in p2(110) DESPITE the three
withdrawals, so the withdrawals never blocked anything. Raising the cap 2 -> 6:
```
  p2(110) cap=2 :   6 included,  3 withdrawn -> mac loaded the Tower normally
  p2(111) cap=6 : 146 included,  2 withdrawn -> mac NEVER reached activity:in_world
                                                (stuck at initial_slice_set_loading,
                                                 PRV48.48); rig stayed peers 0x3
  128 of 146 `included` lines carry ack=1 - `acknowledged()` resets the counter on
  most passes, so the cap rarely trips at all and the peer body republishes forever.
```
That is the documented storm (`settings/server/definition.h:111`: "the 127-body storm
that blocked a client's Tower load"); we hit 146. `membership_peer_retry_cap = 2` is a
TUNED BRAKE, not a misconfiguration. REVERTED to 2. Do not raise it again.

## 20.173 *** THE APPEARANCE GATE IS ONE HARDCODED ZERO IN OUR OWN ENCODER. THE ***
## *** HARVEST SUCCEEDED AND PROVED REGION A IS IDENTITY, NOT APPEARANCE - SO THE ***

### RESULT 1 - THE p2(112) NULL WAS MY ARITHMETIC (INCIDENT, and it cost a boot)
`profile_harvest` shipped `kWrapperRva = 0x1A6040` for `0x1417A6040`, whose RVA is
`0x17A6040` - a dropped digit. The wrong value STILL SAT INSIDE THE MODULE, so the
observer's `module_range` guard passed and the detour attached to an unrelated
function. p2(112) logged `install result=ok` and then nothing, through a Tower dwell,
a subclass swap AND a full character switch - all three read as content negatives
against an anchor that was never on the path.
LESSON, AND IT IS NOW A GATE, NOT PROSE: a range check proves an address is IN the
image; it never proves it is the RIGHT address. Only .pdata does.
INSTRUMENT ADDED: `RE_scripts/verify_hook_rvas.py` (TOOLS.md registered) - resolves
EVERY client-hook RVA constant against .pdata, exit 1 on any FRAGMENT / MID-FUNCTION /
UNRESOLVED. Run before any boot shipping a new or changed hook address. First run:
34 RVAs, 0 bad after the fix, 6 NOT-CODE (data addresses in other hooks, listed for
review, not gate failures).

### RESULT 2 - THE HARVEST WORKS (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION, p2(113), rva=0x17A6040)
Three commits, 15 dump lines, every one `ok=1`; calls 2 and 3 byte-identical.
```
  call=1 header=0x5 mask=0x1FF   call=2 header=0x7 mask=0x20   call=3 header=0x7 mask=0x1FF
  regionA 232B + tail 20B recovered per call
```
`mask=0x1FF` (nine bits) matches 20.145's "9-bit-masked copy of 8 verbatim sub-chunks".
Artifacts: RE_output/captures/p2-113_profile_harvest/ (call{1,2,3}_{regionA,tail}.bin,
mac_log_p2-113.log, lookup3_check.py).

### RESULT 3 - REGION A IS IDENTITY, NOT APPEARANCE. THIS IS THE FINDING.
Every nonzero byte of the full-mask blob (48 of 232), decoded:
```
  +  0..14   B088DD64FC7B71CE 67AAB6F1 3031      opaque ids ("3031" = ASCII "01")
  +128       0x0110000130AA9EC5                  THE MAC'S XUID, exactly
  +176       0xFFFF003D                          the two patch bytes; FFFF = both
                                                 sources were -1 (raw PHASE 2's sentinel)
  +192       0x9EAA300100100100                  account primary SOID (matches the
                                                 server's own ev=account identity line)
  +200       0x9EAA300100100101                  character SOID
  +208       0x44848000 = 1060.0f                a power/light value
  +212..219  FFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF                    -1 sentinels
  tail(20B)  all zero except bytes 11,12 = 01 01
```
NOT ONE gear hash, shader, ornament or geometry reference. Replaying region A would
give a peer an IDENTITY AND NO BODY. The client-memory harvest lane (handoff item 4,
"the last mile to two VISIBLE guardians") is aimed at the wrong data.

### RESULT 4 - RETRACTION: THE COMMIT HASH IS NOT AN ORACLE (mine, from the p2-112 brief)
BOOT_BRIEF_p2-112 and the observer header called arg8 "the blob's own expected hash
and therefore an ORACLE the dump can be checked against offline". WRONG - raw PHASE 2
says the hash is computed "over the PREVIOUS cached copy (a change-detection
optimization, NOT assembly-time signing)". Tested: lookup3 over the harvested blob AND
over the prior call's blob, at init 0xdeadbfd6 and 0, hashword and hashlittle - none
match arg8 (0xF88209E3). THE HARVEST IS THEREFORE UNVALIDATED: nothing proves these
232 bytes are complete and correctly ordered. That matters for any replay, since
20.145's whole point is byte-exactness. Do not claim a validated blob.

### RESULT 5 - THE ACTUAL GATE, IN CODE WE OWN (VERIFIED-BY-READING)
`middleware/gameplay/group/session_messages.cpp`:
```c
  /** A clear flag ends a player row after its identity group. The profile block it
   *  would gate has no writer here, so no row carries one. */
  constexpr std::uint64_t kPlayerProfileAbsent = 0;
  ...
  bool write_player_delta(...) {
      return ... && writer.write(kPlayerProfileAbsent, kFlagWidth);
  }
```
EVERY player row we publish carries a hardcoded 0 in the profile-present bit. That is
`pc=0` on every group_target row on both machines, and the fork's own comment says the
block it gates HAS NO WRITER. Combined with PHASE 5 (region B is written ONLY from a
wire delta) and PHASE 6 (no profile is ever on our wire): region B - the remaining
136B, and the only place appearance can live - is SERVER-SOURCED, and our server has
never written it.

### CONSEQUENCE - THE LANE CHANGES
The appearance milestone is SERVER-SIDE AUTHORING of the profile block, not a
client-memory harvest and not a replay of region A. The server already holds each
character's gear (this is the inventory fork). What is UNKNOWN and must be settled
BEFORE any encoder work - do not skip this the way the last three diagnoses were
skipped:
  1. What region B's 136 bytes actually contain. NOT established anywhere; PHASE 5
     found only its apply leaf (0x1417af2d0, one caller inside the L9 apply
     0x141781800). Its LAYOUT is unread.
  2. Whether the profile-present bit alone changes client behaviour, or whether a
     row with the bit set and a malformed block is worse than absent (20.145: the
     apply's ONE flag at delta+0x21 gates region A + region B + tail together).
  3. Whether `write_player_delta` is even the row the Tower render path reads.
The harvested region A remains useful as a FORMAT REFERENCE for the identity half.

### RESULT 1 - THE PROFILE GATE, READ END TO END (VERIFIED-BY-READING)
```
  0x141782408  mov dword [r11+0x1c], 0xffffffff     ; region A header <- absent
  0x141782410  mov dword [r11+0x108],0xffffffff     ; region B header <- absent
  0x14178241b  cmp byte [rbx+0x19], 0               ; rbx = delta+8, so delta+0x21
  0x14178241f  je  0x14178246e                      ; ABSENT -> skip BOTH helpers
  0x141782443  mov byte  [rsp+0x30], 0              ; arg7 VERIFY FLAG = 0
  0x14178243b  mov dword [rsp+0x38], 0xffffffff     ; arg8 expected hash = DUMMY
  0x141782450  mov dword [rsp+0x20], 0x1ff          ; arg5 mask = all 9 sub-chunks
  0x141782458  call 0x1417af360                     ; region A + both headers + tail
  0x14178245d  lea r8,[rbx+0x110]                   ; = delta+0x118 = region B
  0x141782469  call 0x1417af2d0                     ; region B
  0x141782479  add rbx, 0x1b8                       ; delta stride 440, per player
```
This CONFIRMS l9-profile-layout CLAIM 2/5 offsets independently (rbx = delta+8 makes
+0x19/+0x1c/+0x20/+0x28/+0x110/+0x198 read as delta +0x21/+0x24/+0x28/+0x30/+0x118/+0x1a0
- exactly the documented map).

### RESULT 2 - THE WIRE PATH VALIDATES NOTHING. THIS IS THE FINDING.
Inside helper A 0x1417AF360:
```
  0x1417af6d3  cmp byte [rbp+0x7d0], r13b   ; r13b == 0
  0x1417af6da  je  0x1417afa54              ; VERIFY == 0  ->  SKIP THE WHOLE HASH
  0x1417af6e0  mov r9d, 0xdeadbfd6          ; (only reached when verify != 0)
  0x1417af78a  cmp r13d, 0x13               ; 19 mix rounds (matches CLAIM 3)
  0x1417af824  cmp r9d, dword [rbp+0x7d8]   ; the ONLY use of arg8, inside that branch
```
FRAME ARITHMETIC (so the arg mapping is derived, not assumed): 8 pushes then
`lea rbp,[rsp-0x758]` puts the return address at rbp+0x798, hence arg5=rbp+0x7c0
(mask, matches `mov ebx,[rbp+0x7c0]`), arg6=rbp+0x7c8 (region A source, matches
`mov rcx,[rbp+0x7c8]`), **arg7=rbp+0x7d0 = the verify flag** - the exact byte compared
at 0x1417AF6D3 - and arg8=rbp+0x7d8 = the expected hash.
CLAIM: on the WIRE apply the client copies region A, both headers and the tail with NO
integrity check whatsoever. A server-authored profile block DOES NOT need a correct
lookup3(0xdeadbfd6) hash. The registry-commit path DOES pass a real hash and verify flag
(0x1417a6040's call site), which is what made the hash look mandatory in 20.173 R4.
RUNTIME CONFIRMATION IS PRE-NAMED as BOOT_BRIEF_p2-114 content negative 2.

### RESULT 3 - REGION B IS AN UNCONDITIONAL MEMCPY (VERIFIED-BY-READING)
0x1417AF2D0 in full: 8 x `movups` + 1 `movsd` = EXACTLY 136 bytes, source [r8],
destination `[rdx + rcx + 0x3c68]` with rdx = index*0x1a8 - i.e. table base 0x3b60 plus
entry offset 0x108, matching CLAIM 2 exactly. Then `inc dword [rcx+0xc]`,
`inc dword [rcx+0xe0e0]`, `ret`. NO branch, NO validation, NO hash.

### RESULT 4 - 0x1417AF2D0 IS NOT A FUNCTION START (and the new gate caught it)
`pdata_bounds 0x1417af2d0` -> "entry 0x1417AF28C..0x1417AF2C9 offset=0x44 ** OUTSIDE -
address is in a gap ** [FRAGMENT: primary fn 0x1417AE4B0..0x1417AEC2A]". Detouring it
would have been p2(112)'s error again and WORSE - a mid-function patch. It is therefore
NOT hooked; the p2(114) observer takes region B as regionA+0xE8 (delta+0x118 minus
delta+0x30), valid for the WIRE caller only, which is why every line carries a caller
RVA. `verify_hook_rvas.py` has now caught two real errors on its first day.

### RESULT 5 - O1 IS DEAD. CLOSE IT.
`serverDefaultEntity` has exactly THREE references tree-wide - the declaration and two
lines of settings parsing. NOTHING reads the gate. Its own TODO said so ("nothing reads
this gate... a visible class chain and a live outcome path do not"). FRONT_e2e-stack.md
listed it as the most on-target unexplored gate; it is not a lane, it is an empty
setting. Do not enable it expecting anything.

### RESULT 6 - A THIRD INSTRUMENT LIMIT, PRE-NAMED BEFORE IT COST ANYTHING
`handle_message_observer.cpp:118` HARDCODES `dir=down`. Every "the client never sends X
upstream" reading taken from that tape is therefore an instrument limit, NOT a finding.
Whether the client ever pushes a membership body (and with it a profile block) upstream
is OPEN and is answerable only by a pcap - which is why p2(114) captures tcp/30975 on
BOTH interfaces in BOTH directions.

The wire ENCODING of the profile block. The wire->delta DECODER was never located

## 20.176 *** THE DECODER IS CONFIRMED: 0x14173BFC0. ONE CANDIDATE REFUTED. THE PROFILE ***
## *** BLOCK'S WIRE ENCODING IS NOW SPECIFIED FIELD BY FIELD - IT IS PRESENCE-BIT ***
## *** PREFIXED SUB-CHUNKS, NOT RAW BYTES. (2026-08-29 ~15:2x, STATIC - NO BOOT) ***

Confirmation pass on 20.175 R5's two candidates. Both resolved by reading them.

### RESULT 1 - 0x14173B920 IS **REFUTED**. It is a COMPARATOR, not a decoder.
```
  0x14173b955  imul rcx, rax, 0x1b8      ; walk struct A entries
  0x14173b966  call 0x1416d33a0          ; normalize each
  0x14173b986  imul rcx, rax, 0x1b8      ; walk struct B entries
  0x14173b9a9  mov  r8d, 0x7980
  0x14173b9b5  call 0x14187e83e          ; memcmp 0x7980 bytes
  0x14173b9cb  sete al
```
Two update structures in, a bool out. It READS +0x174a and never writes it. Its byproduct
is still useful: the delta array base is **+0x4280**, stride 0x1b8, and one whole update
structure is **0x7980 bytes**.

### RESULT 2 - 0x14173BFC0 IS **CONFIRMED** AS THE WIRE->DELTA DECODER (V-R)
Bit-reader trio, now named: `0x1403513b0` read(width) x17, `0x140350ef0` read-flag x9,
`0x140351070` read-wide(r8d bits) x3.
```
  0x14173c0af  edx=6 ; call read  -> mov word [r14+0x1748], ax   ; MEMBER count, 6 bits
  0x14173c0d1  edx=6 ; call read  -> mov word [r14+0x174a], ax   ; PLAYER count, 6 bits
  0x14173c0e6  cmp cx,0x40 / ja bail                             ; members <= 64
  0x14173c0f0  cmp ax,0x40 / ja bail                             ; players <= 64
```
6 bits is exactly the fork's own `kPlayerMemberWidth = 6`. The apply's count field is
written HERE and nowhere else outside the diff.

### RESULT 3 - THE PLAYER ROW ENCODING, FIELD BY FIELD (rdi = entry+8, stride 0x1b8)
Every offset lands on l9-profile-layout CLAIM 5, independently derived:
```
  entry+0x00  slot             read 5 bits          (bounds: <= 0x1f)
  entry+0x04  present          read 1 bit           (!= 1 -> row skipped entirely)
  entry+0x08  identity-changed read flag
  entry+0x09  playerId         read 64 bits         (0x140351070, r8d=0x40)
  entry+0x14  memberIndex      read 6 bits          == kPlayerMemberWidth
  entry+0x18  ownedIndex       read <width from a .data global at rip+0x90c82b>
  entry+0x1c  addSequence      read 20 bits         == kPlayerSequenceWidth
  entry+0x20  flag             read flag
  entry+0x21  PROFILE-PRESENT  read flag   <<< THE GATE; 0 -> the whole block is skipped
  entry+0x24  header1          read 32 bits
  entry+0x28  header2          read 3 bits, then DEC (value = read(3) - 1)
  entry+0x30  REGION A  -> call 0x1416d3670  (bool; fail aborts the row)
  entry+0x118 REGION B  -> call 0x1416d3dd0  (bool; fail aborts the row)
  entry+0x1a0 TAIL      -> call 0x14170f4f0
```
Our fork's `write_player_delta` matches every width it currently writes. The block after
the gate bit is what it has never written.

### RESULT 4 - THE BLOCK IS NOT RAW BYTES. THIS CORRECTS THE OBVIOUS GUESS.
Both region wrappers add a zero-initialised 4th argument and tail into the real readers
(A -> 0x1416D33C0, B -> 0x1416D3C30). Region A's reader:
```
  0x1416d33f6  test r8d, r8d            ; the wrapper's 0
  0x1416d33f9  jne  <skip>
  0x1416d33ff  call 0x140350ef0         ; READ ONE PRESENCE BIT
  0x1416d3406  je   <skip>              ; 0 -> this sub-chunk is ABSENT
  0x1416d340c  or   dword [rdi], 1      ; set bit 0 of the MASK
  0x1416d340f  lea  r9d, [rbp+0x40]     ; width = arg + 64
  0x1416d3428  lea  rdx, [rip+0x5c78e9] ; descriptor table
  0x1416d343a  call 0x14129aa80         ; the payload read
```
So REGION A IS A SERIES OF OPTIONAL SUB-CHUNKS, EACH PREFIXED BY A 1-BIT PRESENCE FLAG,
and the 9-bit "mask" the apply receives is BUILT BY THE READER from those wire bits - it
is not a field anyone sends. That is why `mask=0x1FF` (all nine present) is what the apply
passes on a full row, and it explains 20.145's "9-bit-masked copy of 8 verbatim
sub-chunks" from the other side of the wire.
CONSEQUENCE: the natural guess - "two 32-bit headers then 232+136+20 raw bytes" - IS
WRONG, and shipping it would have produced a body the client mis-parses from the profile
bit onward, corrupting every later field including the trailing groups and the state hash.
Not shipping that guess was correct.

### WHAT REMAINS FOR A WRITER (small, named, and static)
  1. The sub-chunk table behind `rdx = rip+0x5c78e9` (region A) - how many chunks, each
     one's width. CLAIM 3's sizes (128+48+2+1+1+8+16+13+4, 11B of gaps) are the candidate
     but were read from the COPY side; confirm against this table.
  2. The same for region B (0x1416D3C30) and the tail (0x14170F4F0, 168B).
  3. Then mirror it in `write_player_delta` behind a settings switch, with the harvested
     region A (20.173) as the identity-half content.
No boot is needed for 1-3. The gate bit, all field widths, the entry offsets, the absence
of any hash check (20.174 R2 / 20.175 R3) and the block's structure are now all known.

## 20.177 *** THE PROFILE BLOCK'S WIRE ENCODING IS FULLY READ. A MINIMAL WRITER IS ***
## *** SPECIFIED BIT BY BIT WITH NOTHING GUESSED, AND ITS ONE HAZARD (REGION A ***
## *** RETURNING FALSE) IS DISCHARGED BY CONSTRUCTION. (2026-08-29 ~15:4x, STATIC) ***

Continuation of 20.176. Every width below was read from the decoder, not inferred.
NO CODE WAS WRITTEN - the session ended at the spec. This entry IS the implementation
contract; HANDOFF_2026-08-29_PROFILE-WRITER.md carries it forward.

### RESULT 1 - THE THREE REGION READERS, RESOLVED
```
  region A  entry+0x30   0x1416D3670 -> 0x1416D33C0   9 optional sub-chunks, returns bool
  region B  entry+0x118  0x1416D3DD0 -> 0x1416D3C30   ONE presence bit, then optional body
  tail      entry+0x1a0  0x14170F4F0                  FIXED, unconditional, no presence bit
```

### RESULT 2 - REGION A'S SUB-CHUNK TABLE (read from the code, in WIRE ORDER)
Each entry = one presence bit, then (if set) the payload. **THE WIRE ORDER IS NOT BIT
ORDER** - 0x100 is emitted FIFTH. An encoder driven off the mask value desyncs.
```
 # | mask  | payload read                                  | destination
 1 | 0x001 | 0x14129AA80: 16-bit words until a ZERO word,   | rsi+0x00 (128B)
   |       | max 64; then XOR-deobfuscated in the caller    |
 2 | 0x002 | 0x140A496E0                                    | rsi+0x80
 3 | 0x004 | read 8 bits                     -> word        | rsi+0xb0
 4 | 0x008 | read 6 bits (0x140351530), DEC  -> byte        | rsi+0xb2
 5 | 0x100 | read 6 bits (0x140351530), DEC  -> byte        | rsi+0xb3
 6 | 0x010 | 0x1416D3B20  (its result feeds `setne r15b`)   | rsi+0xb8
 7 | 0x020 | two 64-bit reads                               | -
 8 | 0x040 | 0x1404C74B0                                    | -
 9 | 0x080 | read 32 bits, then 0x1416BE1E0                 | rsi+0xe0
```
NOTE chunks 4 and 5 `dec al` AFTER the read: the stored byte is `read(6) - 1`. To store
0 you must write **1**. Writing 0 stores 0xFF.
Both bit readers are MSB-first over a 64-bit accumulator ([rcx+0x28] value, [rcx+0x30]
count, [rcx+0x24] position), so a 6-bit zero is six zero bits - the same convention our
`bits::Writer` already uses, which is why the client decodes our bodies today.

### RESULT 3 - THE TAIL (0x14170F4F0), FIXED AND UNCONDITIONAL
```
  read 32 -> [rdi+0x00]   read 32 -> [rdi+0x04]   read 32 -> [rdi+0x08]
  read  5 -> [rdi+0x0c]   read  2 -> [rdi+0x0d]   read flag -> [rdi+0x0e]
  then: test byte [rdi+0x0c], 0x10 ; je end   <- a CONDITIONAL extra section
```
104 bits minimum. **Bit 0x10 of the 5-bit field must be CLEAR** or a further section is
read and the stream desyncs.

### RESULT 4 - THE HAZARD, AND WHY THE MINIMAL WRITER IS SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION
Region A returns bool. On FALSE the decoder SKIPS region B's read
(`0x14173c31e je 0x14173c33b`) while still reading the tail - so a false return desyncs
the body from that point on, corrupting the trailing groups and the state hash. Its exit:
```
  test r15b,r15b            -> false unless chunk 6 ran and failed
  call 0x1416BE1E0          -> name-terminator scan; TRUE iff a zero word exists in 64
  byte[rsi+0xb2] +1 <= 0x20 -> range check
  byte[rsi+0xb3] +1 <= 0x20 -> range check
  mov al,1
```
The minimal writer discharges ALL FOUR deterministically, without depending on the delta
buffer's prior contents (which nothing is proven to zero - the only two 0x7980 immediates
in .text are the comparator's memcmp and one in the diff, neither a memset):
  - chunk 6 ABSENT -> `setne r15b` never runs -> r15b keeps its entry value 1.
  - chunk 1 PRESENT with a single 0x0000 word -> word[0] deobfuscates to
    (0 * 0x7b4f) XOR 0 = 0 -> the scan finds its terminator at index 0 -> TRUE.
  - chunks 4 and 5 PRESENT with 6-bit value 1 -> stored 1-1 = 0 -> 0+1 = 1 <= 0x20, twice.

### RESULT 5 - THE WRITER CONTRACT (drop-in for `write_player_delta`)
Replace `writer.write(kPlayerProfileAbsent, kFlagWidth)` with, when enabled:
```
  write(1, 1)                    // profile-present            entry+0x21
  write(header1, 32)             //                            entry+0x24
  write(header2 + 1, 3)          // decoder DECs it            entry+0x28
  // --- region A, 9 presence bits IN WIRE ORDER ---
  write(1, 1); write(0, 16)      // #1 0x001 name: one zero word = terminator
  write(0, 1)                    // #2 0x002 absent
  write(0, 1)                    // #3 0x004 absent
  write(1, 1); write(1, 6)       // #4 0x008 -> byte 0xb2 = 0
  write(1, 1); write(1, 6)       // #5 0x100 -> byte 0xb3 = 0
  write(0, 1)                    // #6 0x010 absent  (KEEPS r15b = 1)
  write(0, 1)                    // #7 0x020 absent
  write(0, 1)                    // #8 0x040 absent
  write(0, 1)                    // #9 0x080 absent
  // --- region B ---
  write(0, 1)                    // absent
  // --- tail, fixed ---
  write(t0,32); write(t1,32); write(t2,32);
  write(t3, 5);                  // bit 0x10 MUST be clear
  write(t4, 2); write(t5, 1);
```
Total 150 bits. Suggested initial values, REPLAYED from the p2(113) harvest rather than
invented (region A's tail bytes were 00*11, 01, 01, then zeros):
`header1=0, header2=0, t0=0, t1=0, t2=0x01000000, t3=1, t4=0, t5=0`.

### WHAT THIS WRITER DOES AND DOES NOT DO
It sets `pc=1` on the peer's player row with an EMPTY profile. It will NOT render a
guardian - there is no name, no appearance, and region A carries identity only (20.173
R3). It is the decoder-correct minimum that tests whether the present bit alone changes
client behaviour, and it is the scaffold every richer block is written into.
A FULL block additionally needs the payload encodings of chunks 2, 6, 7, 8, 9
(0x140A496E0, 0x1416D3B20, 0x1404C74B0, 0x1416BE1E0's writer side) - all unread.
NO HASH IS REQUIRED anywhere: the wire apply passes verify=0 (20.174 R2, corroborated at
runtime 20.175 R3).

Writer implemented exactly per 20.177 RESULT 5 in write_player_delta (session_messages.cpp),

### RESULT 1 - THE WRITER'S BITS ARE CORRECT (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION, LOCAL SMOKE)
A native smoke harness compiled session_messages.cpp + read the body back field-by-field the
way the client decoder would: flag-off body 1265 bits, flag-on +177 bits exactly (gate bit 1
replaces the absent flag; 32+3 header, region A 37 bits -> reader-built mask 0x109, region B

the gate bit (177 after it). The decoder-read field list wins; the code comment records this.

### RESULT 2 - THE A/B: FLAG OFF EVERYTHING WORKS, FLAG ON WEDGES (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION)
Flag OFF: stage=player result=added, join completed, zero fails - identical to p2(114).
Flag ON: fail loop. The A/B ran twice (16:26 fresh server; 17:0x fresh server), same result
both times. The flag is the only delta; clients unchanged (45ef17f93b901db9) throughout.

### RESULT 3 - THE MECHANISM, MEASURED WITH THE p2(115) INSTRUMENT LINES (VERIFIED-BY-EXECUTION)
The new svc/dtls instrument lines (deployed with the wedge live) name every step:
  - The link FLAPS between connecting (3) and connected (5): 1652 skip lines at stage=3 vs
    99 at stage=5. application_ready (peer_transport.cpp:967) requires stage==connected, so
    publish_snapshot flaps held/released with it (the stage=publish result=held lines).
  - At connected+ready, the player-add publish ENQUEUES the flag-on body: count=12 fragments
    (queue slots are 32B each; kOutboundSlots=24). The server RESENDS every 250ms (resendDue).
  - THE CLIENT NEVER ACKS: the queue never drains (count=12 -> 24 = two stacked copies hit
    the 24-slot cap -> every further enqueue fails = the 1/sec "stage=player result=fail"
    loop). ~15s later the client REBUILDS the channel (connect result=ok rebuilt=1), and the
    rebuild does *peer = {} (peer_transport.cpp:268) WHICH WIPES THE QUEUED BODY. Cycle.
  - THE CLIENT NEVER DECODES THE BODY: ev=ingress stage=apply fired ZERO times with a wire
    caller (all 8/machine calls remain the local registry commit 0x17A6101). The profile-apply
    helper fires DURING decode, so the refusal happens BEFORE the player row's profile bit.
  - The client stays HEALTHY and in-world throughout (it publishes its own membership to the
    server: inbound large=1..4 fragments, drop=0). Only the server->client membership dies.
SIZE IS RULED OUT: p2(114) accepted 14-fragment flag-off bodies (members=3 players=2); the
refused flag-on body is 12 fragments (members=2 players=1). FRAGMENT COUNT IS RULED OUT.
The client's refusal is CONTENT-DRIVEN, somewhere inside the 178-bit block, and it aborts
before the apply helper. NOTE: earlier "server sends nothing" readings were CAPTURE WINDOW
ARTIFACTS - the captures kept landing in the connecting phase. Full-traffic capture (any
port, both hosts) confirms zero S>R during connecting-phase windows and the resend cadence
exists only inside connected windows. Trust the svc instrument lines over capture timing.

### RESULT 4 - WHAT THE FLAG-ON BODY DOES NOT DO
It never reaches the apply (so pc stays 0 everywhere), it never corrupts client state (the
client is healthy, moving, in-world; its own membership publishes continue), and it costs
nothing to recover (the queue wipe is automatic via the client's own rebuild; the flag is a
settings flip). The p2(110) baseline behaviour is fully preserved with the flag off.

### NEXT - THE DECISIVE EXPERIMENT (small, named)
Extend write_minimal_profile (or add a sibling) to replay the p2(113) HARVESTED REAL region-A
block byte-for-byte (RE_output/captures/p2-113_profile_harvest/, ~260B marker+regionA+header+
tail) instead of the minimal 178-bit shape, behind the same flag. If the client ACCEPTS the
real-shaped block: the minimal block's content is subtly wrong and the byte diff names where.
If the client REFUSES it too: the refusal is STRUCTURAL (an unstated hash/shape validation) -
and the "no hash is required" conclusion (20.174 R2) needs re-examination for the TRANSPORT
layer as opposed to the profile apply. Either answer closes the mechanism question.
The refused body is reproducible byte-exact locally: the smoke harness renders it (flag=true,
members=2/players=1) - no wire capture is required to obtain it.

## 20.179 *** STATIC: THE PROFILE BLOCK'S WIRE ENCODING IS NOW ~FULLY DERIVED (ALL CHUNKS ***
## *** BUT 8), THE NAME OBFUSCATION FORMULA IS COMPLETE, AND THE APPLY HELPER HAS NO ***
## *** PRE-CALL GATE - THE WEDGE LIVES IN DECODE-OR-REASSEMBLY, NOT THE APPLY. ***
## *** (2026-08-29 ~19:xx, STATIC - disasm_fn.py on destiny2_unpacked_full.exe) ***

Follows 20.178. Goal: make the real-sheet replay buildable and localize the wedge.

### RESULT 1 - NAME DEOBFUSCATION FORMULA, COMPLETE (VERIFIED-BY-DISASSEMBLY 0x1416D3460)
The region-A caller deobfuscates the name IN THE SCRATCH BUFFER, in place, per word:
    plain[i] = key16(i) ^ ((wire_word[i] * 0x7b4f) & 0xFFFF)
    key16(0) = 0;  key16(i>0) = rotl32(0xC245B0C4, i mod 31) & 0xFFFF
    (the 0x8421085/0x1f sequence at 0x1416d347b is a divide-by-31; the rol is 32-bit)
The loop writes the word back BEFORE the zero test, so the TERMINATOR word is stored as
key16(L) (0 only when L=0 - which is why the empty name survives the zero-scan). Writer
inverse: wire_word[i] = ((plain[i] ^ key16(i)) * inv(0x7b4f mod 2^16)) & 0xFFFF.

### RESULT 2 - CHUNK-BY-CHUNK WIRE<->STORED MAP (VERIFIED-BY-DISASSEMBLY, orchestrator 0x1416D33C0)
  #1 name   0x14129AA80: words until zero word into scratch rsp+0x30, deobf in place, then
      lands at rsi+0x00 (128B). Empty name safe: key16(0)=0 keeps index 0 zero.
  #2 0x140A496E0: read_wide 288 bits -> 36B copied VERBATIM to rsi+0x80 (+4B from the read's
      tail dword; output struct 40B zeroed first). Gate 0x14034E9B0 = reader-OVERRUN check
      only ([rcx+0x24] > [rcx+0x10]*8, or error flag [rcx+0x1c]) - NOT content-based.
  #3 read 8 bits -> word rsi+0xb0.  #4/#5 read 6 bits (0x140351530), DEC, -> bytes 0xb2/0xb3.
  #6 0x1416D3B20: TWO 16-bit reads, SIGN-extended (bt eax,0xf) -> dwords rsi+0xb8/0xbc.
      Returns al=1 ALWAYS (when r8d=0) - so chunk 6 present also keeps r15b=1; only ABSENT
      leaves it at the entry value. 20.177 R4's hazard analysis holds either way.
  #7 two read_wide(0x40=64-bit) -> rsi+0xc0, rsi+0xc8 verbatim.
  #8 0x1404C74B0 is a THUNK -> 0x1404C1930 = a SCHEMA-TABLE-DRIVEN engine (schema id from a
      global dword; bucket=row via >>13/&0x1fff hash walk; generic field dispatch). Wire form
      of the 13B stored region depends on SCHEMA CONTENT - the only chunk NOT yet derivable.
      (The disasm spill at 0x1404C74D0+ is the schema_capture-hooked function - consistent.)
  #9 read 32 bits -> dword rsi+0xe0 verbatim.
CONSEQUENCE: the real-sheet replay is buildable with 8 of 9 chunks (all but #8) from the
p2(113) harvest + RESULT 1. The stored layout matches CLAIM 3 (128+48+2+1+1+8+16+13+4+gaps).

### RESULT 3 - THE APPLY HELPER HAS NO PRE-CALL GATE (VERIFIED-BY-DISASSEMBLY 0x141782458)
The wire apply 0x141781800 calls the region helper 0x1417AF360 per delta entry (stride 0x1b8,
rbx) gated ONLY by `cmp byte [rbx+0x19], 0` - the decoded profile-present byte (entry+0x19 =
the +0x21 field of 20.176 R3 with rdi=entry+8). Args: r9d=[rbx+0x20] = the reader-built 9-bit
mask; stack arg 0x1ff = mask cap. Then 0x1417AF2D0 (tail applier) - it IS called directly, so
it is a valid entry despite pdata-bounds flagging it mid-function. NO STATE-HASH COMPARE
precedes the helper. Therefore: had the flag-on body reached this apply, ingress would have
fired. It did not => the body dies BEFORE the apply: in reassembly, dispatch, or DECODE.
Combined with 20.177 R4 (region A exit conditions discharged by construction), the decode's
remaining suspects are the name reader walk (0x14129AA80 + its descriptor table) and the
scratch->destination copy; the transport (fragment consumer/ack) is the other candidate.
Note: 0x1417AF2D0 being a real call target softens HANDOFF item 6's "do not hook" - it is
mid-function of something larger but legitimately entered; re-check before any hook.
The msg-30 handler registration was located: entry = id 0x1e, declared struct size 0x7980
(= 31104 = our kMembershipUpdateSize, corroborated), decoder 0x14173BFC0 at entry+0x28,
register helper 0x1416E2C50, entry stride 0x40, table object passed by rcx.
